# pyfect ↔ Effect v4.0.6 parity plan

## 1. Verdict

**No, pyfect is not 1:1 with Effect.** Weighted overall parity is about **94%** after M16: the average of the table below by its weights (9,439 / 100; 9,383 after M15, 9,313 after M14). Earlier entries carried about 90% by hand after M11, while the rows had already moved past it (90% after the M11 completeness pass, 90% after the collections coverage pass, 89% after the core-B coverage pass, 86% after the core-A coverage pass, 84% after M10g, 83% after M10f, 82% after M10e, 80% after M10d, 79% after M10c, 77% after M10b, 76% after M10a, 76% after M10 wave 1, 75% after M9, 72% after M8, 64% after M7, 59% after M6, 50% after M5, 43% after M4, 38% after M3, 34% after M2, 27% after M1, 16% at the audit). Counting only the runtime core, the same table without its Platform row, it is about **94%** (8,449 / 90). The 89% recorded here through M15 was carried by hand and no longer followed the rows; that earlier series reads 88% before the M11 completeness pass, 85% before the core-B coverage pass, 83% before the core-A coverage pass, 79% after M8, 71% after M7, 66% after M6, 56% after M5, 48% after M4, 42% after M3, 37% after M2, 30% after M1, 18% at the audit.

The fiber runtime (fiber loop, `gen`, interruption regions, supervision) follows v4, and the divergences the audit confirmed are fixed (§2, "Fix status" and "Resolved after M0"); what still differs on purpose is under "Still open" and in §4. What is missing is listed by name below; the largest single item is Effect's own OTLP exporters (§3, M16). Four things wait on a decision by the user (§5, items 12 to 15): a bare number as a `Duration`, 39 security-hardening rows, 7 Effect-bug rows and custom `PubSub` strategies.

How to read the numbers: a Python-native equivalent (for example `timedelta` for Duration) counts as covered. Weights reflect how much downstream code depends on each area. These percentages are judgments per area, except where a row names a count; `docs/coverage/` holds the export counts and §3 the title counts they rest on.

| Area | Coverage | Weight | Biggest gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Effect core A (constructors, errors, Exit, Cause, Result) | 96% (was 93% before the M11 completeness pass, 80% before M11) | 10 | none listed: every `Effect.test.ts` title is ported or omitted with a reason (M11 completeness pass); every export of Effect, Effectable, Exit, Cause, Result, Option, Filter, Predicate and Function is ported or a §4 decision (docs/coverage/core-a.md, from `scripts/api_coverage.py core-a`) |
| Effect core B (concurrency, resources, runtime entry) | 90% (was 70%) | 10 | a bare number as a `Duration` is seconds where Effect's is milliseconds, open for a decision (§5); every export of Scope, Layer, Context, ManagedRuntime, Fiber, References, Scheduler, Semaphore, Latch and Runtime is ported or a §4 decision (docs/coverage/core-b.md, from `scripts/api_coverage.py core-b`) |
| Fibers and sync primitives | 95% (93% before M16 ported `Scheduler` and `MixedScheduler` with Effect's custom dispatcher tests; was 88%) | 6 | fibers under one provided custom scheduler share a dispatcher where Effect makes one per fiber (§4); two rows are Effect bugs left for a decision (§5) |

| Schedule, Clock, DateTime, Cron, Random | 92% (90% before M16 ported Intl formatting on the `intl` extra; was 22%) | 5 | ICU's canonical zone names, V8's legacy date parser, `Schema.Duration` past `timedelta`'s range (§4 `c open`); DateTime pure parts are the standard library |
| Observability (log, trace, metric, console) | 90% (was 3%) | 7 | Effect's own OTLP exporters (pyfect exports through the OpenTelemetry SDK), 71 titles open since M16 reclassified the row as `c open`; `PrometheusMetrics.layerHttp` is `pyfect.http.prometheus.layer_http` (M14), `Effect.fn` pipeables were done in the core-B coverage pass |

| Collections and pure utilities | 100% (was 90% before Graph, 60% before the collections coverage pass) | 3 | every export of the pure modules (Graph, Chunk, Iterable, Record, Tuple, Struct, Number, BigInt, BigDecimal, Boolean, String, Duration, ByteSize, HashMap, HashSet, MutableHashMap / Set, MutableList, MutableRef, Trie, HashRing, SemVer, SemVerRange, TextDiff, the binary encodings) and Crypto is ported or a §4 decision (docs/coverage/collections.md, from `scripts/api_coverage.py collections`); Effect v4 has no RedBlackTree, SortedMap or SortedSet |

was 95% before M12, 91% after M10g, 86% after M10f, 72% after M10e, 53% after M10d, 47% after M10c, 29% after M10b, 15% after M10a, 3% before) | 10 | M10a done (PlatformError, Path, FileSystem, Stdio, Terminal, final `run_main`, ChildProcess, HttpClient on httpx, socket with WebSocket and UDP, net); M10g done (the cluster runtime: Entity, Sharding, Runners, RunnerServer, ClusterCron, EntityProxy, ClusterWorkflowEngine, socket and HTTP runners); M10h done (atom-core: `AsyncResult`, `Atom`, `AtomRegistry`, `AtomRef`; atom-integrations: `Hydration`, `AtomRpc`, `AtomHttpApi`, `AsyncResult.Schema`; the `packages/atom` React, Solid and Vue bindings are out of scope); M12 ai-openai done (`pyfect.ai.openai`, below); M12 structured-output done (`to_codec_anthropic`, `to_codec_openai`, below); M12 decision-model done (`decision`, `decision_model`, `pyfect.ai.cloudflare`, `pyfect.ai.typesafe`, below); M12 ai-openrouter done (`pyfect.ai.openrouter`, below); M12 mcp-features done with M14 mcp-server-rest (strict tool schemas, log levels, HTTP admission, batches, subscriptions, `input_required` round trips, `layerHttpRequestScoped`). The figure is the share of Effect's platform source lines ported, about 170k of 171k: all of `encoding`'s 14.9k is ported since M15. (M14 added the rest of the MCP server features, `encoding/Toml` and RPC over stdio. M12 added about 2.5k: `Decision` / `DecisionModel` 1.1k, `AnthropicStructuredOutput` / `OpenAiStructuredOutput` with the internal `structured-output.ts` 1k, and part of the MCP server features; M10h added about 6.5k: `effect/src/reactivity` but `Reactivity`, counted under M10b, and `Atom.searchParam`; M10g added the 8.4k cluster runtime; M10f added about 26k: `graphql` 4.3k, `eventlog` 9.7k but the IndexedDB journal, `devtools` 1.1k, `cluster` 10.6k of its 19k (all but the 8.4k runtime above), `SqlModel` 0.4k; `VariantSchema` / `Model`, 1.6k, count under Schema; `effect/src`'s platform directories and `platform-node(-shared)`; M10e added about 31k: `workflow` 4.1k, `ai` about 27k of its 33k (not `DecisionModel` / `Decision`, the structured-output codecs, `Telemetry`'s GenAI helpers, and the MCP features listed under M10e mcp); M10d added about 9.8k: `persistence` 8.5k, `workers` with `RpcWorker` 0.8k, platform-node's `NodeRedis` / `NodeWorker` / `NodeWorkerRunner` 0.5k; M10c added about 31k: the server half of `http` with `FindMyWay` and `MultipartParser` 12.6k and its compression internals 1k, `http-api` 10.5k, `rpc` 7.3k but `RpcWorker`, stdio and SchemaBinary, platform-node's HTTP server files 1.9k but HTTP/2; M10b added about 24k: `sql` but `SqlModel` 4.5k, `cli` 18.4k, `Yaml` / `Ini` 0.9k, `Reactivity` 0.4k). The drivers' own packages are outside that count: `sql-sqlite-node` (0.5k) is ported, and of `sql-pg` (8.2k) everything but the wire protocol and codecs, which psycopg and libpq provide; so are the AI provider packages: `ai-anthropic` (20k), `ai-openai-compat` (4.2k), `ai-openai` (77k, most of it `Generated.ts`, which a script converts; `OpenAiClientGenerated` included since M14), `ai-openrouter` (40k, most of it generated, its client included), `ai-cloudflare` (0.6k) and `ai-typesafe` (0.6k) are ported; M13 ported `sql-mysql2` (0.7k), `sql-mssql` (1.2k), `sql-libsql` (0.5k), `sql-clickhouse` (0.7k) and `sql-d1` (0.4k) |

## 2. Confirmed bugs and divergences in existing code (fix first)

Every item below was reproduced by running pyfect against Effect's source and tests. The repro scripts are in `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-tylergibbs-pyfect/80c08bf0-144a-402b-8209-aeaa4f25c92f/scratchpad/parity/<group>/`. Several audit groups found the same bug independently; those findings are merged into one row here.

**Status (2026-10-26): all 32 items are fixed.** Each one has a test that pins Effect's behaviour (see the table at the end of this section), and every repro script now prints Effect's result. The remaining differences are listed under "Still open" below.

### Critical / high

| # | Divergence | Merged IDs | Fix direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **No defect election.** `catch_all`, `catch_tag`, `match`, `match_effect`, `result`, `ignore`, `tap_error` and `retry` recover from a cause containing Fail+Die. Finalizer and sibling causes are combined without promoting Fail→Die, so a failing finalizer can be "recovered". | A-DIV-1, B-D3, ERR-1, FIN-1, retry-retries-with-defects | Add `Cause.elect_defects()` and `Cause.find_recoverable_error()`, then route **every** typed-recovery combinator through them. Add one `combine_finalizer_cause` helper for `_run_finalizer`, `CloseableScope.close` and `_concurrency`. Delete public use of `first_failure` so the mistake can't come back. |
| 2 | **Interruption that skips a recovery handler keeps typed Fails**, so `run_sync` raises the typed error instead of `Interrupted`. | A-DIV-2, B-D4, INT-1 | When unwinding past a recovery `_MatchCause` while interrupted and interruptible, strip Fail reasons before adding the Interrupt. Interruptibility-restore frames must not count as recovery frames. |
| 3 | **Yielding a non-Effect inside `gen` parks the fiber forever.** | A-DIV-3 | `_GenFrame._on_success` should check `isinstance(nxt, Effect)` and die with `TypeError`. The fiber loop's else-branch should match an explicit `SUSPEND` sentinel instead of "anything else". |
| 4 | **`acquire_use_release` leaks the resource when `use` raises synchronously.** | B-D1, AUR-1 | Wrap the call as `_Suspend(lambda: restore(use(a)))` inside the `on_exit` region. Root cause: add a single `_call_user(f, *args)` helper that always runs user callbacks under `_Suspend`, and use it in every resource combinator. |
| 5 | **An `on_exit`/`ensuring` callback that raises drops the original cause.** | B-D2, FIN-2 | Evaluate the finalizer lazily through the same `_call_user` helper, so the raise becomes a Die that is combined with the original cause (via #1). |
| 6 | **Interrupt delivery is asynchronous.** A fiber interrupted before it starts still runs its body, and a self-interrupt does not stop the running fiber. | fiber-interrupt-before-start, FIB-1 | Set `_interrupt` synchronously when on the scheduler thread. Finish not-yet-started or parked interruptible fibers immediately. `_run` must no-op once `_exit` is set. |
| 7 | **`Queue.end()`/`fail()` drop offers suspended on a full queue.** Items are lost and the offer returns False. | queue-end-drops-pending-offers, Q-1 | Model state as `Open | Closing(cause) | Done(cause)`. Parked offers own their item and are admitted as space frees, and the queue becomes Done only when drained. This is a prerequisite for #19. |
| 8 | **`Deferred.complete_with` hangs forever if the completing fiber is interrupted, runs its effect even when already done, and its name means the opposite of Effect's `completeWith`.** | deferred-complete-with-interrupted, deferred-complete-with-runs-when-done, DEF-1 | Rename to match Effect: `complete(exit)`→`done(exit)`, the memoizing version→`complete(eff)` = `_Suspend(done-check) + uninterruptible_mask(restore(eff).exit().flat_map(done))`, and a new lazy `complete_with(eff)` that stores the effect. |
| 9 | **Scope finalizers are never deregistered.** `fork_scoped` fibers and closed child scopes accumulate in long-lived scopes without bound. | B-D5, SCOPE-1, ctx-scope-finalizer-leak, fork-scoped-finalizer-leak | Store finalizers in an insertion-ordered dict keyed by owner. A child scope removes itself from its parent on close, and `fork_scoped` removes its entry through a fiber exit observer. |
| 10 | **A `fork_scoped` fiber that closes its own scope deadlocks** (its finalizer interrupts and awaits itself). | ctx-fork-scoped-self-close-deadlock | Make the finalizer `_WithFiber(lambda cur: void if cur is fiber else fiber.interrupt())`. |
| 11 | **`fork_scoped` into an already-closed scope still runs the work.** | SCOPE-2 | Check that the scope is closed before starting, and return an already-interrupted fiber. |
| 12 | **A concurrent `for_each`/`all` interrupted from outside reports only Interrupt** and drops child defects and sibling failures. | CONC-1 | On external interrupt, wait for all workers, then combine their causes (with defect election) plus the Interrupt. |
| 13 | **`TestClock.adjust` hangs forever if any fiber busy-loops.** | TC-1 | Settle on the wake set (track the tasks scheduled transitively by woken sleepers) rather than global idleness, and warn after `warning_delay` instead of hanging. |
| 14 | **`sleep(nan)` hangs SyncScheduler** (even an enclosing `timeout` never fires) and raises TypeError on asyncio. | sleep-nan-hangs | `to_seconds` maps NaN→0.0 (Effect semantics). Parse at the boundary so every Duration consumer is fixed at once. |
| 15 | **`Layer.fresh` memoizes its dependencies in the caller's MemoMap.** | ctx-layer-fresh-deps, LAY-1 | `fresh` builds its subgraph with a new `MemoMap()`. |
| 16 | **Nested `provide` never reuses the outer MemoMap**, so pyfect always behaves like Effect's `local: true`. | ctx-nested-provide-memo, LAY-2 | Add a `CurrentMemoMap` tag and a parent-chained MemoMap. `provide` forks the ambient map; `provide(local=True)` creates a fresh one. |
| 17 | **`run_main` ignores SIGTERM**: no interruption, finalizers skipped, exit 143. | run_main-sigterm | Install a SIGTERM (and SIGINT) handler that interrupts the main fiber, then exit. |
| 18 | **`TaggedError` lacks structural eq/hash and ignores `message`/`cause` fields.** Dataclass errors are unhashable, plain errors compare by identity, `str()` shows a field dump, and `__cause__` is never set. | tagged-error-unhashable, tagged-error-plain-identity-eq, tagged-error-message-cause | Install structural `__eq__`/`__hash__` (type + fields or args) in `__init_subclass__` after `@dataclass` runs. Use a `message` field for `str()` and a `cause` field for `__cause__`. Do not force `frozen=True`. |

### Medium / low

| # | Divergence | IDs | Fix direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| 19 | `bounded(0)` is rejected; Effect supports rendezvous queues. | queue-capacity-zero-rejected, Q-2 | Allow `capacity >= 0` and hand off directly from pending offers (needs #7). |
| 20 | `offer_all` returns None (Effect returns the rejected items) and is not atomic. `shutdown` returns None (Effect returns bool). `take_up_to(0)` blocks on an empty queue. | queue-offer-all-returns-none, Q-3, queue-shutdown-returns-none, queue-take-up-to-zero-blocks | `offer_all -> Effect[list[A]]` with admission in one step; `shutdown -> Effect[bool]`; `n <= 0` returns `[]` after the Done check. |
| 21 | Only the first interruptor is recorded. | B-D6, fiber-multiple-interruptors | Add `Cause.add_interrupts` (dedupe by fiber_id) and use it in `_deliver_interrupt` and `_with_interrupt`. |
| 22 | `for_each`/`all` read the iterable when the effect is built, not when it runs. | B-D7 | Move `list(items)` inside `_Suspend`. Validate concurrency eagerly if desired. Check `queue.py:93` for the same pattern. |
| 23 | `or_die` turns every Fail into `Die(UnhandledError)` (Effect dies once, with the first error). | A-DIV-4 | `or_die = catch_all(lambda e: die(_as_exception(e)))` once #1 lands. |
| 24 | Schedule: `fibonacci` yields 1,1,2,3 (Effect: 1,2,3,5). `fixed` waits for the next beat when behind (Effect: delay 0). `up_to` stops at elapsed == limit (Effect keeps going while `<=`). | sched-fibonacci-sequence, sched-fixed-running-behind/SCH-1, sched-upto-boundary/SCH-2 | Port Effect's step logic exactly and update the tests that pin the current behavior. |
| 25 | `retry` re-raises the full original cause (including Interrupts) when exhausted. Effect fails with the last error only. | retry-exhausted-cause | `return (yield from fail(last_error))`. |
| 26 | `run_main` exits 1 with a traceback on an interrupt-only cause (Effect exits 130) and has no per-error exit-code hook. | obs-runmain-interrupt-exit, run_main-exit-codes | Map the Exit: 0 on success, `SystemExit(130)` when interrupt-only, otherwise raise or use the error's `exit_code: ClassVar[int]`. |
| 27 | `Cause.pretty()` has no tracebacks or cause chain. | obs-cause-pretty-no-trace | Use `traceback.format_exception` for Die, and for Fail when the error is a BaseException. |
| 28 | Merged layers release one after another (Effect releases in parallel). | ctx-merge-parallel-release | `CloseableScope(strategy='parallel')`; `merge` forks a parallel scope with one sequential child per layer. |
| 29 | `Cause.__add__` does not dedupe equal reasons. | A-DIV-5 | Equality scan that keeps order (no set, because errors may be unhashable). |
| 30 | `Cause(()).squash()` returns `Interrupted()`. | A-DIV-6 | Raise a distinct `EmptyCauseError` for an empty cause. |
| 31 | `Fiber.interrupt()` returns the Exit (Effect returns void). `Semaphore.take`/`release` return None (Effect returns counts). | fiber-interrupt-return-type, semaphore-return-values | Return `Effect[None]` and `Effect[int]`; audit internal callers that use the Exit. |
| 32 | `TimeoutException` carries no message or duration. | TO-1 | Make it a dataclass with a `duration` field and an Effect-style message. |

### Resolved after M0

| Item | Resolution | Pinned by |
|---|---|---|
| `race`/`race_first`/`race_all`/`timeout` interrupted from outside | Already matched Effect: `raceAll`/`raceAllFirst` interrupt and await the racers in a `void` exit finalizer, so Effect also reports the Interrupt alone (not the #12 merge). Checked against Effect 4.0.6. | `tests/test_race_interruption.py` |
| `sliding(0)` | `offer` keeps the newest item (one slot) and leaves a parked taker parked, `offer_all` keeps nothing, as Effect's `offerUnsafe` / `offerAllUnsafe` | `test_queue.py::test_offer*_sliding_queue_of_capacity_zero_*`, `test_offer_to_a_full_sliding_queue_leaves_a_waiting_taker_parked` |
| `try_async` / `try_blocking` with a `catch` that raises | The fiber used to hang, because `catch` ran in the task's done callback and the exception was lost there. Now `catch` runs on the fiber through `_failure_of`, so a raising `catch` is a defect, as in Effect's `tryPromise`. A synchronous raise from `make` goes through `catch` too. | `tests/oracle/test_effect_errors.py` (`tryPromise` ports), `test_error_combinators.py::test_a_raising_catch_in_try_blocking_is_a_defect` |
| Resume queued just before an interrupt | The interrupt is held until the resumed run finishes, parks or yields (`Fiber._resume`), so the Fail wins alone, like Effect's synchronous resume. The loop no longer takes a `resumed` flag. | `test_interrupt_delivery.py::test_a_resume_that_lands_before_*`, `test_a_fiber_resumed_before_*`, `test_an_interrupt_after_a_queued_resume_*`, `test_an_uninterruptible_wait_resumed_before_*` |
| Semaphore / Queue waiter paused before it parks | A fiber could check "no permits" (or "queue empty"), pause for its op budget, and only then park; a release landing in that gap woke nobody and the waiter hung with permits free. `WaitList.wait` now takes the awaited condition and checks it again as the fiber parks, as Effect's `waitForPermits` does, so no caller can leave the gap open. | `test_primitives.py::test_a_semaphore_waiter_paused_before_it_parks_still_sees_the_release`, `test_a_queue_taker_paused_before_it_parks_still_sees_the_offer` |
| Queue taker woken, then interrupted before it retries | The queue woke one taker per item and relied on the woken taker's interrupt handler to pass the wake-up on, but a taker can pause for its step budget after the wake-up and before its retry, outside that handler; interrupted there, it left the taker behind it parked next to the item. `WaitList` no longer wakes an exact count: `notify_ready` wakes every waiter whose condition holds, as Effect's `releaseTakers`. | `test_queue.py::test_a_waiter_interrupted_between_its_wake_up_and_its_retry_strands_no_item` |
| A non-effect where an `Effect` is expected | `fx.never.on_interrupt(lambda: ...)` built fine and the lambda only died inside the finalizer, where nothing reported it, so the finalizer silently did nothing. Every public combinator now checks each `Effect` parameter when it is called (`pyfect._effect_args.require_effect`, a `TypeError` naming the parameter and suggesting `fx.sync(f)` for a function); the run loop is unchanged. A guard test walks the public API (every public module, every `__all__`, every reachable class's methods) and fails for any `Effect` parameter that does not reject a non-effect, so a new combinator cannot skip the check. Collections read when the effect runs (`all_dict`, `first_success_of`) still die with `not a valid effect` at that point. `Layer` parameters get the same check (`_layer_core.require_layer`, suggesting `fx.layer.suspend(f)`; `provide`'s source says "a Layer or a Context"), and the guard also walks `__call__` (the runtimes `FiberSet.runtime()` and friends return now check their effect). | `tests/test_effect_arguments.py` |
| A `BaseException` raised by user code (`sys.exit`, `asyncio.CancelledError`, a custom subclass) and Ctrl-C while user code runs (QA core 1, 2) | The loop caught only `Exception`, so the step was lost: `run_sync` hung on Ctrl-C or re-raised raw past the finalizers, and `run_async` hung. Every call into user code in the loop now goes through `Fiber._raised`: anything raised is a defect where it happens, as Effect makes anything thrown; Ctrl-C ends the step as interrupted and propagates to the runner, which interrupts the main fiber and waits for its finalizers, as when Ctrl-C lands while parked. The schedulers keep the rest of a batch a task raised in. The same holds for a coroutine under `from_async` (asyncio re-raised `SystemExit` out of the loop) and for a generator's `finally` raising as it closes on a failure (the failure was dropped), and the handlers that make a raise a defect on the spot (`from_async`'s `make`, an RPC handler) let Ctrl-C through. Guards walk `_fiber.py` for user calls outside a `try` that catches `BaseException`, and all of `src` for an `except BaseException` that would turn Ctrl-C into a defect. | `tests/test_base_exceptions.py`, `test_fibers.py::test_a_join_from_another_thread_sees_an_exit_landing_as_it_registers` |
| Concurrent `for_each` / `all` inside `uninterruptible` (QA core 4, 5) | Workers took new items after a sibling failed, so an item Effect never starts ran, and a `Fail` merged with its defect became a `Die`. No worker takes an item after the first failure (or the caller's interrupt), and a later exit adds only its non-interrupt reasons, as Effect's `forEach`. | `tests/oracle/test_effect_for_each_uninterruptible.py` |
| `interrupt_as_unsafe` from the run's own thread outside its fibers (QA core 6) | It was queued, and lost when the run finished first. It is delivered at once when the calling thread runs the fiber's run (`Tracking.runs_here`), as Effect's `interruptUnsafe`. | `tests/oracle/test_effect_interrupt_unsafe.py` |
| `ManagedRuntime` first used from two threads at once (QA core 8) | The build was check-then-set, so both threads built the layer. The build is claimed under a lock, and `await_exit` re-checks the exit after registering, so a join from another thread cannot miss it. | `test_managed_runtime.py::test_a_sync_run_and_a_loop_on_another_thread_first_using_it_share_one_build` |
| Resumes went through the scheduler (QA core 3, concurrency 3) | As in Effect's `FiberImpl.evaluate`, a resume, an interrupt (also of a fiber that yielded or paused, which is unwound at once) and a `start_immediately` fork on the run's own thread run the fiber at once, nested in the caller; past `_MAX_NESTED_RUNS` (16) nested runs it runs right after the innermost one, so deep chains stay stack safe. Other threads still schedule. Races, `all` and `for_each` fork one racer or worker at a time and stop forking once decided, and the winner interrupts the losers in fork order. Wakes Effect itself defers to a task stay deferred (`wake_soon` / `notify_ready_soon`: Semaphore release and resize, Queue offers and fail, Pool wakes, one task per wake as Effect's). `Effect.forever` yields after each run, as Effect's (`disable_yield=True` where Effect passes `disableYield`). Interrupt cascades now end in the same turn as Effect's. The single-entry context-view cache is dropped when a fiber whose context nothing else uses ends, not after every scheduler task, so forks keep hitting it (`benchmarks/runtime.py` alternately with the previous commit: map chain 3.03–3.06M vs 2.86–3.13M steps/s, gen 2.18–2.19M (one outlier run at 1.77M) vs 2.07–2.17M, fork+join 162–176k vs 152–160k). | `tests/oracle/test_synchronous_resume.py` (the QA repros and the fuzzer's differing programs, with Effect's output) |
| A decided result lost at a step-budget pause (concurrency 1) | The loop could pause between a step's `Exit` and its continuation; an interrupt landing in the pause dropped a taken item. The loop never pauses there now, and claiming code returns its result as an `Exit` or through `run_known(...)` in the same step; a frame that claims (`Semaphore`'s woken retry, a parked PubSub take collecting its message, `PartitionedSemaphore.take`'s give-back guard) sets `Frame.claims`, so its exit is not paused either. A transaction (`TxQueue.take`) still fails its caller with the commit applied when an interrupt arrived during the commit, as Effect's `tx` does. | `tests/test_decided_results.py` (every claiming primitive, interrupted at each yield, for several step budgets, and again with every continuation a possible pause) |
| Register/resume race under free-threaded 3.14t (concurrency 2) | A resume from another thread while the fiber was still registering its wait could be lost. `_Wait` now claims the resume and ends the registration under one lock (`end_registration`), so exactly one side runs the fiber. | `tests/test_free_threaded.py` (runs `tests/free_threaded/stress.py` under `python3.14t` with the GIL off; skipped without it) |
| `Semaphore.take` stranded a permit after a woken retry (concurrency 4) | The woken waiter's turn returned its permit as an effect for a later step, so an interrupt in between kept the permit counted for nobody. `_Turn` returns `run_known(...)`. | `tests/test_decided_results.py::...[semaphore.take (woken)]`, `tests/oracle/test_semaphore.py::test_take_interruption_does_not_leak_permits` |

| `S.Struct(TypedDict, key=schema)` accepted a schema decoding to another type than the key's (`quantity=S.String` for `quantity: int`, a wider `S.Literals`) (QA types 1) | Pyright cannot type a keyword by a TypedDict key (`research/typing/schema_6_struct_overrides.py`), so `Struct` (and `TaggedStruct`) check each given schema's decoded side against the key's annotation when the struct is made (`schema/_assignable.py`): a definite mismatch is a `TypeError` naming the key; a pair it cannot judge (a declaration, a suspension) passes. It found `GraphQLError.path` typed `int` while decoding any number (now `float`, as Effect's `number`). | `tests/test_schema_struct_fields.py` |
| A subclass of a tag counted as provided by its parent (`provide_service(Db, ...)` satisfied `ReplicaDb(Db)`, then `MissingServiceError`) (QA types 2) | `R` holds tag classes and union matching counts a subclass as its parent (`research/typing/tags_1_subclass.py`), so subclassing a tag is a `TypeError` when the class is made, unless the parent is declared `base=True` (`Reference`, the middleware and service families); a base is never read or provided itself. A derived `HttpApiGroup` stays the same tag (`origin`). | `tests/test_tag_identity.py` |
| `retry` / `repeat` with `until=<TypeIs>` and `while_=` narrowed the error or value though `while_` can stop on another one (QA types 3) | The narrowing overload takes `until` alone (Effect narrows here too, unsoundly). | `tests/type_checks.py::check_schedule_refinements` |
| `graphql.make(Group)` left out per-operation middleware, which it read from the context (QA types 4) | Pyright cannot gather it from the class attributes, so a group declares it as its second type argument, `GraphQLGroup[GM, OM]`; a group whose operations run undeclared middleware (attached with `.middleware(M)` or taken from another group) is a `TypeError` when the class is made, and `make` requires `GM` and `OM`, as Effect's does. A subclass of a group keeps its group middleware. | `tests/test_graphql_group.py`, `tests/type_checks.py::graphql_checks` |
| A group with a missing handler type-checked (HttpApi, RPC), and `builder.group` / `client.group` took a group the api lacks (QA types 5, 6) | A group the api lacks is a type error (`http_api.api.ApiWith`, matched contravariantly so `G` cannot widen). A missing handler cannot be (`research/typing/handlers_1_missing.py`): RPC `to_layer` raises `ValueError` naming each RPC and its attribute; an HttpApi group's layer dies when built naming each endpoint and its attribute (its handlers are made then; Effect rejects both at compile time). `RpcGroup.to_layer_effect`, `builder.group` with handlers made by an effect, and `rpc.middleware.layer_client_effect` gave `Unknown` requirements when nothing was needed (`R | RX` of two solved `Never`s); each now shares one `RR` TypeVar between the handlers and the effect. | `tests/test_missing_handlers.py`, `tests/type_checks.py` |
| `layer.merge` of layers that build nothing gave `Layer[Unknown, ...]` (QA types 9) | `merge` first takes layers building nothing through a covariant protocol view (`_Builds[Never, E, R]`), giving `Layer[Never, E, R]`. A `Layer[Any]` matches it too, so merging only `Any` layers builds `Never`. | `tests/type_checks.py::check_merge_of_layers_building_nothing` |
| `Stream.changes` dropped `True` after `1` and kept every NaN (QA schema-data review) | It compares with `pyfect._equal.equals`, Effect's `Equal.equals`; a guard bans a bare `a == b` equality function anywhere in `src`, besides `set` / `dict` decoders and `len(set(...))` | `tests/oracle/test_equal_keys.py::test_stream_changes_*`, `test_no_code_keys_or_compares_values_with_python_equality` |
| `Cause +` returned `self` when the result was `==` to it, losing `fail(True)` after a repeated `fail(1)` (QA schema-data review) | `self` only when every reason is the same object, as Effect returns `self` only when the result `Equal.equals` it | `test_equal_keys.py::test_cause_combine_keeps_a_new_reason_python_calls_equal_to_a_repeated_one` |
| INI: a `[section]` over a plain value raised even with nothing set in it (QA schema-data review) | Kept as the value; setting a property in it raises Effect's `Cannot create property 'b' on string '1'` | `tests/oracle/test_ini.py::test_a_section_over_a_value_*` |
| The JSON logger's `to_json` and `structured_message` raised `RecursionError` on a value about 1000 levels deep (QA schema-data review) | They continue on a fresh stack (`with_fresh_stack`), as the schema walks do | `test_schema_recursion_depth.py::test_the_json_log_text_of_a_deep_value_is_written_in_full` |
| MCP JSON-RPC ids used before they were parsed: a `notifications/cancelled` with a list `requestId` killed the stdio server (500 over HTTP), `requestId: true` cancelled request 1, an id `6.5` was refused (QA ai-workflow MCP-1, 2, 5) | Ids are read once with `mcp_schema.parse_request_id` (Effect's `RequestId`: a string or a finite number, never a `bool`); a request is a `dispatch.Request` whose id is typed, so the server never casts one; a guard bans casting a wire value to `RequestId` | `tests/oracle/test_mcp_request_ids.py`, `test_mcp_request_id_guard.py` |
| A `run_stdio` server exited 130 at the end of input once its writer was waiting (M14 mcp-server-rest: a watcher interrupted the server when the writer ended, and the server itself ended the writer from its finalizer); `layer_stdio(...).launch()` never ended at all (dogfood depot) | The output ends after the server has exited, so only a writer that ends early interrupts anything. `run_stdio` has no Effect counterpart and returns (exit 0). `layer_stdio` interrupts the fiber that built it, as Effect's does (`Layer.launch` exits 130 under Node). Under `layer.merge` neither ends, in Effect too; the docs give `provide_merge`. `tests/test_stdio_end_of_input.py` |
| A tool defect in a `run_stdio` server was logged to stdout, corrupting the protocol (QA ai-workflow MCP-4) | The stdio server runs with `LogToStderr` set, so console loggers write to stderr (Effect leaves `LogToStderr` false and its default logger writes to stdout; pyfect keeps stdout for the protocol); a guard bans `print` / `sys.stdout` under `pyfect.ai` | `test_mcp_stdio_stdout.py` |
| MCP resource template params were not percent-decoded; query, fragment and slashes differed (QA ai-workflow MCP-3) | A template matches through find-my-way as Effect's `makeUriMatcher` (`/` + the URI, case sensitive, trailing and duplicate slashes ignored, JavaScript's first-only `replace(":", "::")`) | `tests/oracle/test_mcp_uri_template.py` |
| `RateLimitError` left out a zero `retry_after`; `QuotaExhaustedError` wrote the reset time as bare ISO (QA ai-workflow AI-2, 3) | `is not None` (a `Duration` is always truthy in JavaScript), and `date_time._iso.utc_to_string` (`DateTime.Utc(...)`), also used by the schema formatter; a guard bans truthiness tests of `timedelta` / `datetime` fields under `pyfect.ai` | `tests/oracle/test_ai_error_messages.py`, `test_ai_error_truthiness_guard.py` |
| Provider JSON with whole floats (`created: 1.0`) failed `S.Int` (a stream silently lost text); embeddings overflowing to infinity were accepted; unknown Anthropic block and delta types were skipped (QA ai-workflow PROV-1, 2, 3) | Provider wire JSON is read only through `_provider.wire_decoder` / `wire_match`, which take whole floats in the safe range as ints (`_json_text.whole_numbers_as_ints`, JavaScript has one number) and return that JSON; embeddings are `S.Finite`; Anthropic blocks and deltas must have a type Effect's generated `Beta` unions know (`compaction_delta` included, which the first fix rejected) | `tests/oracle/test_ai_provider_wire.py`, `test_ai_provider_wire_guard.py` |
| The eventlog server reserved `[None] * total` for a chunk's declared total before authentication; two totals for one id raised `IndexError` (QA ai-workflow EL-1) | Parts are held in a `dict` as they arrive (Effect's sparse array) and joined in index order; as in Effect, a total past `2**32 - 1` (`Invalid array length`) and a count reaching a total over an empty slot are defects (the review found the first fix returning such a message with a gap); a guard bans lists sized by anything but `len(...)` in the wire packages | `tests/oracle/test_event_log_chunks.py`, `test_wire_preallocation_guard.py` |
| `decode_identity_string` accepted junk characters, the standard alphabet and whitespace (QA ai-workflow EL-3) | It decodes with Effect's `Base64Url.decode` / `Base64.decode` (`schema.getter._binary`); a guard bans Python's lenient base64 decoders | `tests/oracle/test_event_log_identity_string.py`, `test_lenient_base64_guard.py` |
| `write_many([])` died on the SQL journal (`sql.insert([])`) but not in memory (QA ai-workflow EL-4) | The SQL journal inserts through one `_insert` that writes nothing for no rows (Effect types the batch non-empty) | `test_event_journal_empty_write.py` |
| NaN / infinite GraphQL variables killed a POST and were sent as a `NaN` token over GET and graphql-ws (QA ai-workflow GQL-1) | GraphQL frames and `http.body.json_unsafe` write with `_json_text.js_stringify` (exactly `JSON.stringify`: `null`); a guard bans plain `stringify` in `pyfect.graphql` but the cache keys | `tests/oracle/test_graphql_non_finite_variables.py`, `test_graphql_wire_json_guard.py` |
| A lone surrogate in a workflow key raised `UnicodeEncodeError` (QA ai-workflow WF-2), and so did `Stream.encode_text`, raw HTTP text bodies and the cluster lock namespace (review) | Text is encoded with `_js.text_encode` (U+FFFD, as `TextEncoder`) everywhere; a guard bans `str.encode()` in all of `src/pyfect` but `_js.py` and `json_pointer.py` (``decodeURIComponent``'s literal text, where JavaScript keeps a lone surrogate) | `tests/oracle/test_workflow_lone_surrogate.py`, `test_text_encoder_lone_surrogate.py`, `test_text_encode_guard.py` |

| No CI ran without the extras or without the GIL (QA root causes 1 and 7) | `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: `base-install` installs `pyfect-dev` with no extras into a new venv, runs a program and `tests/test_install.py`; `free-threaded` runs the cross-thread stress and the runtime suites (fibers, interrupts, races, `for_each`, queues, decided results, synchronous resumes) on CPython 3.14t with `PYTHON_GIL=0`, and `PYFECT_REQUIRE_FREE_THREADED=1` makes the stress test fail instead of skipping when 3.14t is missing. Per-finding status: `pyfect-qa/FIX-STATUS.md` | `tests/test_install.py`, `tests/test_free_threaded.py` |
| `schedule.exponential` died with `OverflowError` once `base * factor ** n` passed float range (DOGFOOD outlay #10: the workflow memory engine's `min(exponential(0.2, 1.5), spaced(30))` poll killed runs suspended ~14.5 h); `fibonacci`, the TestClock and the nanosecond helpers had the same exposure | Delays saturate to infinity as JavaScript's numbers do: `exponential` and `fibonacci` compute Effect's float milliseconds (`_js.power`, `duration.from_millis`), so they turn infinite at the same step as Effect; `nanos_or_infinite`, `from_nanos` and `add_nanos` keep nanosecond counts within float range (a count past it, over ~1.8e299 s, is infinite where Effect's milliseconds stay finite up to ~1.8e305 s); the TestClock adds deadlines with `add_nanos`; `to_nanos` and `round_nanos` refuse an infinity with `ValueError`; the histogram boundaries' private `Math.pow` copy is replaced by `_js.power`, the only place `math.pow` may be called | `tests/oracle/test_schedule_saturation.py`; guards `tests/test_schedule_saturation_guard.py` (every public constructor and `Schedule` method, 10,000 recurrences to infinite time), `tests/test_duration_overflow_guard.py` (every `pyfect.duration` function on extreme values; no raw `**` in time code; no `math.pow` outside `_js.py`) |
| `activity.make` without `success=` / `error=` type-checked with any `execute`, then died encoding (QA ai-workflow WF-1) | Overloads per omitted schema: without `success`, `execute` must succeed with `None`; without `error`, it must not fail | `tests/type_checks.py` (activities) |

| Schema-leftovers review: CAA `tag` unchecked in JSON; `RegExp` encoded `re.VERBOSE` / `re.ASCII` patterns as different ones; `DurationFrom*` did not round to whole nanoseconds as Effect (`1.0000001` ms, `999.5 nanos` refused), `"Infinity"` gave the wrong message, long digit runs died, long timedeltas with microseconds did not decode back; `ScopedIpv6Literal` and `SemVer` had no usable strategy (the pydantic catalog guard failed, the builder never ran the full suite); `BigDecimal` was generated by filtering text, so bounded checks were unsatisfiable | CAA `tag` checks `^[a-z0-9]+$` (i); RegExp encode fails on flags JSON cannot carry, equivalence compares all flags; durations round ties away from zero (text exactly, milliseconds from the float's shortest text); `ScopedIpv6Literal` carries an `arbitrary_constraint`, `SemVer` / `SemVerRange` Effect's `toCodecArbitrary`; `BigDecimal` has Effect's constructive arbitrary within its `is_*_big_decimal` bounds (`schema/_strategy/_big_decimal.py`); the network declarations (no strategy in Effect) draw special and uniform addresses (`schema/_strategy/_net.py`), `DomainName` normalized names, the DNS records their decoded fields, as `tests/test_schema_round_trip.py` needs; like Effect's, those declarations have no JSON form, which the round-trip guard now skips; `SchemaAsserts.verify_generation()` ports `verifyGeneration` | `tests/oracle/test_schema_net.py`, `test_schema_decimal_regexp.py`, `test_schema_duration_semver.py`, `test_arbitrary.py` (the BigDecimal / RegExp titles), `test_schema_template_literal.py` (branded half), `tests/test_pydantic.py` |
| Core-gaps pass, the dogfood leftovers: `str(LogLevel.INFO)` was `'20'`; a schedule's `_since` raised `OverflowError` for instants of opposite signs near ±1.7e308 s; `cli.prompt.number` raised past a precision of 308; `tests/test_service_markers.py` found requirement classes by their docstring; `a: Layer[Db] = layer.succeed(Other, x)` type-checked (the tag widened to `Db \| Other`); `atom_http_api.query` / `mutation` took any group; an idle httpx streaming body cost `warning_delay` per `TestClock.adjust` | `LogLevel.__str__` / `__format__` give Effect's label (`Info`), renderers upper-case it and no source reads `.name`; `_since` saturates to ±infinity; the prompt rounds with `_js.power` / `divide` (NaN past 308, as JavaScript); the marker check walks every return annotation's requirement position structurally; calling a tag gives `TagEffect[S, R, T]`, holding the tag invariantly, and `layer.succeed` / `sync` / `effect` / `mock` take `Provides[S, T]` (`research/typing/layer_1_widening.py`); `AtomHttpApiClient[T, G]` takes only `type[G]`; a body chunk pull is an `"event"` wait (`_future` in `httpx_client.py` now requires `waits_for`) | `tests/test_log_level_labels.py` (with a guard on `.name`), `tests/oracle/test_schedule_saturation.py`, `tests/oracle/test_cli_edges.py` and `tests/test_duration_overflow_guard.py` (no float `**` outside `_js.power`), `tests/test_service_markers.py`, pins in `tests/type_checks.py`, `tests/test_httpx_test_clock.py` |
| Core-gaps review: `HashMap.set(2**1024 - 1, x)` raised `OverflowError` (Effect's hash port converted every big int to `float` to ask whether it is a JavaScript number); `TxHashMap.get_hash` / `has_hash` of a list key died with `TypeError` (they compared against `hash(key)`, not the key's `equal_key`); `cli.prompt.number`, `hash_ring` and the persistence durations rounded with `floor(x + 0.5)`, which takes `0.49999999999999994` to 1 and odd numbers past 2**52 to even, and gave `+0` for `-0.4` (`Math.round` gives 0, the number itself and `-0`); the HashMap title "preserves traversal order across every node kind" was omitted although keys with a custom `__hash__` give its hashes | `_effect_hash._is_float` checks a big int's significant bits without converting; `_hash` is `hash(equal_key(key))`; `_js.round` is `Math.round`, used by all three; the title is ported | `tests/test_effect_hash.py` (hashes printed by Effect under Node), `tests/test_tx_collections.py::test_get_hash_and_has_hash_take_list_keys`, `tests/test_js_round.py` (values printed under Node, and a guard banning `floor(x + 0.5)` in `src`), `tests/oracle/test_hash_map.py::test_preserves_traversal_order_across_every_node_kind` |

| M11 completeness pass: a fiber that parked resumed in the `contextvars` context of whatever woke it (a `Deferred.succeed` from another task, a pool release), not its own, because resumes run nested in the waker; Effect's fiber captures the async context when it yields and resumes in it (`AsyncLocalStorage` in `Effect.test.ts`) | `Fiber._run` keeps the context the fiber parked or paused in (`contextvars.copy_context()`, once per park) and runs the next run inside it; the first run stays in the starter's context, as Effect's | `tests/oracle/test_context_vars_resume.py` |

Effect's own source runs under Node 24 without a build: write a `.ts` script that imports `Effect.ts` from the reference checkout (`pyfect-ref/effect/packages/effect/src`, next to this repo) by absolute path and run `node script.ts`. Check an expected result there before pinning it.

### Still open

Walked in M16 (see the title guard entry): what is left here cannot follow Effect, for the reason each gives, or does follow it.

- **`catch_tag` with a parent class or a `type[X]` variable** (QA types 7, 8): pyright's union matching keeps one subclass of the caught class in `E` (sound, imprecise: name the subclasses, `catch_tag((A, B), ...)`), and a `type[Parent]` variable has the class's own type, so a subclass held in it catches less than pyright removes (unsound). Neither can be expressed (`research/typing/catch_1_subclasses.py`).
- **Left open by the dogfood fixes** (`~/pyfect-apps/FIX-STATUS.md`):
  - Under the TestClock, a sharding runner's periodic loops (reply poll, assignment and lock refresh, storage poll) wake on every interval, so real time grows with virtual time on the cluster engine (DOGFOOD outlay #3 part b). Kept in the core-gaps pass: the loops are Effect's polling design (`Sharding.ts` sleeps each interval under whatever clock it runs), so every adjust past an interval must run them; skipping them would diverge from Effect.
- **TaggedError**: pyright treats a plain `@dataclass` subclass as unhashable in set literals (runtime hashing works); `@dataclass(eq=False)` is hashable to both (pinned in `tests/type_checks.py`). A `dataclass_transform` base was tried and rejected: pyright then gives every plain subclass a field-only `__init__`, so `Boom("msg")` and `Done()`-style args errors stop type-checking, and making the runtime agree would re-run `@dataclass` on explicit dataclass subclasses (losing `default_factory` defaults). Revisited for forge #4 (`research/typing/tagged_error_1_fields.py`): still no design gives both, since `dataclass_transform` has no default that turns off the synthesized `__init__`. Fields need `@dataclass`; constructing a subclass whose annotated fields neither `@dataclass` nor a hand-written `__init__` sets (`class TooHeavy(fx.TaggedError): task: str`, then `TooHeavy("a")`) raises `TypeError` naming the decorator, checked the first time each class is built (a class with fields and no `__init__` gets a `__new__` hook, since `@dataclass` runs after `__init_subclass__`), and subclassing such a class fails where the subclass is defined (`tests/test_tagged_error.py::TestFieldsNeedADataclass`); `S.TaggedError` needs no decorator. Frozen dataclass errors don't set `__cause__` and don't `copy.copy`; Python itself doesn't support frozen exceptions well, so don't freeze TaggedErrors.

## 3. Milestone roadmap (dependency-ordered)

Effort scale: S < 1 day, M a few days, L 1–2 weeks, XL several weeks.

Every milestone ends with green ports of the named Effect tests in `tests/oracle/test_<module>.py`, one pytest per Effect test title. Cases that are not supported yet are marked `xfail(strict=True)`, so each fix flips a test and the remaining gap stays visible.

### M0 — Semantic correctness and the oracle harness (L)
- **Fix:** every item in §2. Order: the shared helpers first (`elect_defects` / `find_recoverable_error`, `_call_user`, keyed finalizers, the `SUSPEND` sentinel, `add_interrupts`), then the individual bugs.
- **Infra:** a `tests/oracle/` skeleton, plus a lint rule or grep check that bans `first_failure` outside `cause.py` and eager user-callback calls in resource combinators.
- **Exit criteria:** the cases cited in §2 pass: `Effect.test.ts` (recovery/defect-election block at ~4117, interruption ~2355/2418, finalizers ~3088, acquireUseRelease ~348, forEach interruption ~833/851, forkIn ~3011, TestClock ~2214, provide memo ~5280); `Fiber.test.ts:137`; `Queue.test.ts` ("fail doesnt drop items", "bounded 0 capacity", offerAll, shutdown); `Scope.test.ts:159`; `Layer.test.ts` (fresh variants); `Deferred.test.ts:34`; `Schedule.test.ts` (fixed, upTo, fibonacci); `Data.test.ts` (TaggedError equality and message).

### M1 — Test harness and the core Effect/Cause/Exit/Result surface (L)

**Status (2026-10-26): done.** Every API listed below exists, is exported, has `assert_type` pins in `tests/type_checks.py`, and is tested. 251 Effect test cases are ported to `tests/oracle/` (Cause 75, Effect.test.ts error handling and constructors 56, Effect.test.ts combinators 50, TestClock 19, @effect/vitest 25, Result 15, Option 10, Exit 1). Each oracle file's docstring lists the titles it omits and why. The full suite has 667 tests. The completeness pass also made `try_async` / `try_blocking` default to `UnknownError` (Effect's `tryPromise`), added `ignore_cause`, and built `effect_fixture`.

Deferred from the M1 exit-criteria files, with the reason:

- `catchFilter`, `catchCauseFilter`, `tapCauseFilter`, `filterMap`, `filterMapEffect` (done, M7 completeness pass: the methods `catch_filter(filter, f, or_else=)`, `catch_cause_filter`, `tap_cause_filter` and `fx.filter_map` / `fx.filter_map_effect(concurrency=)`; a filter returns `Ok(value)` / `Err(rest)`, as everywhere in pyfect). The Effect.test.ts titles are in `tests/oracle/test_effect_errors.py`, `catch_filter` joins the mixed-cause table in `tests/test_cause_recovery.py`, and `tests/test_filter_combinators.py` pins the edges printed by Effect under Node: a rejected error with no `or_else` re-fails with the original cause, a defect wins before the filter runs, a failing tap replaces the failure, and `filter_map_effect` collects in completion order when concurrent.
- `catchReason`, `catchReasons`, `unwrapReason` (done, M7: `pyfect._catch_reason`, `stream._catch_reason`, `channel._catch_reason`; `tests/oracle/test_effect_reasons.py`, `test_stream_reasons.py`, `test_channel_reasons.py`; `tests/test_reasons.py`). A reason error is any error with a `reason` attribute (`HasReason[R]`); cases match the reason with `isinstance` (Effect: the reason's `_tag`), in the order given, and handlers get `(reason, error)`. They are curried, for `.pipe`. Without `or_else` the parent error stays in the error type (Python has no `Exclude`); with `or_else`, it must accept every reason the cases leave, checked through a phantom self-type, and the parent leaves the type. `catch_reasons` takes up to eight `(class, handler)` pairs (Effect: a record of any size). Each case has its own reason class and handler type, so pyright needs an overload per number of cases; they are generated (`tests/test_catch_reasons_overloads.py` fails when the source differs, and regenerates them when run as a script). Hand-written ones had stopped at three (two for channels).
- `onErrorIf`, `onExitIf` (done, M7: methods `on_error_if` / `on_exit_if`, and `on_error_filter` / `on_exit_filter` for Effect's Filter overloads, whose filter returns `Ok` / `Err`; `tests/oracle/test_effect_finalizers.py`), `reduce`, `findFirst`, `findFirstFilter`, `head` (done, M7: `fx.reduce`, `fx.find_first`, `fx.find_first_filter`, the method `head`; `tests/oracle/test_effect_iterables.py`). Callbacks get the item only; the index titles enumerate. `reduce` reads its iterable when called and `find_first` when it runs, as Effect does (checked under Node, `tests/test_structural_combinators.py`).
- `Effect.ignore` / `ignoreCause` with `log` / `message`: they needed the Logger (done in M5, `tests/oracle/test_effect_errors.py`).
- (Done in the M11 completeness pass: the Cause `IllegalArgumentError` and `ExceededCapacityError` titles; `Done` ported in M4.) (Done in M5: Cause annotations and stack traces.)
- (Done in the core-A coverage pass, M11: the Result and Option long tail, Exit's accessors, `pyfect.filters` and the missing Effect constructors; `scripts/api_coverage.py core-a` lists every export and `tests/test_api_coverage.py` keeps docs/coverage/core-a.md current. `makeEquivalence` and the Do notation are §4 decisions.)
- (Done in the collections coverage pass, M11: `scripts/api_coverage.py collections` lists every export of Effect v4's pure modules and Crypto; Graph was the only gap until its port (below). Native modules are §4 rows naming the Python idiom per export. New ports, each with its Effect test file ported title by title: `fx.Crypto` (`pyfect.crypto`: `random_bytes`, `digest`, UUID v4 / v7, ULID and the `Random`-style draws, in `platform.layer`; `test_crypto.py` ports `Crypto.test.ts` and `NodeCrypto.test.ts`), `encoding.base64` / `base64_url` / `hex` with `EncodingError` (Effect's error messages; the schema getters now share `_binary_codecs.py`), `HashMap`, `HashSet`, `MutableHashMap`, `MutableHashSet` keyed by `Equal` (`test_hash_map.py`, `test_hash_set.py`, `test_mutable_hash_map.py`, plus `tests/test_equal_collections.py` for `1` / `True` / `NaN` / list keys), `Trie` (UTF-16 key order, `tests/test_trie_order.py`), `BigDecimal` (`test_big_decimal.py`), `SemVer` and `SemVerRange` (`test_semver.py`, `test_semver_range.py`). Graph followed in its own pass: `pyfect.graph`, every title of `Graph.test.ts` and `Pathfinding.test.ts` in `tests/oracle/test_graph.py` and `test_pathfinding.py`.)
- (Done in the config-data coverage pass, M11: `scripts/api_coverage.py config-data` lists every export of Config, ConfigProvider, Redacted, Data, Brand, Equal, Hash, Equivalence and Order; none is missing (Data's classes, Equal, Hash, Equivalence and Order are §4 rows). New ports: `tests/oracle/test_data.py` (every `Data.test.ts` title on frozen dataclasses and `errors.TaggedError` but the JavaScript object-model ones), `test_equal.py` (the `Equal.test.ts` titles that apply to Python values, on `pyfect._equal.equals`), `test_hash.py` (the `Hash.test.ts` titles on `hash(equal_key(x))`). `Equivalence.test.ts` and `Order.test.ts` are not ported: their combinators are `==` and `key=` functions (§4). The Equal port found a bug: `equal_key` / `equals` of a value holding itself recursed onto a fresh-stack thread per level until the process could not start one ("can't start new thread"). `equal_key` now raises `TypeError` for a cyclic value and `equals` assumes a pair met again while comparing it equal, so cyclic values compare as Effect's do (bisimilarity, also through an `__eq__` that calls `equals`); the root cause, `with_fresh_stack` nesting without bound, is closed by `MAX_FRESH_STACKS` (an endless walk raises `RecursionError`, `tests/test_fresh_stack_limit.py`). Review found `equals` still failed on depth: a value with an unhashable part was keyed again at every level (quadratic) and compared on one native stack, so 500 nested lists holding a `bytearray`, or a cycle longer than one thread's stack, raised `RecursionError`. `equals` now walks lists, tuples and mappings part by part, moves to a fresh stack when the stack is nearly full, and keeps its in-progress pairs in a context variable that follows the walk across threads (`tests/test_equal_depth.py`).)
- (Done in the core-B coverage pass, M11: `scripts/api_coverage.py core-b` lists every export of Scope, Layer, Context, ManagedRuntime, Fiber, References, Scheduler, Semaphore, Latch and Runtime (Effect.ts itself is in core-a), docs/coverage/core-b.md has none missing. New: `fx.fn(body, *pipeables)`, `fx.fn("name")(body, *pipeables)` and `fx.fn_untraced` (pipeable overloads generated by `tests/test_fn_overloads.py`; `tests/oracle/test_effect_fn.py` ports the two pipeable titles, `tests/test_fn_pipeables.py` pins the edges printed under Node), `scope.make` / `provide` / `use`, `CloseableScope.fork_unsafe` and `strategy`, `scheduler.ShouldYield` (`tests/oracle/test_scheduler.py` ports "PreventSchedulerYield disables shouldYield checks" as written), `Context.get_or_none`, `run.get_error_exit_code` / `get_error_reported` (`default_teardown` now reads any error's `exit_code`, as Effect's `getErrorExitCode` reads any object's). `runtime.py` benchmarks show no change beyond noise.)
- `race_all_first` has no `onWinner`. (The `cached` ports now use `start_immediately` as Effect's do.)
- (Done in M3: `timed` measures whole monotonic nanoseconds, so its port compares exactly.)
- (Done in M5: `TestConsole`; `@testing.effect` provides one as `it.effect` does.) Property tests (`it.prop`, `it.effect.prop`, `it.live.prop`) are `testing.prop`, `testing.effect_prop` and `testing.live_prop` (core-gaps pass): Hypothesis runs them over `S.to_strategy(schema)` or a strategy, `runs` / `seed` map to `max_examples` / `seed`, the active settings profile is `Arbitrary.configureGlobal`, and each effect run gets its own TestClock, TestConsole and Scope.
- `AsyncioRuntime` has no stall detection, so an asyncio test with the timeout disabled can hang where the sync runtime raises `StalledError`.

- **Testing:** pytest plugin with `@testing.effect`, `@testing.live`, `layer_fixture`, `effect_fixture`, `flaky`, a test timeout that drains finalizers, `run_test_async` (asyncio TestClock), `with_live`, `TestClock.layer`, separate wall and monotonic time, assertion helpers.
- **Effect:** `catch_if`, `catch_cause_if`, multi-class `catch_tag` (+`or_else`), `tap_defect`, `tap_error_tag`, `map_both`, `flip`, `sandbox`, `or_else_succeed`, `first_success_of`, `eventually`, `option`, `match_cause`, `fail_sync`, `try_` defaulting to `UnknownError`, `from_result` / `from_optional`, `flatten`, `zip_with(concurrent=)`, `filter_or_fail` / `filter_or_else`, `when`, `replicate_effect`, `cached` / `cached_with_ttl` / `cached_invalidate_with_ttl`, `timeout_option` / `timeout_or_else`, `interruptible_mask`, `all(discard=, mode='result')`, `all_dict`, `partition`, `validate`, `race_all_first`, `acquire_context` / `acquire_async_context`.
- **Cause / Exit / Result:** `Cause.map`, `interruptors`, `find_*` properties, `pretty_errors`; Exit `map` / `map_error` / `map_both` / `as_void` / `match`; Result `map` / `flat_map` / `get_or_else` / `get_or_raise` / `match` / `try_` / `all`.
- **Option:** `Some[A]` with `type Option[A] = Some[A] | None` (see §5).
- **Exit criteria:** `Effect.test.ts` sections on constructors, error handling, cached, timeout and all; `Cause.test.ts`, `Exit.test.ts`, `Result.test.ts`, `Option.test.ts` (subset applicable to the Option decision).

### M2 — Concurrency, runtime and dependency injection (XL; depends on M0)

**Status (2026-10-27): done.** Every item below exists, is exported and has `assert_type` pins. About 300 oracle test functions (more with parametrized cases) port every title of the exit-criteria files below, apart from the omissions each file's docstring lists. Two ports were added in the completeness pass. `tests/oracle/test_scope.py` covers every `Scope.test.ts` title and adds `CloseableScope.close_unsafe` and `is_closed`. `tests/oracle/test_effect_interruption.py` and `test_effect_cleanup_registration.py` cover the fork and interruption cases of `Effect.test.ts`: races, `forEach` interruption, `acquireRelease`, and "interruption before cleanup registration" as a `MaxOpsBeforeYield` sweep. The full suite has 1,171 tests.

The completeness pass also found and fixed a bug. `CloseableScope.close` marked the scope closed before it became uninterruptible, so an interrupt that landed on a step-budget yield between the two left a closed scope whose finalizers never ran. `close` is now uninterruptible from its first step, as Effect's `scopeClose` is (`test_scope.py::test_an_interrupt_landing_anywhere_in_close_never_skips_the_finalizers`, which failed at budget 7 before the fix).

Performance (`uv run python benchmarks/runtime.py`, best of 5, CPython 3.14 on an M-series Mac): map chain 3.4M steps/s, generator steps 2.35M steps/s, fork + join 175k/s. M1 on the same machine measured 3.6M, 2.46M and 200k. During M2, fork + join had fallen to 136k/s: the step-budget references and the run-after-the-step drain each cost a fiber switch more. The completeness pass brought it back by caching the last context's run view (Effect's fiber context cache), keeping the running fiber on the drain instead of in thread-locals, and creating drain queues only when needed. The remaining ~7% on map chains comes from counting continuations against the step budget, which Effect requires.

Deferred from M2, with the reason:

- (Done in the core-B coverage pass, M11: `Scheduler.shouldYield` was a `scheduler.ShouldYield` hook; M16 replaced it with `EffectScheduler`, as Effect has it.) Effect tests ported before it that pause a fiber with a custom `shouldYield` sweep `MaxOpsBeforeYield` budgets instead.
- (Done after M10: synchronous resumes, see "Resolved after M0".)
- (Done in the core-B coverage pass, M11: `scope.make`, `scope.provide(scope)` and `scope.use(scope)`, curried for `.pipe`, and `CloseableScope.fork_unsafe` / `strategy`.)
- (Done in M5: Layer tracing, `with_span` / `with_parent_span` methods and `layer.span` / `layer.parent_span`.)
- `RunOptions.signal` / `onFiberStart`, `makeRunMain`: these are JS-specific (see Runtime below).
- (Done in the M11 completeness pass: every `Effect.test.ts` title is ported or omitted with a reason; see M11.)
- **Fiber:** fork options (`start_immediately`, `uninterruptible`), `fork_in`, `await_all_children`, `Fiber.await_all` / `join_all` / `interrupt_all(_as)` / `run_in` / `get_current`, observer removers (done: `tests/oracle/test_fiber.py`, `tests/oracle/test_effect_fork.py`). A fiber started immediately runs nested inside the forking fiber, as in Effect, up to `_MAX_NESTED_RUNS` deep; past that it runs right after the innermost run, so a chain of immediate forks cannot overflow Python's stack.
- **Sync primitives:** Latch; Deferred (`die`, `interrupt`, `sync`, `is_done`, `into`); Semaphore (`resize`, `take_if_available`, `with_permits_if_available`, `release_all`); PartitionedSemaphore. (done: `tests/oracle/test_latch.py`, `test_deferred.py`, `test_semaphore.py`, `test_partitioned_semaphore.py`, every title ported). `Latch` uses `wait()` for Effect's `await` (a keyword). Effect's tests that interrupt a fiber from a custom `shouldYield` sweep `MaxOpsBeforeYield` budgets instead. Unlike Effect's, PartitionedSemaphore's `with_permits` takes its permits uninterruptibly with only the wait restored (as `Semaphore` does), because pyfect raises a pending interrupt at any continuation; a standalone `take` keeps Effect's give-back-until-it-returns guard. `PartitionedSemaphore[K]` is contravariant in `K` (Effect's `in K`). PartitionedSemaphore's infinite-capacity branch is not ported: permits are an `int`.
- **Fiber collections:** FiberSet → FiberMap → FiberHandle (done: `tests/oracle/test_fiber_set.py`, `test_fiber_map.py`, `test_fiber_handle.py`, every title of `FiberSet.test.ts`, `FiberSetRuntimePropagation.test.ts`, `FiberMap.test.ts` and `FiberHandle.test.ts` ported). `runtime(*tags)` takes the tags its runner's effects need in place of Effect's `runtime(self)<R>()`; the runner forks a root fiber on the calling fiber's run and, called from a fiber of that run, runs it at once up to its first wait (Effect's synchronous `runForkWith`), otherwise schedules it. `runtimePromise` / `makeRuntimePromise` are `runtime_async` / `make_runtime_async`, whose runner returns an asyncio future. Run options take `uninterruptible` and `scheduler`; `signal` and `onFiberStart` are not ported (the runner returns the fiber). The collections are invariant in `A` and `E`, so `run` gives back `Fiber[A, E]` rather than Effect's narrower `Fiber<XA, XE>` (a method's type parameter cannot be bounded by the class's). `clear` walks the live collection as Effect's `Set`/`Map` iteration does, so a fiber that a finalizer adds while clearing is interrupted too; `Fiber.interrupt_all(_as)` interrupts each fiber as it draws it from the iterable. Edge cases checked against Effect under Node (clear with re-entrant finalizers, `propagate_interruption` with the collection's own interrupts, closing, an interruptible `FiberHandle.clear`) are in `tests/test_fiber_collections.py`.
- **Runtime:** `run_fork`, `run_callback`, `run_*` with a context argument, `run_main(teardown=)`, Scheduler and `MaxOpsBeforeYield` as References (done: `CurrentScheduler`, `MaxOpsBeforeYield` and `PreventSchedulerYield` in `pyfect.scheduler`, read from the fiber's context; `tests/test_scheduler_references.py`, `tests/oracle/test_scheduler.py`; `run_fork` / `run_callback` / `context=` / `default_teardown` / `disable_error_reporting` in `tests/test_run_functions.py`, `tests/test_run_main.py`, `tests/oracle/test_effect_runtime.py`). `Context[R]` is generic and contravariant (Effect's `Context<in R>`), so `run_sync(eff, context=ctx)` (Effect's `runSyncWith(ctx)`) checks `ctx` against `R`; `Context.make` and a typed `add` / `merge` build one. `run_fork` joins the current fiber's run, else the running loop, starting at once (Effect's synchronous `runFork`); with neither it needs `scheduler=` (the caller drives it) and raises `NoRunError` otherwise. `RunOptions.signal` and `onFiberStart` are not ported (cancellation is the awaiting task's; the fiber is returned). `run_callback` returns an `Interruptor` taking an optional interruptor id. `run_main` takes effects that can fail, as Effect's `runMain` does (the failure is reported and the teardown maps it to a status). `Teardown` maps the exit to a status (Effect's callback form is JS-specific); `makeRunMain` and the keep-alive timer are not ported (one platform; Python keeps the process alive). Since M5 `run_main` logs a failure through the default loggers, as Effect's does, unless an error sets `error_reported = False` (Effect's `Runtime.errorReported`). A scope finalizer now runs with the closing fiber's services under its own (Effect's `provideContext` merges); it used to replace them.
- **Context and Layer:** typed `Context[T]` (`make` / `get` / `get_option` / `pick` / `omit` / `merge_all`), `fx.context()`, `provide_context`, `update_service`, `provide_service_effect`, `service_option`, `scoped_with`. Public MemoMap (refcounted, scope-owned), `build` / `build_with_scope`, Layer `suspend` / `unwrap` / `flat_map` / `tap*` / `catch*` / `or_die` / `launch` / `mock` / `from_build` / `effect_context` / `effect_discard`. (done: `pyfect.context`, `pyfect._context_effects`, `pyfect._layer_core`, `pyfect.layer`; `tests/oracle/test_context.py`, `tests/oracle/test_layer.py`, `tests/oracle/test_effect_services.py`, `tests/test_context_effects.py`; every title of `Layer.test.ts` but the tracing three and the mock dual form, and of `Context.test.ts` but the overlay/fiber-cache internals, Redactable and `makeUnsafe` identity). `Context` gets `get` (the context's type must hold the tag), `get_unsafe`, `get_option` (an `Option`, a reference falls back to its default), `get_or_else`, `map_unsafe` (Effect's `mapUnsafe`), structural `==` and a `repr`; `Context.pick` / `omit` / `add_or_omit` are curried for `.pipe` (pick needs the tags in the context's type; omit removes them, matching the context through a covariant view because pyright only subtracts union members in a covariant position). `fx.provide(ctx)` provides a context as Effect's `provide(context)` does. `update_service` is an Effect and a Layer method (it adds a requirement); `update_service_scoped(tag, f, reset=)` is ported too. The MemoMap follows Effect's `memoMapObserve`: each entry owns a scope and counts the scopes using it, `get` counts its scope as soon as it is called, and the last scope to close releases the layer (a later build starts afresh). Every built layer gets a child scope closed as soon as its build fails (`from_build`), so a failed shared build is dropped and can be retried. As in Effect, `succeed`, `succeed_context`, `fresh`, `or_die` and the `catch*` layers are not shared; `sync`, `effect*`, `suspend`, `merge` and `provide*` are. `Layer.build(scope)` became `build_with_scope(scope)`; `build()` uses the current `Scope`, as Effect's `Layer.build`. `layer.empty` is now `Layer[Never]` (it was `Layer[object]`, which claimed to provide everything).
- **Resources:** ManagedRuntime (sync and async context managers). RcRef → RcMap → ScopedRef → Resource → LayerRef → LayerMap (done: `pyfect.rc_ref`, `rc_map`, `scoped_ref`, `resource`, `layer_ref`, `layer_map`; `tests/oracle/test_rc_ref.py`, `test_rc_map.py`, `test_scoped_ref.py`, `test_resource.py`, `test_layer_ref.py`, `test_layer_map.py`, every title of `RcRef.test.ts`, `RcMap.test.ts`, `ScopedRef.test.ts`, `Resource.test.ts`, `LayerRef.test.ts` and `LayerMap.test.ts`; `tests/test_layer_ref_schedule.py` checks `invalidation_schedule` against Effect under Node; `tests/test_resource_edges.py` checks, against Effect under Node, which services acquisitions, lookups and refreshes see, ScopedRef's set ordering and failure paths, an interrupted lookup's scope, and memo sharing with the enclosing build). `RcRef` is covariant (Effect's `out A, out E`); `RcMap`, `ScopedRef` and `Resource` are invariant (`in out`), pinned in `tests/type_checks.py`. Acquisitions, lookups and idle timers run on root fibers started at once (Effect's `runForkWith`), as Effect does. `RcMap`'s idle deadlines use the monotonic clock in seconds (Effect: wall-clock milliseconds). `ExceededCapacityError` is a `TaggedError` in `pyfect.errors`. `Resource.auto` and `LayerRef`'s `invalidation_schedule` follow `Effect.schedule` (each run waits for the schedule's delay first) and take any `Schedule` whose requirements join the result's (its errors end the refresh fiber, as in Effect). Effect's interleaving tests that pin one `MaxOpsBeforeYield` budget sweep a range of budgets. ManagedRuntime done (`pyfect.managed_runtime`, `tests/oracle/test_managed_runtime.py`, every title of `ManagedRuntime.test.ts`; `tests/test_managed_runtime.py`, `examples/fastapi_style.py`): `make(layer, memo_map=)`, `run_fork`, `run_callback`, `run_sync(_exit)`, `run_async(_exit)` (Effect's `runPromise(Exit)`), `context_effect`, `context()`, `dispose_effect`, `dispose()` (blocking) and `dispose_async()`, `with` / `async with`. Entering builds the layer so startup errors surface there (Effect only disposes on `await using`). The plain `run_sync` / `run_async` need `ER = Never` (a self-type), as the top-level run functions need `E = Never`. Sync runs share one scheduler the runtime owns, one thread at a time, so fibers a layer forks keep running across sync runs; `run_fork` outside a fiber or loop queues on it. A `run_sync` made from inside one of the runtime's own sync fibers gets a scheduler of its own (driving the shared one from there would stall the outer run). `dispose()` and a sync `with` block, so they raise `RuntimeError` inside a fiber or a running asyncio loop (whose fibers they could never see finish) and point to `dispose_effect` / `dispose_async`. `dispose_effect` is one `uninterruptible_mask`; a call waiting on a disposal in progress waits with the caller's interruptibility (`tests/test_managed_runtime.py`). The "Pool" cases of the cleanup-order test use an `acquire_release` resource (Pool is M4). "a layer finalizer shared through a MemoMap can dispose the runtime releasing it" is ported now that the MemoMap counts its users.
- **Exit criteria:** `Fiber.test.ts`, `Deferred.test.ts`, `Semaphore.test.ts`, `PartitionedSemaphore.test.ts`, `Latch.test.ts`, `FiberSet.test.ts`, `FiberMap.test.ts`, `FiberHandle.test.ts`, `Scope.test.ts`, `Layer.test.ts`, `ManagedRuntime.test.ts`, `RcRef.test.ts`, `RcMap.test.ts`, `LayerMap.test.ts`, `Effect.test.ts` (fork, context and runtime sections).

### M3 — Time (L; depends on M1)

**Status (2026-10-27): done.** Every item below exists, is exported, has `assert_type` pins and is tested. Every title of the exit-criteria files is ported: `Schedule.test.ts` (31), `Cron.test.ts` (46), `Random.test.ts` (25), `Clock.test.ts` (1), the Clock- and zone-dependent `DateTime.test.ts` cases (the rest are the standard library, listed in the oracle docstring), and the 30 `schedule` / `repeat` / `retry` titles of `Effect.test.ts`. Cron and the seeded Random were also swept against Effect under Node (12,000 cron cases, 1,000-draw sequences) with no mismatch. The completeness pass added tests for the public paths no test reached (`to_step_with_sleep`, `Schedule.map_effect`, `recurs` / `up_to` rejecting negative times, minute and hour `start_of` / `end_of` / `nearest`, zoned week / month / year boundaries, cron step-value errors and `cron.next` from now), with expected values printed by Effect under Node. The full suite has 1,561 tests.

Performance (`uv run python benchmarks/runtime.py`, run alternately with the M2 commit on the same machine): map chain 3.22M vs 3.25M steps/s, generator steps 2.29M vs 2.27M, fork + join 166k vs 165k, all within run-to-run noise. The absolute numbers sit about 5% below the M2 figures above because the machine measures lower today; the M2 commit measures the same. Schedules are not in the benchmark: `repeat(recurs(20000))` runs at about 18µs per iteration.

Deferred from M3, with the reason:

- `Effect.withExecutionPlan`, `Stream.withExecutionPlan` and the `ExecutionPlan` module (done, M7: `pyfect.execution_plan`, `stream.with_execution_plan`; `tests/oracle/test_execution_plan.py`, every title of `ExecutionPlan.test.ts`). A plan is `make(Step(provide, attempts=, while_=, schedule=), ...)`; its services type is contravariant, so a plan provides what every step provides. `with_execution_plan(plan, on_event=)` is curried, for `.pipe`; when the plan's `while_` or schedules read the errors, the effect's errors must be the plan's input type. `on_event` gets `AttemptStart` / `AttemptSuccess` / `AttemptFailure` with `Metadata(attempt, step_index)`; durations are `Seconds`. `tests/test_execution_plan_edges.py` pins, from Effect under Node, how a fallback step spends one attempt on the failure it takes over, that `prevent_fallback_on_partial_stream` still falls back before any item, and that `capture_requirements` bakes the services into a layer step.
- Type narrowing for a refinement passed as `while_` to `repeat` / `retry`: Python has no `Exclude` (runtime behaviour matches).
- (Done in M4: `fx.Done(value)` carries a value, so the "Done" ports use `Done(42)` as Effect does.)
- DateTime Intl formatting and Effect's millisecond precision (see §4); a POSIX `TZ` string falls back to `/etc/localtime` in `zone_local()`.
- `next_int_between` with infinite or NaN bounds raises where Effect returns NaN (no Python `int` can hold it).
- **Schedule rewrite:** effectful step with E and R parameters, `Metadata`, `CurrentMetadata`, `from_step`, `to_step*`, `tap`, `modify_delay` / `add_delay`, `passthrough` / `identity`, `once` / `duration` / `during` / `windowed`, `concat(_result)`, `max` / `min`, `up_to(times=, duration=)`, metadata-based `while_`. (done: `pyfect.schedule` package, `tests/oracle/test_schedule.py` ports every `Schedule.test.ts` title, `tests/test_schedule.py`. `Schedule[Out, In, E, R]` with Effect's variance; `jittered` draws from the `Random` reference, so the jittered ports run under `random.with_seed` as Effect's do.)
- **Effect integration:** `retry` / `repeat` option forms, `retry_or_else` / `repeat_or_else`, `Effect.schedule` / `schedule_from`. (done: `Effect` methods in `pyfect._effect_timing`, drivers in `pyfect/schedule/_drive.py`; every `schedule` / `repeat` / `retry` title of `Effect.test.ts` ported in `tests/oracle/test_effect_repeat_retry.py`. Effect's builder form `retry(($) => ...)` takes a function of `schedule.SetInputType[E]` (`eff.retry(lambda typed: typed(schedule.recurs(3)).while_(...))`), so the schedule's metadata sees the effect's error or value type. A `TypeIs` predicate as `until` narrows the error (`retry`) or value (`repeat`) when it is the only keyword, as Effect's refinement does; with `while_` too it does not narrow (Effect narrows there, unsoundly: `while_` can stop on an error `until` never accepted; QA types 3); a refinement as `while_` does not narrow, because Python has no `Exclude`. Passing a schedule together with option keywords is a `TypeError`. `Effect.withExecutionPlan` also consumes schedules but waits for the `ExecutionPlan` module, which is not ported yet.)
- **Other time modules:** Random Reference (`next*`, `shuffle`, `choice`, `with_seed`) and a jittered schedule that uses it; (Random done: `pyfect.random`, `tests/oracle/test_random.py` ports every `Random.test.ts` title, `tests/test_random.py` pins seeded sequences printed by Effect under Node. `with_seed` ports Effect's ISAAC generator (`pyfect/_isaac.py`), so a seed gives Effect's exact numbers (an int seed is read as a JavaScript number, a string through `TextEncoder`, lone surrogates included); it is curried for `.pipe`. Effect's `next*` values are zero-argument functions, as `clock.now()`; `nextIntUnsafe` / `nextDoubleUnsafe` are `next_int_unsafe` / `next_double_unsafe`. `choice` of a non-empty tuple cannot fail, Effect's `NonEmptyIterable` overload. Effect has no TestRandom.) Clock nanosecond methods (done: `ClockService.now_nanos_unsafe` / `monotonic_nanos_unsafe`, `clock.now_nanos()` / `monotonic_nanos()` / `clock_with`; `timed` counts whole monotonic nanoseconds; the live clock's sleep of zero or less is a `yield_now` and an infinite one is `never`, as Effect's `ClockImpl`; `tests/oracle/test_clock.py` ports `Clock.test.ts`, `tests/test_clock.py`). DateTime helpers (done: `pyfect.date_time` package over aware `datetime` + `zoneinfo`: `now`, `now_in_current_zone`, `set_zone_current`, `is_future` / `is_past`, the `CurrentTimeZone` service (a `Tag[tzinfo]`, as Effect's is a service, not a Reference) with curried `with_current_zone` / `_named` / `_offset` / `_local` and `layer_current_zone*`; `add` / `subtract` with elapsed time units and wall-clock calendar units, `start_of` / `end_of` / `nearest`, `make_zoned` with Effect's four disambiguations, `zone_from_string`, `zone_local`, and RFC 9557 `format_iso_zoned` / `make_zoned_from_string`. `tests/oracle/test_date_time.py` ports the Clock- and zone-dependent `DateTime.test.ts` cases, the 40-case disambiguation table included, and lists the omitted pure ones; `tests/test_date_time.py`. `IllegalArgumentError` is in `pyfect.errors`.) Cron (native, not croniter); `schedule.cron`. (done: `pyfect.cron` package: `Cron` / `make`, `parse` / `parse_unsafe` with seconds, names, steps and a zone (a `tzinfo` or a zone string), `CronParseError`, `format`, `match`, `next`, `prev`, `sequence`; `schedule.cron(expression_or_cron, tz)`. The search follows Effect's field-by-field walk on a wall-clock time with JavaScript `Date` overflow (`cron/_wall.py`) and Effect's DST rules: a repeated wall time runs once, at its first occurrence, a skipped one after the gap. `tests/oracle/test_cron.py` ports every `Cron.test.ts` title, `test_schedule.py` the four cron titles; `tests/test_cron.py` pins Lord Howe half-hour DST cases printed by Effect under Node, and a 3,000-expression random sweep over eight zones (next, prev, match, format, parse errors and "Unable to find cron date") matched Effect exactly. The cron infinity port found that `TestClock.now_unsafe` stayed finite after `adjust(math.inf)`; it now reads infinite like Effect's `currentTimeMillis`, so a schedule stepping there stops (`tests/test_test_clock.py`). `schedule.cron` takes a zone only with a string, as Effect's overloads do (a `Cron` brings its own). Effect's own `prev` misbehaves for a wall time a DST gap skips (it stays on the instant after the gap, or throws "Unable to find cron date"); the port matches it and `tests/test_cron.py` pins both.)
- **Exit criteria:** `Schedule.test.ts`, `Cron.test.ts`, `Random.test.ts`, `DateTime.test.ts` (Clock- and zone-dependent cases only).

### M4 — Concurrent data (XL; depends on M0 #7 and M2)
