**Status (2026-10-27): done.** Every item below exists, is exported, has `assert_type` pins and is tested. 449 oracle test functions port the exit-criteria files: `Queue.test.ts` (all but the six `flush` titles and two type guards), `PubSub.test.ts` (all but four), `Ref.test.ts`, `SynchronizedRef.test.ts`, `SynchronizedRef.modifySomeEffect.test.ts`, `SubscriptionRef.test.ts` (all but `isSubscriptionRef`), `Pool.test.ts`, `Cache.test.ts` (all but tracing and `PersistedCache`), `ScopedCache.test.ts`, `Request.test.ts` (all but the persisted and JS object-model titles) and `RequestResolver.test.ts` (all but `withSpan`). A title-by-title script check of these files found no unported title without a stated reason. Each cluster review mutation-tested its logic and added the missing tests (`tests/test_queue.py`, `test_pubsub.py`, `test_refs.py`, `test_pool.py`, `test_cache.py`, `test_batching.py`), with expected values printed by Effect under Node.

The completeness pass made a raised `Done` print as `Done()` / `Done(42)` instead of its bare value (`test_queue.py::test_a_raised_done_names_itself`), and stopped `uninterruptible_mask` from defining a new `restore` class on every call: it now picks one of two module-level restorers, which makes a masked step about 2.8× faster (179k → 509k masked effects/s). Semaphore (so the locked refs), Pool, ScopedCache, Queue, `acquire_release` and scope closing all go through it.

Performance (`uv run python benchmarks/runtime.py`, run alternately with the M3 commit on the same machine): map chain 3.20M vs 3.21M steps/s, generator steps 2.29M vs 2.22M, fork + join 168k vs 164k, within run-to-run noise. Data-structure throughput, measured by the cluster reviews: Queue with one consumer 325k items/s (100 contending consumers 27k, the price of waking every ready taker as Effect does); PubSub with four takers 55k–94k messages/s; Pool 240–280k `use`/s uncontended; SynchronizedRef updates about 15µs (the semaphore); request batching about 120k requests/s.

Deferred from M4, with the reason:

- `Queue.flush` / `flushUnsafe`: pyfect wakes a taker as an item arrives, so nothing waits to be flushed.
- (`RequestResolver.persisted` and `PersistedCache`: done in M10 wave 7, with their `Request.test.ts` and `Cache.test.ts` titles. Done in M5: `RequestResolver.with_span`, the Cache span test, and logging a failing Pool / ScopedCache finalizer at `UnhandledLogLevel`.)
- `RequestResolver.asCache`: pyright cannot read a request's success type out of the resolver's `Q`; write `Cache.make` with a lookup that calls `fx.request`.
- `SubscriptionRef.changes` as a Stream and `Stream.fromPubSub`: done in M6 (`ref.changes()`, `stream.from_pubsub`).
- (Done: PubSub sliding slides before every surplus message, as Effect's `SlidingStrategy`, now that resumes are synchronous; pinned in `tests/test_pubsub.py`.)
- (Done with synchronous resumes: a `from_pubsub` reader gets `[1, 2]` from `publish_all([1, 2])` followed by `shutdown()`, as Effect does under Node; pinned in `tests/test_stream.py`.)
- Open decision: Pool's `invalidate` / `reserve` match items with `==` where Effect uses `===`; `is` would be wrong for small ints and strings. Left as `==` pending a human decision.
- Negative queue capacities are a defect when the effect runs, not a `ValueError` at the call, as with PubSub (Effect dies).
- **Queue completion:** `take_between` / `take_n`, `poll`, `peek`, `clear`, `collect`, `await_done`, `interrupt`, `into`, `is_full`, `Enqueue` / `Dequeue` protocols, `Done[L]`. (done: `pyfect.queue` package; `tests/oracle/test_queue.py` ports every `Queue.test.ts` title but the two type guards and the six `flush` titles (pyfect wakes a taker as an item arrives, so there is nothing to flush), sweeping `MaxOpsBeforeYield` where Effect places a yield with one budget. Also `make(capacity, strategy=)`, `capacity`, `size()` (now a method, as `FiberSet.size()`), `offer_unsafe` / `offer_all_unsafe` / `take_unsafe` / `fail_cause_unsafe` / `end_unsafe` / `shutdown_unsafe` / `size_unsafe` / `is_full_unsafe`. `Done[L = None]` carries Effect's leftover `value` and lives in `pyfect.errors` (exported as `fx.Done`), with `errors.filter_done` (Effect's `Pull.filterDone`) for M6 to reuse: `cause` sits below `TaggedError` in the import graph, so it cannot hold a `TaggedError`. `Enqueue` / `Dequeue` are `Protocol`s that `Queue` subclasses: `Enqueue` is contravariant, `Dequeue` covariant (its batches are `Sequence`); only the invariant `Queue` takes `Done` out of `E` for `clear` / `collect` / `await_done`, through overloads, since pyright has no `Exclude`. `WaitList` keeps each waiter's condition and `notify_ready` wakes every waiter whose condition holds, as Effect's `releaseTakers` does: a batch take short of its minimum is not woken, and no wake-up is counted out to one fiber. (Waking exactly one taker per item lost items: a woken taker can pause for its step budget before it retries and be interrupted there, leaving the taker behind it parked next to the item; `test_queue.py::test_a_waiter_interrupted_between_its_wake_up_and_its_retry_strands_no_item`, checked under Node.) Many takers contending for single items pay for this, as in Effect: 100 consumers on one producer went from 59k to 27k items/s, while one consumer went from 271k to 325k. Not ported: `flush` (above), `isEnqueue` / `isDequeue` / `asEnqueue` / `asDequeue` (an `isinstance` and an annotation). `take_up_to(n)` predated v4 (which has no `takeUpTo`) and was removed: write `take_between(1, n)`; no v4 strategy raises `ExceededCapacityError`.)
- **PubSub:** bounded, dropping, sliding and unbounded variants with replay; Subscription. (done: `pyfect.pubsub` package, `fx.PubSub`; `tests/oracle/test_pubsub.py` ports every `PubSub.test.ts` title but four: the type guard and two fraction/NaN count titles; `Stream.fromPubSub` was ported with M6's Stream core. `tests/test_pubsub.py` pins edge cases printed by Effect under Node. API: `bounded` / `dropping` / `sliding(capacity, replay=)`, `unbounded(replay=)`, `make(atomic, strategy)`, `publish` / `publish_all` / `publish_unsafe`, `end(value)` / `end_unsafe`, `subscribe()` (needs `Scope`), `shutdown` / `is_shutdown` / `await_shutdown`, `size` / `is_full` / `is_empty` / `capacity`; `Subscription.take` / `take_all` / `take_up_to` / `take_between` / `remaining` / `remaining_unsafe`; the store `Atomic` (`bounded` / `unbounded`, `publish(_all)`, `slide`, `subscribe` → `BackingSubscription.poll` / `poll_up_to`, `replay_window`). One deque-backed `Atomic` replaces Effect's four store classes. As in Effect, takes waiting on one subscription are served in the order they started, and a message published while one waits is read for it at once and frees its room; a take that does not wait never reads it. It goes to the subscription's own buffer, not to the parked fiber, and the woken take collects it in the step it resumes (a frame below the wait), so a take interrupted before collecting leaves it to the next take (`tests/test_pubsub.py`, checked under Node). Four takers on a bounded hub run at about 55k (capacity 1) to 94k (capacity 16) messages/s. `Subscription` is covariant, so its batches are typed `Sequence`. A sliding publish slides before every surplus message, as Effect's (`tests/test_pubsub.py`, checked under Node). A parked take that an unsubscribe ends is interrupted by the unsubscribing fiber, as in Effect. Effect's shutdown interrupts parked publishers, but unsubscribing everyone first empties the store and admits them, so they complete in both.)
- **Refs:** Ref (`get_and_update`, `set_and_get`, the `*_some` family); SynchronizedRef; SubscriptionRef (with a queue-based `changes`) (done: `pyfect.ref`, `pyfect.synchronized_ref`, `pyfect.subscription_ref`, `fx.SynchronizedRef`, `fx.SubscriptionRef`; `tests/oracle/test_ref.py`, `test_synchronized_ref.py` and `test_subscription_ref.py` port every title of `Ref.test.ts`, `SynchronizedRef.test.ts`, `SynchronizedRef.modifySomeEffect.test.ts` and `SubscriptionRef.test.ts` but `isSubscriptionRef` (an `isinstance`); `tests/test_refs.py`. `Ref` gains `get_unsafe` (Effect's `getUnsafe`), `set_and_get`, `get_and_update`, `update_some`, `update_some_and_get`, `get_and_update_some` and `modify_some`; `Ref(x)` is `makeUnsafe`. `SynchronizedRef` and `SubscriptionRef` share one implementation (`pyfect/_locked_ref.py`): every update, plain or `*_effect`, holds a one-permit semaphore from reading the value to storing the new one, and `get` takes no permit, as in Effect. A `SubscriptionRef` publishes each value it stores to an unbounded `PubSub` replaying one message; a `*_some` update that keeps the value publishes nothing. Its `changes` is `subscribe()`, a scoped `pubsub.Subscription` whose first take is the current value; the three `changes` ports take from it in place of `Stream.take` / `runCollect`. M6 added the Stream-based `changes()` (`Stream.fromPubSub` of the ref's pubsub); the three `changes` ports now read it. MutableRef is a plain attribute (table below).)
- **Pool:** `make`, `make_with_ttl`, `invalidate`, `reserve`. (done: `pyfect.pool` package, `fx.Pool`; `tests/oracle/test_pool.py` ports all 55 `Pool.test.ts` titles, `tests/test_pool.py` adds argument checks and pyfect-only cases. API: `Pool.make(acquire, size=, concurrency=, target_utilization=)`, `make_with_ttl(acquire, min=, max=, time_to_live=, time_to_live_strategy="usage" | "creation")`, `make_with_strategy(acquire, min=, max=, strategy=)`; methods `get()` (needs `Scope`), `use(f)`, `invalidate(item)`, `reserve(item)` (needs `Scope`); public `config` (`PoolConfig`), `state` (`PoolState`: `items` / `invalidated` as ordered dicts, `waiters`, `usage`, the available list) and `PoolItem`, for custom `Strategy(run, on_acquire, reclaim)` objects. Sizes are checked when the pool is described (`0 <= min <= max`, `max >= 1`, `concurrency >= 1`, else `ValueError`); Effect accepts them and hangs. Items are compared by identity (`is`, Effect's `===`; since M5). Idle times are the pool clock's wall time in whole nanoseconds. As in Effect, waiters are woken in one scheduled task and each woken fiber runs at once, so it acts (say, interrupts the waiter behind it) before the next is woken. One difference remains: a resize that ends with the pool still below target wakes the waiters, because a borrower that found it holding its permit parked without starting one (in Effect that resize has already ended; `tests/test_pool.py`). The `scale to zero` port waits for the last borrowers to return before moving the clock, since they are served a few tasks later than in Effect; the GC titles collect across scheduler turns as Effect's `collectGarbage` collects across jobs; the two custom `shouldYield` titles run the woken fiber on a `TaskQueue` scheduler (one with a step budget of 1, so it pauses there once woken). `reportUnhandledError` logs a failing item finalizer at `UnhandledLogLevel` (since M5). `isPool` is `isinstance`.)
- **Cache:** Cache and ScopedCache. (done: `pyfect.cache` package, `fx.Cache`, `fx.ScopedCache`; `tests/oracle/test_cache.py` ports every `Cache.test.ts` title but two: "make - spans are connected" (tracing; ported in M5) and the `PersistedCache` defect title (ported in M10 wave 7); `tests/oracle/test_scoped_cache.py` ports every `ScopedCache.test.ts` title. API: `Cache.make(lookup, capacity=, time_to_live=, require_services_at="construction" | "lookup")` and the same `ScopedCache.make`; methods `get`, `get_option`, `get_success`, `set`, `has`, `invalidate`, `invalidate_when`, `refresh`, `invalidate_all`, `size`, `keys`, `values`, `entries`, plus `capacity` and `ScopedCache.is_closed`. Concurrent gets of a key share one lookup, which runs on a root fiber started at once (Effect's immediate fork) and is interrupted when its last waiter leaves; eviction is least recently used (`get`, `get_option` and `get_success` count as a use, `has` does not). Keys compare with `==` and `hash`. Each ScopedCache entry's lookup runs in a scope of its own, closed when the entry leaves the cache; closing the scope the cache was made in closes every entry and makes later calls interrupt. The make overloads and methods are pinned in `tests/type_checks.py`.)
- **Request batching:** Request, Entry, RequestResolver (constructors and combinators, `with_cache`), `fx.request`. (done: `pyfect.batching` package, `fx.Request`, `fx.RequestResolver`, `fx.request`, `fx.request_unsafe`; `tests/oracle/test_request.py` ports every `Request.test.ts` title but two JavaScript object-model titles (the six `persisted` titles since M10 wave 7), `tests/oracle/test_request_resolver.py` every `RequestResolver.test.ts` title (`withSpan` since M5); `Effect.test.ts` has no request section. `tests/test_batching.py` pins edge cases printed by Effect under Node (a failing run, an uncompleted request, `around` failures, `lru` / `fifo` eviction, ticks and batches, `from_function_batched` with too few values, `race`); `examples/batching.py` collapses N+1 author lookups into one query. API: `Request[A, E, R]` (subclass it as a frozen dataclass); `Entry[Q]` with `request`, `context`, `uninterruptible`, `complete_unsafe` and the effects `complete` / `complete_effect` / `succeed` / `fail` / `fail_cause`, each typed against the request's `A` / `E` through a self-type; `RequestResolver[Q, E]` built with `RequestResolver(run_all, batch_key=, delay=, collect_while=, pre_check=)` (Effect's `make` and `makeWith`), `make_grouped(key=, resolver=)`, `from_function`, `from_function_batched`, `from_effect`, `from_effect_tagged({Class: handler})` and `never()`; methods `batch_n`, `grouped`, `set_delay`, `set_delay_effect`, `around`, `race`, `with_cache(capacity=, strategy=)`; `fx.request(req, resolver_or_effect)` and `batching.request_unsafe(req, resolver=, on_exit=, context=)`. The engine follows Effect's `internal/request.ts`: a new batch starts a root fiber at once (Effect's synchronous `runForkWith`) that waits out the resolver's `delay` and runs the batch with the first caller's services; a request that makes `collect_while` say no interrupts that wait and runs the batch on a new root fiber; an interrupted caller leaves its batch, and the last to leave interrupts the wait. Each resolver keeps its own pending batches (Effect's `WeakMap` entry), so a derived resolver never joins its parent's batch. Pending batches are also kept per run (per asyncio loop for `run_async`, so concurrent runs on one loop share batches as Effect's runs do): a `run_sync`'s scheduler stops with its run, so a batch a daemon left there never runs, and a later run joining it through a module-level resolver hung (`tests/test_batching.py`). The ports poll a fiber a few yields later than Effect does, since pyfect resumes fibers through the scheduler.)
- **Exit criteria:** `Queue.test.ts` (full), `PubSub.test.ts`, `Ref.test.ts`, `SynchronizedRef.test.ts`, `SubscriptionRef.test.ts`, `Pool.test.ts`, `Cache.test.ts`, `ScopedCache.test.ts`, `Request.test.ts` / `RequestResolver.test.ts`.

### M5 — Observability (L; depends on M2; runs in parallel with M3–M4)
- **Logging:** `LogLevel` (IntEnum), log References, `fx.log*`, Logger model and formatters (simple, logfmt, structured, JSON, pretty TTY), `logger.layer`, `with_logger`, `annotate_logs(_scoped)`, `with_log_span`, `batched`, `to_logging` bridge.
- **Console:** Console service and TestConsole.
- **Tracing:** Tracer and Span (native), `with_span`, `use_span`, `make_span_scoped`, `fn("name")`, span annotations and links, `DisablePropagation`, `tracer_logger`.
- **Metrics:** Metric (counter, gauge, histogram, summary, frequency, timer) with a registry, `track*`, `snapshot`.
- **Error reporting:** ErrorReporter and `with_error_reporting`; `ignore(log=)`; `run_main` logs failures through the loggers.
- **Exit criteria:** `Logger.test.ts`, `LogLevel.test.ts`, `Tracer.test.ts`, `Metric.test.ts`, `Console.test.ts` (as applicable), `ErrorReporter.test.ts`, `Effect.test.ts` (log and span sections).

**Status (2026-10-27): done.** Every item above exists, is exported, has `assert_type` pins and is tested; each cluster's status follows. A title-by-title script check of the exit-criteria files finds every title ported or omitted with a stated reason: `Logger.test.ts` (12), `LogLevel.test.ts` (2), `Tracer.test.ts` (36; the five OTLP titles omitted), `Metric.test.ts` (69; the OTLP exporter title is covered through the OpenTelemetry producer), `PrometheusMetrics.test.ts` (26), the opentelemetry package's `OtelTracer` / `OtelLogger` / `OtelMetrics` / `Resource` tests (44; the HTTP router title omitted), and the log and span sections of `Effect.test.ts` (the `ignore` / `ignoreCause` log cases, `Effect.fn`, and the `tracing` section). Effect 4.0.6 has no `Console.test.ts` or `ErrorReporter.test.ts`; their behaviour is pinned against Effect under Node in `tests/test_console.py` and `tests/test_error_reporter.py`.

The completeness pass added the piece the clusters had left open: **stack frames and Cause annotations** (the M1 deferral "Cause annotations and stack traces wait for tracing", and the `tracing` section of `Effect.test.ts`). Each reason (`Fail`, `Die`, `Interrupt`) carries an `annotations` mapping, part of its equality but not its hash, with `annotate(ctx, overwrite=)`, `Cause.annotate`, `Cause.annotations` and `cause.reason_annotations`. `tracer.CurrentStackFrame` holds the frame chain (`StackFrame(name, stack, parent)`): `with_span`, `with_span_scoped`, `with_parent_span` (not for an external span) and `Layer.with_span` / `with_parent_span` run inside a frame named after the span at their call site (`capture_stack_trace=` turns it off or supplies the location), and an `@fx.fn("name")` call runs inside a frame for the call under one for the definition (`"name (definition)"`). A failure meets its next handler annotated with the frame it failed in (`tracer.StackTrace`, never overwritten), an interruption records the target's frame and the interrupting fiber's (`tracer.InterruptorStackTrace`, from `Fiber.interrupt`, `interrupt_all`, races, `for_each` and closed scopes), and `Cause.pretty()` prints each reason's frames under its traceback as `    at span (file:line)`. Ported: the three `Effect.test.ts` `tracing` titles and "should reuse the definition frame across calls" (`tests/oracle/test_effect_fn.py`), Tracer "should capture only the span frame for a string failure", Layer "withSpan forwards captureStackTrace to the layer stack frame", Fiber "retains distinct target and interruptor stack frames", the `Cause.test.ts` annotation titles and `CauseMapAnnotations.test.ts`. `tests/test_stack_frames.py` pins which frame each reason gets (nested spans, re-failing in an outer span, failing finalizers, `map_error`, defects, raised exceptions, `with_span_scoped`, external parents, races, children interrupted when their parent ends) and the pretty output, each printed by Effect under Node. The pass also covered the OpenTelemetry span view's attribute, event, link and exception writes and links added to a started `OtelSpan` (`tests/test_otel.py`); line coverage of the M5 modules is 98%, the rest being no-op span methods and `repr`s.

The full suite has 3,170 tests (one skipped: the extra-missing check). Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, best of 5, run alternately on one machine): map chain 3.16–3.26M steps/s, generator steps 2.23–2.32M, fork + join 169–175k/s, against 3.22–3.30M, 2.28–2.31M and 171–173k before the pass (within noise) and 3.27–3.37M, 2.30–2.38M and 169–176k at M4. The 2–4% below M4 on long chains is the tracer context-hook check on every step and continuation (Effect's fiber reads `tracerContext` the same way); stack frames cost nothing on the success path, since only a failing continuation checks for a frame.

**Logging status (2026-10-27): done.** `pyfect.logger` (`LogLevel`, the log references, `Logger` with `make` / `map` / `with_console_log` / `with_console_error` / `with_leveled_console` / `batched`, `format_simple` / `format_logfmt` / `format_structured` / `format_json`, `console_logfmt` / `console_structured` / `console_json` / `console_pretty`, `default_logger`, `layer(..., merge_with_existing=)`, `to_logging`), `fx.log` and its levels, `log_with_level`, `annotate_logs_scoped`, the `annotate_logs` / `with_log_span` / `with_logger` methods, `ignore(log=, message=)` / `ignore_cause(log=, message=)`, `Fiber.get_ref`, and a minimal `pyfect.console` (`Console` protocol, `CurrentConsole`, `LiveConsole`) for the console loggers. Pool and ScopedCache finalizer failures are logged at `UnhandledLogLevel`, as in Effect. Every title of `Logger.test.ts` and `LogLevel.test.ts` is ported (`tests/oracle/test_logger.py`, `test_log_level.py`), and so are the `Effect.ignore` / `Effect.ignoreCause` log cases (`tests/oracle/test_effect_errors.py`); `tests/test_logging.py` covers the rest. The cluster review mutation-tested the logic and added tests for what survived: annotation values compared as Effect's `===` / `Object.is` (equal numbers and strings, NaN, objects by identity), the detached `batched` timer, each logger installed once, and `to_logging` honouring the target's own level; each was checked against Effect under Node. (`tracer_logger` is in the default `CurrentLoggers` since the Tracing cluster.)
**Console status (2026-10-27): done.** `pyfect.console` is a package: the full `Console` protocol in Python spelling (`assert_`, `clear`, `count`, `count_reset`, `debug`, `dir`, `error`, `group`, `group_collapsed`, `group_end`, `info`, `log`, `table`, `time`, `time_end`, `time_log`, `trace`, `warn`), `CurrentConsole`, `LiveConsole` (Node 24's output: `table` boxes, `count`, timers in Node's elapsed format, `assert_`, `clear` on a TTY, group indentation on both streams), and an effect per method plus `console_with`, the scoped `group(label=, collapsed=)` / `time(label)` and `with_group(eff, label=, collapsed=)` / `with_time(eff, label)`. `pyfect.testing` adds `TestConsole` (records `Entry(method, parameters)`; `log_lines` / `error_lines` / `entries`; `TestConsole.layer()`), the `testing.log_lines` / `testing.error_lines` effects and `testing.test_console()`. `@testing.effect`, `run_test(_async)` and non-live `layer_fixture`s provide a fresh `TestConsole` as `@effect/vitest`'s `it.effect` and `layer` do; `@testing.live` keeps the live console. Effect has no `Console.test.ts`; the two `Logger.test.ts` titles that read `TestConsole.logLines` now do so (`tests/oracle/test_logger.py`), the TestClock "warnings bypass TestConsole" port checks the console too, and `tests/test_console.py` pins each effect's call and `LiveConsole` output printed by Node. `dirxml` is not ported (in Node it is `log`).

**Tracing status (2026-10-27): done** (stack frames since the completeness pass, above). `pyfect.tracer`: the span model (`Span` protocol, `ExternalSpan`, `AnySpan`, `SpanLink`, `Started` / `Ended` statuses, `SpanKind`, the `SpanOptions` keywords, `SpanRequest`), `Tracer(span, context=)` / `Tracer.make`, `NativeSpan` (lazy ids, the parent's trace id, events, ignores mutations once ended), `NoopSpan`, `native_tracer`, and the references `CurrentTracer`, `ParentSpan`, `DisablePropagation`, `CurrentTraceLevel`, `MinimumTraceLevel`, `TracerEnabled`, `TracerTimingEnabled`, `TracerSpanAnnotations`, `TracerSpanLinks`. Effects: `fx.make_span`, `fx.make_span_scoped`, `fx.use_span`, `fx.current_span`, `fx.current_parent_span`, `fx.annotate_current_span`; methods `with_span`, `with_span_scoped`, `with_parent_span`, `annotate_spans`, `link_spans`, `with_tracer`, `with_tracer_enabled`, `with_tracer_timing`; `@fx.fn("name", **span_options)`; `logger.tracer_logger` (a default logger); `layer.span(name, on_end=)`, `layer.parent_span`, `Layer.with_span` / `with_parent_span`; `RequestResolver.with_span`. A tracer's `context` hook runs around every fiber step and continuation (Effect's `Tracer.context`), for an OpenTelemetry-style bridge. `use_span` pushes the frame that ends the span in the step that makes it, so an interrupt cannot strand a span (swept over step budgets in the port). `tests/oracle/test_tracer.py` ports every `Tracer.test.ts` title but the five OTLP ones; `tests/oracle/test_effect_fn.py` the `Effect.fn` section (pipeables since the core-B coverage pass); the Cache span test, the Layer `withSpan` / `withParentSpan` titles, `RequestResolver.withSpan` and the span checks of `timeoutOrElse` "source and fallback inherit caller context" are ported in their files. `tests/test_tracing.py` pins `fn("name")`, forks, the tracer logger, sampling and propagation edge cases printed by Effect under Node. `Effect.fn` pipeables were added in the core-B coverage pass. Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, run alternately with the previous commit): map chain 3.15–3.19M vs 3.22M steps/s, generator steps 2.21–2.23M vs 2.25M, fork + join 166k vs 166k; the context-hook check costs about 1–2% on long `map` chains.

**Metrics status (2026-10-27): done.** `pyfect.metric` (`fx.metric`, `fx.Metric`): `counter` (`incremental=`, `bigint=True` types it `int`), `gauge`, `frequency` (`preregistered_words=`), `histogram`, `summary`, `summary_with_timestamp`, `timer`, the `linear_boundaries` / `exponential_boundaries` / `boundaries_from_iterable` helpers; `Metric` methods `value`, `update`, `modify`, `remove`, `map_input`, `with_constant_input`, `with_attributes`, and the `value_unsafe` / `update_unsafe` / `modify_unsafe` / `remove_unsafe` reads and writes against a `Context`; `register_unsafe`; the references `MetricRegistry` (a `dict`, provide `{}` for a fresh one) and `CurrentMetricAttributes`; `snapshot`, `snapshot_unsafe` and `dump` (effect values, as Effect's); `FiberRuntimeMetrics` with `fiber_runtime_metrics`, `enable_runtime_metrics` / `disable_runtime_metrics` and their layers (the fiber records its start and end, as Effect's fiber does); Effect methods `track`, `track_successes`, `track_errors`, `track_defects`, `track_duration`; and `prometheus_format` / `prometheus_format_unsafe` (Effect's `PrometheusMetrics.format`; `layerHttp` waits for an HTTP server). Series are keyed by kind, id, description and sorted attributes, and merged attributes are cached per contextual attribute set, as in Effect. `tests/oracle/test_metric.py` ports every `Metric.test.ts` title but the OTLP exporter one (its three OpenTelemetry delta-interval cases are in `tests/oracle/test_otel_metrics.py`); `tests/oracle/test_prometheus_metrics.py` ports every `PrometheusMetrics.test.ts` title. `tests/test_metric.py` pins `dump` and Prometheus text, JavaScript number printing (`1e+21`, `1.5e-7`), boundaries, summary aging (inclusive `max_age`, future and non-finite timestamps, the ring buffer), quantiles on an exact rank, `exponential_boundaries` overflowing to `Infinity`, timer milliseconds of a `track_duration` (`nanos / 1e6`, through `duration.to_millis`) and NaN histogram values, each printed by Effect under Node. Benchmarks (`benchmarks/runtime.py`) are unchanged: the runtime-metrics hook costs one attribute check per fiber start and end.

**Error reporting status (2026-10-27): done.** `pyfect.error_reporter` (`fx.error_reporter`, `fx.ErrorReporter`): the `ErrorReporter` protocol (`report(*, cause, fiber, timestamp)`), `make(f)` (one `Report(cause, error, attributes, severity, fiber, timestamp)` per typed error or defect; interruptions, ignored errors and causes or errors seen before are skipped, by identity, as Effect's `WeakSet`; a built-in exception, which CPython cannot weakly reference, is marked in its `__dict__`), `CurrentErrorReporters`, `layer(reporters, merge_with_existing=)`, `report(cause)`, `is_ignored` / `get_severity` / `get_attributes`; the method `with_error_reporting(defects_only=)`. Errors opt out or tag their report with `report_ignored`, `report_severity` and `report_attributes` (declared on `TaggedError`, read from any object). `run_main` now logs a failure that is not only an interrupt with `log_error(cause)` through the run's loggers (the default logger prints `[time] ERROR (#1): <pretty cause>` on stdout, as Effect's `console.log` does), then exits with the teardown's status; it used to raise the error for a Python traceback. `disable_error_reporting` and an error's `error_reported = False` skip the log. Effect 4.0.6 has no `ErrorReporter.test.ts`: `tests/test_error_reporter.py` pins every behaviour above against Effect's `ErrorReporter.ts` under Node (nested reporting, `defectsOnly`, identity dedupe, primitives, severities, layers, finalizer causes, the clock timestamp), and `tests/test_run_main.py` pins `runMain`'s log output, a finalizer's error in it, `errorReported` and that a program's own logger does not see it. Effect v4 logs nothing for an unobserved fiber failure or a failing scope finalizer (their causes join the exit), so there is nothing more to port there; Pool and ScopedCache already log at `UnhandledLogLevel` (Logging cluster). `Cause` gained a weakref slot so reporters can remember causes without keeping them alive.

**OpenTelemetry bridge status (2026-10-27): done.** The `otel` extra (`opentelemetry-api` and `opentelemetry-sdk` >= 1.45, also in the `dev` group so the tests run) and the `pyfect.otel` package, Effect's `@effect/opentelemetry`: `otel.tracer` (`OtelTracer`, `OtelTracerProvider`, `OtelTraceFlags`, `OtelTraceState` tags, `OtelSpan`, `make`, `make_external_span`, `current_otel_span`, `with_span_context` for `.pipe`, and the layers `layer`, `layer_global`, `layer_global_provider`, `layer_tracer`, `layer_global_tracer`, `layer_without_otel_tracer`), `otel.logger` (`OtelLoggerProvider`, `make`, `layer(merge_with_existing=True)`, `layer_logger_provider(processors, shutdown_timeout=)`, `log_level_to_severity_number`), `otel.metrics` (`MetricProducer` with `collect` / `fork`, `make_producer`, `register_producer`, `layer(lambda: readers, temporality=, shutdown_timeout=)`), `otel.resource` (`OtelResource`, `layer`, `layer_from_env`, `layer_empty`, `config_to_attributes`) and `otel.sdk` (Effect's `NodeSdk`: `Configuration`, `ResourceConfig`, `layer(config | () -> config | effect)`, `layer_tracer_provider`, `layer_empty`). The tracer uses the core's `Tracer.context` hook to attach the current span (marked so a nested run does not inherit it) around each fiber step. `tests/oracle/test_otel_tracer.py`, `test_otel_logger.py`, `test_otel_metrics.py` and `test_otel_resource.py` port every title of `OtelTracer.test.ts`, `OtelLogger.test.ts`, `OtelMetrics.test.ts` and `Resource.test.ts` but the HTTP router one, plus `Metric.test.ts`'s delta interval title; they skip when the extra is missing. `tests/test_otel.py` pins attribute values (ints and floats mixed are one number array, as JavaScript numbers are; an int beyond 64 bits turns a scalar or an array into text, as a `bigint` does), summaries, frequencies, delta frequencies, summaries and negative counter deltas, reader shutdown, links, log events, propagation past a disabled span and the view's last status, and checks no module outside `pyfect/otel` imports `opentelemetry`. `WebSdk` is not ported (browsers only).

### M6 — Stream (XL; depends on M4 for PubSub and Latch, M3 for Schedule)
- **Order:** `Done[L]` + Pull helpers → Take → Channel core (`from_transform`, `to_pull`, `pipe_to`, run functions) → Channel constructors, sources, transforms, errors, concurrency (`merge_all`, `switch_map`, `buffer`) → Stream core, constructors, sources (iterable, `callback`, queue, `from_async_iterable`, `tick`) → map / flat_map / take / drop / filter / chunking / zip / merge / timing (`debounce`, `throttle`) → Sink → `run` / `transduce` → `group_by`, `broadcast`, `share` (need PubSub) → text and bytes → `to_async_iterator`.
- **Exit criteria:** `Pull.test.ts`, `Channel.test.ts`, `Stream.test.ts`, `Sink.test.ts`.

**Pull, Take and Channel core status (2026-10-27): done.** `pyfect.pull` (`fx.pull`, `fx.Pull`): the `Pull[A, E, L, R]` alias (`Effect[A, E | Done[L], R]`), `is_done_failure`, `is_done_cause`, `filter_done` (from `pyfect.errors`), `filter_no_done`, `filter_done_leftover`, `done_exit_from_cause`, and the data-first `catch_done(pull, f)` and `match_effect(pull, on_success=, on_failure=, on_done=)`. `pyfect.take` (`fx.take`, `fx.Take`): `Take[A, E, L]` (`tuple[A, ...] | Exit[L, E]`) and `to_pull`. `pyfect.channel` (`fx.channel`, `fx.Channel`): `Channel[OutElem, OutErr, OutDone, InElem, InErr, InDone, Env]` with defaults `Never`, `None`, `object`, `object`, `object`, `Never`; `from_transform`, `to_transform`, `transform_pull`, `from_transform_bracket`, `from_pull`; constructors `succeed`, `sync`, `end`, `end_sync`, `empty`, `never`, `fail`, `fail_sync`, `fail_cause`, `fail_cause_sync`, `die`, `from_effect`, `from_effect_done`, `from_effect_drain`, `from_effect_take`, `from_iterable`, `from_iterator`, `from_iterable_array`, `from_iterator_array` (`DEFAULT_CHUNK_SIZE` 4096), `suspend`, `unwrap`, `identity`, `acquire_use_release`, `acquire_release`; methods `map`, `map_done`, `map_done_effect`, `concat`, `concat_with`, `pipe_to`, `run_collect`, `run_drain`, `run_for_each`, `run_for_each_while`, `run_fold`, `run_fold_effect`, `run_count`, `run_head`, `run_last`, `to_pull` (the caller's services, one puller at a time, as Effect's semaphore) and `to_pull_scoped`. `tests/oracle/test_pull.py` ports every `Pull.test.ts` title; `tests/oracle/test_channel.py` ports the constructor, destructor and `map` titles of `Channel.test.ts` and lists the rest with the reason (Web Streams; the combinators of the later Channel clusters). `tests/test_channel.py` pins run functions, resource order (`concat` releases the first channel before acquiring the second; `run_head` releases with the run's exit), `to_pull`, `unwrap`'s scope, `Take` and the Pull helpers against Effect under Node. Typing: pyright cannot take `Done` out of a union, so `from_transform`, `from_pull` and the Pull helpers read `E | Done[L]` through overloads (an upstream that cannot fail, a pull that only ends); `assert_type` pins in `tests/type_checks.py` cover every constructor, method and helper, and that a channel needing input cannot run. Runtime: pull loops are a `PullLoop` primitive (one continuation per item) and iterators an `IteratorPull` / `ChunkPull` primitive (one step per item or chunk); every `Channel._transform` call is lazy, so channels nested 20,000 deep (`map`, `pipe_to`, `concat`, `map_done`) build on the fiber's stack instead of overflowing Python's (they recursed before the fix; regression tests in `tests/test_channel.py`). `benchmarks/channel.py`: 1M items through a source, two `map`s and a `pipe_to` run at 1.0M items/s item by item and 21.5M items/s in chunks of 4096 (CPython 3.14, M-series Mac). Fixed on the way: `Cause.elect_defects` turned a `Done` beside a defect into a defect, where Effect's `causeElectDefects` leaves `Done` alone, so a failing `acquire_use_release` release hid the channel's end (`tests/test_cause_recovery.py::test_electing_defects_keeps_a_done_signal`). The `require_effect` guard test now also checks `Channel` parameters (`require_channel`). Review fixes, checked against Effect under Node: a channel's build never fails, as in Effect where every channel goes through `fromTransform`'s `catchCause`. `Channel._transform` itself now turns a failing or raising build into a failing pull, so `to_pull` of `from_iterator`, `end_sync`, `fail_sync` and others with a raising thunk succeeds with a pull that dies instead of dying itself. A chunked generator whose length is a multiple of `chunk_size` ends later pulls with `Done()`, as Effect's does. New tests cover `to_pull` taking turns while a pull is suspended, `to_pull` keeping the services it was built with, and `concat` closing the first channel's scope before building the second (each of these mutations used to pass).

**Channel constructors, transforms, errors and concurrency status (2026-10-27): done.** `Channel` is assembled from method groups as `Effect` is (`pyfect/channel/_base.py` holds `ChannelBase` and the shared helpers `end_with`, `is_done`, `require_channel`; each group delegates to an implementation module imported when called). Transforms (`_transform_methods.py`): `map_effect(concurrency=, unordered=)` (concurrent: a producer fiber forks each `f(item)`, an ordered consumer joins them in turn, as Effect's `mapEffectConcurrent`), `tap`, `map_input`, `map_input_error`, `flat_map(concurrency=, buffer_size=)`, `flatten`, `flatten_array`, `flatten_take`, `drain`, `filter` (`TypeIs` narrows), `filter_effect`, `filter_array`, `filter_array_effect`, `scan`, `scan_effect`, `map_accum` (tuple or effect step, `on_halt`), `or_else_if_empty`, `combine`, `pipe_to_or_fail` (upstream failures travel as `Die(Done(cause))` and are unwrapped after, as Effect). Failures (`_error_methods.py`): `catch_cause` (closes the failed channel's forked scope before building the handler's channel), `catch_cause_if`, `catch_all` (Effect's `catch`), `catch_if(pred, f, or_else=)`, `catch_defect`, `tap_cause`, `tap_error`, `map_error`, `or_die`, `ignore(log=)`, `ignore_cause(log=)`, `retry(schedule or builder)` (a run that writes resets the policy; `schedule.CurrentMetadata` per run); typed recovery goes through `Cause.find_recoverable_error`, so defects win. Lifecycle (`_lifecycle_methods.py`): `on_start`, `on_first`, `on_end`, `on_exit`, `on_error`, `ensuring`, `with_span`, `update_service`, `repeat`, `forever` (each run in a child scope closed before the next), `schedule`. Concurrency (`_concurrency_methods.py`): `merge(halt_strategy=)`, `merge_effect`, `merge_all(concurrency=, buffer_size=16, switch=)`, `switch_map`, `buffer` / `buffer_array(capacity=, strategy=)`, `interrupt_when`, `halt_when`, `embed_input`. Running (`_run_methods.py`): `run_bytes` (Effect's `mkUint8Array`), `run_into_queue(_array)`, `to_queue(_array)`, `run_into_pubsub(_array, shutdown_on_end=)`, `to_pubsub(_array)`, `to_pubsub_take`. Functions in `pyfect.channel`: the type-removing `catch_tag`, `catch_if` (a `TypeIs` refinement), `provide_service`, `provide_service_effect`, `provide_context`, `provide(layer or context, local=)`, `update_context`, `scoped` (curried, used with `.pipe`); sources `callback`, `callback_array`, `from_queue(_array)`, `from_subscription(_array)`, `from_pubsub(_array)`, `from_pubsub_take`, `from_schedule`, `from_async_iterable(_array)` (needs asyncio; closes an idle iterator with `aclose` awaited, a busy one without waiting, as Effect's `return()`); text `decode_text(encoding, fatal=, ignore_bom=)` (an incremental codec; WHATWG's BOM rule), `encode_text`, `split_lines`. `tests/oracle/test_channel.py` now ports every `Channel.test.ts` title but the Web Streams and reason-error ones (listed in its docstring; the `Filter` titles came with the completeness pass); `tests/test_channel_ops.py`, `test_channel_concurrency.py` and `test_channel_io.py` pin about 50 more behaviours, each printed by Effect under Node first. `assert_type` pins cover every group, that `merge_all` needs a channel of channels, that `provide_service` checks the service's type and that `run_into_queue` checks the queue's. Typing: `merge_all` and `combine` use one shared `EE` / `RR` where several channels meet (separate `Never`-defaulted TypeVars widened to `Unknown`), and `callback` / `embed_input` read `R | Scope` / `E | Done[L]` through overloads. Review fixes, checked against Effect under Node: `merge` dropped a finalizer defect of a side that ended (`ensuring(die)` on the left, any halt strategy), because the side's cause still held its `Done`; it now fails as Effect's `onExitPrimitive(doneExitFromCause(cause), close)` does. The merge handlers take `Cause[Any]` rather than `Any`, so pyright checks them. New tests kill mutants that used to survive: `catch_cause` releasing the failed channel before the handler, `repeat` / `retry` releasing each run before the next, `switch_map` finalizing a replaced channel at once, concurrent `map_effect` starting nothing after a failure, `ignore(log=True)` not logging defects, and `callback` shutting its queue down. Merged items interleave differently from Effect under backpressure (`merge_all(buffer_size=1)` writes `[1, 2, 1, 1, 2, 2]` where Effect writes `[1, 2, 1, 2, 1, 2]`), and which inner channels have started when a merge ends can differ; both follow from resumes going through the scheduler.

**Stream core, constructors, sources and run functions status (2026-10-27): done.** `pyfect.stream` (`fx.stream`, `fx.Stream`): `Stream[A, E = Never, R = Never]` (covariant) is a `Channel[tuple[A, ...], E, None, object, object, object, R]` of non-empty tuples (`stream.channel`, `to_channel`, `from_channel`), assembled from method groups as `Channel` is (`pyfect/stream/_base.py`; only `RunMethods` so far, so the operator clusters add groups beside it). Core: `from_pull`, `transform_pull`, `transform_pull_bracket`. Constructors: `empty`, `never`, `succeed`, `make`, `sync`, `suspend`, `fail`, `fail_sync`, `fail_cause`, `fail_cause_sync`, `die`, `from_effect`, `from_effect_drain`, `from_effect_repeat` (ends when the effect fails with `Done()`), `from_effect_schedule` (each run sees the step's `CurrentMetadata`), `service`, `service_option`, `from_iterable(chunk_size=)` (a list or tuple is one chunk unless `chunk_size` is given), `from_arrays`, `from_iterable_effect`, `from_iterable_effect_repeat`, `unfold`, `paginate` (`Option` next state), `iterate`, `range` / `range_inclusive` (open question 9), `unwrap`, `scoped` (curried for `.pipe`). Sources: `callback(f, buffer_size=, strategy=)`, `from_queue`, `from_subscription`, `from_pubsub`, `from_pubsub_take`, `from_schedule`, `tick`, `from_async_iterable`. Runs (methods): `run_collect`, `run_drain`, `run_count`, `run_sum`, `run_fold`, `run_fold_effect`, `run_head`, `run_last` (`Option`), `run_for_each`, `run_for_each_while`, `run_for_each_array`, `run_string` (Effect's `mkString`), `run_bytes` (`mkUint8Array` / `mkArrayBuffer`), `to_pull`. `SubscriptionRef.changes()` is `from_pubsub` of the ref's pubsub. `tests/oracle/test_stream.py` ports the `callback`, `destructors`, `constructors` and `pagination` sections of `Stream.test.ts` (the Web Streams, DOM-event and `toAsyncIterable` titles are listed with their reason; the other sections port with their operators); `PubSub.test.ts`'s "Stream.fromPubSub completes after shutdown" and the three `SubscriptionRef.changes` titles now read streams. The test-only stand-ins this cluster used (`tests/oracle/_stream_ops.py`) were replaced by the real methods and removed by the completeness pass. `tests/test_stream.py` pins 37 behaviours printed by Effect under Node (schedules, `from_effect_schedule` stepping with the latest value and giving the first run fresh metadata, a tuple as one chunk, `paginate` skipping empty pages, `run_head` pulling one chunk, `from_queue` chunking, `unwrap`'s resource lifetime, ...). `assert_type` pins cover every constructor, source and run function, that `from_channel` rejects a channel of bare items and that `run_string` needs a stream of `str`; the argument guard test now checks `Stream` parameters too (`require_stream`). Typing: `from_effect_schedule` shares one `EE` / `RR` between the effect and the schedule (separate defaulted TypeVars widened to `Unknown`). Differences: `callback`'s "backpressure" port yields once after the pull before counting offers, since the offers it frees resume through the scheduler; `from_iterable` copies a list when the stream is built (Effect keeps the array, so later pushes show). Throughput: `range(0, 1_000_000)` sums at 86M items/s with `run_sum`, 27M/s with `run_fold`, and `run_for_each` with an effect per item runs at 2.4M/s (CPython 3.14, M-series Mac). The per-item runs (`run_for_each`, `run_for_each_while`, `run_fold_effect`) call `f` directly on a one-item chunk instead of looping in a generator, which took one-item-chunk streams (`unfold`, `iterate`, `tick`) from 0.43M to 0.64M items/s.

**Stream transforms status (2026-10-27): done but for the Sink-based operators.** `Stream` gains method groups beside `RunMethods`: `MapMethods` (`map`, `as_`, `map_both`, `map_array`, `map_effect(concurrency=, unordered=)`, `map_array_effect`, `flatten_effect`, `result`, `tap`, `tap_both`, `flat_map(concurrency=, buffer_size=)`, `switch_map`, `flatten`, `flatten_array`, `flatten_iterable`, `flatten_take`, `drain`, `drain_fork`, `concat`, `prepend`, `intersperse`, `intersperse_affixes`, `when`), `FilterMethods` (`filter` with a `TypeIs` overload, `filter_map`, `filter_effect`, `filter_map_effect`, `take`, `take_right`, `take_until(exclude_last=)`, `take_until_effect`, `take_while` with a `TypeIs` overload, `take_while_filter`, `take_while_effect`, `limit_bytes` on `Stream[bytes]`, `drop`, `drop_right`, `drop_while`, `drop_while_filter`, `drop_while_effect`, `drop_until`, `drop_until_effect`, `changes`, `changes_with`, `changes_with_effect`), `ChunkMethods` (`chunks`, `collect`, `accumulate`, `rechunk`, `grouped`, `sliding`, `sliding_size`, `split`, `scan`, `scan_effect`, the four `map_accum*`, `zip_with_index`, `zip_with_next`, `zip_with_previous`, `zip_with_previous_and_next`), `ZipMethods` (`zip`, `zip_with`, `zip_with_array`, `zip_left`, `zip_right`, `zip_flatten`, `zip_latest`, `zip_latest_with`, `cross`, `cross_with`, `merge(halt_strategy=)`, `merge_effect`, `merge_result`, `merge_left`, `merge_right`, `interleave`, `interleave_with`, `race`, `combine`, `combine_array`), `ErrorMethods` (`catch_all`, `catch_cause`, `catch_cause_if`, `catch_if`, `catch_filter`, `catch_cause_filter`, `catch_defect`, `or_else_if_empty`, `or_else_succeed`, `tap_cause`, `tap_error`, `tap_error_tag`, `tap_defect`, `map_error`, `or_die`, `ignore(log=)`, `ignore_cause(log=)`, `retry`) and `TimingMethods` (`schedule`, `repeat`, `repeat_elements`, `forever`, `timeout`, `timeout_or_else`, `debounce`, `throttle`, `throttle_effect`, `buffer`, `buffer_array`, `halt_when`, `interrupt_when`, `on_start`, `on_first`, `on_end`, `on_exit`, `on_error`, `ensuring`); functions `merge_all`, `zip_latest_all`, `race_all` and the curried, type-removing `catch_tag` / `catch_if`. `concat` flattens a chain into one walk (`ConcatStream`, `SuspendedStream`), so a chain of n parts costs O(n). Ports: `tests/oracle/test_stream_filtering.py`, `test_stream_errors.py`, `test_stream_combining.py`, `test_stream_timing.py`, `test_stream_zipping.py`, each listing what it adapts and leaves out; `tests/test_stream_transforms.py` pins edge cases printed by Effect under Node (`take_until(exclude_last=)`, `changes_with`, `split` chunking, interleaving a shorter side, `race` closing a loser that also wrote, `zip_latest(_all)` pairs once the turns are fixed, a `timeout` deadline per pull, the `throttle` bucket cap, `catch_filter`'s rejected value, `combine`). Fixed on the way: `throttle` refilled from float seconds, so equal steps refilled unequally (`0.6 - 0.4 < 0.2`) and a `0` duration raised `ZeroDivisionError`; it now counts whole nanoseconds and divides as Effect does (`x / 0` is infinite). Two ports differ from Effect because woken fibers resume through the scheduler: the ordered `map_effect` defect title runs without its one-second timeout, and two `haltWhen` titles yield once after completing the halt deferred. Review fixes, each with a failing test first: `Channel.flat_map` reused a child scope that an inner channel had closed, so the next inner stream's finalizers ran at once ("an inner stream that closes its scope does not affect the next one"); reuse now asks `CloseableScope.is_empty` (Effect's `Empty` state: open, no finalizers), which a closed scope never is. `interleave_with` typed the decider's errors and services with a second pair of `Never`-defaulted TypeVars, so two error-free streams gave `Stream[..., Unknown, Unknown]`; it shares `EE` / `RR`, and `tests/test_typing_rules.py` now fails on any signature with two `Never`-defaulted error (or service) TypeVars. Added `collect`, `accumulate`, `combine` and `combine_array`, which were missing. Ported titles the first pass had left out or wrongly called unportable: the `zipping` section, the `bufferArray` / `buffer` dropping, sliding and suspend titles, the two `throttleShape` clock titles (a delegating `ClockService` stands in for Effect's object spread), the async-iterator `timeout` titles (`testing.live(asyncio=True)`), and three `concat` titles. Differences: `flatten_array` skips an empty tuple where Effect writes an empty chunk (pyfect's chunks are never empty); which pairs `zip_latest` writes for two synchronous streams follows the merge's turn order, which differs from Effect's (Channel merges above). Left for later clusters: `aggregate*`, `groupedWithin`, `tapSink`, `peel`, `transduce` (Sink); `catchReason(s)`, `unwrapReason` (not in `Effect` either); `catchTags` (tags are classes: `catch_tag` takes a tuple); `withExecutionPlan`; the context functions (added by the completeness pass); `groupBy`, `share`, `broadcast`, `partition`.

**Sink, run and transduce status (2026-10-27): done.** `pyfect.sink` (`fx.sink`, `fx.Sink`): `Sink[A, In = object, L = Never, E = Never, R = Never]` (`In` contravariant, the rest covariant) is a transform from an upstream pull of non-empty tuples (`sink.Upstream[In]`, ending with `Done()`) and a `CloseableScope` to an effect ending with an `End[A, L]` (`tuple[A, tuple[L, ...] | None]`: the value and the input read but not used). Constructors: `from_transform`, `from_channel` / `to_channel`, `make` (a stream pipeline, run with the sink's `Scope`), `from_effect`, `from_effect_end`, `succeed(value, leftover)`, `sync`, `suspend`, `fail`, `fail_sync`, `fail_cause`, `fail_cause_sync`, `die`, `never`, `drain`, `timed`, `from_queue` (ends the queue), `from_pubsub`; folds `fold`, `fold_array`, `fold_until`, `every`, `some`, `reduce`, `reduce_array`, `reduce_effect`, `reduce_while`, `reduce_while_array`, `reduce_while_effect`, `reduce_while_array_effect`, `sum()`, `count`, `collect()`, `head()`, `last()`, `find` (`TypeIs` overload), `find_effect`; `take`, `take_while` (`TypeIs` overload), `take_while_filter`, `take_while_effect`, `take_while_filter_effect`, `take_until`, `take_until_effect`; `for_each`, `for_each_array`, `for_each_while`, `for_each_while_array`. Methods: `map`, `as_`, `map_effect`, `map_end`, `map_effect_end`, `map_input`, `map_input_effect`, `map_input_array`, `map_input_array_effect`, `map_leftover`, `ignore_leftover`, `flat_map` (the first sink's leftovers feed the second; unread ones stay leftovers, after the second's), `summarized`, `with_duration` (float seconds, whole monotonic nanoseconds, as `Effect.timed`), `map_error`, `catch_all` (Effect's `catch`), `catch_cause`, `or_else` (the fallback reads the rest of the input), `on_exit`, `ensuring`; the curried `provide_service`, `provide_context` and `unwrap` (removes `Scope`). `Stream` gains `SinkMethods` (`pyfect/stream/_sink_methods.py`, work in `_sinking.py`): `run`, `transduce`, `aggregate`, `aggregate_within(sink, schedule)` (a producer fiber, a zero-capacity buffer, the schedule racing each run once input arrives, its clock leaving out the time between runs), `grouped_within`, `pipe_through`, `tap_sink`, `peel`. As in Effect, a stream's own failures reach a sink as hidden `Die(Done(cause))` defects (`channel._upstream`) and are restored after the sink exits, so `map_error`, `catch_all`, `catch_cause` and `or_else` never see them and `on_exit` sees an interrupt; `transduce` instead ends the current run and fails after writing it. Ports: `tests/oracle/test_sink.py` (every `Sink.test.ts` title but the three `fromWritableStream` ones, Web Streams), `tests/oracle/test_stream_sinks.py` (the sink titles of `Stream.test.ts`: the three error-handling ones, `transduce`, `aggregateWithin` with `groupedWithin`, `tapSink`, `peel`; the commented-out `foldWeighted` title is v3). `tests/test_sink.py` pins 26 more behaviours printed by Effect under Node (the folds' leftovers, `map_leftover` / `ignore_leftover` through `pipe_through`, `or_else` after the end, `flat_map` putting its pending leftovers after the next sink's, the channel round trip, services, `unwrap`, `make`, `from_queue`, `summarized`, `aggregate`, `tap_sink` with a sink that stops early). Review additions, each killing a mutant that used to survive: `flat_map`, `or_else` and `peel` never pull an upstream that has ended, `reduce_while_array_effect` reads nothing when the initial state fails, `unwrap`'s resources close when the sink run ends, `aggregate_within`'s schedule clock leaves out the idle time between runs, and `aggregate` with `schedule.forever` ends a run per upstream chunk under the TestClock. Difference: under the live clock that last case batches more (`[[1, 2, 3, 4, 5], [6]]` where Effect writes `[[1, 2], [3, 4, 5], [6]]`), since the schedule's zero-delay step and the producer resume through the scheduler in a different order. The argument guard now checks `Sink` parameters (`require_sink`); it caught `summarized` taking a non-effect. Typing: generic sinks without arguments are functions (`sum()`, `collect()`, `head()`, `last()`) so `stream.run(sink.collect())` infers the item type from the stream; `transduce`, `aggregate*` and `peel` take a sink whose leftovers are its input (`Sink[B, A, A]`, Effect's types) with an overload for a stream of `Never`, and `flat_map` one for a sink without leftovers, since pyright otherwise reads the input type off a `Never`. `aggregate_within` shares one `EE` / `RR` between the sink and the schedule. Counts are `int`, so the ports pass `-1` / `0` where Effect passes `NaN` and fractions. Throughput (CPython 3.14, M-series Mac): `range(0, 1_000_000).run(sink.sum())` matches `run_sum` (11 ms); an effectful `fold` runs at 2.3M items/s.

**Grouping, partitioning, broadcasting and bridges status (2026-10-27): done.** Two more method groups on `Stream`. `FanOutMethods` (`pyfect/stream/_fanout_methods.py`, work in `_grouping.py` and `_fanout.py`): `group_by(f, buffer_size=, idle_time_to_live=)` and `group_by_key` (an `RcMap` of per-key queues, `GROUP_BUFFER_SIZE` 4096, keys `Hashable` and compared by `==`; a group that stops early shuts its queue down, so it never blocks the others; an idle group ends and its key's next value starts a new one, checked under Node), `group_adjacent_by` (`(key, tuple)` runs), `partition(f, capacity=16)`, `partition_effect(f, capacity=, concurrency=)`, `partition_queue` (the filter returns `Ok` / `Err`; the queues end or fail as the stream does), `broadcast(capacity=, strategy=, replay=)`, `broadcast_n(n, ...)` and `share(..., idle_time_to_live=)` (an `RcRef` of the broadcast). `BridgeMethods` (`_bridge_methods.py`, `_async_iterable.py`): `to_queue`, `run_into_queue`, `to_pubsub`, `to_pubsub_take`, `run_into_pubsub` (over the Channel's tuple variants) and `to_async_iterable` / `to_async_iterable_with(context)` / `to_async_iterable_effect()` (`StreamAsyncIterable` / `StreamAsyncIterator`, exported from `pyfect.stream`). `to_pull` was already there. `tests/oracle/test_stream_fanout.py` ports all 38 titles of the `grouping`, `share`, `groupAdjacentBy`, both `partition`, `broadcast` and `broadcastN` sections and the eight `toAsyncIterable` titles (four adapted, reasons in its docstring); `tests/test_stream_fanout.py` adds the idle-group and failure cases (checked under Node) and Effect's doc examples for the queue and pubsub bridges. `stream.transform_pull` / `transform_pull_bracket` now share one `RR` between the stream and `f`: with two (`R | RR`) a stream needing no services came out `Stream[..., Unknown]` (CLAUDE.md's rule; pinned in `tests/type_checks.py`). Not ported, as in v4: `partitionEither`, `broadcastDynamic`, `distributedWith` (v3 only).

**Text, bytes and the encoding framers status (2026-10-27): done but for the schema variants.** `Stream` gains `ChannelMethods` (`pyfect/stream/_channel_methods.py`, work in `_text.py`): `pipe_through_channel` (the channel sees the stream's failure; its end value is dropped), `pipe_through_channel_or_fail` (Effect's `pipeThroughChannelOrFail`), `decode_text(encoding="utf-8")` on `Stream[bytes]`, `encode_text()` and `split_lines()` on `Stream[str]` (Effect's `Stream.decodeText` / `encodeText` / `splitLines`, over the Channel text channels). v4 has no `splitOn` or `utf8Decode`-style variants, so none were added. `pyfect.encoding` (`fx.encoding`, Effect's `effect/encoding` framers, which only need Channel): `ndjson` (`encode`, `encode_string`, `decode(ignore_empty_lines=, max_line_bytes=)`, `decode_string`, `duplex`, `duplex_string`, `NdjsonError(kind, cause, reason=, unterminated=)`), `json_array` (`decode` / `decode_string(pointer=, max_depth=, max_element_size=)` on an incremental scanner in `_json_array_scanner.py`, `encode`, `encode_string`, `JsonArrayError`) and `sse` (`make_parser` / `Parser`, `decode(max_event_size=, dispatch_empty_named_events=)`, `encode`, `write` for Effect's `encoder.write`, `Event` as a frozen dataclass, `Retry`, `SseError`, `EventTooLarge`); decoders write `Json` values (`fx.encoding.Json`, a recursive alias). Ports: `tests/oracle/test_stream_text.py` (the `encoding` section of `Stream.test.ts`; the property test checks 2000 seeded chunk lists, Schema arbitraries being M8), `test_ndjson.py`, `test_json_array.py`, `test_sse.py` (every title but the schema ones, which need `ChannelSchema`; `EncodingError.test.ts` belongs to the Base64 / Hex codecs, not ported yet). `tests/test_encoding.py` pins 37 more behaviours, each printed by Effect under Node first (error kinds, offsets and messages of the scanner, `unterminated`, the BOM rules, `max_line_bytes` across chunks, chunking of the output, `retry:` mid-stream, `encode` ending on `Retry`). `assert_type` pins cover every method and framer, that a framer's upstream error is the stream's, and that `duplex` checks its channel. Pythonic differences: JSON is `json.loads` / `json.dumps` with `JSON.parse` / `JSON.stringify` semantics (compact, non-ASCII kept), but `NaN` and infinities fail to parse and to write, where `JSON.stringify` writes `null` and `json.dumps` would write invalid `NaN`; `json_array` offsets and `max_element_size` count characters, not UTF-16 code units (an emoji is 1, not 2); option names in messages are the Python ones (`max_depth`); `Retry.duration` is a `Duration` (the parser gives seconds) and `write` prints fractional milliseconds (`retry: 1.5`); type guards (`isNdjsonError`, `isJsonArrayError`, `Retry.is`) are `isinstance`. `examples/stream_pipeline.py` (run and its output pinned by `tests/test_examples.py`) decodes NDJSON split across byte chunks, prices orders four at a time with retries and exponential backoff, bounds how far pricing runs ahead with `buffer`, batches with `grouped` and writes with `sink.for_each`. The argument guard (`tests/test_effect_arguments.py`) now walks the modules of public subpackages too (`pyfect.encoding.ndjson`), and skips a parameter whose annotation also takes a function (`otel.sdk.layer`'s `config`, which the wider walk reached).

**M6 status (2026-10-27): done.** Every item of the order above exists, is exported, has `assert_type` pins and is tested; `to_async_iterator` is `Stream.to_async_iterable`. A script check of the exit-criteria files finds every title ported (one pytest per title, or one parametrized test for a title Effect builds in a loop) or omitted with a one-line reason in the module docstring: `Pull.test.ts` (6 of 6), `Sink.test.ts` (29 of 32; the 3 `fromWritableStream` titles), `Channel.test.ts` (50 of 60; 4 Web Streams titles, 6 reason-error titles), `Stream.test.ts` (all but the Web Streams / DOM event titles, the `catchReason(s)` / `unwrapReason` titles, the commented-out ones and the few listed in `test_stream_combining.py` / `test_stream_zipping.py`). A second script compares every export of `Stream.ts`, `Channel.ts`, `Sink.ts`, `Pull.ts` and `Take.ts` with pyfect's names. The completeness pass closed what it found: the Stream context functions (curried `stream.provide`, `provide_service`, `provide_service_effect`, `provide_context`, `update_context`; methods `update_service`, `with_span`, in `pyfect/stream/_context.py` and `_service_methods.py`, `tests/test_stream_context.py`, each value printed by Effect under Node first), which replace the test-only `tests/oracle/_stream_ops.py`; Channel's `filter_map`, `filter_map_effect`, `filter_map_array`, `filter_map_array_effect`, `catch_filter(or_else=)` and `catch_cause_filter` (a filter returns `Ok` / `Err`, as in `Stream`; the four `Filter` titles and `catchFilter` in the mixed-cause recoveries are now ported); and the two "supports dropping parens in pipe" titles. Not ported, with the reason: `Do` / `bind` / `bindTo` / `bindEffect` (generators, decisions table); `catchReason(s)` / `unwrapReason` (no reason-error design yet, Effect core A); `catchTags` (`catch_tag` takes a tuple of classes); `Stream.withExecutionPlan` (the `ExecutionPlan` module is deferred, M3); `Channel.contextWith` (pyfect has no `Effect.contextWith`; write `channel.unwrap(fx.context(*tags).map(f))`); `fromEventListener`, `fromReadableStream`, `toReadableStream*`, `fromTransformStream`, `fromWritableStream`, `pullIntoWritableStream` (DOM events and Web Streams); `isStream` / `isChannel` / `isSink` (`isinstance`); the `*UpstreamFailure` helpers (internal in Effect; `pyfect/channel/_upstream.py`); the schema framers (M8). Known differences at the time, all from woken fibers resuming through the scheduler (Effect resumes them inline; since the QA pass resumes on the run's thread are synchronous too, see "Resolved after M0"): interleaving under backpressure in `merge_all` / `zip_latest` / live-clock `aggregate`, `from_pubsub` single takes racing a shutdown, and a few ports that yield once more (listed in their docstrings). Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, run alternately with the M5 commit): map chain 3.11–3.16M vs 3.08–3.13M steps/s, gen steps 2.21–2.25M vs 2.17–2.23M, fork+join 164–165k vs 163k/s, so no regression; `benchmarks/channel.py`: 1M items at 0.96M items/s one by one and 20.5M items/s chunked. Test count 4005 (3983 before the pass).

### M7 — Config and data (M; depends on M1 Option and M2 Layer)
- **Config:** ConfigProvider (nodes, `from_env`, `from_unknown`, `from_dot_env(_contents)`, `from_dir`, combinators, `constant_case`, layers); `ConfigError`; `Config[A]` with two-channel resolution (absent vs invalid) and primitives (`string`, `integer`, `port`, `boolean`, `duration`, `literal`, `url`, `log_level`, `byte_size`, `redacted`, `list_of` / `dict_of`), `with_default`, `option`, `or_else`, `all`, `config.gen`, `nested`.
- **Values:** Redacted, ByteSize, Brand (v4's `make` / `check` / `all`; `refined` is the v3 name).
- **Utilities:** case-conversion module, Filter (only if needed by then).
- **Exit criteria:** `Config.test.ts`, `ConfigProvider.test.ts`, `Redacted.test.ts`, `ByteSize.test.ts`, `Brand.test.ts`, `String.test.ts` (case functions only).

**ConfigProvider status (2026-10-27): done.** `pyfect.config_provider` (`fx.config_provider`; `fx.ConfigProvider` and `fx.CurrentConfigProvider` at the top level): nodes as frozen dataclasses (`Value(value)`, `Record(keys: frozenset, value=None)`, `Array(length, value=None)`, a negative length rejected), `Path` (`tuple[str | int, ...]`; `load` and `map_input` functions also take a list, never a bare string), `SourceError(message, cause)`; `ConfigProvider` with `load(path)` and the methods `map_input`, `or_else`, `nested(prefix | path)`, `constant_case()` (Effect's `String.configCase`, `pyfect.case.config_case`); `make(get)`, `from_env(env=None, preserve_empty_strings=)`, `from_env_record`, `from_unknown`, `from_dot_env_contents(contents, expand_variables=, preserve_empty_strings=)` (Effect's regex parser and `${NAME:-default}` expansion, ported line for line), `from_dot_env(path=".env", ...)`, `from_dir(root_path="/", ...)`, and the layers `layer(provider | effect)` and `layer_add(provider | effect, as_primary=)`. `CurrentConfigProvider` (a `Reference`, default `from_env()`) is what `Config` reads. v4 has no path-delimiter or case options on `fromEnv` and no `snakeCase` / `kebabCase` provider combinators, so none were added. `tests/oracle/test_config_provider.py` ports all 106 `ConfigProvider.test.ts` titles (the `fromDotEnv` / `fromDir` ones on real files under `tmp_path`); The review found three gaps, now fixed and pinned against Effect under Node: the `.env` parser used Python's `\s`, `.`, `^` and `$`, which differ from JavaScript's (a byte-order mark lost the first line; `\x1c`, `\x85` and U+2028 were read differently), so the regexes now spell JavaScript's classes from `pyfect/_js.py` (where `js_number` moved from `metric`); `from_unknown` wrote small and large floats as Python's `repr` (`1e-05`), now as `String(number)` (`0.00001`); and `from_env_record` now builds Effect's name trie once when made (values still read at each lookup) instead of scanning every name per lookup. `tests/test_config_provider.py` pins `os.environ` reads, the default reference, Python scalars, real file-system errors, and `configCase`, dotenv parsing and expansion cases printed by Effect under Node.

**Config status (2026-10-27): done but for the schema-based configs (M8).** `pyfect.config` (`fx.config`; `fx.Config` and `fx.ConfigError` at the top level): `Config[A]` is an `Effect[A, ConfigError]` (yieldable in `fx.gen`; running it reads the `CurrentConfigProvider`) with `parse(provider)`, and resolves in Effect's two channels (absent is an `Err` inside a successful evaluation, invalid data and source errors fail). Methods: `map`, `flat_map` (a function returning a `Config` gives a `Config` read under the same prefix; one returning a plain effect gives an `Effect`, as `Effect.flat_map`), `map_effect` (Effect's `mapEffect`, the v4 name of `mapOrFail`), `or_else` (only all-`Fail` causes reach the fallback), `with_default`, `nested`. Functions: `option` (a function, because `cfg.option()` is `Effect.option`, which turns every failure into `None`), `succeed`, `fail`, `all(*configs)` (tuple; Effect's tuple and iterable forms) and `all_dict(mapping)` (Effect's record form), `unwrap`, and `gen` (a generator building a `Config`: each `yield from cfg` reads under the result's prefix and the result is absent as soon as one read is; other effects may fail only with `ConfigError` and need no services; pyfect-only sugar for `flat_map` chains, as Python has no Do notation). Constructors: `string`, `non_empty_string`, `number` (Effect's `Number`, `"Infinity"` / `"-Infinity"` / `"NaN"` included), `finite`, `integer` (`Int`), `port`, `boolean`, `literal(*values, name=)`, `log_level`, `duration`, `byte_size`, `date_time` (`Date`), `url` (`URL`), `redacted`, `list_of(element, name, separator=)` (`Config.Array`) and `dict_of(value, name, separator=, key_value_separator=)` (`Config.Record`). Without Schema, `ConfigError.cause` is a `SourceError` or a `config.SchemaError` over a small issue tree in `pyfect/config/_issue.py` (`InvalidType`, `InvalidValue`, `Forbidden`, `Pointer`, `Composite`) printed with Effect's default formatter; the scalar decoders (`pyfect/config/_scalars.py`, a `Scalar(missing, decode)` each) give Effect's messages for its StringTree encodings. That is the seam for M8: `config.schema(codec, path)` will decode a cursor with a real Schema, `SchemaError` and the issue classes will move to the Schema modules (`config` re-exporting them), and `list_of` / `dict_of` will also take an element schema. `Redacted` and `ByteSize` were added for `Config` and completed in the values cluster (below). `tests/oracle/test_config.py` ports 99 `Config.test.ts` titles (five in part, their `Config.schema` halves waiting for M8) and lists the omitted schema titles; `Schema.String.check(filter)` cases use `map_effect`, and the `Schema.Struct` / `Schema.Record` cases that only exercise loading use `dict_of`. `tests/test_config.py` (30 tests, values printed by Effect under Node) pins number, integer, port, boolean, duration, byte-size, date, URL and literal parsing, missing-value messages, `list_of` / `dict_of` error composites and pair skipping, `ConfigError` messages, `gen` (absence, failure, prefixes, closing the generator), the default `os.environ` provider, `Effect.option` versus `config.option`, argument checks and `Redacted`. v3-only `Config.validate`, `withDescription`, `secret`, `chunk`, `hashSet` / `hashMap` were not added.

**Values and case conversion status (2026-10-27): done but for the Schema-based brand checks (M8).** `pyfect.redacted` (`fx.redacted`, `fx.Redacted`): `make(value, label=)`, `value` (a `ValueError` with Effect's message once wiped), `wipe_unsafe`, `Redacted.pipe`; `str` / `repr` / `format` give `<redacted[:label]>` (a format spec applies to the placeholder), equality and hash follow the value. Every logger format, the console loggers, the `logging` bridge and `Cause.pretty` print the placeholder, pinned in `tests/test_redacted.py`. `isRedacted` is `isinstance` and `makeEquivalence` is `==` on `value` (Pythonic decisions: Equivalence). `pyfect.byte_size` (`fx.byte_size`): `ByteSize` is a `NewType` over `int` (Effect's branded `bigint`), so comparisons, `hash`, `min` / `max` and sorting are native; `zero`, `bytes(int)`, the eighteen unit constructors (`kilobytes` ... `quettabytes`, `kibibytes` ... `yobibytes`; an `int` quantity of any size, a `float` that must give a safe-integer byte count, as Effect's `number`), `from_string(_unsafe)`, `from_input(_unsafe)` (`int | float | str`), `is_byte_size`, `is_zero`, `to_unit`, `sum`, `subtract(_unsafe)`, `times`, `divide` (`None` out of range) and `format(precision=, trailing_zeros=, system=, unit=)` (overloads reject a binary unit with `system="decimal"` and the reverse). `toBigInt` / `toNumber(Unsafe)` are not ported (the value is an `int`), nor `Order`, `Equivalence`, `between` / `clamp` / the comparisons, `ReducerSum` and the combiners (operators, builtins and `functools.reduce`). Errors are `ValueError`s with Effect's messages (`Invalid ByteSize: ...`). `pyfect.brand` (`fx.brand`): `Brand[A, B]` (Effect's `Constructor`) with the class methods `nominal()`, `make(predicate)`, `check(*filters)` and `all(*brands)`, called on the specialised class (`Brand[float, Int].make(...)`); calling raises `BrandError` (a `TaggedError` and `ValueError` with `message`), and `result` / `option` (`Some` or `None`, PARITY §5.1) / `is_` (a `TypeGuard`) report instead. Until Schema lands, a check is a `brand.Filter(predicate, expected=, message=)` with `.abort()`; a predicate gives `True` / `None`, `False` or a message, and the failure text follows Effect's default formatter (`message`, then the predicate's string, then `Expected <expected or "<filter>">`), messages of all failing filters joined by newlines unless an aborted one stops them (printed by Effect under Node, pinned in `tests/test_brand.py`). M8 will let `check` take Schema checks (`isInt`, `isGreaterThan`, ...) and give `BrandError` an `issue`. `pyfect.case` (`fx.case`): `no_case(split=, strip=, delimiter=, transform=)`, `camel_case`, `pascal_case`, `snake_case`, `kebab_case`, `constant_case`, `config_case` (now also behind `ConfigProvider.constant_case`), and `snake_to_camel` / `snake_to_pascal` / `snake_to_kebab` / `camel_to_snake` / `pascal_to_snake` / `kebab_to_snake`; word characters are ASCII letters and digits, as in Effect's regexes, and a pattern always replaces every match (Python has no `g` flag). `tests/oracle/test_redacted.py` (9), `test_byte_size.py` (12), `test_brand.py` (7) and `test_string.py` (the 33 case-function titles) port every title of their files; a differential run against Effect under Node over 3,000 random `format` / `to_unit` calls, the parse and constructor error messages and 27 case inputs found no difference, and `tests/test_case.py` keeps the edge inputs.

**M7 status (2026-10-27): done but for the Schema-based parts (M8).** A script check of the exit-criteria files finds every title ported, one pytest per title with the title in its docstring (prefixed by its `describe` block where Effect nests): `ConfigProvider.test.ts` 106 of 106, `Redacted.test.ts` 9 of 9, `ByteSize.test.ts` 12 of 12, `Brand.test.ts` 7 of 7, the case functions of `String.test.ts` 33 of 33, and `Config.test.ts` 99 of 175. The 76 `Config.test.ts` titles left all run `Config.schema` over Schema codecs ("materialization", "fromEnv provider", "fromUnknown provider" and the schema container titles), listed in the oracle file's docstring; they wait for M8. Filter (the utility item) is a function returning `Ok` / `Err`, with no module of its own. The completeness pass added the Filter-based Effect combinators that M1 had deferred for it (`catch_filter`, `catch_cause_filter`, `tap_cause_filter`, `filter_map`, `filter_map_effect`; see the M1 deferral list). It also rewired `otel.resource.layer_from_env` to read `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME` / `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES` through `Config`, as Effect's `layerFromEnv` does: the `Resource.test.ts` port now provides a `ConfigProvider` as Effect's test does, and `tests/test_otel.py` pins that an empty service name is absent (Effect under Node). Deferred to M8: `Config.schema`, Brand checks built from Schema checks, `BrandError.issue`. The suite has 4,544 tests (4,522 before the pass, 4,126 after the first M7 cluster). Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, run alternately with the M6 commit on one machine, which ran slower than when M6 was recorded): map chain 2.89–2.93M vs 2.87–2.95M steps/s, gen steps 2.08–2.14M vs 2.08–2.16M, fork+join 150k vs 137–154k/s, so no regression; `benchmarks/channel.py`: 0.91M items/s one by one, 18.1M items/s chunked.

### M8 — Schema (XL+; depends on M1, M6 for ChannelSchema, M7)
- **Order:** AST + Issue tree + SchemaError + Parser (`decode_unknown*` / `encode*`, ParseOptions) → primitives, Literal/Enum, checks, brands → Struct (TypedDict) and `S.Class` / `S.TaggedClass` / `S.TaggedError` (`dataclass_transform`, integrated with `errors.TaggedError`) → Union / Tuple / List / Dict / suspend / Json → Getter / Transformation / `decode_to` / `flip` → built-in codecs → JSON codec → JSON Schema → representation, formatter, equivalence → Hypothesis strategies → Optic → Effect data-type schemas → `config.schema` and ChannelSchema → pydantic and msgspec adapters.
- **Exit criteria:** `Schema.test.ts` and the `schema/` test directory (decode, encode, issues, JSON Schema snapshots), `SchemaAST.test.ts`, `JsonSchema.test.ts`, `JsonPatch.test.ts` / `JsonPointer.test.ts`.

**Typing design and parser core status (2026-10-27): done.** The typing was settled by experiments first (`research/typing/schema_1_codec.py`, `schema_2_struct.py`, `schema_3_protocol.py`, run with `uv run pyright -p research/typing <file>`) and is written up in `docs/SCHEMA_DESIGN.md`, with its pins in the `Schema` section of `tests/type_checks.py` as the contract for the later clusters. A schema is `S.Bottom[T, E = T, RD = Never, RE = Never]` (covariant), and every function taking one takes the `S.Codec` protocol (one member, `__codec__`), which schema classes will match through a classmethod; `S.Schema[T]`, `S.Decoder`, `S.Encoder` and `S.Top` are aliases. Structs and classes are declared with plain annotations (the Type, schema derived on first use so self and forward references resolve) plus `S.field(schema)` for other schemas, checked against the annotation; their Encoded is `Mapping[str, object]` (no spelling lets pyright compute it), and Effect's `Struct({...})` becomes `S.Struct(TypedDict)`. `pyfect.schema` (`fx.schema`, `from pyfect import schema as S`): the AST (`S.ast`: `Never`, `Any`, `Unknown`, `Null`, `String`, `Number` (Python `int` / `float`, not `bool`), `Boolean`, `BigInt` (Python `int`); keyword-only snake_case `annotations`; `parse(input, options) -> Exit[object, Issue]`, `flip`, `to_type`, `expected`), the issue tree (`S.issue`: `InvalidType`, `InvalidValue`, `MissingKey`, `UnexpectedKey`, `Forbidden`, `Pointer`, `Composite`, `Encoding`, `is_issue`, `format_issue` (Effect's default formatter), `input` kept as `Some(value)` only under `report_input`), `SchemaError` (a `TaggedError` holding the issue; `str` is the message), `ParseOptions` (a TypedDict given as keyword arguments when an adapter is made and when it is called: `errors`, `on_excess_property`, `disable_checks`, `concurrency`, `report_input`; v3's `exact` is not a v4 option), the adapters `decode_unknown_*` / `decode_*` / `encode_unknown_*` / `encode_*` in the `_effect`, `_exit`, `_option`, `_result` and `_sync` styles, `is_`, `asserts`, and `Bottom.annotate`, `pipe`, `make`, `make_option`, `make_effect` (failing with the bare issue). A sync adapter that meets a defect raises `RuntimeError` with Effect's adapter message (`"Sync adapter can only throw schema errors"`), chained from the defect; `make` raises `SchemaError` (Effect throws an `Error` whose cause is the issue). `testing.SchemaAsserts` is Effect's `TestSchema.Asserts` (`make()` / `decoding()` / `encoding()` with `succeed` / `fail`). The M7 seam is gone: `pyfect/config/_issue.py` was removed, and `Config` builds its issues from the schema classes (a node with no schema yet, such as `"array"`, is an `Unknown` node named by its `identifier`), `config` re-exporting them. Not ported: `Undefined`, `Void`, `Symbol`, `UniqueSymbol`, `ObjectKeyword`, `StringForLiteralAutocomplete` (no Python counterpart) and, until parsers can be effectful, the Promise adapters. Ported: the primitive, `SchemaError`, `isSchema` and `toString` titles of `Schema.test.ts` (`tests/oracle/test_schema.py`, 12), the `SchemaIssue.test.ts` titles but the union one (`test_schema_issue.py`, 4), and the primitive-only `SchemaParser.test.ts` titles (`test_schema_parser.py`, 4); each docstring lists what waits for later clusters. `tests/test_schema_parser.py` (53 cases) pins `report_input` messages, annotation messages, the formatter's leaves and paths, option merging, Python values (`bool` is not a number, `NaN` is), `make`, the typed adapters and the non-schema failure paths, with expected text printed by Effect under Node. The suite has 4,617 tests.

**Primitives, literals, checks and brands status (2026-10-27): done but for the titles needing later schema kinds.** `pyfect/schema/ast/` is now a package: `_base.py` (`AST` with `checks`; `parse` runs the node's `_parse` then its checks, a check that raises being a defect; `annotate` on a checked node annotates its last check; `issue_of`), `_check.py` (`Filter[T]` and `FilterGroup[T]`, contravariant, with `annotate`, `abort` and `and_`; `make_filter`, `make_filter_by_guard`, `collect_issues` with Effect's `errors` / abort semantics), `_keywords.py`, `_literal.py` (`Literal`, `Enum`) and `_union.py` (`Union` with `any_of` / `one_of` and Effect's candidate filtering, a Python `int` being a number and a bigint). `issue.py` gains `Filter`, `AnyOf` and `OneOf`, and the formatter follows Effect's `defaultLeafHook` / `defaultCheckHook`.
