Not ported, with the reason: `PgTypes`, `PgProtocol`, `PgAuth` and the in-process wire tests (pyfect does not implement the wire protocol; psycopg and libpq do), multiplexing / pipelining, `maxMessageSize`, `stream` factories and the `db.connect` span, `PgConnection.integration`'s stale-plan-in-transaction cases (psycopg drops its prepared statements after `ALTER`, `DROP` or `DEALLOCATE ALL`, so the errors never happen), the `SqlModel` repository case, and the KeyValueStore / Persistence / EventJournal / EventLog suites (those modules are not ported). `tests/test_sql_pg_error.py` and `test_sql_pg_config.py` pin authentication failures at connect (libpq reports them as text, without a SQLSTATE) and the config checks; `tests/type_checks.py::sql_pg_driver` pins the driver's types. Porting the tests found and fixed: a terminated listener lost PostgreSQL's `57P01` (libpq hands a FATAL that arrives between statements to the notice processor; the session now keeps it and reports it for the lost connection); `process_id` raised once a session closed (it read libpq live; it is fixed at connect); a statement queued on a pin when the pin was released closed the session for the next owner (statements and streams on a pin now take the session's wire permit, and only one holding it counts as in flight); closing a connection left its listeners' takers waiting (the session shuts their queues); `UNLISTEN`'s five-second timeout counted the time spent queued behind a busy statement and then cancelled gracefully into a server that had stopped answering (it now starts once `UNLISTEN` is sent, and on expiry closes the session at once); a session that finished connecting just as the connect timeout or an interrupt stopped it was left open (`_loop.call` now hands a value it can no longer return to a `release`, which closes the session, ends the stream or drops the listener's mailbox); and releasing a pin with work outstanding now reports v4's "Pinned connection released with work outstanding". The full suite has 7,843 tests (one skipped).

**M10 wave 5, cli (2026-10-28): done.** `pyfect.cli` (`fx.cli`), Effect's `effect/cli`: a command's params are a `Config` class of `Flag` and `Argument` attributes (`nested(Other)` for a nested group), so the handler's config is typed from the class (`tests/type_checks.py` pins it). `Flag` and `Argument` (`string`, `boolean`, `integer`, `finite`, `date`, `literals`, `choice_with_value`, `path`, `file`, `directory`, `file_text`, `file_parse` (ini, json, toml, yaml), `file_schema`, `redacted`, `key_value_pair`; `optional`, `with_default` (a value or an effect), `with_alias`, `with_description`, `with_metavar`, `variadic(min=, max=)`, `with_optional_value`, `with_negation` / `without_negation`, `map`, `map_effect`, `filter_map`, `with_schema`, `with_fallback_config`, `with_fallback_prompt`), on `param` and `primitive`; `command` (`make`, `with_handler`, `validate`, `with_subcommands` with `group`, `with_shared_flags` (the parent's flags read in a subcommand with `yield from parent`), `with_description`, `with_short_description`, `with_alias`, `with_unlisted`, `with_examples`, `annotate`, `provide` / `provide_sync` / `provide_effect` / `provide_effect_discard`, `with_global_flags`, `run`, `run_with`, `wizard`, `show_help`); `cli_error` (Effect's errors with its messages and suggestions, `ShowHelp`, `UserError` and `__cli_user_message__`), `help_doc`, `cli_output` (`Formatter`, `CurrentFormatter`, `default_formatter`, presenters and their events), `global_flag` (`Setting`, `Action`, the built-in `--help`, `--version`, `--wizard`, `--completions`, `--log-level`), `cli_config`, `completions` (bash, zsh, fish and PowerShell scripts from one descriptor), `prompt` (string, password, hidden, integer, number, date, confirm, toggle, select, multi-select, auto-complete, file, custom, run on `Terminal`), `terminal_capabilities`, `encoding.ini` and `encoding.yaml`. Every title of `test/cli` (Ansi, Arguments, Command, Errors, Help, Lexer, LogLevel, Param, Primitive, Prompt, TerminalCapabilities, `completions/descriptor` and `completions/completions`) and `encoding/Ini.test.ts` and `encoding/Yaml.test.ts` are in `tests/oracle/test_cli_*.py`, `test_command.py`, `test_param.py`, `test_primitive.py`, `test_prompt.py`, `test_terminal_capabilities.py`, `test_completions.py`, `test_completions_descriptor.py`, `test_ini.py` and `test_yaml.py` (621 tests) but one omitted with its reason (`__proto__`); the `ComprehensiveCli` fixture and the `MockTerminal` / `TestActions` services are `tests/oracle/_comprehensive_cli.py`, `_mock_terminal.py` and `_cli_env.py`. The PowerShell replay test runs only where `pwsh` is installed, as in Effect. `examples/cli.py` is a small subcommand app run with `run_main`, checked by `tests/test_examples.py`. Help, error and completion text match Effect's byte for byte (the inline snapshots are compared as strings). The public-API guard in `tests/test_effect_arguments.py` now knows a value-or-effect parameter (`with_default`, `prompt.custom`'s initial state) takes any value, and found `with_fallback_prompt` unguarded: it now rejects anything but a `Prompt` or an effect when called (`param.require_prompt`). `provide` / `provide_sync` / `provide_effect` given a value rather than a function of the config fit a command of any config instead of widening its config to `Any`. Review (differential runs against Effect under Node: 1,400 generated command lines over the `ComprehensiveCli` fixture, every prompt kind with scripted keys in color and plain, the four completion scripts, the lexer, the primitives, 1,500 generated YAML documents; all byte-identical but the date cases below) found and fixed: `Date` values read with `datetime.fromisoformat` took text JavaScript rejects (`20240115`, `2024-W03-1`, `T10Z`, `+02`) and rejected text it reads (`+002024-01-15`, a lowercase `z`), now `cli/_date.py` reads the ECMAScript format; `file_parse` lacked YAML, now `encoding.yaml`; a hand-built `Config` took any keyword; `Flag.literals` / `Argument.literals` keep literal types for a tuple of choices (pinned, and used in `examples/cli.py`). `tests/oracle/test_cli_edges.py` pins what Effect's suite leaves open, each value printed by Effect: help lists a command's own flags before its shared ones, global-flag and parse errors show together, a prompt's quit interrupts the run, stacked `with_global_flags` keep both, number prompts round half up, Tab restores a cleared text prompt's default, auto-complete keeps the highlighted match. The full suite has 8,519 tests (two skipped).

**M10b completeness pass (2026-10-28): done; M10b (waves 4 and 5) is complete.** A title-by-title script check of every Effect test file for the M10b modules (`effect/test/sql`, `effect/test/Migrator.test.ts`, `effect/test/cli` with `completions/`, `encoding/Ini.test.ts` and `Yaml.test.ts`, `sql-sqlite-node/test` and `sql-pg/test`: 959 titles) finds every title ported or omitted with a reason, but one file: `effect/test/Migrator.test.ts`, whose loader tests nobody had ported, and `from_file_system` had no test at all. Its three `fromFileSystem` titles are now in `tests/oracle/test_migrator.py` (`.py` modules where Effect takes `.js` / `.ts` / `.mjs` / `.mts`; Python imports from a path, so the two file URL titles check that the module is imported from the path the platform's `Path` joins and that a module that will not import is a defect of the load, never a typed failure); `fromGlob` / `fromBabelGlob` are omitted (bundler import maps). `tests/test_sql_migrator_files.py` runs a real directory of migration modules through the SQLite migrator, in id order and once, and checks a module that does not import fails the run with an `ImportError` `MigrationError`. `SqliteClientService.update_values`, which Effect types as `never`, is now `@deprecated`, so pyright strict rejects a call (pinned in `tests/type_checks.py::sql_statements`); it still raises `TypeError` if called anyway. The `pg` extra now needs psycopg 3.3 or later (it allowed 3.2, which was never tested and may not drop prepared statements after `ALTER` or `DEALLOCATE ALL`, the premise of the stale-plan omission). Whole files not ported, with the reason: `PgProtocol`, `PgTypes`, `PgAuth` and the wire-level `PgConnection.in-process` titles (libpq and psycopg implement the protocol, codecs and SCRAM), `ContainerTeardown` (Effect's Docker test harness; pyfect's starts a throwaway server with `initdb`), `SqlModelServices` (needs `SqlModel`, deferred until Schema has `Model` / `VariantSchema`), and the `KeyValueStore`, `Persistence`, `SqlEventJournal` and `SqlEventLogServer*` suites of both drivers (persistence, wave 7; eventlog, wave 9). Other drivers (`mysql2`, `mssql`, `clickhouse`, `libsql`, `d1`, `pglite`, the browser and Bun SQLite ones) are not planned. Known differences left as they are: interrupting a statement running on the SQLite worker waits for it to finish (Effect's `node:sqlite` cannot interrupt one at all); `Queue`'s `WaitList` still wakes every ready waiter (as Effect's `Queue` does); a curried `command.provide` given a lambda cannot infer the config (pass a `def`). Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, run interleaved with the M10a commit because the machine was loaded, load average about 10): map chain 2.72–3.15M steps/s against 2.75–3.01M for M10a, generator steps 2.12–2.23M against 2.03–2.08M, fork + join 150–158k/s against 146–159k/s, so no regression (the absolute numbers are below the 3.3M / 2.36M / 175k recorded at M10a only because of the load). The full suite has 8,550 tests (two skipped: PowerShell without `pwsh`, and Effect's own skipped vitest case).

**M10 wave 6, HTTP server (2026-10-28): done.** The server half of `effect/http` under `pyfect.http`: `server` (`HttpServer`, `serve`, `serve_effect`), `server_request`, `server_response` (now with `file` / `file_web` through `HttpPlatform`), `server_respondable`, `server_error`, `http_effect` (`to_handled` uninterruptible, as Effect's `fiberEnterUninterruptibleUnsafe`), `router` on `find_my_way` (routes interruptible unless `uninterruptible=True`; `schema_json` and `schema_body_form_json` take a `reviver`), `middleware` (logger, tracer, cors, compression, decompression, search params), `platform` (`layer_live` compresses one-shot bodies whole with an exact content-length and streams through sync-flush transforms; web file bodies open lazily, so a HEAD never acquires them), `static_server`, `multipart` (`FilesSchema`, `SingleFileSchema`) and its parser, `mime`, `template`, `status` and `etag`. `pyfect.http.live_server` is the standard-library HTTP/1.1 server (Effect's `NodeHttpServer`; no third-party dependency): `make` / `make_unix` / `make_tls`, `layer`, `layer_server`, `layer_services`, `layer_test`, `Options`, `TlsOptions`; head parsing with 400 / 417 / 431 / 501 / 505, keep-alive, chunked and length bodies, `100-continue`, Node's `keepAliveTimeout` (`keep_alive_timeout`, 5s, advertised in `keep-alive: timeout=`) and `headersTimeout` (`headers_timeout`, 60s; 408 for a started head), `HTTP/1.1` status lines for every request version, graceful shutdown bounded by `graceful_shutdown_timeout` (as Node's, a connection busy when it starts goes on answering keep-alive requests until it is idle), and Node's abort semantics (a client EOF interrupts an in-flight request; an upgrade aborts only on transport loss, drains its body up to `MaxBodySize` and validates its headers). `Signal.wait_until` waits through spurious wakes (`Signal.wait` returns at the first). Ported: every server-side title of `effect/test/http` and of platform-node's `NodeHttpServer`, `NodeHttpPlatform`, `NodeHttpCompression`, `NodeMultipart`, `MultipartParser` and `HttpStaticServer(Conditional)` tests, omissions with reasons in each oracle module's docstring. `to_web_handler` / `to_web_handler_layer(_with)` take `memo_map=`. Configured middleware (`cors`, `compression`, `decompression`, `x_forwarded_headers`) is an `AppMiddleware[RM]`, generic in the app it wraps, so `server.serve(app, middleware)`, `serve_effect` and `router.serve(middleware=)` keep the app's requirements (they used to widen to `Any`). Not ported: HTTP/2, brotli (no standard-library codec; `disablePreemptiveShutdown` followed in M14 loose-ends), and NodeHttpServer's IPv4-mapped-peer `xForwardedHeaders` case (the middleware's own cases are ported). Known typing limits: middleware `provides` (`router.provide_request`) cannot remove types from the app, and `layer.merge` of layers with no output infers Unknown. The review pass fixed: graceful shutdown closed busy keep-alive connections (Effect's test expects the next request answered; the port had inverted it), idle keep-alive connections were never closed although `timeout=5` was advertised, a `live_server` (and `socket.server`) layer or `make` effect built twice shared one listener so the second build's connections were reset, HTTP/1.0 requests got `HTTP/1.0` status lines, and serving with middleware erased the app's requirements. The route-handler error and requirement types flow into the app, pinned in `tests/type_checks.py`.

**M10c ASGI serving (2026-10-28): done.** `pyfect.http.asgi`: `to_asgi(app, layer, middleware=, memo_map=)` is an ASGI 3 application (`AsgiApp`) for uvicorn, hypercorn, granian or a host framework; its `lifespan` scope builds the layer in a `ManagedRuntime` (`AsgiApp.runtime`) and disposes it at shutdown, the first request builds it when the server sends no lifespan, and `AsgiApp.lifespan()` is the async context manager a host framework's own lifespan enters (Starlette sends none to mounted apps). `to_asgi_with(layer, to_app)` and `router.to_asgi(routes, ...)` serve an app made of the layer's services (Effect's `HttpRouter.toWebHandler` for ASGI). Requests run on their own fibers on the server's loop (`web_handler.to_web_handler_with` gained `fork=`, the hook that starts each request's fiber); request bodies are streams read from `receive` by one task that buffers up to `HIGH_WATER_MARK` (64 KiB) unread bytes; streamed responses are sent chunk by chunk; an `http.disconnect` before the response ends fires the request's signal (a client abort, 499) and stops the body; invalid response headers get a 500 and are raised to the server, as Node's `writeHead` throws; `root_path` is removed from the request URL. A `websocket` scope is a `GET` request whose `upgrade` is a `from_web_socket` socket over the connection (`AsgiWebSocket`, pausable): acquiring its reader sends `websocket.accept` with the client's first subprotocol (as Node's `ws`), the reader's scope closes it with 1000 / 1001 / 1011 by the handler's exit unless the handler closed it first, custom upgrade headers fail as Effect's, a connection lost without a closing handshake (1005 / 1006, or any disconnect before the accept) interrupts the app, and an app that does not upgrade answers through the `websocket.http.response` extension (403 from servers without it); middleware sees the `101`. `from_asgi(asgi_app, state=)` is an app answering with another framework's ASGI application (FastAPI, Starlette), mounted under a router prefix with the prefix as `root_path`, and `lifespan(asgi_app)` runs its startup and shutdown in a scope and gives its `state` (both need an asyncio run, as `from_web_handler`). `pyfect.http.uvicorn_server` (extra `pyfect[server]`, uvicorn): `make`, `layer_server`, `layer` (with `live_server.layer_services`), `layer_test` (a loopback server and an httpx client pointed at it, Effect's `layerTest`) and `Options` (`port`, `host`, `graceful_shutdown_timeout`, `config` for more `uvicorn.Config` arguments); the socket is bound when the server is made, uvicorn runs on the building fiber's loop under `run_main` / `run_async` and on a loop thread of its own otherwise, closing the serving scope stops uvicorn (requests in flight get `graceful_shutdown_timeout`, then are cancelled and interrupted), and requests with no app served get a 503. Ported: `tests/oracle/test_uvicorn_http_server.py` reruns the server-independent `NodeHttpServer.test.ts` titles already ported for the live server through uvicorn and ports the WebSocket titles the live server omits (status 101 logged and traced, the fallback status of a failed upgrade, uvicorn options forwarded, the handler's close codes, a reset upgrade connection, no HTTP response on an upgraded connection, the HTTP response of an unconsumed upgrade); omissions with reasons in its docstring. `tests/test_asgi.py` drives the adapter in memory (lifespan, streaming both ways, HEAD, disconnects, root paths, WebSockets, mounted ASGI applications and their lifespans) and `tests/test_uvicorn_server.py` the server (503, shutdown, the program's loop under `run_async`, graceful-shutdown cancellation, a `to_asgi` app under uvicorn's own lifespan).

**M10c http-api (2026-10-28): done.** `pyfect.http_api`: `HttpApi`, `HttpApiGroup` (a class of endpoints, or `HttpApiGroup.make(...).add(...)`), `endpoint` (`get`/`post`/...), `schema`, `error`, `middleware`, `security`, `builder` (`group(api, Group, lambda h: h.handle(endpoint)(fn))`, `layer(api, openapi_path=)`), `client` (`make`, `make_with`, `group`, `endpoint`, `url_builder`; `client[Group].attr(...)` for declared attributes, `client[group].method("id")(...)` for added endpoints), `test`, `openapi`, `swagger` and `scalar` (Swagger UI and Scalar bundled under `http_api/_assets`; `scalar.layer_cdn` loads Scalar from jsDelivr). Divergences: an `int` has no safe-integer bounds in JSON Schema and an optional key is the bare schema (no `undefined`), `handleAll` is not ported (it cannot be typed per endpoint), and `as_multipart_stream(schema)` takes its documenting schema positionally. Ported: `HttpApi`, `HttpApiEndpoint`, `HttpApiSchema`, `HttpApiError`, `HttpApiSecurity`, `HttpApiTest`, `HttpApiDocumentation`, `OpenApi` and `OpenApiRepresentation` tests, the platform-node `HttpApi.test.ts` (its OpenAPI snapshot is `tests/oracle/fixtures/http_api_openapi_fixture.json`) and `OpenApi.test.ts`; `HttpApiBuilder.test.ts` (`tests/oracle/test_http_api_builder.py`, `test_http_api_builder_with_headers.py`; omitted with reasons: the `handleAll` titles and the `Schema.decodeTo` spy) and every `HttpApiClient.test.ts` title (`test_http_api_client.py`). `builder.handler(endpoint, f)` is Effect's `HttpApiBuilder.handler` (returns `f`, typed by the endpoint); `client.endpoint` takes `transform_client`. The skeptical review found and fixed: a reused `client.endpoint` effect handed out the client its first run built (a list outside the per-run build; `_reflect_calls` now returns the calls), and the live server timed its keep-alive, headers, close-grace and shutdown waits with the effect `Clock`, so under a `TestClock` a response that closes the connection hung (they run on the system clock now, as Node's socket timers, `live_server/_timers.py`; the original `BadRequest` test runs under `TestClock` again, as Effect's).

**M10c rpc (2026-10-28): done.** `pyfect.rpc`: `make(tag, payload=, success=, error=, defect=, stream=)` gives an `Rpc` (or a `StreamRpc`), `.middleware(M)` adds an `RpcMiddleware` per RPC, and `RpcGroup` is a class of RPCs whose `handlers().handle(rpc)(fn)` chain becomes a layer (`to_layer`, `to_layer_effect`, `to_layer_handler`; a middleware's provided services are taken out of the handler's requirements and the middleware is required instead). The client is the group instance: `client.get_user({"id": "1"})` is an `Effect[S, E | ME | RpcClientError]`, a stream RPC a `Stream` (or a scoped `Dequeue` with `as_queue=True`), and `with_headers` sets `CurrentHeaders`. `rpc_schema` (`Stream`, `get_stream_schemas`, `ClientAbort`), `message`, `serialization` (`json`, `ndjson` / `make_ndjson(max_buffer_size=)`, `json_rpc`, `nd_json_rpc`, `with_codec_for`), `middleware` (server and client sides, `layer_client`), `server` (`layer`, `make`, `layer_http(group, path=, protocol=)`, the HTTP, WebSocket and socket-server protocols, `to_http_effect`), `client` (`make`, `layer_protocol_http(url, transform_client=, batch=Batch(window, max_size))`, `layer_protocol_socket` with pings, transient-error retries and `ConnectionHooks`), `client_error` and `test.make_client` (Effect's `RpcTest`). Spans are named after the tag unless `span_prefix` is given (v4; v3 prefixed `RpcServer.` / `RpcClient.`). Not ported: `RpcSerialization.layerSchemaBinary` (binary schemas), the worker and stdio protocols and `Transferable`, group-wide `RpcGroup.middleware` (add it per RPC), `RpcGroup.prefix`/`merge` flattening and the typing of client-middleware errors. Ported: `Rpc.test.ts` (`tests/oracle/test_rpc.py`), the text serializations of `RpcSerialization.test.ts` (`test_rpc_serialization.py`; the prototype-pollution and SchemaBinary cases omitted with reasons), the non-stdio, non-worker titles of `RpcClient.test.ts` (`test_rpc_client.py`) and `RpcServer.test.ts` (`test_rpc_server.py`, with platform-node's request defect isolation cases), and the platform-node `RpcServer.test.ts` e2e suite over HTTP ndjson / JSON-RPC, WebSocket ndjson / JSON / JSON-RPC and TCP ndjson / JSON-RPC, `RpcTest` and the custom defect schema (`test_rpc_server_node.py`, fixtures in `tests/oracle/_rpc.py`); `tests/test_rpc.py` pins behaviours read from Effect's source. `Queue.is_done_unsafe()` is new (the HTTP protocol asks whether a response queue has ended). The skeptical review found and fixed: v3 span names (`RpcServer.<tag>`), the missing server span link to the writing fiber's span, HTTP batching (now `Batch`), a malformed chunk's cleanup turning a caller's interrupt into a defect (now under `uninterruptible_mask`, as Effect), `ConnectionHooks.on_disconnect` skipped when the socket never opened, a connection defect leaving calls waiting (every cause is now told to the clients, as Effect's `tapCause`), a middleware's value ending a stream RPC failing to encode (a stream ends with no value, Effect's `Schema.Void`), and the missing `stream(as_queue=True)` / `discard()` overloads for RPCs without a payload. Under a `TestClock`, a socket reconnect takes more scheduler turns than the one `adjust` gives before it moves time (pyfect resumes callbacks through the scheduler; Effect at once), so two retry tests settle with `yield_now`s first.

**M10c completeness pass (2026-10-28): done; M10c (wave 6) is complete.** A title-by-title script check of every Effect test file for the M10c modules (`effect/test/http` server files with `FindMyWay/`, `effect/test/httpapi`, `effect/test/rpc`, and platform-node's `HttpApi`, `HttpStaticServer(Conditional)`, `MultipartParser`, `NodeHttpCompression`, `NodeHttpPlatform`, `NodeHttpServer`, `NodeMultipart`, `OpenApi` and `RpcServer` tests: 834 titles) finds every title ported or omitted with a reason in its oracle module's docstring, but four. Three `NodeHttpServer.test.ts` tables nobody had ported: "sends no body for $method with status $status" (passed as it was), "aborts the connection when a $body body doesn't match content-length" and "answers a $method response with status $status with a 500 and destroys the raw body". The last two failed and are fixed in `http.live_server`: a body longer or shorter than its declared `content-length` now aborts the connection (Node's `strictContentLength`, which Effect turns on; the server used to replace a `bytes` body's declared length with the real one and to keep the connection after a short stream, so the next response read as body), and a response whose head cannot be written closes its raw async-iterable body (Effect's `cancelResponseBody`; `wire.cancel_body`). The fourth, `RpcServer.test.ts`'s "isolates synchronous handler throws when fatal defects are disabled", passed as it was and is now in `tests/oracle/test_rpc_server.py`. Deferred, each with its reason in the cluster paragraphs above: HTTP/2 and brotli (not in the standard library), raw upgrades over ASGI (ASGI has none), `handleAll` (cannot be typed per endpoint), `RpcSerialization.layerSchemaBinary`, the rpc worker and stdio protocols, group-wide `RpcGroup.middleware` and `prefix` / `merge`, the typing of client-middleware errors, and middleware `provides` (typed `Any`: pyright cannot subtract a requirement from a union reliably). Known typing limit: `layer.merge` of layers that all output `Never` (several `router.add` layers) infers `Layer[Unknown, ...]`; `router.add_all` and `router.use` infer correctly. Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`): map chain 3.23M steps/s, gen 2.35M, fork+join 173k/s, against 3.16M / 2.22M / 162k recorded before, so no regression. Full check green with 9704 tests.

**M10 wave 7, KeyValueStore / Persistence / PersistedCache (2026-10-28): done.** `pyfect.primary_key` (`PrimaryKey`, a `primary_key()` method) and `pyfect.persistence` (`fx.persistence`): `key_value_store` (`KeyValueStore`, `KeyValueStoreError`, `make` / `make_string_only`, `store.prefix`, `store.to_schema_store(schema)` giving a `SchemaStore` of JSON text by the schema's JSON codec, `layer_memory`, `layer_file_system(directory)` on M10a `FileSystem` / `Path` with keys as `encodeURIComponent` file names and `""` / `"."` / `".."` (and lone surrogates) rejected, `layer_sql(table=)` on M10b sql with SQLite / PostgreSQL / MySQL / MSSQL DDL and upserts, `layer_storage(evaluate)` over any `MutableMapping[str, str]`); `persistable` (`Class(success=, error=)` makes the base class of a frozen-dataclass request with `primary_key()`, so its success and error types come from the schemas: a `Request[A, E | PersistenceError | SchemaError, R]`; `exit_schema`, `serialize_exit`, `deserialize_exit`); `persistence` (`Persistence` / `PersistenceStore` with `get` / `get_many` / `set` / `set_many` / `remove` / `clear` and a time to live per outcome, `BackingPersistence` with `layer_backing_memory`, `layer_backing_kvs`, `layer_backing_sql` (one table, an `expires` index and batched cleanup every five minutes) and `layer_backing_sql_multi_table`, and `layer_memory` / `layer_kvs` / `layer_sql` / `layer_sql_multi_table`; `PersistenceError` is a schema `TaggedError`, so a request may fail with it and have the failure stored); `persisted_cache` (`make(lookup, store_id=, time_to_live=, in_memory_capacity=, in_memory_ttl=, require_services_at=)`, `get` typed from the request's schemas, `invalidate`); `user_directories` (`UserDirectories`, `make(platform=)`, `layer`, `layer_platform`). `RequestResolver.persisted(store_id=, time_to_live=, stale_while_revalidate=)` is a method returning the effect that builds the resolver. Every title of `persistence/KeyValueStore.test.ts`, `KeyValueStoreTest.ts` (on `layer_sql` for SQLite and PostgreSQL, with and without a custom table), `PersistedCache.test.ts` with `PersistedCacheTest.ts` (memory, and PostgreSQL multi- and single-table), `UserDirectories.test.ts`, platform-node's `KeyValueStore.test.ts`, the `BackingPersistence` and SQL cleanup titles of `sql-sqlite-node/test/Persistence.test.ts` and of `sql-pg/test/Persistence.integration.test.ts`, the `PersistedCache` title of `Cache.test.ts` and the six `persisted` titles of `Request.test.ts` are in `tests/oracle/test_key_value_store.py`, `test_sqlite_key_value_store.py`, `test_pg_key_value_store.py`, `test_persisted_cache.py`, `test_pg_persistence.py`, `test_sqlite_persistence.py`, `test_user_directories.py`, `test_node_key_value_store.py`, `test_cache.py` and `test_request.py`; `tests/test_persistence.py` runs the store suite on the memory, file-system and mapping stores and pins time to live, stored failures and defects, and entries the schemas no longer read; `tests/type_checks.py::check_persistence` pins the request, resolver, cache, store and layer types, and that a request without `primary_key()` cannot be made. Not ported, with the reason: `persistable.Class`'s `requires` / `requestError` config (a request's services and extra errors come from its schemas and the fixed persistence errors); the `PersistedQueue`, `RateLimiter`, `CircuitBreaker` and `Redis` modules and their suites (other wave 7 clusters). Known differences: a stored defect reads back as a `DecodedDefect` (Effect's `Schema.Defect` reads back a plain object too); a `KeyValueStoreError`'s `method` is the Python method (`get_bytes` for `getUint8Array`); a `PersistedCache` lookup's result is not checked against the request's schemas by pyright (the store rejects a mismatch at run time with `SchemaError`). Review (2026-10-28): `PersistenceStore.set` now calls the `time_to_live` function when the write runs, so a raising one is a defect of the write instead of an exception while the effect is built (`tests/test_persistence.py`); mutation checks on the background removal of unreadable entries, the SQL cleanup batching, KVS-backed expiry, stale-while-revalidate and the unfinished-entry failures each fail a test. Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, two runs): map chain 3.26–3.27M steps/s, generator steps 2.30–2.31M, fork + join 173–174k/s, so no regression.

**M10 wave 7, RateLimiter / CircuitBreaker / Redis (2026-10-29): done.** `pyfect.persistence.redis`: the `Redis` service (`send`, `eval(script)`, `subscribe(channel)` giving a `Dequeue` of `RedisMessage`, `reserve` for a pooled connection of its own), `script(params, lua=, number_of_keys=, decode=)` (a `Script` whose arguments are its params function's and whose result its decoder's, run with `EVALSHA` after one `SCRIPT LOAD`, reloaded on `NOSCRIPT`), `RedisError`, `PoolOptions` and `make(send=, subscribe=, connect=, pool=)` for backends. `pyfect.persistence.redis_py` (extra `pyfect[redis]`, Effect's `NodeRedis`): `layer(url, pool=, **redis_options)` / `layer_config` / `make` on `redis.asyncio` driven on its own loop thread, raw replies, a fail-fast initial connection and reserved connections that do not reconnect, providing `Redis` and `RedisPy` (`client`, `use(f)`). `persistence.layer_backing_redis` / `layer_redis`. `pyfect.persistence.rate_limiter`: `RateLimiter` with `consume(key=, limit=, window=, algorithm="fixed-window" | "token-bucket", on_exceeded="fail" | "delay", tokens=)`, `adaptive_consume` / `adaptive_feedback`, `sleep`, `make_with_rate_limiter`, errors `RateLimiterError` over `RateLimitExceeded` / `RateLimitStoreError`, and `RateLimiterStore` with `layer_store_memory` and `layer_store_redis` (Effect's four Lua scripts) / `layer_store_redis_config`; the store works in milliseconds as Effect's does, the public API in `Seconds`. `pyfect.persistence.circuit_breaker`: `make` / `layer(trip_policy=consecutive(n) | rate(...), open_duration=Duration | Schedule, max_open_duration=, on_store_error=)`, `execute(effect, key=, classify=)` or `execute(key=)` for `.pipe`, `state`, `transitions`; outcomes `success` / `ignored` / `failure(retry_after=)`, states `Closed` / `Open` / `HalfOpen`, the three transitions, errors `CircuitBreakerError` over `CircuitOpen` / `CircuitHalfOpen` / `CircuitBreakerStoreError`, and `CircuitBreakerStore` with `layer_store_memory(idle_time_to_live=)` and `layer_store_redis(prefix=, probe_lease=, idle_time_to_live=)` (Effect's prelude and admit / record / state / renew scripts, probe leases renewed every third). Every title of `persistence/RateLimiter.test.ts` with `RateLimiterTest.ts`, `CircuitBreaker.test.ts` with `CircuitBreakerTest.ts`, `Redis.test.ts` (but `preserves __proto__ as an own script option`, a JavaScript object model case) and `CircuitBreakerRedisTest.ts`, and of platform-node's `NodeRedis.integration.test.ts` but its `PersistedQueue` suite and queue titles (now in `test_redis_py_persisted_queue.py`), is in `tests/oracle/test_rate_limiter.py`, `test_circuit_breaker.py`, `test_redis.py` and `test_redis_py.py`; the Redis ones run on a throwaway `redis-server` started in a temporary directory (`tests/oracle/_redis.py`, skipped without one). `tests/type_checks.py::check_resilience` pins the service, layer and script types and rejects an unknown algorithm, an unknown `on_store_error` and a script argument of the wrong type. Not ported: a Redis `KeyValueStore` (Effect v4 has none); `HttpClient.withRateLimiter` / `withCircuitBreaker` / `circuitBreakerClassifier` and their `http/HttpClient.test.ts` titles (wave 3 left them for when persistence was ported; done in the M10d completeness pass below). Review (2026-10-29): Effect's `Duration` arithmetic is in whole nanoseconds, so a refill rate (`Duration.divideUnsafe(window, limit)`) and every `Duration.millis` result are rounded to them; pyfect divided floats, so `window=1, limit=3` reset after `666.6666666666666` ms where Effect gives `666.666666` (`persistence/_seconds.py`: `divide_millis`, `millis`). A zero or infinite limit died with `ZeroDivisionError` or `OverflowError` (fixed window with `limit=0` in `"delay"` mode, token bucket with `limit=inf`), where JavaScript's arithmetic gives infinities and NaN; `_js.divide` / `floor` / `ceil` follow it. An infinite `open_duration` or `probe_lease` on the Redis circuit-breaker store raised `OverflowError` (in `make_store_redis`, or as a defect of the call) where Effect sends `Infinity` and the script rejects it as a store error. `tests/test_resilience.py` pins all of these with values printed by Effect under Node. Known difference: for a window of a fractional millisecond count and a fractional limit, Effect's `divideUnsafe` throws on `BigInt(limit)` and gives a zero refill rate, so the limiter never limits; pyfect rounds the quotient instead. Mutation checks: of 15 mutations on trip policies, probe release, hint capping, overflow, `NOSCRIPT` reload, reserved-connection reuse, interrupt classification, reset deadlines, lease renewal, bucket refill, store-error handling, subscription shutdown, adaptive cooldown, TTL rounding and reply decoding, two survived (a failure rate exactly at the threshold, and a shorter same-epoch `Retry-After` shortening a cooldown; Effect's tests check neither boundary), and both now fail a test in `tests/test_resilience.py`. The redis-py layer runs under `run_async` too, and interrupting a blocking `BLPOP` on a reserved connection ends it. Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`): map chain 3.25M steps/s, generator steps 2.32M, fork + join 172k/s, so no regression.

**M10 wave 7, PersistedQueue (2026-10-29): done.** `pyfect.persistence.persisted_queue` (`PersistedQueue` and `PersistedQueueError` also from `pyfect.persistence`): `make(name=, schema=, max_attempts=10, retry_schedule=)` / `PersistedQueueFactory` (`make_factory`, `layer`), a `PersistedQueue[A, R]` with `offer(value, id=, delay=)` (an id de-duplicates; an infinite delay never stores) and `take(f)` running `f(value, Metadata(id, attempts))` in a scope whose exit settles the element at least once: success completes it, a failure retries it after the schedule's delay for that attempt until `max_attempts`, then fails it; an interrupt puts it back without counting the attempt; an element the schema cannot decode is dead-lettered and the next one taken. `layer_cleanup(interval=, time_to_live=, failed_time_to_live=)`. Stores (`PersistedQueueStore`): `layer_store_memory`; `layer_store_sql(table_name=, poll_interval=, lock_refresh_interval=, lock_expiration=)` on M10b sql with Effect's two migrations and its offer / poll / settle / cleanup SQL for SQLite, PostgreSQL, MySQL and MSSQL (claims under a lock per poll, renewed while held; a settlement only touches its own claim; rows whose worker crashed on the final attempt flipped to failed; nudges after commit); `layer_store_redis(prefix=, ...)` with Effect's ten Lua scripts (lock refresh, periodic reset of lapsed locks, a failed list, delayed sorted set, batched failed trimming). Every title of `persistence/PersistedQueue.test.ts`, `PersistedQueueTest.ts` (memory, SQLite, PostgreSQL, Redis), `PersistedQueueSqlTest.ts` (SQLite, PostgreSQL), the `PersistedQueue SQL locks` titles of `sql-pg/test/Persistence.integration.test.ts`, `sql-pglite/test/PersistedQueue.test.ts` (on PostgreSQL: pyfect has no PGlite driver) and the queue titles of platform-node's `NodeRedis.integration.test.ts` are in `tests/oracle/test_persisted_queue.py`, `test_sqlite_persisted_queue.py`, `test_pg_persisted_queue.py` and `test_redis_py_persisted_queue.py` (shared suites `_persisted_queue_suite.py`, `_persisted_queue_sql_suite.py`); `SqlCleanupTest.ts` was already in `_backing_suite.py`. `tests/test_persisted_queue.py` pins that interrupted takes do not use up attempts (on memory, SQLite and Redis, values printed by Effect under Node); `tests/type_checks.py::check_persisted_queue` pins the queue, take, factory and layer types. Root-cause fix: `_json_text.stringify` escapes lone surrogates as `JSON.stringify` does (it wrote them raw, so encoding the text to UTF-8 raised; `tests/test_encoding.py`). Known differences: ids with lone surrogates are stored with U+FFFD, as Node's drivers write them (sqlite3 and redis-py raise); the SQL store's take loop past a malformed row stays interruptible (Effect re-enters it inside its uninterruptible mask); the memory suite's live sleeps are scaled to zero since the memory store runs on the test clock; MySQL and MSSQL SQL is ported but has no server to run on. Mutation checks: of 14 mutations (interrupt attempt accounting in each store, the max-attempts boundary, cleanup of pending entries, per-attempt retry delay, decode dead-lettering, taker accounting, claim ownership, nudge before commit, exhausted-row flipping, lock refresh, Redis requeue and id normalization, held-claim release), the memory interrupt one survived and now fails `tests/test_persisted_queue.py`; releasing held claims after settling survived too, since only lock refresh reads them and a settled row's lock no longer matches. Review (2026-10-29): a failing SQL offer or cleanup died with a `SchemaError` instead of failing with `PersistedQueueError`, since the store passed the `Cause` as the error's `cause`; it now passes `cause.squash()`, and `PersistedQueueError.cause` is typed `BaseException | None` so pyright rejects a `Cause` there (`tests/type_checks.py::check_persisted_queue`). Effect reads the SQL and Redis stores' `pollInterval`, `lockRefreshInterval` and `lockExpiration` with `options.x ? x : default`, so a zero means the default; pyfect clamped it to 1 ms, so `lock_expiration=0` let any poll take over a live claim. Both pinned in `tests/test_persisted_queue.py`. Review mutation checks: of 8 mutations, three survived the store suites (the memory store's decode dead-lettering, which Effect's title cannot tell from ten quick retries; the simulated time in the retry-schedule replay; the SQL store's malformed-row dead-lettering, caught by the PostgreSQL title only) and now fail `tests/test_persisted_queue.py` (decode failures free their id at the first failed cleanup, on memory, SQLite and Redis; `exponential(1).up_to(duration=2.5)` gives 1, 2, 2 s). Passing an interrupted decode through without dead-lettering is not reachable with a synchronous schema, so no test covers it.

**M10 wave 7, workers and RpcWorker (2026-10-29): done.** `pyfect.workers` (`fx.workers`), Effect's `effect/workers`: `worker_error` (`WorkerError(reason)` over `WorkerSpawnError`, `WorkerSendError`, `WorkerReceiveError`, `WorkerUnknownError`, schema classes as Effect's, `WORKER_ERROR_REASON`, `is_worker_error`); `worker` (`Worker[O, I]` with `send(message, transfers=())` and `run(handler, on_spawn=)`, `make_unsafe`, `Spawner` / `layer_spawner`, `WorkerPlatform`, and `make_platform(setup=, listen=)` with Effect's buffering of sends until the ready message, fail-fast before it and interruptible wait for it); `worker_runner` (`WorkerRunner[O, I]` with `run`, `send`, `send_unsafe`, `disconnects`; `WorkerRunnerPlatform`); `transferable` (`Collector`, `make_collector(_unsafe)`, `add_all`, `getter_add_all`, `schema(s, f)`). The platform, Effect's `NodeWorker` / `NodeWorkerRunner`: `python_worker.layer(spawn)` / `layer_platform` with `ThreadWorker(target)` (a function on a daemon thread running its own runtime; messages handed over as they are) and `ProcessWorker(args, executable=, cwd=, env=)` (a child Python process given one end of a socket pair as the descriptor named by `PYFECT_WORKER_FD`, as Node's `NODE_CHANNEL_FD`; length-prefixed pickles, a reader and a writer thread per side, so no fiber waits on the socket), and `python_worker_runner.layer`, which finds its parent through a thread-local or that variable (taken out of the environment). As Node's: closing a run posts the close message, waits five seconds (`CLOSE_TIMEOUT`) for the exit, then kills; an exit, a raised exception (`error` event) or an unreadable message (`messageerror`) fails the run with Effect's `WorkerReceiveError` messages, and a process that cannot start fails it with a `WorkerSpawnError`. RpcWorker (deferred from M10c): `pyfect.rpc.worker` (`InitialMessage`, `make_initial_message`, `layer_initial_message`, `initial_message`), `rpc.client.make_protocol_worker` / `layer_protocol_worker(size=` or `min_size=, max_size=, time_to_live=`, `concurrency=, target_utilization=)` (a `Pool` of workers, respawned a second after a failure that is broadcast as a `ClientProtocolError`) and `rpc.server.make_protocol_worker_runner` / `layer_protocol_worker_runner` (interrupts the fiber that made it when the parent closes the worker, so `layer.launch` ends); the server `Protocol` has `initial_message` now. Every title of `test/workers/Worker.test.ts` and `WorkerError.test.ts` (`tests/oracle/test_worker.py`, `test_worker_error.py`), platform-node's `NodeWorkerRunner.test.ts` on both backends (`test_node_worker_runner.py`, fixture `fixtures/workers/worker_runner.py`), `RpcClient.test.ts`'s "releases a worker pool slot when the worker run fails" (no longer omitted) and the browser / Deno `RpcWorker.test.ts` e2e suite as "e2e worker thread" and "e2e worker process" in `test_rpc_server_node.py` (server in `_rpc.worker_main`, child module `_rpc_worker`). `tests/test_workers.py` pins the Python platform: buffered sends in order, a failing handler logged while the worker keeps serving, a closed run ending the worker's program (thread and process), exit codes, error events, spawn and pickling failures, the kill after the close timeout, and `start` outside a worker; `tests/type_checks.py::check_workers` pins the worker, runner and layer types. Not ported: `RpcClient` / `RpcServer` transferable collection (`supportsTransferables`: Python workers copy or share messages, never move them), `Transferable.ImageData` / `MessagePort` / `Uint8Array` (browser objects), Effect's JSON passthrough annotation on `Transferable.schema` (its JSON codec is the wrapped schema's), and `RpcWorker` over `RpcSerialization` binary protocols (Effect has none either). Review (2026-10-29): a runner handler that raised killed the Python runner's message loop on a forked fiber, so the worker hung and its parent waited forever; the loop now runs on the run's own fiber, so the raise ends the worker as Node's uncaught exception does (the parent sees the error event, or the process's exit code). `make_platform` asked the `Spawner` once at `spawn`; Effect asks it at each `run`, so a retried run (the RPC pool's respawn) gets a fresh worker. `make_protocol_worker(size=1, min_size=1)` slipped past the boundary check. All three pinned in `tests/test_workers.py`.

**M10d completeness pass (2026-10-29): done; M10d (wave 7) is complete.** A title-by-title script check of every Effect test file for the M10d modules (`effect/test/persistence` and `effect/test/workers`, the persisted titles of `Cache.test.ts` and `Request.test.ts`, `RpcClient.test.ts`, `http/HttpClient.test.ts`, platform-node's `KeyValueStore`, `NodeRedis.integration`, `NodeWorkerRunner` and `RpcServer` tests, `sql-sqlite-node`'s and `sql-pg`'s `Persistence` and `KeyValueStore` tests, `sql-pglite/test/PersistedQueue.test.ts`, and the browser / Deno `RpcWorker` suite: 311 titles) finds every title ported or omitted with a reason, but the 29 `withRateLimiter` and `withCircuitBreaker` titles of `http/HttpClient.test.ts`, which no wave owned (wave 3 left them for persistence, wave 7's resilience cluster for the HTTP cluster). They are now ported: `HttpClientService.with_rate_limiter(limiter=, window=, limit=, key=, algorithm=, tokens=, times=, response_headers=RateLimitHeaders(...), disable_response_inspection=, disable_adaptive_learning=)` and `with_circuit_breaker(breaker=, key=, classify=)` are methods (they add `RateLimiterError` / `CircuitBreakerError`, so nothing is erased), `circuit_breaker_classifier` is in `pyfect.http.client` (`http/client/_rate_limit.py`, `_circuit_breaker.py`), with Effect's header parsing (`ratelimit-*` / `x-ratelimit-*`, epoch resets in seconds or milliseconds, a reported remaining budget holding requests until its reset, stale counts from out-of-order responses ignored), 429 retries through the limiter, `Retry-After` feedback to the adaptive pacing, and request-origin circuit keys. The titles are in `tests/oracle/test_http_client_resilience.py` (40 tests); `tests/type_checks.py::check_http_client_resilience` pins the client types and rejects an unknown algorithm and an unknown header field. Mutation checks: of 14 mutations (the whole limiter, budget hold, stale-count guard, in-flight accounting, adaptive feedback and its epoch, reset-over-`Retry-After`, cooldown pacing, two retry bounds, origin keys, the token floor, inferred-limit growth, longest hint), three survived Effect's titles (an inferred limit shrinking on later responses, the classifier keeping the last hint instead of the longest, a cost below one token) and now fail `tests/test_http_client_resilience.py`. Still not ported, with the reason: the browser and Deno platform suites (`BrowserKeyValueStore`, `BrowserPersistence`, `BrowserWorkerRunner`, `DenoKeyValueStore`, `DenoWorkerRunner`, `DenoRedis`: no browser or Deno runtime in Python; the `DenoRedis` URL-credential titles test Deno's own client); `Persistable.Class`'s `requires` / `requestError`, `RpcClient` / `RpcServer` transferables and the browser `Transferable` schemas (reasons in the cluster paragraphs above). Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, two runs): map chain 3.24–3.25M steps/s, generator steps 2.30–2.35M, fork + join 174–175k/s, against 3.23–3.38M / 2.30–2.39M / 172–176k recorded in the wave 7 clusters, so no regression.

**M10e workflow (2026-10-29): done.** `pyfect.workflow` (`fx.workflow`, about 4.1k Effect source lines): `workflow` (`make(tag, payload=, idempotency_key=, success=, error=, suspended_retry_schedule=, annotations=)` giving a `Workflow[P, A, E]` with `execute` (overloaded on `discard=True`, giving the execution id), `execution_id`, `poll`, `interrupt`, `resume`, `to_layer`, `with_compensation`; `Complete` / `Suspended` results, `CaptureDefects` / `SuspendOnFailure`, `into_result`, the execution `scope`, `provide_scope`, `add_finalizer`, `suspend`), `activity` (`make`, `retry`, `idempotency_key`, `race_all`, `CurrentAttempt`, the interrupt retry policy), `durable_deferred` (`make`, `wait` / `into` / `token*` / `done` / `succeed` / `fail` / `fail_cause`, base64url tokens, `race_all`), `durable_clock` (`sleep` in memory under a threshold, else through `schedule_clock`), `durable_queue` (`process` on a `PersistedQueue` named `DurableQueue/<name>`, `make_worker` / `worker`), `workflow_engine` (`WorkflowEngineService`, `make_unsafe` over an `Encoded` engine, `DeferredState`, `layer_memory`), `workflow_proxy` (`to_rpc_group`, `to_http_api_group`) and `workflow_proxy_server` (`layer_rpc_handlers`, `layer_http_api`), imported by name so that `import pyfect` does not load the RPC and HTTP API packages (pinned in `tests/test_workflow_proxy.py`). Replays, suspension, compensation, interruption (safe and `interrupt_unsafe`) and resumption follow Effect's in-memory engine. Every title of `test/workflow/WorkflowEngine.test.ts` but `supports class extension` and of `DurableQueue.test.ts` is in `tests/oracle/test_workflow_engine.py` and `test_durable_queue.py`; `Workflow.test.ts`'s one title is in `tests/oracle/test_workflow.py`, sweeping `MaxOpsBeforeYield` budgets in place of its `Scheduler.shouldYield` hook. `tests/test_workflow_proxy.py` covers the proxies (Effect has no tests for them), `tests/type_checks.py::check_workflow` pins the inferred types and rejects a wrong payload, a handler or activity effect of the wrong success type in either argument order, and a worker of the wrong type. Typing research: `research/typing/workflow_1_schemas.py`, `workflow_2_exact_schemas.py` (pyright solves arguments in written order, so `Exactly[T]` pins an activity's type through the schema's `make`; handlers needing nothing get a first overload so their requirements are `Never`, not `Unknown`). The review pass added `tests/test_workflow_edges.py`, cases Effect's tests leave open, each run under Node first (a suspended activity reruns on replay, a detached child does not wake its parent, a safe interrupt ends a failure-suspended run, a suspension waits for an activity in flight and for a child still computing its id, a settled race's loser does not preempt the run, a sleep equal to the threshold stays in memory, an abandoned run skips workflow finalizers); each kills a mutation the ported tests let through. It also gave `into_result`, `provide_scope` and `DeferredState.track_run` the first overload a body needing only `Scope` / `WorkflowInstance` needs (their requirements were `Unknown`), and `tests/test_requirement_overloads.py` now fails on any `pyfect.workflow` function taking `Effect[A, E, R | Tag]` without one. Known gaps: `Exactly` does not check that a result schema needs no services; `layer.merge` of layers that all build `Never` (workflow and worker layers) infers `Unknown` outputs.

**M10e ai core (2026-10-29): partly done.** `pyfect.ai` (`fx.ai`), the provider-neutral half of Effect's `effect/ai`: `ai_error` (`AiError(module, method, reason)` and every reason, `reason_from_http_status`, the HTTP and usage schemas, Effect's messages and JSON form), `prompt` (parts, messages, `Prompt` with `concat` / `set_system` / `prepend_system` / `append_system`, `make`, `from_messages`, `from_response_parts`, part and message schemas in Effect's camelCase wire format), `response` (every part, `Usage`, `tool_call_part_schema`, `tool_result_part_schema` choosing its branch by `isFailure`, `all_parts` / `part` / `stream_part` for a toolkit), `tool` (`make`, `dynamic`, `provider_defined`, handlers with preliminary results, failure modes, `needs_approval` / `set_needs_approval`, annotations, `get_json_schema`), `toolkit` (`make`, `merge`, `empty`, `to_handlers`, `to_layer`, `WithHandler.handle` with parse `options`, `PreliminaryBuffer`), `language_model` (`generate_text`, `generate_object`, `stream_text`, the tool-call resolution loop, tool approvals (`_approvals`: approval requests, pre-resolving approved and denied calls, stripping answered approvals from the prompt, `ToolkitRequiredError`), `tool_choice` with `one_of`, `disable_tool_call_resolution`, the incremental-prompt fallback (not retried once a stream emitted content), the `IdGenerator` of the context the model was made in, span attributes on all three methods), `embedding_model` (`embed` batched through a `RequestResolver`, `embed_many`), `model`, `id_generator`, `tokenizer`, `response_id_tracker` and `chat` (added by the completeness pass: `Chat` / `chat.Service` with `history`, `export` / `export_json`, `generate_text` / `generate_object` / `stream_text` run one at a time on the history, a cut-short stream keeping the parts it streamed; `empty`, `from_prompt`, `from_export`, `from_json`; `Persistence` from `layer_persisted(store_id=)` over a `BackingPersistence`, whose `get` / `get_or_create(chat_id, time_to_live=)` give a `Persisted` chat saved after every call with Effect's message ids under `PERSISTENCE_KEY`, and `ChatNotFoundError`). `tool_call_validation="deferred"` (with `disable_tool_call_resolution=True`) returns calls as the provider sent them, calls to tools outside the toolkit included, and still checks provider-executed calls against their encoded parameters; the providers keep an undeclared call's parameters instead of failing with `ToolNotFoundError`. Its first overload types the call names as `str`, and the other overloads take only `"strict"`, so deferred validation without disabled resolution (which Effect ignores) is a type error. `stream_text` resolves tool calls as Effect does (`_stream_resolution`: a queue and a `FiberSet`, each handler started once the stream moves past its call, bounded by `concurrency`, interrupted on a truncating finish or a provider failure, finish parts after every result). Ports: `tests/oracle/test_ai_error.py`, `test_ai_prompt.py`, `test_ai_response.py`, `test_ai_tool.py` (with `ToolRepresentation.test.ts`), `test_ai_language_model.py` (with `LanguageModelRepresentation.test.ts`), `test_ai_language_model_tracker_lifecycle.py`, `test_ai_response_id_tracker.py` (with `Model.test.ts` and `Tokenizer.test.ts`, one pytest per title), `test_ai_embedding_model.py` and `test_ai_chat.py` (every `Chat.test.ts` title), each listing its omitted titles. `tests/test_ai_language_model_edges.py` pins cases Effect's tests leave open, each run under Node first (the `streamText` span, a failed tool call leaves the tracker unmarked, `one_of` excluding a called tool, an empty toolkit sends tool choice `"none"`, the make-time `IdGenerator`). `tests/type_checks.py::check_ai_toolkit` pins tool, handler, toolkit and result types and rejects a wrong handler; research in `research/typing/ai_1_toolkit.py`. Not yet ported: `DecisionModel` / `Decision`, `Telemetry`'s GenAI attribute helpers, `AnthropicStructuredOutput` / `OpenAiStructuredOutput` (and their Representation tests) and the OpenAI codec transformer.

**M10e mcp (2026-10-29): partly done.** The MCP cluster of `effect/ai`: `mcp_schema` (every MCP message as a TypedDict in its JSON wire form with its schema, `Method` pairing a method name with its params and result codecs, the `McpError` family with JSON-RPC codes, `McpRequestContext` / `McpServerClient` and the reverse client), `mcp_protocol` (`ProtocolAdapter` per revision 2024-11-05 through the stateless 2026-07-28, covariant in its version so a revision's adapter fits a version union; `make_registry`; per-revision result projection in `_mcp/projection.py`), `mcp_server` (`make`, `layer`, `run_stdio` (serves until stdin ends; a Python addition so a script can be the server process), `layer_stdio`, `layer_http` (admission statuses 403 / 415 / 406 / 405, sessions, DELETE with `allow_session_termination`, JSON or SSE replies), `register_toolkit` / `toolkit` (annotations from `Readonly` / `Destructive` / `Idempotent` / `OpenWorld`, an `outputSchema` (listed before 2026-07-28 only for object results), parameters decoded with all errors reported (`WithHandler.handle` takes Effect's parse `options`), parameter errors as InvalidParams, declared failures as `isError`, everything else scrubbed to Effect's internal-error message; a toolkit registers whole or not at all), `register_resource` / `resource`, `uri_template` (positional segments and schemas, Python's spelling of Effect's tagged template) with `register_resource_template`, `register_prompt` / `prompt`, completions, `elicit`, `client_capabilities`), `mcp_client` (`make`, `Transport`, `stdio` / `http` transports, `layer_stdio` / `layer_http`, `list_*` with Effect's discovery limits and the 60-second default deadline, `call_tool` overloaded on `schema=`, `get_prompt`, `read_resource`, `toolkit(client, prefix)` exposing remote tools to a `LanguageModel`, stdio and legacy-HTTP cancellation, routing headers, the sticky session-expired close) and `mcp_client_error` (`McpClientError(reason)` and every reason). Binary content is base64 text as on the wire; a tool's default `S.Null` success is a void result with no content. `examples/mcp_server.py` serves a toolkit over stdio and `tests/test_mcp_example.py` drives it with this client over a subprocess on both client revisions. Ports: `tests/oracle/test_mcp_schema.py` (every title), `test_mcp_protocol.py` (all but the RPC-namespace title), `test_mcp_client.py` (every title of `McpClient.test.ts`), `test_mcp_server.py` (a subset of `McpServer.test.ts`, listing its omitted titles and why) and `test_mcp_protocol_adapters.py` (tool failures and duplicate tools from `ProtocolAdapters.test.ts`, the result envelopes and sessionless cancellation of `v2026_07_28.test.ts`, and cases checked under Node: invalid tool input and execution failures are `isError` results from 2025-11-25 on and JSON-RPC errors before, non-object `outputSchema` / `structuredContent` only on 2026-07-28, `resources/read` is cacheable, a stateless request never changes a stdio session's client, a cancelled HTTP request ends with an empty event stream; it lists the omitted titles of those files, `Workerd.test.ts` and `LayerHttpRequestScoped.test.ts`); `tests/type_checks.py::check_mcp` pins the public types and rejects a template handler of the wrong type and a client on an unsupported revision. Not yet ported: JSON-RPC batches (2025-03-26), resource subscriptions and HTTP listen streams, list-change notification coalescing, `layerHttpRequestScoped`, `input_required` multi-round-trip results, the bridge from fx logs to `notifications/message`, `-32020` header-mismatch validation, strict-mode tool schemas, most of `ProtocolAdapters.test.ts`, and the conformance suites under `test/ai/McpServer/` (with the per-revision `v*.test.ts` files that run them).

**M10e ai providers (2026-10-29): partly done.** Two of Effect's provider packages, each over the M10a `HttpClient` with the provider's own JSON wire format and streaming through the SSE framer (no vendor SDK, no new dependency). `pyfect.ai.anthropic` (`@effect/ai-anthropic`): `client` (`AnthropicClient`, `make` / `layer` / `layer_config`, `create_message` on `/v1/messages?beta=true` with the gateway check for a 4xx without Anthropic's envelope, `create_message_stream` until `message_stop`, `x-api-key` redacted), `config` (`AnthropicConfig`, `with_client_transform`), `error` (the `anthropic` metadata), `language_model` (`model` / `layer` / `make`, `Config`, `with_config_override`, prompt conversion with cache-control breakpoints, citations, thinking and redacted thinking, mid-conversation system messages, the model capability table with optimistic defaults for unknown models, native structured output or the forced response tool, betas, provider-executed tool calls and results, fallback blocks, usage with cache tokens on the span), `telemetry` and `tool` (all sixteen provider-defined tools). `pyfect.ai.openai_compat` (`@effect/ai-openai-compat`): `client` (`/chat/completions` and `/embeddings`, bearer token and the redacted `openai-organization` / `openai-project` headers, chunks until `[DONE]`, events that are not chunks as `UnknownEvent`), `config`, `error`, `language_model` (Responses-style options sent as Chat Completions, unknown keys passed through, `file_id_prefixes`, `strict_json_schema`, reasoning under `reasoning` or `reasoning_content` replayed under the field it came in, `extra_content` echoed, fragmented tool-call deltas), `embedding_model` (`model(..., dimensions=)` with `Dimensions`, index checks) and `telemetry`. Shared plumbing: `pyfect/ai/_provider.py` (request and response details, `ErrorMapper`, `Retry-After`, client and config overrides), `_provider_tools.py` (tool JSON Schemas, parameter normalization) and `_openai_errors.py` (the OpenAI-style status mapping, by `Dialect`). Ports with a mocked `HttpClient` answering recorded JSON and SSE (`tests/oracle/_ai_http.py`): `test_anthropic_client.py`, `test_anthropic_language_model.py`, `test_openai_compat_client.py` (with `OpenAiTelemetry.test.ts`), `test_openai_compat_embedding_model.py` and `test_openai_compat_language_model.py`, each listing its omitted titles; `tests/test_ai_provider_edges.py` pins edges checked against Effect under Node (an OpenAI-compatible error body is the response text, an array body decodes only when every item is an error, an empty error type or message is kept, a `null` stream event is an `UnknownEvent`, an empty prompt is one empty user message). `tests/type_checks.py::check_ai_providers` pins the layer, model and client types and rejects a misspelled config option. With deferred tool-call validation, a call to a tool the request did not offer keeps its parameters (completeness pass). Not yet ported: `@effect/ai-openai` (the Responses API client and its WebSocket mode, `OpenAiLanguageModel`, `OpenAiEmbeddingModel`, `OpenAiDecisionModel`, `OpenAiTool`, `OpenAiSchema` and their tests); deferred to later milestones: `@effect/ai-cloudflare`, `@effect/ai-openrouter` and the typesafe provider.

**M10e completeness pass (2026-10-29): done; M10e (wave 9's workflow and ai) is complete for what it ports, with the deferrals below.** A title-by-title script check of every Effect test file for the M10e modules (`effect/test/workflow`, `effect/test/ai` with `McpServer/`, and the `ai-anthropic`, `ai-openai-compat` and `ai-openai` test folders: 973 titles in `*.test.ts` files, plus 217 titles of the `McpServer/McpConformance` suites that the per-revision files run) finds 560 ported or omitted with a reason in their oracle module's docstring and 413 not ported, all in areas deferred below. The pass ported what the clusters had left open that fits this milestone: `Chat` (`pyfect.ai.chat`, the 10 `Chat.test.ts` titles as 16 tests in `tests/oracle/test_ai_chat.py`, `tests/type_checks.py::check_ai_chat`, a section and checked snippet in the AI guide); deferred tool-call validation (the 3 `LanguageModel.test.ts` titles, the 2 `ai-openai-compat` and 1 `ai-anthropic` titles, each failing before the change); the per-revision titles `MUST NOT advertise completions` (2024-11-05) and `does not expose experimental Tasks without an extension` (2025-11-25) in `tests/oracle/test_mcp_protocol_adapters.py`; and `ResponseIdTracker.test.ts` split to one pytest per title. It also fixed `McpClientError` messages, which replaced an empty description by the default where Effect's `??` keeps it (checked under Node, `tests/test_mcp_client_error.py`); the `??` helper `or_default` moved to `pyfect._js` beside the other JavaScript rules so ports outside the providers use it. Deferred, with the reason (`@effect/ai-openai` was ported in M12 ai-openai, below): `@effect/ai-openai` (146 titles; 77k source lines, most generated from the OpenAI spec, the Responses API and WebSocket mode; `ai-openai-compat` covers OpenAI's Chat Completions API meanwhile); `AnthropicStructuredOutput` / `OpenAiStructuredOutput` and their Representation tests (a JSON Schema rewriter for each provider's strict mode; ported in M12 structured-output, below); `DecisionModel` / `Decision` (46 titles; a typed-judgment API that only `ai-openai` and the out-of-scope providers implement); and the MCP server titles that need ported features (122 titles of `McpServer.test.ts`, `ProtocolAdapters.test.ts`, `v2025_03_26`, `v2026_07_28`, `LayerHttpRequestScoped` and `Workerd`, plus the conformance suites: JSON-RPC batches, subscriptions and listen streams, list-change coalescing, `input_required` round trips, the log bridge, `-32020` header checks, strict-mode tool schemas, `layerHttpRequestScoped`, workerd), listed per file in the oracle docstrings. Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, run alternately against the commit before M10e on the same machine and load): map chain 3.07–3.16M steps/s against 3.11M, generator steps 2.18–2.20M against 2.20–2.22M, fork + join 161–162k/s against 160–161k/s, so no regression (no runtime core file changed in M10e; the lower figures than the 3.2M recorded under M10d come from the machine's load, which the baseline shows too). The full check collects 10,868 tests: 10,865 pass and 2 are skipped; the remaining one, `tests/oracle/test_rpc_server_node.py::test_never[e2e worker process]`, fails under heavy machine load (a fixed 0.5 s wait before the interrupt, taken from Effect's test, as recorded under M10e ai providers): in this pass it passed on its own at a load average of about 3 and failed at 9 to 18, before and after these changes alike. Fixed after M10e: the cause was not the wait but pyfect's RPC server scheduling a handler fiber where Effect's `runForkWith` runs it at once; a process worker hands over the messages sent before it is ready together, so the `Never` request and its `Interrupt` met a handler that had not started and never ran its `on_interrupt`. The server now starts each handler immediately (`tests/test_rpc_server_interrupt.py`). Waiting on a second request while `Never` is pending hangs in the worker suites by design: they use `layer_protocol_worker(size=1)`, whose one worker serves one request at a time (`concurrency=1`, as in Effect). The worker suites now also wait for one reply before a test starts (`tests/oracle/_rpc.py::worker_suite`), so a process worker's start-up, seconds on a loaded machine, no longer runs into the fixed waits of Effect's tests (`test_stream[e2e worker process]` failed the same way at a load average above 15).

**M10f VariantSchema, Model and SqlModel (2026-10-30): done.** The M10 wave 4 leftover (`SqlModel` needed `Model`). Typing first (`research/typing/model_1_variants.py`): pyright has no mapped types, so a variant's keys cannot be computed from the fields; a model declares them. `pyfect.schema.variant_schema` (`S.variant_schema`, Effect's `effect/schema/VariantSchema`): `make(variants=, default_variant=)` gives a `VariantSchema` toolkit with `Struct({...})`, `Field(**schemas)`, `field_only([...])` / `field_except([...])` (a sequence of names; a bare string is a type error, as Effect's v4 key arrays), `field_evolve`, `Union([...])` (`variants[name]` per variant), `extract` and a `Class` base; `Field`, `Struct`, `VariantStructSchema` (a struct schema whose `make` / `make_option` / `make_effect` fill constructor defaults), `extract`, `fields`, `is_field`, `is_struct`, `field(spec)` (a class field defined by a variant field, struct or schema), `with_constructor_default`, `Overrideable`, `override`. A variant class is a schema class of the default variant (`S.Class` machinery: annotations, `dataclass_transform`, validation, subclassing extends with per-class variant caches); a field the default variant lacks is assigned without an annotation (not a dataclass field, removed from the class); an annotated field outside the default variant, or an unannotated one inside it, is a `TypeError` when the class is made. `pyfect.schema.model` (`S.model`, Effect's `Model`): `Class[Insert, Update, Json, JsonCreate, JsonUpdate]` (TypedDicts typing the variants, each defaulting to `Mapping[str, object]`, checked against the fields' keys and constructor defaults when the class is made; `select` / `insert` / `update` / `json` / `json_create` / `json_update` descriptors typed from those arguments), `extract` (overloads per variant; `select` of a class is the class), `fields`, `Struct`, `Union`, `Field` (keyword per variant; without `select` its Type is `NotSelected`, which fits no annotation), `field_only`, `field_except`, `field_evolve`, `GeneratedByDb`, `GeneratedByApp`, `Sensitive`, `FieldOption`, `optional_option`, `BooleanSqlite`, `Date`, `DateWithNow`, `DateTimeWithNow`, `DateTimeFromDateWithNow`, `DateTimeFromNumberWithNow`, `DateTimeInsert` / `DateTimeInsertFromDate` / `DateTimeInsertFromNumber`, `DateTimeUpdate` / `...FromDate` / `...FromNumber`, `JsonFromString`, `UuidV4Insert`, `UuidV7Insert` (RFC 9562 v7 from the `Clock`), `UuidV4BytesInsert` and their `...WithGenerate`. `S.DateTimeUtcFromDate` (an aware `datetime` to UTC) was missing from M8 and is added. `pyfect.sql.model` (Effect's `SqlModel`): `make_repository(model, table_name=, span_prefix=, id_column=, soft_delete_column=)` gives a `Repository[A, Insert, Update]` (`insert`, `insert_void`, `update`, `update_void`, `find_by_id`, `delete`; a missing returned row is a defect; spans `<prefix>.insert` ...), `make_resolvers` gives `Resolvers[A, Insert]` (`insert`, `insert_void`, `find_by_id`, `delete` resolvers with their spans); both solve their types from the class through `__model_variants__`, and check `id_column` / `soft_delete_column` at the boundary (`ValueError`). Effect test titles: every title of `schema/VariantSchema.test.ts` (12) in `tests/oracle/test_variant_schema.py`, of `sql-sqlite-node/test/SqlModelServices.test.ts` (6) in `test_sql_model_services.py`, of `sql-mysql2/test/Model.integration.test.ts` (9, run on SQLite: pyfect has no MySQL driver) in `test_sql_model.py`, and the `sql-pg` "round trips Model.DateTimeInsertFromDate through a repository" case M10b had omitted, in `test_pg_client.py`; `effect/test/sql` has no SqlModel titles. `typetest/VariantSchema.tst.ts`'s pins and the repository's types are in `tests/type_checks.py::check_model` / `check_sql_model`. Not ported: the dual (data-last) forms of `extract` and `fieldEvolve` beyond `field_evolve(mapping)(field)` (Python calls pass the struct), `Model.Uint8Array` (it is `S.Bytes`). Known limits: services a field schema needs are not tracked in the types (a model's schema is `Codec[Self, Mapping[str, object]]`, and `sql.schema` / `sql.resolver` take `Any` services), `find_by_id` / `delete` take `object` (a column name cannot type the id; the id field's schema checks it), and a subclass that extends a model keeps its base's variant type arguments.

**M10f graphql (2026-10-30): done.** `pyfect.graphql`, Effect's own GraphQL client (Effect does not use graphql-js; neither does this port). Typing first (`research/typing/graphql_1_client.py`). `operation` (`query`, `mutation`, `subscription` from a printed document or a `template.DocumentTemplate`, joined with the generator printer's spacing rule; `Operation[V, A, ME, MR]` / `Subscription[...]` with `middleware(Tag)`, `Method` / `SubscriptionMethod` with `partial=True`, `headers`, `url_params`, per-call `context`; `NoVariables`, `PartialResult`, `other_typename`, `enum_literals`), `group` (`GraphQLGroup[M1 | M2]`, middleware read from the type argument, outermost first; class access carries the group's middleware, which is Effect's `merge`; duplicate names are a `ValueError`), `middleware` (`GraphQLMiddleware[E, R]` tags, `Implementation(execute=, subscribe=)`, `map_request`), `client_error` (`GraphQLClientError(operation, reason)` with `ResponseError`, `TransportError` (`from_http_client_error`, close codes), `EncodeError`, `DecodeError`, `PaginationError`; schema classes that round-trip, headers as a string record, `retry_after` as milliseconds; `is_retryable`, `retry_after`, `status`, `headers`; `GraphQLError` keeps unknown top-level keys), `protocol` (`GraphQLRequest`, `ExecutionResult`, `Answer`, `GraphQLProtocol`; `make_http` / `layer_http` with `query_method="GET"`, a URL that is a string, an `Effect` or a function of the caller's context and operation, `application/graphql-response+json` / `application/json` bodies, non-GraphQL responses as `TransportError`s with `Retry-After` measured from the `Clock`, graphql-sse distinct-mode subscriptions with `retry:` and `complete`, or graphql-transport-ws subscriptions with `web_socket=WebSocketOptions(...)`; `make_web_socket` / `layer_web_socket`: one shared graphql-transport-ws connection opened on the first operation, `connection_params` per connection, `connection_ack_timeout`, `keep_alive` pings, `idle_timeout`, 4400 on an invalid server message (messages parsed in `_ws_messages`), fatal after the scope closes), `resolver` (`ordered`, `find_by_id`, `aliased` with at most 10 slices per request, `request(resolver)`; batches split by `batch_key` and by the protocol URL each lookup resolves, which the transport reuses; batches always POST; spans as Effect's), `client` (`make`, `default_subscription_retry`, `retryable_only`, `pages`, `items`, `make_test`, `layer_test`, `UnimplementedError`). Every title of `effect/test/graphql` `GraphQL.test.ts`, `GraphQLClientError.test.ts`, `GraphQLClient.test.ts`, `GraphQLClient.pages.test.ts`, `GraphQLClient.makeTest.test.ts`, `GraphQLClient.subscriptions.test.ts`, `GraphQLProtocol.test.ts` (the 502 / 400 table as two tests) `GraphQLProtocol.sse.test.ts`, `GraphQLProtocol.webSocket.test.ts` (the invalid-message table as one parametrized test), `GraphQLResolver.test.ts` (the two URL forms as one parametrized test) and platform-node's `GraphQLProtocol.test.ts` (a threaded `websockets` server stands in for Node's hand-rolled one; the live server's upgrade has no WebSocket framing) is in `tests/oracle/test_graphql.py`, `test_graphql_client.py`, `test_graphql_protocol.py`, `test_graphql_ws.py`, `test_graphql_resolver.py`, `test_graphql_node.py` (94 tests); `tests/test_graphql_ws_edges.py` adds an answered keep-alive and an idle timeout restarted by a new operation, `tests/test_graphql_group.py` a subclass overriding an operation (it was rejected as a duplicate name); `tests/type_checks.py::graphql_checks` pins the operation, method, group-middleware, merge, `make`, resolver and transport types. Schema fix on the way: deriving a schema for a recursive `type` alias (`JsonValue`) recursed forever; `JsonValue` now derives `S.Json` and any other self-referring alias is a `TypeError` naming it (`tests/test_schema_composites.py`). Known limits: middleware attached to a single operation is declared as the group's second type argument (`GraphQLGroup[GM, OM]`, checked when the class is made) so that `make`'s type has it (QA types 4); a per-call `context` does not remove services from the types (as rpc); `make_test` handlers are keyed by attribute name and typed loosely; resolver lookups succeed with `Any` (Effect checks `path` at the type level and narrows by `typename`), and `request` is only the curried `request(resolver)(key)` (with the key first, pyright solves the key type from the key before the resolver).

**M10f cluster core (2026-10-30): partial.** Typing first (`research/typing/cluster_1_entity_client.py`): an entity client can be an instance of the entity's RPC-group class, with a nominal `ClientErrors[TE]` base read by a first `__get__` overload replacing `RpcClientError` by the cluster's client errors; not yet applied. `pyfect.hash_ring` (Effect's `HashRing`, a mutable `HashRing[A: PrimaryKey]` with `add`, `add_many`, `remove`, `has`, `get`, `get_shards`, Effect's exact assignment through `pyfect._string_hash.string_hash`, Effect's `Hash.string` on UTF-16 code units with 32-bit arithmetic). `pyfect.cluster` (a submodule): `shard_id` (`ShardId(group, id)` schema class, `make`, `from_string`), `entity_id`, `entity_type`, `machine_id` (`NewType`s with branded schemas), `entity_address`, `runner_address`, `runner` (equal by address and weight, as Effect), `snowflake` (`make`, `timestamp`, `date_time`, `machine_id`, `sequence`, `to_parts`, `SnowflakeFromBigInt`, `SnowflakeFromString`, `SnowflakeGenerator` with the clock-drift rules, `Generator`, `layer_generator`), `deliver_at`, `cluster_error` (the seven errors, `MalformedMessage.refail`, `PersistenceError.refail`), `cluster_schema` (`Persisted`, `WithTransaction`, `Uninterruptible`, `InterruptOnTermination`, `ShardGroup`, `ClientTracingEnabled` references, `is_uninterruptible_for_client` / `_server`), `sharding_config` (`ShardingConfigService` with `Seconds` durations, `DEFAULTS`, `layer(settings)`, `layer_defaults`, `config`, `layer_from_env(update)`, `shard_group_config`), `runner_storage` (`RunnerStorageService`, `make_memory`, `layer_memory`), and the internals `_hash` (shard numbers), `_interruptors` and `_resource_map`. Ports: `tests/oracle/test_hash_ring.py` (7) and `tests/oracle/test_cluster_core.py` (ShardingConfig 3, interruptors 3, RunnerStorage 2, ResourceMap 4); `tests/type_checks.py::cluster_core_types`. Pythonic decisions: `ShardingConfig.layer({...})` is `layer(dataclasses.replace(DEFAULTS, ...))`; absent addresses are `None`. Not ported yet (the rest of the milestone): Entity, EntityResource, Sharding, Runners, RunnerHealth, RunnerServer, TestRunner, Singleton, SingletonAddress, ShardingRegistrationEvent, ClusterCron, ClusterMetrics, EntityProxy, EntityProxyServer, ClusterWorkflowEngine, and their tests (Entity 8, EntityProxy 1, Runners 14, RunnerServer 7, Sharding 101, ClusterCron 7, ClusterWorkflowEngine 64); Envelope, Message, Reply, MessageStorage, `Abandon` / `Dynamic`, `RunnerStorage.makeEncoded` and `ResourceRef` came with cluster storage (below). Review: `HashRing` checked against Effect under Node on 300 random rings (fractional weights, removals, non-BMP keys, `get` and `get_shards`), all equal; snowflake `date_time` and `DeliverAt.to_millis` use exact epoch milliseconds (`tests/test_epoch_millis_guard.py` bans float `timestamp() * 1000` / `fromtimestamp(ms / 1000)` and fixed the same truncation in `http.etag`); `shard_id.from_string` reads ASCII digits only.

**M10f cluster storage and Kubernetes (2026-10-30): partial.** `pyfect.cluster`: `envelope` (`Request`, `AckChunk`, `Interrupt`, `make_request`, `primary_key`, the `Partial*` schemas and `encode` / `decode` to Effect's JSON), `reply` (`WithExit`, `Chunk`, `ReplyWithContext` with `from_defect` / `interrupt`, `reply_schema(rpc, codec_for)`, `serialize`, `serialize_last_received`), `message` (incoming and outgoing requests and envelopes, `serialize_envelope`, `deserialize_local`), `message_storage` (`MessageStorageService` with reply handlers and transaction-deferred notifications, `make`, `make_encoded`, `EncodedStorage`, the memory driver `MemoryDriver` / `layer_memory`, `layer_noop`), `runner_storage.make_encoded` with `EncodedRunnerStorage`, `cluster_schema.Abandon` / `Dynamic`, the internal `_resource_ref`, `sql_runner_storage` (rows in `<prefix>_runners`; shard locks as PostgreSQL / MySQL advisory locks on one reserved connection rebuilt through `ResourceRef` when a statement fails, or leases in `<prefix>_locks` on SQLite, SQL Server and with `shard_lock_disable_advisory`; Effect's FNV-1a lock namespace, checked against Node; every lock operation under the effective refresh deadline; `make`, `make_storage`, `migrations`, `run_migrations`, `layer_migrations`, `layer_storage`, `layer_with`, `layer`), `sql_message_storage` (Effect's tables, migrations 0001-0003 and statements per dialect, SHA-256 `message_id` for keys over 255 characters with the SQLite legacy plaintext fallback, claims through `last_read`; `make`, `make_encoded_storage`, `layer*`), `k8s_types` (the 117 `TypedDict`s of `Pod`'s closure, generated from Effect's `K8sTypes.ts`) and `k8s_http_client` (`K8sHttpClient`, `layer` with the one-minute token cache, `make_get_pods`, `make_create_pod`, `Pod` / `PodStatus` with `is_ready` / `is_ready_or_initializing`). MySQL and SQL Server statements are ported but not run. Ports: `tests/oracle/test_envelope.py` (2; the two `SchemaBinary` cases wait for that codec), `test_message_storage.py` (18; `MessageStorageTest.ts`'s five memory cases are the same titles), `test_resource_ref.py` (8), `test_sql_runner_storage.py` (5 titles on SQLite and PostgreSQL, with and without advisory locks, plus the namespace check), `test_sql_message_storage.py` (24 titles on SQLite and PostgreSQL), `test_k8s_http_client.py` (2, plus `make_get_pods` / `make_create_pod` against a test `HttpClient`); `tests/type_checks.py::cluster_storage_types`.
