Omitted with reasons in the files: the SqlRunnerStorage cases that partition a connection through Effect's packet-dropping proxy, the DML-only-role cases (they revoke `CREATE` on the shared server's schema), the no-advisory deadlock stress test, the PostgreSQL reset-versus-claim lock-order race (needs `SET LOCAL` plans and a sleeping trigger), the SQL Server index-name case and the entity-layer case (needs `Sharding`). Pythonic decisions: envelopes and replies keep Effect's camelCase JSON with string snowflakes (through `pyfect._wire_codec`, moved from `ai._wire`), so stored rows match Effect's; payload classes give their primary key with a `primary_key()` method (Effect's `Rpc.make({ primaryKey })`); Option is `X | None`; SHA-256 comes from `hashlib` (no `Crypto` service); Python's exact integers make `SafeIntegers` unnecessary. Not ported yet: `Runners`, `RunnerServer`, `RunnerHealth`, `Sharding`, `Entity` and the rest listed under cluster core, so `SocketRunner`, `HttpRunner`, platform-node's `SocketRunner.test.ts` and a two-runner end-to-end test wait for them. Review fixes (each with a failing test first): `message_id` measures keys in UTF-16 code units as Effect's `length` does (`pyfect._js.js_length`; a key of 128 emoji is hashed, digest checked under Node); a stored envelope that does not decode ends its request with Effect's defect string (`SchemaError(... at ["envelope"]...)`, not an object); a defect while decoding a reply stays a defect (Effect catches only the failure); `ResourceRef.rebuild_unsafe(from_=...)` compares by identity (Effect's `!==`); `Pod.is_ready_or_initializing` tells a null transition time from a missing condition, and `make_create_pod` posts the spec with its own metadata winning, as Effect's spread does (both under Node); `tests/oracle/test_message_storage.py` pins `with_transaction`'s notification deferral (chunks at once, exits after the outermost commit, none after a rollback), which Effect tests only through `Sharding`.

**M10g Entity, Runners, RunnerHealth and RunnerServer (2026-10-31): done (Sharding followed; see the M10g completeness pass).** The typing of `research/typing/cluster_1_entity_client.py` is applied: `pyfect.rpc.ClientErrors[TE]` is a nominal base whose first `Rpc.__get__` / `StreamRpc.__get__` overloads give `UnaryMethod[P, S, E, TE]` / `StreamMethod[P, A, E, TE]` (`TE` defaults to `RpcClientError`), so an entity client's payload, success and error are inferred at the call site, with `ClusterClientError` (`MailboxFull | AlreadyProcessingMessage | PersistenceError | EntityNotAssignedToRunner`) in place of the transport error (pinned in `tests/type_checks.py`). `pyfect.cluster.entity`: `Entity` (a class of RPCs, `class Counter(Entity, type=..., annotations=..., rpc_annotations=...)`, with `annotate` / `annotate_merge` / `annotate_rpcs` / `annotate_rpcs_merge` deriving subclasses, `get_shard_group`, `get_shard_id`, `protocol`, `handlers()`, `to_layer`, `to_layer_effect`, `to_layer_queue`, `to_layer_queue_effect`), `EntityHandlers`, `Request` (with `last_sent_chunk`), `Replier`, `CurrentAddress`, `CurrentRunnerAddress`, `KeepAliveLatch`, `keep_alive` / `KeepAliveRpc`, `make_test_client`; `sharding` (the `Sharding` service interface and tag only); `entity_resource` (`EntityResource`, `CloseScope`, `make`, `make_k8s_pod`); `singleton.make`; `singleton_address.SingletonAddress`; `runners` (`Runners`, `RunnersTransport`, `make`, `make_noop` / `layer_noop`, the `Rpcs` protocol, `make_rpc_client`, `make_rpc_runners` / `layer_rpc` over an `RpcClientProtocol`); `runner_health` (`layer_noop`, `make_ping` / `layer_ping`, `make_k8s` / `layer_k8s`); `runner_server` (`layer_handlers`, `layer`). Oracle ports: `Entity.test.ts` all 12 cases (the `disableFatalDefects` loop parametrized), `Runners.test.ts` the 10 `Runners.makeRpc` / local-reply cases. Deferred: `Sharding.layer` (and `Sharding.getRegistrationEvents`), so `RunnerServer.layerWithClients` / `layerClientOnly`, all 9 `RunnerServer.test.ts` cases and the 2 RunnerServer cases of `Runners.test.ts` wait for it (`tests/test_runner_server.py` covers `layer_handlers` against a sharding that answers directly); `HttpRunner` and its 2 `Runners.test.ts` cases come with the socket and HTTP runners.

**M10g entity-runners review (2026-10-31).** Fixed (failing test first, `tests/test_entity_resource.py`): `entity_resource.make` made its shutdown flag when `make` was called, not per run, so one `make` effect run by two activations shared it, and after the first activation shut down the second never released its resource or its keep-alive on `close`. Effect's `fnUntraced` locals are per run, and so is the flag now. Root cause guard: `tests/test_run_time_state_guard.py` fails when a function makes a mutable cell outside the nested `@gen` / `@fn` body that uses it. Coverage added where mutations survived: a failing `to_layer_queue` behaviour fails the waiting requests, and `to_layer` / `handle` / `concurrency` checks (`tests/test_entity.py`); the stream reply that fails to serialize (the builder's notes said `tests/test_runner_server.py` pinned it, but only the unary case was there), and the persisted `Effect` / `Stream` paths of `layer_handlers`: answered through the reply handler, `EntityNotAssignedToRunner` passed on, `AlreadyProcessingMessage` waiting for the running request (`tests/test_runner_server.py`); a duplicate persisted request runs under its original id while its caller sees its own (`tests/test_runners.py`). Checked, not gaps: `rpc_annotations` precedence matches `RpcGroup.annotateRpcsMerge`, the test client's shard ids match `makeTestClient`, and entity clients run on both the sync and asyncio runtimes. Still uncovered until `Sharding.layer` is ported: `Runners.make` keeping persisted stream replies on the storage loop (`_can_reply_locally`). Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`): map chain 3.27M steps/s, generator steps 2.33M, fork + join 192k/s, no regression (no runtime core file changed).

**M10g Sharding, TestRunner, SingleRunner, EntityProxy (2026-10-31): done, with the Sharding deferrals of the M10g completeness pass.** `pyfect.cluster.sharding` is now a package: `_service` (the `ShardingService` protocol, with `get_registration_events`, and the `Sharding` tag), `layer` (Effect's `Sharding.layer`, with its own snowflake generator and `EntityReaper`; like Effect it exports only `Sharding` and `layer`), split by concern: `_state` (shard sets, latches, residency cap), `_acquisition` (lock acquisition, refresh and liveness probe, per-shard and forced releases, the shutdown handoff), `_storage_loop` (the batched storage inbox with the entity cap, pending `wait_until_read` notifications, claim resets, resumption of entities whose mailbox was full), `_routing` (`send_local`, `notify_local`, `send_outgoing` with retries and abandonment on shutdown, the registration deadline), `_clients` (entity clients over the RPC client core, acks and interrupts, `ClientAddress`), `_singletons` (`SingletonShard`, stopping singletons count as running) and `_runners_sync` (the `RunnerStorage` sync with a `HashRing` per group, and the runner health singleton); `_make` adds the finalizers in Effect's order. Internals: `_entity_manager` (a package: Effect's `internal/entityManager`), `_entity_reaper`, `_shard_lock.effective_interval` (now shared with `sql_runner_storage`), `_cluster_abandon`, `ResourceMap.remove_ignore`. Public: `sharding_registration_event`, `cluster_metrics`, `test_runner.layer`, `single_runner.layer(sharding_config=..., runner_storage_kind=...)`, `entity_proxy` (`to_rpc_group`, `proxy_rpcs`, `to_http_api_group`), `entity_proxy_server` (`layer_rpc_handlers`, `layer_http_api`). Oracle ports: `tests/oracle/test_sharding.py` 44 of `Sharding.test.ts`'s 100 titles (the others listed with the reason in the module docstring), `tests/oracle/test_entity_proxy.py` (1, plus the RPC handlers and the HTTP API end to end), `tests/oracle/test_single_runner.py` (`TestRunner.layer` and `SingleRunner.layer` over SQLite, end to end); `tests/type_checks.py::cluster_sharding_types`. Fixed (failing test first, `tests/test_rpc_client_sends_at_once.py`, and the oracle "durable stream while mailbox is full"): the RPC client started a call's send on the next scheduler turn, where Effect's `runForkWith` runs it at once, so requests forked together did not reach storage before the storage loop they woke read it. Root cause guard: `Fiber._fork`'s `start_immediately` has no default, so every fork states which of Effect's two it ports; the forks that port `runForkWith` (RPC server request fibers, `RunnerServer`'s notify, sharding's `run_fork` and singleton stops) now start at once. Review fixes: the RPC client told the server about an interrupt only when the calling fiber was interrupted (a callback canceller), where Effect's `onInterrupt` around the call sends it for any interrupted result: the client closing under a waiting call (so volatile requests were never interrupted on shutdown, the Sharding title "interrupts are sent for volatile messages on shutdown", now ported) and the server's own interrupted exit; the `Interrupt` now carries the interruptors (also from a stream's scope), and a decided call's sender is interrupted in a scheduled task, as Effect's (`tests/test_rpc_client_interrupt_on_any_interrupt.py`, expected values from Effect under Node). Ported in review: the mailbox and entity metric titles that run over `TestSharding` and the shards gauge title; `EntityProxy.toHttpApiGroup` and `EntityProxyServer.layerHttpApi`. Not ported yet: `RunnerServer.layerWithClients` / `layerClientOnly` and their tests, and the Sharding titles above.

**M10g ClusterCron and ClusterWorkflowEngine (2026-10-31): done.** `pyfect.cluster.cluster_cron` (`make(name=, cron=, execute=, shard_group=, calculate_next_run_from_previous=, skip_if_older_than=)`, `CronPayload`, `CurrentScheduledTime`; the layer needs only `Sharding` and what `execute` needs beyond the scheduled time and its scope) and `pyfect.cluster.cluster_workflow_engine` (`layer`, `make`; a package: `_entities` for the per-workflow entity with its `run` / `activity` / `deferred` / `resume` RPCs, the partial entity and the durable clock entity, `_engine` for Effect's engine). `workflow.result_schema(success=, error=)` is Effect's `Workflow.Result` schema. Ports: `tests/oracle/test_cluster_cron.py` (7 of 7) and `tests/oracle/test_cluster_workflow_engine.py` (69 tests for Effect's 64 titles, parametrized titles counted once per case; none omitted), with the workflows in `_cluster_workflows.py`, `_cluster_race_workflows.py` and `_cluster_parallel_workflows.py`; `tests/type_checks.py::cluster_cron_workflow_types`. Fixed (failing test first, `tests/test_message_storage_respond.py`): messages read through `MessageStorageService.unprocessed_messages` (`_by_id`) answered through the storage that decoded them, not the one that read them, so a storage wrapping `save_reply` (Effect's `{ ...storage, saveReply }`) never saw entity replies; `respond` is now bound to the reading storage, as Effect's `this`. A stale cron test (`fx.interrupt.ensuring`, an AttributeError dying in place of an interrupt) now calls `fx.interrupt()`. Review fixes (failing test first): the fix above rebound `respond` after decoding and left the defect of a malformed request on the decoding storage; `StorageBase.unprocessed_messages` (`_by_id`) now takes the reading storage as `responder`, which answers decoded requests and saves those defects, so the base keeps no back-reference (`tests/test_message_storage_respond.py`). A durable clock of whole milliseconds woke a millisecond late (2007 ms gave 2008, from the float product `2.007 * 1000`); `schedule_clock` uses `duration.to_millis`, and a guard bans a duration, ttl, timeout, idle, interval or delay times 1000 anywhere in `src` (`tests/test_duration_millis_guard.py`), which also moved the sharding registration timeout, the entity reaper's idle time and the persistence TTL to `to_millis` (`tests/test_cluster_workflow_engine_clock.py`). `result_schema` wrote `failed: false` on every `Suspended`, where Effect leaves the key out unless true; `_wire_codec.key(..., unset=False)` leaves it out (`tests/test_workflow_result_schema.py`, values from Effect under Node). Pinned: `skip_if_older_than` skips stale cron runs and the chain moves on, as Effect under Node (`tests/test_cluster_cron_skip.py`). Checked: all 64 titles are ported with Effect's assertions; the engine and cron suites also pass on the asyncio runtime; a mutation pass over the engine, cron and storage found the engine's ClientAbort and self-interrupt branches uncovered, as they are by Effect's own tests.

**M10g SocketRunner, HttpRunner and the platform-node cluster layers (2026-10-31): done.** `pyfect.cluster.runner_server` gains `layer_with_clients` and `layer_client_only` (Effect's `RunnerServer.layerWithClients` / `layerClientOnly`; the client-only layer clears `runner_address` and uses `RunnerHealth.layerNoop`). `pyfect.cluster.socket_runner` (`layer`, logging "Listening on: …" as Effect does, and `layer_client_only`) and `pyfect.cluster.http_runner` (`layer_client_protocol_http` / `_websocket` with `path` and `https`, their `_default` layers, `to_http_effect`, `to_http_effect_websocket`, `layer_client`, `layer_http_options`, `layer_websocket_options`, `layer_http`, `layer_websocket` and their `_client_only` layers), both exported from `pyfect.cluster`. Platform-node's `NodeClusterSocket` and `NodeClusterHttp` are `pyfect.cluster.cluster_socket` (`layer_client_protocol`, `layer_socket_server`, `layer`) and `pyfect.cluster.cluster_http` (`layer_http_server`, `layer_k8s_http_client`, `layer(transport="http" | "websocket")`), imported on their own; each `layer` has six overloads (client-only or not, by `storage="sql" | "local" | "byo"`), so what a layer still needs (`SqlClient`, the two storages, nothing) and whether it can fail to listen are typed (pinned in `tests/type_checks.py::cluster_runner_layer_types`). Pythonic decisions (the first until M15, which made `SchemaBinary` the default): the layers serialize with ndjson, since `SchemaBinary` (Effect's default) is not ported, so platform-node's "uses SchemaBinary by default" is ported against ndjson and "preserves an explicit binary selection" is omitted; Effect's nested `runnerHealthK8s: { namespace, labelSelector }` is flattened to `runner_health_k8s_namespace` / `runner_health_k8s_label_selector`; `sharding_config` is a function updating the `ShardingConfigService` read from the environment (Effect's `Partial<ShardingConfig>`); the Kubernetes client trusts the service account's CA through `ssl` when the file exists. Ports: `tests/oracle/test_socket_runner.py` (4 of `SocketRunner.test.ts`'s 5), the 2 HttpRunner path cases of `Runners.test.ts` in `test_runners.py`, and all 56 `cluster-integration` titles on a harness ported from Effect's `harness.ts` (`tests/oracle/_cluster_harness.py`: real socket runners in one process over localhost, per-cluster table prefixes, runner-storage freeze and kill, lock-connection faults through a wrapped `reserve`, directional socket cuts, a raw runner client), each on SQLite and on the throwaway PostgreSQL server where Effect uses PostgreSQL and MySQL: `test_cluster_integration_smoke.py` (Smoke, Transport, StreamDisconnect, Residency), `_persistence.py` (15), `_entity.py` (15), `_cron.py` (5), `_workflow.py` (13), `_locks.py` (4). On PostgreSQL only, with the reason in the file: Locks (SQLite has no reserved lock connection) and "retains frozen advisory locks until the session closes". The `MalformedMessage` checks read the `ErrorInstance` name of the `DecodedDefect` a defect becomes on the wire, where Effect reads `Cause.pretty`. `tests/test_cluster_socket_e2e.py` runs two runners from the public `cluster_socket.layer`, each with its own SQLite connection to one file: a message sent from one runner reaches an entity owned by the other, and a durable workflow suspended on a runner that is stopped resumes after it restarts, with its completed activity replayed, not rerun. No runtime fix was needed: every scenario passed against the existing cluster runtime. Still not ported: the 8 `RunnerServer.test.ts` titles in `tests/oracle/test_runner_server.py`, whose omission reason (`Sharding.layer`) no longer holds. Review fixes: `cluster_http.layer` and the HTTP and WebSocket runner layers were never built by a test; `tests/test_cluster_socket_e2e.py` now runs its two-runner routing and workflow-restart scenario over `cluster_socket.layer`, `cluster_http.layer(transport="http")` and `cluster_http.layer(transport="websocket")`. A mutation pass found the listen-address choice (`runner_listen_address` before `runner_address`) and the leading-slash fix-up of client paths unchecked, as they are by Effect's tests; `tests/test_cluster_listen_address.py` and `tests/test_http_runner_urls.py` pin them, with the `https` / `wss` schemes and the defect when no address is set. `cluster_http.layer_http_server` is typed with the services it provides (it was `Layer[Any, ...]`). The cron title "runs once per scheduled instant and continues after failure" failed about half the time when run first: while three runners start, the cron singleton can move and schedule a second chain, and the first run, on a moving shard, then runs after a later one (pyfect's shard handoff matches Effect's: a failed `acquire` is retried a second later). The test now records ticks once the assignments are stable, as Effect's next title already arms its executions; its assertions are Effect's. Fixed (failing test first, `tests/oracle/test_sql_runner_storage.py::test_an_interrupted_lock_operation_keeps_the_reserved_connection`): "persists queued work across restart and consumes it once" [pg] failed about one run in ten with shards no runner could acquire; `pg_locks` showed an idle pooled session holding a killed runner's 100 advisory locks. A runner shutting down interrupts its in-flight lock operation, `with_lock_operation_deadline` took the interrupt for a failure and rebuilt the reserved connection, and the closing storage scope then interrupted the old connection's release before its `pg_advisory_unlock_all`, so the session went back to the pool with the locks. Effect's `withLockOperationDeadline` has the same `onError`; pyfect now rebuilds only on a failure that is not interrupts only, the rule Effect's `useLockConn` already states, through one helper (`_rebuild_on_failure`) both paths share; a deadline still rebuilds (it fails with a timeout, not an interrupt). The title then passed 20 of 20 runs. Seen once in five runs of the new suites and not reproduced in eight more: `test_resumes_a_terminal_stream_on_the_replacement_owner[sqlite]` timing out with the surviving reply unsaved (`unprocessed=1`); a kill in that title takes the 15-second `entity_termination_timeout`, as Effect's drain does, so the title runs about 18 seconds per backend.

**M10g completeness pass (2026-10-31): done; M10g (the cluster runtime) is complete, with the deferrals below.** A title-by-title script check of the M10g exit-criteria files (`effect/test/cluster`'s `Sharding`, `ClusterWorkflowEngine`, `Runners`, `Entity`, `RunnerServer`, `ClusterCron` and `EntityProxy` tests, platform-node's `cluster/SocketRunner` and `cluster-integration/`) found `ClusterCron`, `ClusterWorkflowEngine`, `Entity`, `EntityProxy`, `SocketRunner` (but the SchemaBinary title, as M10c omits SchemaBinary) and all 56 `cluster-integration` titles ported, and three gaps, now closed: `RunnerServer.test.ts` (0 of 7 titles; all 8 cases now in `tests/oracle/test_runner_server.py` with helpers in `_runner_server_handlers.py`), the two serialization titles of `Runners.test.ts` (a unary reply and a stream reply that fail to serialize), and `Sharding.test.ts`, 44 of 98 titles before and 80 now: the WithTransaction and storage-less replay cases (`test_sharding_transactions.py`, over a `MemoryEncoded` that discards replies of a rolled-back transaction, Effect's `RollbackMemoryStorage`), entity-manager generations and mailbox size (`test_sharding_defect_recovery.py`), active teardowns (`test_sharding_active_teardowns.py`), claim releases (`test_sharding_claims.py`), the live-clock shutdown cases (`test_sharding_shutdown.py`) and the first nine shard lock failover cases (`test_sharding_failover.py` over a port of Effect's `FailoverStorage`, `_failover_storage.py`); the shared fixtures moved to `_sharding_fixtures.py`. Fixed (failing test first): `MalformedMessage.cause` and `PersistenceError.cause` were typed `object` while their schema (`S.Defect()`) takes only exceptions, so `PersistenceError(cause="down")` type-checked and raised `SchemaError` when built; three `ClusterWorkflowEngine` tests built one that way and so tested a defect instead of the typed failure. Both fields are now `BaseException` and `MalformedMessage.refail` takes an effect failing with one; `tests/type_checks.py::cluster_core_types` pins that a string or a number is rejected. `Cause.pretty` rendered a defect read back from JSON (`DecodedDefect`) as `pyfect.schema.DecodedDefect: <message>`; it now renders as Effect renders the decoded error, `<name>: <message>` under the name it was written with (`tests/test_cause_pretty.py`), so the `MalformedMessage` checks of Effect's tests read `Cause.pretty` as Effect does and the persistence integration tests lost their adaptation. Deferred, with the reason (since ported, see "M10g Sharding follow-up"): 18 `Sharding` titles: the two "replays a durable caller parked on a queued request when its target closes" variants (Effect's `RestartSharding` restart over shared, unread storage), "initializes singleton synchronization before startup fibers run" (it hooks `Scheduler.shouldYield`, which pyfect does not expose), and the remaining failover, shard handoff and singleton reassignment cases (two-runner handoff, singleton storage and forced-release fakes over `FailoverStorage`, a follow-up now that the fake is ported). Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, run alternately against the commit before this pass on the same machine at a load average of about 2.6): map chain 3.13–3.19M steps/s against 3.15–3.19M, generator steps 2.23–2.29M against 2.28M, fork + join 185–189k/s against 184–187k/s, so no regression (no runtime core file changed). The full check collects 12,711 tests and passes.

**M10h atom-core (2026-10-31): done.** `pyfect.reactivity.async_result`, `pyfect.reactivity.atom` (a package: types, constructors, effect and stream reads, `fn` / `fn_sync` / `pull`, runtimes and runtime factories, `subscription_ref`, `swr`, `optimistic` / `optimistic_fn`, `serializable`, `kvs`, combinators, `family`, the registry effects `get` / `set` / `update` / `modify` / `refresh` / `get_result` / `mount` / `to_stream(_result)`), `pyfect.reactivity.atom_registry` (the registry: nodes, batches, idle TTL buckets, initial values with `skip_initial_run`, `layer` / `layer_options`) and `pyfect.reactivity.atom_ref`. Tests: of `Atom.test.ts`'s 141 distinct titles 139 are ported (count corrected by the completeness pass; it was given as 112) (`tests/oracle/test_atom.py`, `test_atom_combinators.py`, `test_atom_swr.py`, `test_atom_optimistic.py`, `test_atom_reactivity.py`; the two `searchParam` titles are browser-only), all 8 of `AsyncResult.test.ts` (`test_async_result.py`) and the one `AtomRef.test.ts` title (`test_atom_ref.py`). Tests run on fake timers (`tests/oracle/_atom_timers.py`). Fixed (failing test first): `Reactivity` ran a handler that an earlier handler of the same invalidation had unregistered, and ran batched handlers in hash order; it now follows JavaScript `Set` iteration, and `tests/test_reactivity_order.py` bans `set` in `pyfect.reactivity` (a `set` of fibers had also reordered concurrent `fn` calls). Not in this cluster: `Hydration`, `AtomRpc`, `AtomHttpApi` and `AsyncResult.Schema` (ported in M10h atom-integrations, below). Typing probe: `research/typing/atom_1_spelling.py`; type pins in `tests/type_checks.py` (`atom_types`). Review fixes (failing test first, expected values printed by Effect under Node, `tests/oracle/test_atom_pins.py` and `tests/test_reactivity.py`): an atom's build now runs what its fiber yields before returning, as Effect's dispatcher flush does (a long synchronous effect or one calling `yield_now` was left waiting); `family` keys by `Equal` (`True` and `1` shared an atom, NaN keys leaked, lists were refused); `Reactivity` handlers registered during an invalidation run in it, as with a JavaScript `Set`. Listener and handler loops go through `reactivity/_live.py`'s `live_keys`, and `tests/test_reactivity_order.py` bans snapshot iteration of them.

**M10h atom-integrations (2026-10-31): done.** `pyfect.reactivity.hydration` (`dehydrate(registry, encode_initial_as=)`, `hydrate`, `to_values`, `DehydratedAtom` with `to_json` / `from_json` in Effect's wire shape), `pyfect.reactivity.atom_rpc` (`make(tag, group=, protocol=, make_effect=, span_prefix=, span_attributes=, generate_request_id=, disable_tracing=, runtime=)` giving an `AtomRpcClient` with `runtime`, `query` (an `AsyncResult` atom, or a pull atom for a streaming RPC; `headers`, `reactivity_keys`, `time_to_live`, `serialization_key`) and `mutation` (a function atom taking a `MutationRequest`)), `pyfect.reactivity.atom_http_api` (`make(tag, api=, http_client=, transform_client=, transform_response=, base_url=, runtime=)` giving an `AtomHttpApiClient` with `query` / `mutation` in the three response modes, `sse_options` part of a query's key; `HttpClientError` and `SchemaError` become defects as in Effect) and `async_result.Schema` (`AsyncResultSchema`, Effect's `AsyncResult.Schema` with its JSON codec). `atom_rpc` and `atom_http_api` load on first use (`pyfect.reactivity.__getattr__`), so `sql`, a user of `Reactivity`, does not import rpc and http_api. Tests: all 8 titles (10 cases) of `Hydration.test.ts` (`tests/oracle/test_hydration.py`), all 5 titles (6 cases) of `AtomRpc.test.ts` (`test_atom_rpc.py`; the type assertions of "includes client middleware errors in atom failure types" are pins in `tests/type_checks.py`, `atom_rpc_types`) and all 6 titles (7 cases) of `AtomHttpApi.test.ts` (`test_atom_http_api.py`); type pins `atom_rpc_types` and `atom_http_api_types`. `tests/test_atom_integrations.py` covers what Effect's tests leave out (reactivity keys of queries and mutations, headers, which atoms are serializable, transport and schema errors as defects, middleware error schemas, the streamed hydration fallbacks), checked under Node; an empty `serialization_key` makes no atom serializable, as Effect's falsy check. A node keeps a hydrated `Initial` through `release_hydrated_initial` (`atom_registry/_node.py`) rather than the hydration module reaching into its fields. Out of scope: `packages/atom`'s `react`, `solid` and `vue` packages, JavaScript UI-framework adapters (hooks, providers, signals) with no Python counterpart. Known typing gap: pyright widens a covariant type parameter across arguments, so `query(rpc, payload)` does not reject a payload of the wrong type (the RPC's success and errors are still inferred); the client's `UnaryMethod` does check it.

**M10g Sharding follow-up (2026-10-31): done; every title of `Sharding.test.ts` is ported.** The 20 cases the M10g completeness pass left out (it counted 18: the two singleton cancellation titles were missed by its check, and the parametrized title counts twice) are ported: `tests/oracle/test_sharding_forced_release.py` (the last 7 shard lock failover titles: the graceful timeout and release-duration metric across a failed release, a client interrupt and stream acknowledgements reaching a request still draining on the lock holder, a remote request held until the shard lock is acquired, and the three forced-release cases), `test_sharding_handoff.py` (the 7 shard handoff titles with Effect's `buildSharding`, gated teardowns and the releasing-shards gauge read from the runner's registry; the 2 singleton cancellation titles over Effect's singleton reassignment storage; and the persisted discard send to a shard awaiting its lock), `test_sharding_restart.py` (both `preemptiveShutdown` variants of "replays a durable caller parked on a queued request when its target closes", over Effect's `makeSharedMemoryStorage`, `unreadStorage` and `RestartSharding`) and `test_sharding_startup.py` ("initializes singleton synchronization before startup fibers run": Effect pauses the constructing fiber from a custom `shouldYield` every four operations, so the fibers it forked run to their first suspension each time; the port gives the build a `MaxOpsBeforeYield` of 4 and a `CurrentScheduler` wrapper that holds the build fiber's resumes until every other task of the build has run, which gives the same order, and asserts no cause is logged through startup and shutdown). `FailoverState.release_failures` is a `float` so a test can make every release fail (`math.inf`, Effect's `Infinity`). No runtime fix was needed: every case passed against the existing cluster runtime. Mutation checks: altering an expected warning time, the `releaseAll` release count or the finalizer interrupt count fails the handoff and singleton cases. Review fix: the builder's port swept `MaxOpsBeforeYield` budgets 1 to 40 instead, and removing the acquisition loop's `singletons_ready` wait did not fail it: the forked fibers inherit the budget, so they advanced one slice per slice of the build fiber and never reached the singletons before they existed. With the scheduler wrapper, removing that wait fails the case (`AssertionError: the loops wait on singletons_ready`, logged by the acquisition loop). Removing the refresh loop's wait still passes, as in this scenario the healthy refresh path never reaches the singletons.

**M10h completeness pass (2026-10-31): done; M10h (the Atom family of `effect/reactivity`) is complete.** A title-by-title script check of the exit-criteria files (`effect/test/reactivity`'s `Atom`, `AsyncResult`, `AtomRpc`, `AtomHttpApi` and `Hydration` tests, and `effect/test/AtomRef.test.ts`: 169 distinct titles) finds 167 ported, each named in a test docstring, and 2 omitted with a reason (the `searchParam` titles, browser-only). The earlier status paragraphs gave `Atom.test.ts` 112 titles; it has 141, and all but the two `searchParam` ones are ported. An export check of the seven Effect modules against the pyfect modules finds every function and class ported but `Atom.searchParam`; `AsyncResult.getOrThrow` is `get_or_raise`, `AtomRpc.Service` / `AtomHttpApi.Service` are `make`, the `TypeId`s and `AtomRegistry`'s `BatchPhase` / `batchState` internals have no counterpart, and eight kind-preserving combinators are methods only (decisions table). Cleanup: `Atom`'s default equality used its own copy of `Object.is`; it now uses the shared `pyfect._js.same_value` (same results but for `complex` values, which JavaScript has no counterpart for). Deferred, with reasons: `Atom.searchParam` (no browser URL in Python); the `packages/atom` React, Solid and Vue bindings (JavaScript UI adapters); a typed payload check in `AtomRpcClient.query` (pyright widens the covariant payload parameter); a schema for `RpcClientError` (its socket, worker and HTTP client reasons have none yet, so such a failure is left out of `dehydrate`); a test that fails without the Sharding refresh loop's `singletons_ready` wait (the healthy refresh path never reaches the singletons in Effect's scenario). Full check: ruff format, ruff check, pyright (0 errors), pytest 12949 passed, 8 skipped (17 minutes; the earlier reports of a stall near 9% were the slow PostgreSQL cluster-integration tests, which finish). Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, run alternately against the commit before M10h at a load average of about 3.7): map chain 2.99–3.07M steps/s against 2.96–3.00M, generator steps 2.14–2.16M against 2.17–2.18M, fork + join 171–177k/s against 169–176k/s; no regression (M10h changed no file outside `pyfect.reactivity`).

**M10f completeness pass (2026-10-30): done; M10f is complete for graphql, eventlog, devtools and `SqlModel`, and partial for cluster, with the deferrals below.** A title-by-title script check of every Effect test file for the M10f modules (`effect/test/cluster`, `eventlog`, `graphql`, `devtools`, `schema/VariantSchema.test.ts`; `sql-sqlite-node`'s `SqlModelServices`, `SqlEventJournal` and `SqlEventLogServer*`; `sql-mysql2`'s `Model.integration`; `sql-pg`'s `SqlEventJournal` and `SqlEventLogServer*`; `sql-pglite`'s `SqlRunnerStorage`; platform-node's `GraphQLProtocol`, `cluster/SocketRunner`, `cluster/SqlRunnerStorage`, `cluster/SqlMessageStorage` and `cluster-integration/`: 650 titles) finds 371 ported, 21 omitted with a reason in their oracle module's docstring (the 10 `EventJournalIndexedDb` titles, browser-only; 9 `SqlRunnerStorage` titles that partition a connection through Effect's packet-dropping proxy or revoke `CREATE` on the shared server; the PGlite title, whose reason this pass added; one `GraphQLProtocol` title parametrized by status, ported as two tests) and 258 not ported, all in the cluster runtime deferred below. Fixed (failing test first, `tests/test_eventlog_memory_storage.py`): the encrypted and unencrypted memory server storages kept a pubsub per store in a plain `dict` and never shut it down, so a `changes` stream outlived its storage and the pubsubs were never freed; they now sit in an `RcMap` with Effect's one-minute idle time and `PubSub.shutdown` on release, so `make_storage_memory` needs a `Scope`, as Effect's does. Checked, not a gap: Effect's `decodeMessages` decoding `lastSentReply` (a review note) checks nothing, since `Reply.Encoded` is `OpaqueHole` and `Schema.Option` of it accepts any value. Found unplanned: the Atom family of `effect/reactivity` (`Atom`, `AtomRegistry`, `AtomRef`, `AsyncResult`, `Hydration`, `AtomRpc`, `AtomHttpApi`; 6.5k source lines, about 170 titles in `effect/test/reactivity` and `AtomRef.test.ts`). Wave 9 lists reactivity, but only `Reactivity` (M10b) was ever planned or ported. Deferred, with the reason: the cluster runtime, as milestone M10g: `Entity`, `EntityResource`, `Sharding`, `Runners`, `RunnerHealth`, `RunnerServer`, `TestRunner`, `SingleRunner`, `SocketRunner`, `HttpRunner`, `Singleton`, `SingletonAddress`, `ShardingRegistrationEvent`, `ClusterMetrics`, `ClusterCron`, `EntityProxy`, `EntityProxyServer`, `ClusterWorkflowEngine`, the internal `entityManager` / `entityReaper` / `entityActivation` and `ResourceMap.removeIgnore` (8.4k Effect source lines; titles: `Sharding` 100, `ClusterWorkflowEngine` 60, `Runners` 14, `Entity` 8, `RunnerServer` 7, `ClusterCron` 7, `EntityProxy` 1, platform-node `SocketRunner` 5, `cluster-integration` 56). It is one dependency chain: entity clients need the entity-client typing (`research/typing/cluster_1_entity_client.py`) applied to `pyfect.rpc` first, `Entity.toLayer` registers into `Sharding`, `Sharding` needs `Runners` and `RunnerHealth`, and `ClusterCron`, `EntityProxy` and the workflow engine run on `Sharding`; porting `Entity` alone would fix its API before the service it registers into exists. The Atom family, as its own milestone after M10g: a client-side state library whose Python API (registry, subscriptions, hydration) needs its own design pass. Tools, not library modules: `tools/graphql-generator` (a TypeScript code generator) and `tools/eventlog-server` (a private Docker image of the encrypted server, which `event_log_server_encrypted` with `sql_event_log_server_encrypted` already gives). Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, run alternately against the commit before M10f on the same machine at a load average of 7 to 9): map chain 3.06–3.07M steps/s against 3.02–3.13M, generator steps 2.20–2.21M against 2.17–2.21M, fork + join 160–162k/s against 159–164k/s, so no regression (no runtime core file changed in M10f). The full check collects 11,783 tests (2 added in this pass) and passes.

### M11 — Export coverage of the core modules (L; after M10)

Every value export of the core Effect modules is listed by `scripts/api_coverage.py <cluster>` and labelled ported, renamed, a §4 decision or missing; `docs/coverage/<cluster>.md` holds each table and `tests/test_api_coverage.py` fails when a table is stale or a cluster that is complete gains a missing export.

- **Clusters:** core-a (Effect, Effectable, Exit, Cause, Result, Option, Filter, Predicate, Function), core-b (Scope, Layer, Context, ManagedRuntime, Fiber, References, Scheduler, Semaphore, Latch, Runtime), config-data (Config, ConfigProvider, Redacted, Data, Brand, Equal, Hash, Equivalence, Order), collections (the pure modules and Crypto), schema-leftovers (the Schema family), core-gaps (STM's Tx collections on the Equal/Hash HAMT, `testing.prop`, the dogfood leftovers). Each cluster's work is under its own "Done in the … pass, M11" notes above.
- **Exit criteria:** each cluster's Effect test files ported title by title (`Effect.test.ts`, `Effectable.test.ts`, `Cause.test.ts`, `Exit.test.ts`, `Result.test.ts`, `Option.test.ts`, `Scope.test.ts`, `Layer.test.ts`, `Context.test.ts`, `ManagedRuntime.test.ts`, `Fiber.test.ts`, `Scheduler.test.ts`, `Semaphore.test.ts`, `Latch.test.ts`, `Config.test.ts`, `ConfigProvider.test.ts`, `Redacted.test.ts`, `Data.test.ts`, `Brand.test.ts`, `Equal.test.ts`, `Hash.test.ts`, `Crypto.test.ts`, `HashMap.test.ts`, `HashSet.test.ts`, `MutableHashMap.test.ts`, `MutableHashSet.test.ts`, `Trie.test.ts`, `BigDecimal.test.ts`, `SemVer.test.ts`, `SemVerRange.test.ts`, `TxHashMap.test.ts`, `TxHashSet.test.ts`, `schema/TemplateLiteral.test.ts`, `testing/TestCrypto.test.ts`, `testing/TestSchema.test.ts`), and no export missing in core-a, core-b and config-data.

**M11b SchemaRepresentation (2026-11-03): done but for `toCodeDocument` and the JSON Schema importer.** `pyfect.schema.representation` (`R`): the document model as frozen dataclasses (`Document`, `MultiDocument`, every node, `Filter` / `FilterGroup` with their `representation` identities); `to_representation(s)` on the shared reference allocator (`References`, the `~identifier` fallback, `<Identifier>Encoded` names); `from_representation(s)` with declaration, filter and filter-group revivers (`make_reviver_*`, payloads decoded through `S.to_codec_json`, errors as `RepresentationError` with Effect's document paths) and `BUILTIN_REVIVERS` for every check and declaration pyfect has; `to_json` / `from_json` (and the multi-document forms) in Effect's JSON form; `to_json_schema_(multi_)document` compiling a document through the `S.to_json_schema` compiler; `make_code`. Declarations now carry Effect's `representation` ids (`Option`, `Result`, `Cause`, `Exit`, `Redacted`, `Map` as `ReadonlyMap`, `FrozenSet` as `ReadonlySet`, `Bytes` as `Uint8Array`, the date-time and value schemas, `ErrorInstance` as `Error`). Fixed on the way: a `Class`'s type side was a new node on each `to_type` (now memoized, so it is one definition); cardinality checks (`is_min_length(1.8)`, ...) floor their bound and reject a non-finite one as Effect's `normalizeCardinality`; a bare document `Number` writes its non-finite strings `NaN`-first as Effect's representation compiler; an index-signature pattern reuses its compiled definition (its callback runs once). Tests: `tests/oracle/test_schema_representation_*.py`, with each omitted title's reason in the module docstring. Not ported: `toCodeDocument` (TypeScript source) and `fromJsonSchemaDocument` / `fromJsonSchemaMultiDocument`, with their test files (toCodeDocument, fromJsonSchema*, and the `toJsonSchemaDocument` titles that import), and `stringTreeRoundtrip.test.ts` (it exercises `S.to_codec_string_tree` on bigint and symbol tags). Also not ported: the `toJsonSchemaMultiDocument` title "deduplicates fallback definitions that share a recursive dependency" (it needs `S.to_codec_json` memoized across calls, as Effect's is; pyfect's memoizes within one derivation, and AST nodes hold no weak references). One difference by design: `to_json_schema_document` writes a built-in check's fragment from its `representation` id, so a document read back with `from_json` keeps its `minLength`, `uniqueItems`, ... (Effect only runs live `toJsonSchema` callbacks, which JSON cannot carry, and drops them). Review fixes: `S.Redacted` gained `disallow_json_encode` (Effect's `disallowJsonEncode`: the JSON codec fails with `Cannot serialize Redacted` and the payload carries it); the `Redacted` and `Error` revivers reject payloads Effect rejects (`{}`, unknown keys, a `false` flag) instead of ignoring them; a key or tuple element filled by a decoding default is optional on the encoded side in the representation and in JSON Schema (`minItems`), through `PropertySignature.optional_when_encoded` / `Element.optional_when_encoded`, which the JSON Schema compiler and `to_representation` now share (`tests/test_schema_representation_payloads.py`, `tests/test_schema_encoded_optional.py`, expected values printed by Effect under Node).

**M11b schema-rest (2026-11-03): done; 0 exports of Schema, SchemaAST, SchemaGetter, SchemaTransformation, SchemaIssue and SchemaParser are missing.** Ported: `S.with_constructor_default(effect)` / `S.tag` (`ConstructorDefault` is invariant, so a default of another type is a type error; the default sits on the AST node as `constructor_default`, and every `make` / `make_option` / `make_effect` fills missing struct keys, tuple elements and class fields with it, running effectful defaults concurrently under `concurrency=`; `TaggedStruct` and `tag_default_omit` now use it, and the variant structs' own `make` overrides are gone). Also ported: `S.encode_keys`, `S.extend_to`, `S.Tree`, `S.link`; `T.duration_from_string` / `_nanos` / `_millis` and `T.time_zone_offset_from_number` (the schemas now build on them); `G.make_tree_record` / `G.collect_bracket_path_entries`; the issue formatters `make_formatter_default`, `make_formatter_standard_schema_v1` and `default_check_hook`, plus `has_input`; the SchemaAST constructors `struct`, `tuple_`, `union`, `struct_with_rest`, `tuple_with_rest` and the `resolve_*` / `is_optional` readers. The HTTP schemas went into `pyfect.http.schema` (`Headers`, `Cookie`, `Cookies`, `RecordFromCookies`, `UrlParams`, `RecordFromUrlParams`, `JsonFromUrlParamsField`, `RetryAfter`, `RetryAfterFromString`, `DateTimeUtcFromHttpDate`). Renamed: `catchDecodingWithContext` is `catch_decoding`, `makeTransformation` is `Transformation`, `composeTransformation` is `Transformation.compose`, `defaultLeafHook` is `leaf_message`, `defaultFormatter` is `format_issue`, and `toRepresentation` is `representation.to_representation`. The rest are §4 rows: Effect's `@internal` helpers, `SchemaAST.Context`, `Date`, the web globals, the `toIso` family and the other codec families. One behavior change: `ast.tuple_with_rest` now rejects a tuple with an encoding, as `struct_with_rest` already did. Tests: `tests/oracle/test_schema_constructor_default.py`, `test_schema_struct_keys.py`, `test_schema_issue_report_input.py`, the new titles in `test_schema_getter.py`, `test_test_schema.py`, `test_schema_tagged.py`, `test_headers.py`, `test_cookies.py`, `test_url_params.py` and `test_to_codec_json.py`, `tests/test_schema_ast_constructors.py`, and the `schema_rest_types` pins in `tests/type_checks.py`. Review (skeptical pass), each with a failing test first: a union's `make` ignored constructor mode, so `S.Union(tagged A, tagged B).make({"a": "x"})` failed where Effect fills the tag (a missing sentinel key now selects every member that has it under a constructor, Effect's `getCandidateIndex(input, true)`; its "constructor mode" title is ported); `encode_keys` accepted a mapping key that is not a field and then renamed an encoded field away when decoding (now a `ValueError`, Effect's type allows only the fields); `extend_to` accepted `derive` keys other than the new keys, failing later with a missing key (now a `ValueError`); `verify_round_trip_effect` is ported (below).

**M11b completeness pass (2026-11-03): done; M11b is complete but for the two `SchemaRepresentation` generators below.** M11b took the M11 deferrals: Graph (`pyfect.graph`, all of `Graph.test.ts` and `Pathfinding.test.ts`), SchemaRepresentation, the rest of the Schema family exports, and `TestSchema.verifyRoundTripEffect`. A title-by-title script check of the exit-criteria files (`Graph.test.ts`, `Pathfinding.test.ts`, `schema/Graph.test.ts`, `testing/TestSchema.test.ts` and every file of `schema/representation/`) finds every title in a test docstring, ported under the expanded name of a template title, or omitted with a reason in its module's docstring, except the files of the two unported generators (`toCodeDocument*.test.ts`, `fromJsonSchema*.test.ts`, about 400 titles) and `stringTreeRoundtrip.test.ts` (bigint and symbol tags). One omission had no reason; it now has one (toRepresentation's "retains only the media type when a reviver changes the content": `S.from_json_string` takes no `JSON.parse` reviver). Added, failing tests first: `S.Graph(kind, node, edge)` (Effect's `Schema.Graph`, `src/pyfect/schema/_graph.py`): a declaration of immutable graphs of one kind that parses its payloads through the snapshot form, so an issue is at `["nodes"][i]["data"]`; JSON writes the snapshot, and decoding reports an index that does not increase, or an endpoint that names no node, at its path, before the graph is built (Effect's messages and JSON Schema, checked under Node); Effect's arbitrary (`None` or nodes plus wrapping `(source, target, data)` edges) and equivalence (endpoints in either order for an undirected edge); and `R.graph_reviver` (Effect's `GraphReviver`, in `BUILTIN_REVIVERS`). Tests: `tests/oracle/test_schema_graph.py` (all 14 titles of `schema/Graph.test.ts`), "revives Graph" in `test_schema_representation_revivers.py`, `tests/test_schema_graph_declaration.py`, and `tests/type_checks.py::check_schema_graph` (the kind is a literal type; a graph of the other kind is a type error). Coverage (export tables): schema 781 exports, 3 missing (99%, the two generators' `toCodeDocument`, `fromJsonSchemaDocument`, `fromJsonSchemaMultiDocument`); `Schema.Graph` and `GraphReviver` moved from §4 to ported; collections 985, 0 missing; core-a, core-b and config-data unchanged at 0 missing. Section 1: Schema family 90% → 91%; the weighted total stays ≈90%. Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, run alternately against the M11 completeness commit in a worktree on one machine, today slower than at M11): map chain 3.07–3.09M vs 3.01–3.08M steps/s, generator steps 2.17–2.20M vs 2.17–2.21M, fork + join 172–175k vs 172–174k per second: within noise (M11b touched no runtime file). The full suite collects 14,845 tests (14,196 after the M11 completeness pass); in the final run one failed, `test_cluster_integration_entity.py::test_transfers_frozen_row_locks_after_expiry[pg]`, the flake the schema-rest review recorded (it times out in about 1 run in 3 at earlier commits too, and its file passes on its own). Fixed since (dogfood cluster-flake): the new owner can take the frozen runner's lock up to a `refresh_assignments_interval` before the client stops routing to the frozen runner, which still serves requests, so the test's assertion failed (Effect's test has the same race); the failure then showed as a 60 s hang because the frozen runner's storage waits uninterruptibly while its scope closes. The harness now kills a runner still frozen at teardown (`tests/oracle/test_cluster_harness.py`), and the test waits for `wait_for_client_sync` before it sends. Deferred: `toCodeDocument` (Effect generates TypeScript source; a Python port would generate Python, so none of its 150 expected outputs carry over, and its design is its own task) and `fromJsonSchemaDocument` / `fromJsonSchemaMultiDocument` (a 1,339-line JSON Schema importer with about 257 titles; too large for a completeness pass, it needs its own builder pass).

**M11 completeness pass (2026-11-03): done; M11 is complete, with the deferrals below.** A title-by-title script check of the exit-criteria files (1,503 distinct titles) finds 1,428 in a test docstring; the other 75 are omitted with a reason in their module's docstring (most by group: JavaScript symbols, prototypes and `Error` objects in `Equal.test.ts`, the `TypeId` / `is*` guards and JSON forms in `Cause.test.ts`) or ported under the expanded name of a template title. Before the pass about 85 had neither, all in `Effect.test.ts`, `Effectable.test.ts`, `Cause.test.ts` and `testing/`. Added:

- `Effect.test.ts` long tail (`tests/oracle/test_effect_long_tail.py`, 33 tests): the finalization and acquireUseRelease cases, the interruption and uninterruptibleMask block, the forEach worker cases (a mapper that raises while workers run, a sibling's cleanup defect), raceAll's election (a non-exception typed error is elected as `UnhandledError`, §4 `Cause.squash`), 100,000 nested lazy maps, and the generator protocol. A defect whose `__hash__` raises was checked under Node: one hash call, then `[Die(defect), Interrupt]`, as pyfect gives.
- `pyfect.effectable` (`fx.effectable.Class[A, E, R]`, Effect's `Effectable.Class`: an instance runs `as_effect()` on every run; `Mixin` is multiple inheritance and `Prototype` a subclass, one new §4 row). `Effectable.test.ts` is ported (`tests/oracle/test_effectable.py`), the two `Effectable.Class` titles of `Effect.test.ts` with it; core-a now counts 515 exports, none missing.
- Bug fixed, failing test first (`tests/oracle/test_context_vars_resume.py`, Effect's `AsyncLocalStorage` titles with `contextvars`): a fiber that parked resumed in the `contextvars` context of whatever woke it (a `Deferred.succeed` from another task, a released pool item), because resumes run nested in the waker. Effect's fiber captures the async context when it yields and resumes in it. `Fiber._run` now keeps the context the fiber parked or paused in and runs its next run inside it; the first run stays in the starter's context, as in Effect. Root cause: no test pinned which context a resumed fiber sees, so making resumes synchronous moved it silently; the new oracle file pins it for a deferred, a pool and the fiber's own `set`.
- `testing.TestCrypto` (`make(seed)`, `layer(seed)`: a seeded `Crypto` that restarts on each build and keeps the digests of the `Crypto` it is built over; `tests/oracle/test_test_crypto.py`).
- `SchemaCheck.succeed_effect` / `fail_effect` (Effect's `succeedEffect` / `failEffect`: lazy, run with the calling fiber's services, a mismatch is an `AssertionError` defect, other causes keep their reasons with typed errors made defects) and `SchemaAsserts.verify_round_trip(runs=, seed=)` over Hypothesis (`tests/oracle/test_test_schema.py`, 34 tests).
- The Cause `IllegalArgumentError` / `ExceededCapacityError` titles (the docstring called them not ported; both classes existed), and BigDecimal's property suite, which Effect skips for speed, ported with `testing.prop` (its `toExponential` reference is checked against values printed by Node).

Coverage (export tables): core-a 515 exports, 0 missing; core-b 140, 0 missing; config-data 121, 0 missing; collections 985, 93 missing (Graph), 90%; schema 781, 227 missing, 70%. Section 1: core A 93% → 96%, Testing support 95% → 97%, runtime core ≈88% → ≈89%, weighted total stays ≈90%.

Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, best of 5, run alternately against HEAD in a worktree on one machine): map chain 3.17–3.21M vs 3.17–3.18M steps/s, generator steps 2.31–2.36M vs 2.32M, fork + join 180–185k vs 179–187k per second: within noise of each other and of the M11 cluster reviews (3.04–3.25M, 2.19–2.26M, 177–189k). Keeping the parked context costs one `copy_context()` per park.

The full suite collects 14,196 tests (13,397 after the config-data pass).

Deferred from M11, with the reason:

- **Graph** (`Graph.ts`, 93 exports; `Graph.test.ts` and `Pathfinding.test.ts`): too large for the coverage passes. Now ported: `pyfect.graph` (`fx.graph`), split by concern (`_store` / `_mutate` construction and mutation, `_compact` the cached index view Effect calls CSR, `_traverse` / `_walker` walkers, `_shortest` / `_all_pairs` / `_enumerate` paths, `_cycles`, `_components`, `_lowlink`, `_matching`, `_flow`, `_forest`, `_combine`, `_snapshot`, `_render` for GraphViz and Mermaid). `Graph` (immutable, `==` / `hash` by structure) and `MutableGraph` (a handle, identity) share the read methods of `GraphView`; every Effect function is a method, config objects are keyword arguments. Indexes, iteration order, live `nodes` / `edges` / `externals` iterators, per-iterator snapshots, the transformation guard and every error message follow Effect. Kind-specific methods take a kind-typed `self`, so pyright rejects `degree` on a directed graph; the runtime check stays. `Graph`, `Edge`, `Snapshot` and the results are covariant (the records use explicit covariant TypeVars: a frozen dataclass's `__replace__` makes PEP 695 inference invariant). All 144 tests of `Graph.test.ts` (143 titles, one template run for integer and floating-point weights) are in `tests/oracle/test_graph.py`, the 3 terrain tests of `Pathfinding.test.ts` in `test_pathfinding.py`; `tests/type_checks.py::check_graph` pins the types. Found while porting: the compact view built its rows from the live store on first use, so a projection read after a mutation described the new graph; the view now copies the nodes, edges and adjacency lists it is made from and keeps no store reference (`tests/test_graph_compact_snapshot.py`). Review: a differential run against Effect under Node (thousands of random directed and undirected graphs built with every mutation, every query, walker, path, flow, component, set-operation and render function compared, errors included) found no mismatch; fixed, failing test first: `mutate` / `make` / `directed` / `undirected` typed their callback as returning `None`, so pyright rejected `g.mutate(lambda m: m.add_node(x))` (Effect's callback is `=> void`; now `object`, `tests/type_checks.py::check_graph`), and `from_snapshot` handed integral float indexes (`1.0`, which it accepts as JavaScript does) back as floats (now `int`s, `tests/test_graph_snapshot_boundary.py`); `GraphError` named its private module (`pyfect.graph._errors`), which `tests/test_public_names.py` caught once the full suite ran (now `publish(globals())`); `tests/test_graph_store_invariants.py` checks adjacency against the live edges after random mutations (the oracle titles missed a stale undirected adjacency entry). Collections coverage: 985 exports, 0 missing.
- **Schema exports** (closed in M15; 3 missing in `docs/coverage/schema.md` after the M11b completeness pass, all `SchemaRepresentation`): `toCodeDocument` (TypeScript source generation; a Python equivalent writes Python, its tests do not carry over) and `fromJsonSchemaDocument` / `fromJsonSchemaMultiDocument` (a JSON Schema importer, 1,300 lines in Effect). `Schema.Graph` is ported (M11b completeness pass). The not-ported Schema exports (the `toIso` family, `toDifferJsonPatch` / `toEncoderXml` / `toCodecGoJson`, the web globals) are §4 rows with their reasons.
- **`TestSchema.verifyRoundTripEffect`**: done in the schema-rest review as `SchemaAsserts.verify_round_trip_effect(runs=, seed=)`, an effect needing both directions' services. It reads the running fiber's context, then Hypothesis draws and shrinks synchronously and runs each value's round trip to its end with `run_sync_exit(..., context=ctx)` (so no `from_async` getters, and no interruption midway). A mismatch is an `AssertionError` defect with Hypothesis's shrunk-input note; any other cause ends the check at once and fails the effect as it is. `verify_round_trip` is `run_sync` of it. Four of the five titles are ported; "roundTrip propagates interruption and runs finalizers" is omitted for that reason.
- **`TestSchema.Asserts.ast.fields.equals`**: its titles test JavaScript symbol keys and own-key rules; a struct's fields are a `dict`.

### M12 — The AI and MCP deferrals of M10e (after M11b)

M12 takes what M10e deferred in the AI packages: `@effect/ai-openai`, the structured-output codecs, `Decision` / `DecisionModel` and the providers that implement it, `@effect/ai-openrouter`, and the MCP server features.

- **Clusters:** ai-openai, structured-output, decision-model, openrouter, mcp-features, each under its own entry below.
- **Exit criteria:** the Effect test files of those modules ported title by title: `packages/ai/openai/test/*`, `packages/ai/openrouter/test/*`, `packages/ai/cloudflare/test/*`, `packages/ai/typesafe/test/*`, `effect/test/ai/{Anthropic,OpenAi}StructuredOutput{,Representation}.test.ts`, `effect/test/ai/DecisionModel.test.ts`, and `effect/test/ai/McpServer/` with its `McpConformance` suites.

**M12 ai-openai (2026-11-03): done (`OpenAiStructuredOutput` came in M12 structured-output and `OpenAiDecisionModel` in M12 decision-model, below).** `pyfect.ai.openai` (`@effect/ai-openai`) over the `HttpClient` with OpenAI's own JSON wire format and SSE, no vendor SDK and no new dependency. Generated: `scripts/gen_openai_schemas.py` reads Effect's `Generated.ts` (a parser for the subset Effect's generator emits; an unknown shape fails naming the declaration) and writes `pyfect.ai.openai.generated` (`types.py` with a `TypedDict` per struct, `_schemas_N.py` / `_recursive.py` with the schemas, checks and annotations); `--check` fails when the checked-in package is stale, and `tests/test_gen_openai_schemas.py` runs it when the reference checkout is present. Hand-written: `client` (`OpenAiClient` with `create_response`, `create_response_stream` until a terminal event, `create_embedding`, `create_decisions`; `layer` / `layer_config`; bearer token and the redacted organization / project headers; `max_response_bytes`), WebSocket mode (`with_web_socket_mode`, curried, `layer_web_socket_mode`: one connection at a time over an `RcRef` with a 60 s idle TTL, reopened when it closes, the xAI not-found retry with the full prompt, and the `ResponseIdTracker` cleared when a connection ends), `config` (`OpenAiConfig`, `with_client_transform`), `error`, `language_model` (the Responses API with stored-history item references, encrypted reasoning replay, provider-executed history replayed inline or failing locally, prompt-cache breakpoints, the reasoning-model table with an optimistic default, `include` merging, streaming with summaries, tool-parameter deltas and the apply-patch diff, `with_config_override`), `embedding_model` (batched by `Config` and client config, index checks), `tool` (the nine provider-defined tools, their schemas picked from the generated ones) and `telemetry`. The AI guide has an OpenAI section with a checked snippet (`website/snippets/ai/openai.py`, a test HTTP layer). Tests: `tests/oracle/test_ai_openai_client.py`, `_language_model.py`, `_embedding_model.py`, `_schema.py` and `_telemetry.py`, one pytest per Effect title (144 of the 160 titles; parametrized titles as one parametrized test), each module docstring listing its omissions: the 3 `OpenAiClientGenerated` titles (the generated client of every operation is not ported), the 3 titles that needed `OpenAiStructuredOutput` (ported since, see M12 structured-output) and the 10 `OpenAiDecisionModel` titles (ported since, see M12 decision-model). Fixed on the way, each with a failing test first: a nested stream `error` event took the nested `type` (`insufficient_quota`) over `error` (only `code`, `message`, `param` are lifted now, as Effect's `NestedResponseErrorEvent`); a web-search item with `action: null` was read as no action instead of being rejected; generator errors from the tokenizer or the emitter did not name the declaration. Differences: `FileSearch`, `ImageGeneration` and `WebSearchPreview` take any call parameters (`S.Unknown`) where Effect leaves `Schema.Void`, because pyfect's provider-defined default (`S.Null`) rejects the `{}` the response carries; URL citations are written as WHATWG `href`s by the provider (`https://example.com/`), since `S.URLFromString` does not normalize. `tests/type_checks.py::check_ai_openai` pins the client, model, WebSocket-mode and tool types and rejects a misspelled config option. Skeptical review: the generator passed `Generated.ts`'s JavaScript patterns to `is_pattern` unchanged, so a `$` also matched before a final newline (`^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$` accepted `"abc\n"`); it now writes `$` as `\Z` (messages keep the JavaScript source) and fails on syntax the two engines read differently (`\d`, `\w`, `\b`, `.`, `\p`), `tests/test_gen_openai_schemas.py`. A WebSocket error event's details were the JSON as it came (an undeclared `param` was shown, in the server's key order) where Effect decodes `{type?, code?, message}`; it is decoded now. `tests/oracle/test_ai_openai_web_socket_edges.py` pins what Effect's tests leave open: a server close mid-response fails the turn with a `TransportError` and reconnects, a consumer leaving mid-response replaces the connection, concurrent streams are sent one at a time, `previous_response_not_found` keeps the connection, and a closed connection clears the response id tracker (each found by a surviving mutant). Stream and response parts and request bodies were compared with Effect's `OpenAiLanguageModel` under Node over recorded events covering every output item kind: identical but for the documented `params` of `FileSearch` / `ImageGeneration` calls (`{}` here, `undefined` in Effect). Open: without `OpenAiStructuredOutput`, function tools and JSON response formats are sent with `strict: true` and the default JSON Schema (`additionalProperties: true`, optional keys not required), which OpenAI's strict mode rejects with a 400; set `strict_json_schema=False` (or a tool's strict annotation) until the codec is ported. The generated client of every operation (`OpenAiClientGenerated`, about 18k lines of `Generated.ts`) is not generated.

**M12 structured-output (2026-11-03): done.** `pyfect.ai.anthropic_structured_output.to_codec_anthropic` and `pyfect.ai.openai_structured_output.to_codec_openai` (Effect's `effect/ai/AnthropicStructuredOutput` / `OpenAiStructuredOutput`) give a `StructuredOutputCodec(codec, json_schema)`: the JSON Schema for the provider's strict mode and the codec decoding the model's output. `_structured_output_codec.py` is Effect's internal `toCodec`: on the encoded side of `to_codec_json(schema)` a tuple becomes an object with `"0"`, `"1"`, ... keys (`"__rest__"`, `"__tail_N__"`), an object with index signatures an array of `[key, value]` pairs (lower size bounds carried over as `min_items`), an optional key a required nullable one, a `one_of` union an `any_of` one; checks with a JSON Schema fragment stay on the rewritten nodes as no-op compiler checks, and `is_finite` / `is_int` lose their `expected` text; the schema itself comes back when nothing is rewritten. `_structured_output_json.py` walks the document; the Anthropic rewriter keeps its keyword and format subset, hoists `allOf` descriptions and rejects recursive schemas; the OpenAI one (`_openai_all_of.py`) merges `allOf` patterns into lookaheads and bounds into the tightest, drops keywords that do not apply to the type, adds `properties: {}` to closed empty objects and requires an object root. The Anthropic, OpenAI and OpenAI-compatible language models use them for tool parameters, response formats, tool-call decoding and `generate_object` (the compatible one prepares each tool's codec once per request, Effect's `toolCodecs`). Tests: `tests/oracle/test_anthropic_structured_output.py`, `test_openai_structured_output.py` and their `_representation` modules, 124 of the 130 titles, the 6 omitted (undefined, bigint literals, symbol keys) listed in their docstrings; the provider titles that waited for the codecs (5 in `test_openai_compat_language_model.py`, 2 in `test_ai_openai_language_model.py`, the OpenAI-transformer title of `test_ai_tool.py`, and the Anthropic client-tool title now compiling through `to_codec_anthropic`). Fixed on the way, with a failing test first (`tests/test_schema_ast_flip.py`): `AST.flip` was not memoized, so the flip (and `to_encoded`) of a recursive schema unrolled into a new node at each level, and a rewrite of the encoded side recursed forever; it is made once per node now, as Effect's `flip`. The review found the OpenAI `allOf` fold keeping a keyword two members gave as equal lists (`enum`, `anyOf`), which Effect's `!==` drops as different arrays; it compares as `===` now (`tests/test_structured_output_all_of.py`, with that file's other `allOf` edges printed by Effect under Node).

**M12 decision-model (2026-11-03): done.** `pyfect.ai.decision` (Effect's `Decision`): `classify(instructions=, criteria={label: description})`, `rate(instructions=, criteria=[labels] | {label: description})` and `probability(instructions=, criteria={"false":, "true":})` build typed decisions (labels written as literals stay `Literal` types, computed ones `str`); `make(input=schema, decisions={name: decision})` is a `Definition` whose input is encoded with `to_codec_json`; `ClassifyAnswer`, `RateAnswer`, `ProbabilityAnswer`, `Unanswered(reason, description)`. `pyfect.ai.decision_model` (Effect's `DecisionModel`): the `DecisionModel` tag, `make(decide=, probability_precision=, supports_images=)` over a provider function of `ProviderOptions(state, decisions, images)`, `decide` / `decide_settled` (spans `DecisionModel.decide` / `decideSettled`), `Image`, `DecisionUsage` with Effect's JSON form, and the provider answer dataclasses. Answers are validated with Effect's messages: distributions must cover every label and sum to 1 within the provider's rounding (rescaled), the rate label is the argmax, a refusal is a `ContentPolicyError`, any other unanswered decision an `InvalidOutputError` (`decide_settled` gives `Err(Unanswered)` instead). Providers: `pyfect.ai.openai.decision_model` (`/decisions`, data-URL images only), `pyfect.ai.cloudflare` (`@effect/ai-cloudflare`: `client` with the result envelope, AI Gateway header, `layer_config` from `CLOUDFLARE_*`, `cf-ray` and envelope errors in the error mapping; `decision_model` for Clef, base64 and data-URL images) and `pyfect.ai.typesafe` (`@effect/ai-typesafe`: `client` with `system_one` / `list_models`, `TypeSafeConfig.with_client_transform`, `retry-after-ms`, `x-typesafe-request-id` metadata; `decision_model` for System One, no images); the two share `_noul_answers` (score indices mapped to level labels). Every title of `DecisionModel.test.ts` (47), `OpenAiDecisionModel.test.ts` (10), `CloudflareClient.test.ts` (10), `CloudflareDecisionModel.test.ts` (7), `TypeSafeClient.test.ts` (27), `TypeSafeSchema.test.ts` (8) and `TypeSafeDecisionModel.test.ts` (22) is ported (`tests/oracle/test_ai_*decision_model.py`, `test_ai_cloudflare_client.py`, `test_ai_typesafe_{client,schema}.py`), with two Python-only tests (`make` rejects one decision under two names, `rate` duplicate labels). `tests/type_checks.py::check_ai_decision_model` pins the literal labels, answer lookups, requirements and provider layers; docs: the Decisions section of `ai.mdx` with `website/snippets/ai/decisions.py` on a test layer.

**M12 ai-openrouter (2026-11-03): done.** `pyfect.ai.openrouter` (`@effect/ai-openrouter`) over the `HttpClient`, no vendor SDK and no new dependency. Generated: `scripts/gen_openai_schemas.py` became `scripts/gen_ai_schemas.py` with a target per provider (`--provider openai|openrouter`, all by default; `--check` for each), the shared schema helpers moved to `pyfect/ai/_generated_support.py`, and it now reads annotated constants (`export const X: Schema.Schema<X> = ...`; an unmatched constant fails naming it) and writes `\d` / `\w` as ASCII classes (rejecting forms the engines read differently); an empty `Schema.Struct({})` (TypeScript's `{}`, any object) is typed `Mapping[str, Any]`, not an empty `TypedDict` that takes no keys (found by the `Generated.test.ts` audio payload, whose `provider.options.openai` carries a `prompt`). `scripts/gen_ai_client.py` turns `Generated.ts`'s client into `pyfect.ai.openrouter.generated.client` (all 96 operations: JSON operations through the transform hook with their documented error statuses, `-Sse` operations as typed SSE data streams, binary operations as byte streams; an unknown operation shape fails naming the operation) over a shared runtime, `pyfect/ai/_generated_client.py`; `tests/test_gen_ai_schemas.py` pins both and checks every target is fresh when the reference checkout is present. Hand-written: `client` (`OpenRouterClient` with `create_chat_completion` over the generated operation, `create_chat_completion_stream` until `[DONE]`, `create_decisions` on `/alpha/decisions` beside the versioned URL, the generated client as `client`; bearer token redacted, `HTTP-Referer` / `X-Title`; `layer` / `layer_config`; `max_response_bytes`), `config` (`OpenRouterConfig`, `with_client_transform`, not applied to streamed chat completions, as in Effect), `error` (the `openrouter` metadata), `language_model` (the chat request options as a `TypedDict` plus `strict_json_schema`, the codec of the model's family by prefix, cache-control breakpoints on messages and parts, audio formats, reasoning details replayed once per request, encrypted reasoning with a tool call finishing as `tool-calls`, disjoint cached and reasoning counts, citations, images, streamed tool-parameter deltas, `with_config_override`), `decision_model` (`OpenRouterDecisionModel`: choice / score / noul questions, score distributions re-keyed by level label, routing and tracing `Config`, cost and serving provider under `metadata["openrouter"]`) and `schema` (the decisions wire format). Tests: `tests/oracle/test_ai_openrouter_client.py`, `_generated.py`, `_schema.py`, `_language_model.py` and `_decision_model.py`, one pytest per Effect title (all 72 titles, Effect's loops as parametrized tests; 109 tests); they were written after the modules, so they did not fail first. Differences: a documented error status of the generated client is one `OpenRouterClientError` class with the tag as `.tag` (Effect has a tagged class per status); `upload_file` takes its form fields as a mapping (Effect passes the object to `bodyFormData`); Effect's unserializable `bigint` state is a `set` in the client test; an explicitly `undefined` optional input field is a `None` field. `tests/type_checks.py::check_ai_openrouter` pins the client, model and generated-operation types and rejects a mistyped config option. The AI guide has an OpenRouter section with a checked snippet (`website/snippets/ai/openrouter.py`, a test HTTP layer). Skeptical review: `ConfigService` (a hand-written copy of the generated `ChatRequest` minus Effect's omitted keys) lacked `prompt_cache_options`, `route` and `stop_server_tools_when`, and typed `plugins` as `Any`; it has them now, and a test pins its keys to the generated request's. `api_url=""` was replaced by the default URL (`or` where Effect writes `??`), here and in the Anthropic (also `api_version`), OpenAI, OpenAI-compatible, Cloudflare and TypeSafe clients; they use `or_default`, and `tests/test_ai_option_default_guard.py` bans an `or` fallback to a `DEFAULT_*` constant under `pyfect.ai`. 12 of 23 mutants of the hand-written modules survived Effect's tests; `tests/oracle/test_ai_openrouter_edges.py` pins those edges with values printed by Effect's `@effect/ai-openrouter` under Node (reasoning details sent once per request, a message's cache breakpoint on its last text, `image/*` as JPEG, the streamed `include_usage`, reasoning details on the first tool call only, streamed text details merged and unfinished calls flushed by index, an error chunk finishing with `error`, a chat 429 whose generated schema drops `type` against a decisions 429 that keeps `insufficient_quota`, an undocumented 409, each model family's tool schema, the decisions URL behind a proxy); the port agreed with Effect on all of them.

**M12 mcp-features (2026-11-03): partly done.** Ports some of the McpServer features M10e mcp deferred. Tool schemas are built the way Effect's `toolJsonSchema` builds them (`_mcp/tool_schema.py`): the root is inlined, definitions go under `$defs`, and a `tool.Strict` tool lists closed objects. Elicitation schemas are decoded through `ElicitRequestFormParams`. Declared `Error` failures without a message are encoded as JSON, as Effect does. Every failed request's cause goes to the `ErrorReporter`. Log levels (`_mcp/log_levels.py`) follow Effect's `mcpLogLevels` and `canDeliver`: before `logging/setLevel`, a session's threshold is the server's level (Info by default), so `debug` is dropped. Handlers run with `CurrentLogLevel` at the session's level. On 2026-07-28, a request's `_meta` level sets `CurrentLogLevel` and filters that request's logs, and progress goes only to the request's own token. HTTP admission (`_mcp/http_admission.py`) ports `admitHttp` / `selectHttpProtocol`: -32022 for unsupported versions (with `supported` / `requested`), -32020 for header mismatches (version against session or `_meta`, `Mcp-Method`, `Mcp-Name` with `=?base64?` decoding), InvalidParams for missing 2026-07-28 metadata, 404 for unknown stateless methods, and a parse error answered 200. DELETE gets the same session version checks. 2025-03-26 JSON-RPC batches are served over HTTP and over stdio (`_mcp/batch.py`): one JSON array, or over HTTP an event stream of the server's notifications then that array; 202 when nothing expects an answer; cancelled requests write nothing, and a batch whose requests are all cancelled gets an empty event stream; items that are not objects are dropped, as Effect's JSON-RPC decoder drops them. A session's threshold before `logging/setLevel` is the `CurrentLogLevel` the server was built with (Effect's `defaultLogLevel`), not a fixed Info. Ports: `tests/oracle/test_mcp_batch_cancellation.py` (the batch SSE framing title and the conformance batch cancellation titles), `tests/oracle/test_mcp_server_registrations.py` (the direct-service and `registerToolkit` titles of `McpServer.test.ts`), `test_mcp_logging.py` (the conformance logging titles, per stateful revision and for 2026-07-28) and `test_mcp_http_admission.py` (POST and DELETE version checks, legacy batches, the 2026-07-28 transport and metadata titles). Each lists its omitted titles. Still not ported: resource subscriptions and `subscriptions/listen` streams, `allowSubscriptions`, list-change coalescing, `layerHttpRequestScoped`, `input_required` round trips, `Mcp-Param-*` mirroring checks, DELETE interrupting a pending POST with a 404, most of `ProtocolAdapters.test.ts`, and the rest of the conformance suites.

**M12 completeness pass (2026-11-04): done; M12 is complete but for the MCP server features listed at the end.** A title-by-title script check of the exit-criteria files (871 titles, conformance suites counted once) finds every title of the AI provider, structured-output and decision files in a test docstring (template titles under their expanded names), so the openai, openrouter, cloudflare, typesafe, structured-output and decision clusters are complete; `OpenAiClientGenerated` (3 titles, the generated per-operation OpenAI client) was the one omission there (done in M14 openai-generated, below). In `McpServer/` the check found about 300 titles unported before the pass. The pass ported the conformance suites whose features pyfect already has, over the stdio harness for every revision whose Effect file runs them (the lifecycle suite over the in-memory HTTP server): `tests/oracle/test_mcp_conformance_prompts.py` (PromptsTest and CompletionTest, 25 titles), `_tools.py` (ToolsTest, 24 titles and one Python-only half of a title that also needs `input_required`), `_resources.py` (ResourcesTest, 14), `_lifecycle.py` (LifecycleTest, 10) and `_roots.py` (RootsTest, 5): 78 titles, 342 tests. All passed when first run, so they found no bug; they pin behavior that had no conformance test. Content is given as base64 text where Effect builds it from a `Uint8Array`, and each module docstring lists its omissions (list-change coalescing, subscriptions, `Mcp-Param-*` headers, `input_required`). Coverage: section 1's Platform row goes from 95% to 96% (about 165k of Effect's 171k platform source lines; M12 added `Decision` / `DecisionModel`, the structured-output modules and part of the MCP server). The provider packages, counted apart, are now all ported: `ai-openrouter` (40k lines, mostly generated) and `ai-cloudflare` / `ai-typesafe`. The weighted total stays at about 90%. Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, three runs alternated against the M11b completeness commit in a worktree): map chain 3.08–3.11M steps/s against 3.02–3.12M, generator steps 2.17–2.25M against 2.14–2.23M, fork + join 200–202k/s against 175–179k/s. No regression; M12 changed no runtime core file, and the fork + join gain comes from the dogfood merge before M12. The full check collects 16,809 tests. The suite run at the start of the pass (16,467 tests, before the conformance files) passed with 8 skipped, and the 342 new tests pass on their own. Deferred, with the reason: the MCP features that are not ported, with their titles. These are resource subscriptions and the 2026-07-28 `subscriptions/listen` streams (SubscriptionsTest, 17 titles, plus 3 in ResourcesTest), `input_required` round trips (MultiRoundTripTest, 7), `layerHttpRequestScoped` (8), list-change coalescing (2), and `Mcp-Param-*` mirroring (6). Also deferred: the HTTP-transport conformance suites that read SSE streams as they arrive, which the in-memory HTTP harness buffers whole (TransportsTest 34, ElicitationTest 9, most of BaseProtocolTest's 30 and UtilitiesTest's 14). Most of `ProtocolAdapters.test.ts` (34) and 22 `McpServer.test.ts` titles are deferred too; they need those features or that harness. `Workerd.test.ts` does not apply to Python. Each is listed in its oracle module's docstring. The next MCP pass needs a streaming HTTP test harness first.

### M13 — SQL drivers

M13 ports the SQL driver packages M10b left: `@effect/sql-mysql2`, `-mssql`, `-libsql`, `-clickhouse` and `-d1`, plus a title pass over `sql-pg` and `sql-sqlite-node` and a decision on PGlite and the other SQLite runtimes.

- **Clusters:** mysql, mssql, libsql, clickhouse, d1-pg-parity, each under its own entry below.
- **Exit criteria:** the Effect test files of those packages ported title by title: `packages/sql/{mysql2,mssql,libsql,clickhouse,d1,pg,sqlite-node}/test/*`, the runtime-independent titles of `packages/sql/{pglite,sqlite-bun,sqlite-do,sqlite-react-native,sqlite-wasm}/test/*`, and no export missing in `docs/coverage/sql-drivers.md`.

**M13 sql-mysql2 (2026-11-04): done.** `pyfect.sql.mysql` (extra `pyfect-dev[mysql]` on asyncmy, never imported by `pyfect`; Effect's `@effect/sql-mysql2`): `config` (`MysqlClientConfig`: `url` or `host` / `port` / `database` / `username` / `password: Redacted`, `max_connections` (default 10), `connection_ttl` (idle timeout, default 60 s), `connect_options` for the driver (Effect's `poolConfig`), `disable_prepared_statements`, `span_attributes`, the name transforms; the URL is parsed when the config is made), `client` (`MysqlClient` / `MysqlClientService` with its `config`, `make`, `layer`, `layer_config`, `layer_from`, `make_compiler`: `?` placeholders, backtick names), `error` (MySQL error numbers to `SqlError` reasons, the key of a duplicate entry as `UniqueViolation.constraint`), `migrator` (no dump), and `ResultSetHeader(affected_rows, insert_id)`.
