As mysql2: `execute` prepares on the server (cached per connection, 256 statements) unless `disable_prepared_statements`, `unprepared` and streams use the text protocol, a statement without rows gives `[]` and `.raw` the `ResultSetHeader`, several statements give one result each; operation names `execute` / `executeUnprepared` / `stream`, the `SELECT 1` connect check with its 5 s "MysqlClient: Connection timeout", span attributes `db.system.name=mysql`, `server.address`, `server.port`, `db.namespace`. Interrupting a statement closes its connection and `KILL`s the server thread from a side connection, so the statement's locks go; the pool replaces the connection. `pyfect.sql._loop` (was `pg._loop`) is the asyncio loop thread both drivers share. `pyfect.sql.result.affected_rows(raw)` reads a raw result's row count (the cluster's message storage read mysql2's `affectedRows` by name, which no pyfect driver gives). The eventlog SQL storages gain their MySQL branches (`SqlEventLogServerEncrypted` / `Unencrypted`: `BINARY` / `VARCHAR(191)` keys, `INSERT IGNORE`, `ON DUPLICATE KEY`, `FOR UPDATE`, the prefix index created unless it exists). Tests run on a real MySQL (`mysql:lts`) in a throwaway Docker container per session (`tests/oracle/_mysql.py`: labelled, removed at exit, containers of dead runs reaped; skipped without Docker). Every title of `packages/sql/mysql2/test` is a test docstring: the client, model, SqlError classification, KeyValueStore, Persistence (PersistedCache single and multi table, PersistedQueue and its SQL suite, cleanup, large payloads) and both eventlog storage suites, run on MySQL alongside SQLite and PostgreSQL where Effect shares the suite. The layer-reuse guard has the four MySQL layers; the docs have a MySQL page with a checked snippet (skipped without Docker). The cluster's `SqlMessageStorage` (21 tests) and `SqlRunnerStorage` (4) suites run on MySQL too, as platform-node's do; they pin the `affected_rows` read (reverting it to the `affectedRows` name fails two of them). Review fixes: a stream whose statement fails after its columns (an error packet mid-result) hung its fiber and every later statement on the connection, since asyncmy leaves that result active; a dict or list parameter was sent as its Python `repr`, and now goes as JSON text over both protocols, as mysql2's `execute` does. The API reference now lists the names a module's `__all__` exports with a leading underscore (the OpenAI schemas' `_MisalignmentErrorType`, as Effect's `Generated.ts` names them), which `test_api_reference` had flagged since M12.

**M13 sql-mssql (2026-11-04): done.** `pyfect.sql.mssql` (extra `pyfect-dev[mssql]` on python-tds and pyOpenSSL, never imported by `pyfect`; Effect's `@effect/sql-mssql`): `config` (`MssqlClientConfig`: `server`, `port` (default 1433) or `instance_name` (resolved through the SQL Browser), `database`, `username`, `password: Redacted`, `encrypt` (default on; off encrypts only the login, as tedious), `trust_server`, `connect_timeout`, `cancel_timeout`, `connection_retry_interval`, `max_retries_on_transient_errors`, `min_connections` / `max_connections` / `connection_ttl` (1 / 10 / 45 minutes, by creation, as `Pool.makeWithTTL`), `parameter_types`, `span_attributes`, the name transforms; checked when made), `types` (`TYPES`, `DataType`, `ParameterOptions(length, precision, scale)`, `default_parameter_types`), `parameter` / `procedure` (`Parameter[A](name, type, options)`, called with a value to bind it; `procedure.make(name).param(...).output_param(...).with_rows(Row)`, `compile(*bound)` checking every input is given once; `Result(output, rows)` whose `output[parameter]` is typed by the parameter), `client` (`MssqlClient` / `MssqlClientService` with `config`, `param(type, value, options)` and `call(procedure)`, on the transaction's connection inside one; `make`, `layer`, `layer_config`, `layer_from`, `make_compiler`: `@1`, `@2` placeholders, `[name]` escaping with `]` doubled and `.` splitting, `returning` as `OUTPUT INSERTED.*` before `VALUES` / `FROM (values ...)`), `error` (Effect's error numbers to `SqlError` reasons; a unique violation's constraint from the error's `constraint`, else the index or constraint named in its message, else `unknown`), `migrator`. As tedious: every statement runs through `sp_executesql`, a request's rows are those of all its result sets, sessions start with tedious's initial SQL (ANSI options, `READ COMMITTED`, `XACT_ABORT OFF`), transactions use TDS transaction-manager requests and `SAVE TRANSACTION effect_sql_N` savepoints (never released; a `with_transaction` statement is ignored, as Effect's mssql client ignores it), transient login errors are retried, interrupting a request sends an attention and waits for the server's answer (the connection closes after `cancel_timeout`), and a connection the driver ends is retired from the pool, also when the end comes before the pool has it; streams die with "executeStream not implemented"; the `SELECT 1` connect check fails with "MssqlClient: Failed to connect" / "MssqlClient: Connection timeout"; span attributes `db.system.name=microsoft.sql_server`, `db.namespace`, `server.address`, `server.port`. The encrypted eventlog storage gains its SQL Server branch (`IF OBJECT_ID ... CREATE TABLE`, `VARBINARY` keys, `MERGE ... WITH (HOLDLOCK) ... OUTPUT inserted.entry_id` for new ids); the persistence, persisted queue and cluster stores already had theirs, and pass on a real server unchanged. Tests run on a real server in a throwaway Docker container per session (`tests/oracle/_mssql.py` on `tests/oracle/_docker.py`, which `_mysql.py` now shares): `mcr.microsoft.com/mssql/server:2022-latest`, or Azure SQL Edge (the SQL Server 15 engine, native ARM64) on ARM machines, where SQL Server 2022 does not start under amd64 emulation and 2025 runs too slowly under it; `PYFECT_MSSQL_IMAGE` overrides; skipped without Docker. Every title of `packages/sql/mssql/test` is a test docstring (78 tests): the client (compiler, default parameter mapping, procedure outputs, cancellation, the single-connection pool and the three connection lifecycle titles on a fake driver standing in for `vi.mock("tedious")`), Binary, SqlErrorClassification, Persistence (PersistedCache single and multi table, PersistedQueue and its SQL suite, the deadlock capture through an XEvent session, and the three XACT_ABORT titles on a third-party client built on the driver's connections, `tests/oracle/_mssql_xact_abort.py`) and both eventlog storage suites, run on SQL Server alongside the other dialects. Eleven `(pyfect)` tests pin the driver on the server: transactions and savepoints, a procedure with inputs, outputs and rows, typed parameters, server-side cancellation, error classification, a rejected login, login-only encryption, migrations, and lengthless output parameters. That last one, and the docs snippet, found a bug: an `NVARCHAR` / `VARCHAR` / `VARBINARY` output parameter without a length was declared as length 1, so its value came back cut to one character; tedious sizes a null value at the type's maximum, and the length function now requires each sized type's maximum, so a type cannot be declared without one. The layer-reuse guard has the four SQL Server layers; the docs have a SQL Server page with a checked snippet (skipped without Docker); `tests/type_checks.py::sql_mssql_driver` pins the constructors, `call`'s `Result` row type and `output[parameter]`'s type. Review (2026-11-04): an interrupt that reached a call after its request had ended, while the call was still finishing on its worker thread, armed the cancel for the connection's next request, so an unrelated later statement failed with "Canceled."; the worker now has each call's connection forget its cancel when the call ends, under the lock the interrupt takes (`_worker.Cancellable`), and a server test holds a call in that window. A string with a character outside the BMP (an emoji) was declared by its code points, so `NVARCHAR(1)` could not hold `"😀"` and the server rejected the request; lengths are now counted in UTF-16 code units, as tedious counts a JavaScript string's. Both failed first in `tests/oracle/test_mssql_client.py`; dropping the forget fails the first again. Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`): map chain 3.20M steps/s, generator steps 2.29M, fork + join 209k/s, so no regression.

**M13 sql-libsql (2026-11-04): done.** `pyfect.sql.libsql` (Effect's `@effect/sql-libsql`; no native dependency): a `file:` / `:memory:` database on `sqlite3`, and a remote sqld or Turso server over Hrana over HTTP (v2 pipeline requests) on httpx (the existing `pyfect-dev[httpx]` extra, imported only when a remote client is made). The choice: the Hrana-over-HTTP subset the client needs (execute, a baton-carrying stream per transaction, close) is about 200 lines, while the official `libsql` package is a Rust extension whose wheels lag new Pythons and free-threaded builds; `pyfect.sql.libsql` therefore adds no `libsql` extra. `config` (`LibsqlClientConfig`: `url`, `auth_token: Redacted`, `tls`, `span_attributes`, the name transforms; the URL is parsed into its `target` when the config is made, with `@libsql/core`'s rules: `?tls`, `?authToken`, `?cache` for memory, `libsql:` to HTTPS or, with `?tls=0` and a port, HTTP; `LibsqlLiveConfig(live_client=...)` for a caller-owned client), `sdk` (the async `LibsqlSdk` / `LibsqlSdkTransaction` protocols a live client implements, `ResultSet(columns, column_types, rows, rows_affected, last_insert_rowid)` that `.raw` gives, `LibsqlError(message, code, extended_code, raw_code)`), `client` (`LibsqlClient` / `LibsqlClientService` with `config` and `sdk`; `make`, `layer`, `layer_config`, `layer_from`) and `migrator`. As Effect: one semaphore serializes statements and transactions; a transaction's `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` is the SDK's `transaction("write")`, run uninterruptibly once the permit is taken, so an interrupt that lands while it is pending rolls back (a failing rollback is a defect); savepoints `effect_sql_<n>` are released after commit and rollback; `BeginTransaction` is ignored; streams die with "executeStream not implemented"; failures are classified from `LibsqlError.code` (SQLite's primary code name, so a unique violation is a `ConstraintError`); `db.system.name=sqlite`; a local database that cannot be opened is a defect. An interrupt never abandons a remote request half way: the request goes on and the stream's next one (the rollback) waits for its baton, as Effect's unawaited `fetch` does, so an interrupted transaction still rolls back on the server instead of holding its write lock until sqld expires the stream (review fix; `tests/oracle/test_libsql_driver.py`). The local client keeps libSQL's pool rules: a transaction takes the connection, a file database opens another for the statements meanwhile, an in-memory one fails them with `TRANSACTION_ACTIVE`. Remote tests run on a throwaway `ghcr.io/tursodatabase/libsql-server:main` container per session (`tests/oracle/_libsql.py`, the mysql fixture pattern; skipped without Docker or httpx). Every title of `packages/sql/libsql/test` is a test docstring (Client 12, Resolver 6), plus `(pyfect)` tests of URL parsing, value round trips locally and remotely, error codes, file persistence, the in-memory connection rule and open failures. Layer-reuse cases for the four libSQL layers; a docs page with a checked snippet (on `:memory:`).

**M13 sql-clickhouse (2026-11-04): done.** `pyfect.sql.clickhouse` (Effect's `@effect/sql-clickhouse`; no native dependency): ClickHouse's HTTP interface, as `@clickhouse/client` speaks it, sent with a pyfect `HttpClient` (by default one `make` creates on httpx, the existing `pyfect-dev[httpx]` extra, closed with the client; `http_client=` takes another). The choice: the subset of `@clickhouse/client` Effect's client uses (query with an appended `FORMAT`, command, insert, ping, the `param_` / settings URL encoding, error parsing) is about 400 lines over `HttpClient`, which already gives interrupt-as-abort and streamed bodies, so no `clickhouse-connect` extra was added. `config` (`ClickhouseClientConfig`: `url` parsed when the config is made with `@clickhouse/client`'s rules (credentials, database path, `ch_` / `clickhouse_setting_` settings, `http_header_` headers, `application`, `pathname`, `session_id`, `request_timeout`, `access_token`; other parameters fail as unknown), `username` / `password: Redacted` or `access_token: Redacted`, `database`, `clickhouse_settings`, `http_headers`, `application`, `session_id`, `role`, `request_timeout` (30 s, the timeout of the httpx client), `http_client`, `span_attributes`, the name transforms), `params` (`format_query_param`, `format_setting`, `type_of`, `TupleParam`), `driver` (`ClickhouseDriver` with `query` / `command` / `exec` / `insert` / `ping`, `ResultSet` (`text`, `json`, `stream` of row lines), `CommandResult`, `InsertResult`), `error` (`ClickhouseError(message, code, type)` parsed from a failed response with `@clickhouse/client`'s regex; `classify`: 516 / 497 / 36, 60, 62, 242 / 159, 469 to the reasons Effect names, else `UnknownError`, `ConnectionError` while connecting), `client` (`ClickhouseClient` / `ClickhouseClientService` with `config`, `client`, `param`, `as_command`, `insert_query` (`JSONEachRow` by default, `columns` a list or `{"except": [...]}`), `with_query_id`, `with_clickhouse_settings`; the `ClientMethod`, `QueryId`, `ClickhouseSettings` references; `make`, `layer`, `layer_config`, `layer_from`, `make_compiler`: `{pN: Type}` placeholders, `"quoted"` names, the `clickhouse` dialect) and `migrator`. As Effect: `make` checks `/ping` within 5 s ("ClickhouseClient: Connection timeout"); statements run as queries with `FORMAT JSON` (`JSONCompact` for `.values`, `JSONEachRow` for streams) or, under `as_command`, as commands; parameters are `p1`, `p2`, ...; a fresh UUID query id unless `with_query_id`; interrupting a statement or insert aborts the request and sends `KILL QUERY WHERE query_id = {queryId:String}` (the id a parameter), a stream does so unless read to the end; `BEGIN TRANSACTION`; `db.system.name=clickhouse`, `db.namespace`. The HTTP requests get no span of their own (`TracerDisabledWhen`): the statement's span covers them, and their URLs hold the parameter values. From 25.11 a server that fails after sending rows ends the body with a tagged exception (`X-ClickHouse-Exception-Tag`); streams and whole bodies read it back as the `ClickhouseError` (`@clickhouse/client` does so for streams only), also when the server then cuts the chunked body short, as it does: the transport's incomplete-read error gives way to the exception read (review: this path failed with that `HttpClientError` on a real server, untested because the tests' errors came before the first byte; they now force the early send with `max_block_size=1`, `output_format_parallel_formatting=0`). An aware `datetime` before the epoch with microseconds is its signed magnitude (`-1.500000`); `@clickhouse/client` floors the seconds and adds the fraction, a second early (review, server test). URL options of `@clickhouse/client`'s agent and logger (`compression_*`, `keep_alive_enabled`, `max_open_connections`, `log_level`) fail as not offered rather than unknown; `request_timeout` and string error codes are read as JavaScript's `Number` reads them (no `_`, no `NaN`, ASCII digits). Divergences found on a real server (`tests/oracle/test_clickhouse_driver.py`; both tests failed before their fixes): a statement without output (DDL, `INSERT ... SELECT`) answers a query with an empty body, which Effect's `result.json()` fails to parse ("Failed to parse result"), so `CREATE TABLE` and Effect's own migrator failed on any server; here an empty body is no rows. Effect's migrator records migrations with `INSERT ... VALUES` through a query, and the server reads the appended `FORMAT JSON` as data (`CANNOT_PARSE_INPUT_ASSERTION_FAILED`); `pyfect.sql.migrator`'s ClickHouse branch records them with `INSERT ... SELECT ... UNION ALL ...`. Tests run on a throwaway `clickhouse/clickhouse-server` container per session (`tests/oracle/_clickhouse.py`, the mysql fixture pattern; skipped without Docker or httpx); Effect's `vi.mock("@clickhouse/client")` is `tests/oracle/_clickhouse_fake.py`, a fake `HttpClient` seeing the requests the client sends. Every title of `packages/sql/clickhouse/test` is a test docstring (Client 12, SqlErrorClassification 3), plus 10 `(pyfect)` tests on the server and of the URL. Layer-reuse cases for the four ClickHouse layers; type pins in `tests/type_checks.py`; a docs page with a checked snippet (run on the test server).

**M13 sql-d1, PGlite and the pg / sqlite-node title pass (2026-11-04): done.** `pyfect.sql.d1` (Effect's `@effect/sql-d1`; no new dependency): D1's HTTP API (`POST /accounts/{account}/d1/database/{id}/query` and `/raw`, one statement or a `{"batch": [...]}` D1 commits as one transaction) sent with the `HttpClient` in context, its own HTTP spans disabled so the statement's span covers it. `config` (`D1ClientConfig(account_id, database_id, api_token: Redacted, base_url, span_attributes, transform_*_names)`), `api` (`D1Api.query` / `raw`, `D1Result(results, meta: D1Meta, success)` that `.raw` gives, `D1RawResult`, `D1ApiError(status, errors)` with Cloudflare's error envelope), `client` (`D1Client` / `D1ClientService` with `batch`, `config`, `api`; `make`, `layer`, `layer_config`, `layer_from`; `make_compiler_sqlite`). As Effect: transactions and `batch` inside one are a defect (`transactions are not supported in D1`), streams die (`executeStream not implemented`), `update_values` is `@deprecated`, every failure is an `UnknownError` (`Failed to execute statement` / `Failed to execute batch`, operation `execute`), `batch` names its span `BATCH <target>` with `db.operation.name` / `db.operation.batch.size` / the joined `db.query.text`, runs `CurrentTransformer` on each statement with the batching client, and renames each statement's rows with its own client's transform; `without_transforms` also drops a query-only transform. All 19 titles are ported (`tests/oracle/test_d1_client.py`, `test_d1_resolver.py`) against `_d1_fake.FakeD1`, D1's HTTP API in process (`http_client.layer_test`) over a real SQLite database. PGlite: not ported (§4; `py-pglite` runs PGlite under Node behind its socket server, which `pyfect.sql.pg` can use). sqlite-bun / -do / -react-native / -wasm: §4 "not applicable", with their runtime-independent titles ported in `tests/oracle/test_sqlite_other_runtimes.py`; that found a bug: a readonly client given a `file:` URI quoted the URI into a path and failed to open (`node:sqlite` opens `file:` URIs), fixed by adding `mode=ro` to the URI's own parameters (`tests/test_sql_sqlite_file_uri.py`). Title script over `sql-pg/test` (351 titles) and `sql-sqlite-node/test` (84): sqlite-node has every title; pg was missing the two "PgPool connection spans" titles, which the M10b pass had omitted for a `db.connect` span libpq could not give. libpq connects in one call, but the pool can still time it: `PgPool` now records each connect's start and end on its clock and, on the session's first checkout, reports a `db.connect` client span under the statement that waited (`db.system.name`, `db.client.connection.connect_time_ms`, from the later of the connect's start and the statement's), only with tracing, `SpanPropagationEnabled` and span timing, as Effect. Both titles run through a paused `CancelGate` (now with `resume()` and `accepted`). The other pg titles remain omitted as recorded (wire protocol, codecs, SCRAM, multiplexing, the Docker harness). New coverage cluster `sql-drivers` (`docs/coverage/sql-drivers.md`: D1Client and sqlite-node, 100%). The full suite has 17,327 tests (8 skipped), all passing. Review fix: `PgPool` kept a session's connect times (for `db.connect`) in a plain dict until its first checkout, so a session closed before anyone checked it out (a checkout that timed out, then idle past `idle_timeout`, or a minimum session that died) stayed referenced for the pool's life; the record now goes with the session's scope (Effect keeps it in a `WeakMap`), pinned by `test_pg_pool.py::test_forgets_the_connect_of_a_session_closed_before_its_first_checkout`.
**M13 completeness pass (2026-11-04): done; M13 is complete.** A title-by-title script check of the exit-criteria files (every `it` / `test` title of `packages/sql/*/test`, matched against the test docstrings) finds every title of `sql-mysql2` (22), `sql-mssql` (31), `sql-libsql` (17, the interrupted-begin template under both expanded names), `sql-clickhouse` (15), `sql-d1` (19) and `sql-sqlite-node` (80) ported. `sql-pg` has 343 titles; the 191 not ported are the wire-protocol files M10b omitted with reasons (PgProtocol 69, PgTypes 85, PgAuth 15, the wire-level `PgConnection.in-process` 19, ContainerTeardown 3), since libpq and psycopg implement the protocol, codecs and SCRAM. The check found `sql-pglite` untested: 15 of its 30 titles are PostgreSQL's behavior rather than PGlite's (basic insert / select, the `in` helper inside a line comment, JSON fragments and the JSON / JSONB string round trips, listen + notify and a listener closed with its scope, transaction commit and rollback, `BeginTransaction`'s isolation level, nested commits and savepoint rollbacks, the migrator's records and a migrations table whose name needs quoting), and `tests/oracle/test_pglite_on_pg.py` now runs them on `pyfect.sql.pg` against the local server (22 tests). All passed when first run, so they found no bug; the rest of the file's titles are in the pg suites or are PGlite's own (`dumpDataDir`, its driver name, `refreshArrayTypes`, a caller-owned instance, the single shared connection), each with its reason in the module docstring. The titles of `sqlite-bun`, `-do`, `-react-native` and `-wasm` not ported are their runtimes' bindings (§4). Export coverage: the `sql-drivers` cluster (`scripts/coverage_maps/sql_drivers.py`) covered only D1 and sqlite-node; it now lists every value export of the M13 packages' modules, MysqlClient / MysqlMigrator, MssqlClient / MssqlMigrator / Parameter / Procedure, LibsqlClient / LibsqlMigrator, ClickhouseClient / ClickhouseMigrator, D1Client, PgClient / PgMigrator / PgPool / PgConnection, SqliteClient / SqliteMigrator: 71 exports, 0 missing (`Parameter.make` is the `Parameter` constructor; `Procedure`'s `param` / `outputParam` / `withRows` / `compile` are its methods). Coverage: section 1's Platform row stays at 96%; the driver packages are counted apart from it, and of them `sql-mysql2` (0.7k lines), `sql-mssql` (1.2k), `sql-libsql` (0.5k), `sql-clickhouse` (0.7k) and `sql-d1` (0.4k) are now ported, `sql-pglite` (0.6k) and the four other SQLite runtimes (2.2k) are §4. The weighted total stays at about 90%. Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, two runs alternated against the pre-M13 merge commit `b939212` in a worktree, with the machine under load): map chain 2.93–3.07M steps/s against 2.52–3.01M, generator steps 2.12–2.18M against 1.96–2.17M, fork + join 178–194k/s against 182–197k/s; within the noise, and in line with M12's 3.08–3.11M / 2.17–2.25M / 200–202k. No regression: M13 changed no runtime core file. The full check collects 17,350 tests and passes (8 skipped). Deferred, with the reason (each recorded in the cluster entries above): the asyncmy workaround that clears a private unbuffered-result flag (no public API upstream); ClickHouse's curried `with_query_id` / `with_clickhouse_settings` (data-first only, §4). The other deferrals of this pass were fixed in M14 loose-ends.

### M16 — Do what Effect does

**Scope.** The user decided "do everything Effect does": where pyfect differs from Effect it changes to Effect's behaviour, except for language mappings and what Python can't express. Three clusters. `decided-behaviour`: (1) `S.Literal(1)` takes `1.0`, (2) `sliding(0)` with a parked taker, (3) `S.is_int`'s safe bounds, (4) the cluster's and event log's camelCase JSON keys, (5) §5 item 5, strictness above Effect, (6) M15's leftovers (an explicit `None`, self-aliases, integer-like keys). `section4-audit`: every §4 row in a class, class (c) changed to Effect's in priority order, named rows decided with evidence. `guards-flakes`: the title guard as a test, flakes with no known cause, the xfails and skips explained, the "Still open" list walked, the docs site built. **Exit criteria:** each item done or its reason named; every §4 row classed, checked by `tests/test_parity_section4.py`; every Effect title ported or omitted with a reason, checked by `tests/test_effect_titles.py`; what is left named, here. This list was first written down in the completeness pass, from the brief the clusters were given.

**M16 status (2026-11-07): done, with the §4 rows and titles below open.** Counts at the end of M16: a 250, b 90, c done 48, c open 74 (the 76 in the review entry below is before `guards-flakes` moved the `toIso` and `toDifferJsonPatch` rows to `c done`). The 74: 39 security hardening, 7 Effect bugs, a bare number as a `Duration` and custom `PubSub` strategies, all open for the user's decision (§5, items 12 to 15), and 26 not reached, each with its reason in its row. Not reached means work left: OTLP, `Date` and `URL` parsers, `path.win32`, `StandardSchema`, the JIT and AOT compilers, `Zip`'s inflater, ICU zones, `watchfiles`, `perMessageDeflate`, `RpcServer.Protocol` transports, `StdoutConfig`, ClickHouse options and the others named in §4. They are follow-up work and were kept out of this milestone on purpose.

Titles: 87 titles NOT_PORTED, all open: `Otlp*` 71, `SqlRunnerStorage` chaos proxy 9, HTTP/2 on Node's server 3, client `Upgrade` 2, `handleAll` 2. Two limits of the guard, so that number is a floor. It files 179 more titles under §4 rows that are themselves `c open` (JIT and AOT 142, `~standard` 37), since they say the same results or Python's other interop point. And it matches by text: of the 13,833 titles counted per file, 10,865 are carried by a test, and 1,646 of those share their text with a title in another Effect file, so one port stands for all of them. That hid the `ObjectKeyword` gap below: one port of that title stood for the others in `toCodec`, `toFormatter`, `toJsonSchemaDocument` and `toCodeDocument`. Reasons for the other 2,294 omissions were written per rule, not checked title by title.

The completeness pass walked the list and each cluster's report, ran what the `section4-audit` review had not compared with Node, and found, each failing first: `S.ObjectKeyword` was exported but only half a schema: `to_codec_json` said `Expected object | array | function` where Effect says `Expected array | object` and took any value inside, `to_codec_string_tree` and `to_encoder_xml` raised, `to_json_schema_document` gave `{}`, `config.schema` raised the wrong error, it refused bytes (a `Uint8Array` is an object), and it, `S.Void` and `S.Undefined` had no representation node, so `to_code_document` raised and the JSON form was a bare declaration (`tests/oracle/test_schema_object_keyword.py`, with Effect's titles; `docs/coverage/schema.md` called all three not ported). `S.Defect()` wrote a value JSON has no form for as text, where Effect's `encodeUnknownAsJson` follows `JSON.stringify`: a function is `null` or left out, a set `{}`, `NaN` `null`, a cycle dropped, a nested error `{name, message}`, an instance its attributes, a `datetime` `toISOString` (`tests/oracle/test_defect_values_without_json.py`, each from Node, plus the cyclic case of Effect's own "toCodecJson / Defect"); `logger._text.to_json`, the `formatJson` of the loggers, changed with it. `path.posix.parse("/..")` has the ext `.` and the name `.` in Node, whose `..` check misses a first segment (one case in 4,000 generated paths compared with Node, none after the port, `test_posix_parse_matches_node_around_dots`). `to_logging` never named level 5 `TRACE`, comparing `Level Trace` with `Level 5`; the test passed in a full run only because importing uvicorn names it (`test_to_logging_names_trace_with_no_other_library_loaded`, in a fresh interpreter). Added: `assert_type` pins for `replicate`, `on_exit_primitive`, `Queue.flush`, `flush_unsafe`, `Cause.raisable`, `cause.done`, `tar.FarDate` and the unknown promise functions, a test of `encode_unknown_promise`, the extras `http2`, `intl` and `regex` in the README and the installation page, and two guards so hand-carried counts stop drifting: `tests/test_parity_section4.py` checks the class counts above and the row counts in §5, `tests/test_effect_titles.py` the NOT_PORTED total above. Stale docstrings that still called `DecodedDefect`, `ObjectKeyword`, `Void` and `Undefined` absent are corrected.

Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, alternated against the pre-M16 commit `b7e17a3` on one interpreter): fork + join had regressed, 190–191k/s against 197–200k/s in six runs each at load averages of 7, which the `section4-audit` review had read as noise (193k against 197–200k) and the `guards-flakes` run recorded as 190k. The cause is M16's: `SyncScheduler` split its loop so each batch took the lock twice, and read the clock after each batch for the stall clock. It now takes the batch under the one lock and reads the clock only with a stall timeout: 196k/s against 196k/s in four runs each. Map chain 3.02–3.05M against 3.06–3.08M and generator steps 2.19M against 2.18–2.19M in the same runs, within noise. Three runs at the end on a quieter machine (load 4): 3.06–3.10M map steps/s, 2.17–2.25M generator steps/s, 197–200k fork + join/s, against M15's recorded best of 3.10M / 2.22M / 199k.

Deferred, with the reason: the 26 not-reached §4 rows and the 182 titles (95 since ported by `schema-titles`, below) are follow-up work, too large for this pass. Open for the user: §5, items 12 to 15. Not verified: the `guards-flakes` review has no entry in the cluster reports, as its session ended before it reported; what it did is the entries below. Not compared with Node by anyone since the audit: batch keys, `date_time.intl` beyond the audit's 90-case matrix, `http.hypercorn_server` beyond its tests. The event log over ndjson has no Effect peer to compare with, as Effect cannot run it (§4). The flakes under load named in the entries below were not chased again here. The docs site builds (`generate:api`, `build`, `check-links`); `check:layout` was not run. Full check: ruff and pyright clean, pytest 21,658 tests, 21,646 passed, 8 skipped (the same 8 as in the title guard entry), 4 xfailed.

**Schema titles (2026-11-07): done; the 95 open `Schema` titles are ported.** `SchemaAST` projections (`tests/oracle/test_schema_ast_projections.py`; the contextual-projection titles in `test_schema_representation_to_multi.py`): `ast.copy` / `replace_checks` / `replace_encoding` / `map_or_same` (Effect's identity-preserving updates; `Suspend` refuses checks), `to_type` as Effect's (memoized, identity when nothing changes, encoding checks promoted only when no part transforms, else only the `"~structural"` ones: lengths, sizes, key counts, `is_property_names`; a `Suspend`, a class's or an `Enum`'s decoding counts as transforming), `flip` and `to_encoded` through the context owner so contextual copies share one reference, `Declaration(encoding_run=)`, `collect_sentinels` (with `Declaration`'s `"~sentinels"` annotation, `Suspend`, function-valued sentinels), `get_index_signature_keys`, and `IndexSignature` rejecting a parameter that is not a key node (`ValueError` "Invalid index signature parameter ..."; `Enum` and `Suspend` keys are Python's extension). `Schema.test.ts` (`test_schema_leftovers.py`, `test_schema_middleware.py`, `test_schema_class.py`): `S.with_decoding_default` / `with_decoding_default_type`, `S.BrandSchema` and `from_brand(identifier=)`, `Literal.transform` / `Literals.transform`, `name` on `S.Error` / `S.TaggedError` (identifier, tag or class name; a `name` field wins), the Promise adapters rejecting a defect with a `RuntimeError` (failing first); 23 titles went to §4 rows (`mutableKey`, `UndefinedOr`, `mapFields`, subclassing) and "explicit ignore" stays a Python signature. `toJsonSchemaDocument` (`test_to_json_schema_document*.py`): `S.is_ecma_pattern(source, flags)` with Effect's flag rules, `from_json_string(reviver=, replacer=, space=)` and `G.stringify_json`, a flipped codec's JSON form (it used the owner's encoding, failing first), and keys optional on the type side only: `S.optional_key(s).pipe(S.encode_to(t, ...))` is an `OptionalKey` whose encoded key is required (`ast.encoded_optional`). Where a Python string counts code points, the length and surrogate titles keep exact bounds instead of Effect's UTF-16 loosening. Also ported: 15 titles the guard matched only by text in another file (`SchemaAST` 7, `Schema.test.ts` 5, `toJsonSchemaDocument` `Date`; `toFormatter` `TemplateLiteral`; `toCodec` "arg: message: string") and 16 hidden by a module docstring. Fixed on the way: candidate selection was exponential on a union graph with shared members (2^depth, `test_from_json_schema_document_ref_imports_a_deeply_shared_union_graph` took 58 s on main); `_candidates.py` now memoizes per index.

**M16 title guard, flakes and leftovers (2026-11-06): done, with the open titles named.** `tests/test_effect_titles.py` now does what the milestone scripts did by hand: it reads every `it` / `test` title under `pyfect-ref/effect/packages/**/test` (12,102 distinct, `tests/_effect_titles.py`) and fails on one that is neither carried by a test (docstring, name, parametrized id or table string) nor listed in `tests/_effect_title_omissions.py`, or named by a module docstring, the older form; an omission that matches nothing also fails. It is skipped without the checkout, as in CI. Of the 13,833 titles (counted per file), 2,476 are in that list and 492 are named by module docstrings. 182 of the 2,476 are the honest `NOT_PORTED` bucket: `Otlp*` 71, `SchemaAST` projections 42, `Schema.test.ts` 30 not checked one by one, `toJsonSchemaDocument` 23, `SqlRunnerStorage` chaos 9, HTTP/2 on Node's server 3, client upgrades 2, `handleAll` 2; these are open, not decisions. The rest are JavaScript, TypeScript tooling or §4 `a` rows (Python's own types, Hypothesis, psycopg); those reasons were written per rule, not checked title by title. Ports the guard forced: all of `EffectEager.test.ts` (`tests/oracle/test_effect_eager.py`, which found that `fn_untraced_eager` did not forward the call's arguments to pipeables, failing first), `flush` / `flushUnsafe` in `Queue.test.ts`, `rechunk` and `rechunk` of a large chunk, "unbounded fail", `Char`, "should throw on async decoding".

The review tightened the guard and ported the codec families it had left open. The builder's matcher took a few of a title's words inside any sentence as a port, and read the omission list's own patterns as ports; a title must now stand between a docstring's separators, and that file is not read. New: `S.to_iso` and family, `S.to_differ_json_patch` / `S.Differ`, `S.to_encoder_xml`, `S.to_codec_go_json`, each with Effect's titles (`tests/oracle/test_schema_to_iso.py`, `test_schema_to_differ_json_patch.py`, `test_schema_to_encoder_xml.py`, `test_schema_to_codec_go_json.py`), and the `toCodecArrayFromSingle` titles (`test_schema_to_codec_array_from_single.py`, the builder's "toCodecEnsureArray" was a wrong name for them). Those ports found, each failing first: `to_codec_string_tree` re-derived an array-from-single node (Effect's `stopAt`), and `to_codec_array_from_single` was not idempotent through a `Suspend` or a struct; a derived codec dropped a key's constructor default and did not put the key's context on its last link (`_codec_derivation.py`, Effect's `makeCodecDerivation`); a tuple refused to encode a position whose `encoding_strategy="omit"` left it out (`Missing key`); `S.Undefined` and `S.Void` were the text `null` in a string tree, where Effect keeps `undefined` (an empty XML element). `instance_of` still says its class where Effect says `<Declaration>`, pinned in `test_to_codec_json.py`.

The flakes under "Still open", with what was found. (1) `test_retries_an_early_activity_...`: the cause is a repeated request id, 1 run in 1,024. A runner sends under its random machine id until it registers, the next engine's memory runner storage hands out id 0, and at one millisecond of the TestClock the second request repeats the first's snowflake, so storage takes it as a duplicate and every fiber waits; the `StalledError` is what that looks like at the 5 s deadline. Forcing the random value reproduces it at once, and Effect's own test hangs the same way with `Math.random` at 0 (hook timeout under vitest), so the runtime keeps Effect's behaviour and the port's `make_test_workflow_engine` starts an engine on a shared storage a millisecond on (`test_engines_sharing_a_storage_never_reuse_a_request_id`, failing first). Found on the way and kept: an interrupt at the deadline lands while its `save_reply` waits for `release_reply`, which only the interrupted test opens, so the runner now reports `TestTimeoutError` when a finalizer sticks after the deadline (`tests/test_testing_harness.py`, failing first); `tests/conftest.py` freezes what collection imported (`gc.freeze`, generation 2 pauses of up to 2.5 s fell to 0.7 s), and the stall clock no longer counts time spent running a task (`scheduler.py`, failing first). (2) `test_resumes_a_terminal_stream_on_the_replacement_owner[sqlite]` had two causes. `harness.kill` closes the runner's scope, which waits for the gated handler, while that runner's read loop goes on: a read right after the replacement's `reset_shards` claims the message for ten minutes (`last_read`) and drops it as `closing` (2 of 176 at 44 processes). A dead process does not read, so the harness's killed runner now reads nothing. Effect's `Sharding.ts` has the same skip, so a live runner in that state strands a message there too. Then, at the same load (13 of 220): a lock operation past its 500 ms makes a healthy owner give up its shards, as in Effect, so the handler runs a second time before the kill and an exact count of 2 never holds. The port now reads the owner just before the kill and asks for one more entry after it; the title's "exactly one reply" stays asserted, the exact count of entries does not. 0 of 176 afterwards. (3) `test_the_client_routes_by_the_runners_of_its_last_sync[sqlite]` (1 of 48, logging `Shard lock storage is unhealthy`): the same lock timeout moves the owner during the call. It is a pyfect-only test; it now retries when the owner moved; 0 of 48.

Structs read objects: `S.Struct` and `S.Record` read any object's attributes as JavaScript reads properties (`ast/_objects.py`, enumerating its own), so `User.json` encodes a `User` instance (QA GQL-3), compared with Node; numbers, strings, lists, functions and `None` stay refused, and the new `NotAnObject` marks `undefined` stand-ins. The 2 strict xfails rechecked under vitest on the current checkout: `force-interrupts a stuck handler ...` and `rebalances on runner addition ...` fail on pg and mysql in Effect, "All fibers interrupted without error" and "The stopped runner did not hand over its entity". The docs site's failed link check was a stale `.generated/pyfect.json` missing the M15 modules; `scripts/check-api.mjs` now says so before building. Skips: SQLite has no advisory locks (1) or reserved lock connection (4), `pwsh` is not installed (1), Effect's own `it.skip` port (1), `Never` has no strategy (1). Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, one run on a loaded machine): 3.05M map steps/s, 2.20M generator steps, 190k fork + join/s, in line with M15. Full check: ruff and pyright clean, pytest 21,614 tests, 21,602 passed, 8 skipped, 4 xfailed.

**M16 section 4 audit, review (2026-11-06).** Running the builder's changes against Node and porting Effect's custom-`Scheduler` tests in their own form (`tests/oracle/test_custom_dispatchers.py`, 18 titles, where earlier ports only swept budgets) found, each failing first: a custom `Scheduler` was first asked at the second step of a run, so a pause right after a resume landed in the uninterruptible region and `Fiber.interrupt` hung on a held dispatcher (it is now asked before each step, as Effect's loop does); `MixedScheduler` lost a wake made outside any fiber (`offer_unsafe` with a parked taker), since it read the run from the running fiber; `run_sync` flushed every fiber where Effect flushes the root's own dispatcher, so a child that yields succeeded (Node: `AsyncFiberError`), while a queue a child offers to still wakes the root, as its dispatcher is the maker's; `ManagedRuntime.run_sync` gave `AsyncioRequiredError` for an awaitable and flushed children too (it now shares `run_sync`'s rule; held tasks go on at the next drive); `S.Void` refused to encode a value and read any JSON, `S.Undefined`'s JSON form said `Expected undefined` for bad input (Node: `Expected null`); `TaggedUnion.match` and `match_or_else` had no curried form; `on_exit_primitive` had no `interruptible` and its finalizer could not fail; `and_then` called an effect that is also callable; `as_cache` had no `require_services_at`; `effectify` read `[]` and `{}` as no error; `(0, 1.5)`, `(inf, -inf)`, `{seconds: NaN, days: 1}` and fractional nanoseconds as a `Duration` differed from Node; `Cause.pretty` named a value that is no exception `pyfect.cause.UnhandledError` (Node: `Error: x`, `Error: Unknown error: null`). Older pins changed: `tests/test_stack_frames.py`, `tests/oracle/test_tracer.py` and the `S.Void` pin in `tests/type_checks.py`. Reclassified to `c open` as the row did not hold: a bare number as a `Duration` (it was `c done`), `Schema.Duration` and its JSON (`timedelta`), `FileSystem.watch` (an extra could back it) and `1` with `True` in a decoded set or map (`b`). The rows left for a decision that the standing instruction settles (match Effect) now match it, and their dogfood additions are gone: `Decision.make` with a repeated object, an unknown tool's `InvalidOutputError`, AI errors' `describe_schema_error`, the MCP `{"result": ...}` wrapping and its client unwrap, `SocketOpenError.message` (`target` stays a field), `Migrator.from_record`'s skip. Counts now: a 250, b 90, c done 46, c open 76.

**M16 section 4 audit (2026-11-06): classification complete, the `c open` rows remain.** Every row of §4 now has a class in its fourth column (`tests/test_parity_section4.py` fails on a row without one, or a class (b) row naming no reason): `a` same behaviour in another spelling, `b: reason` Python cannot express it, `c done` changed to Effect's, `c open: reason` differs and not changed. Largest changes: `run_sync` now dies with `AsyncFiberError` where an effect does not finish synchronously, and `fx.run_blocking` is the old blocking runner; a defect is any value and `Cause.squash` returns it as it is (`Cause.raisable()` wraps for raising); `S.ErrorInstance` is an exception and `S.DecodedDefect` is gone; `EffectScheduler` / `MixedScheduler`; `stream.range` includes its end; bare `@fx.fn` has frames; `path.posix` follows Node; `date_time.intl` (extra `intl`, on babel); `http.hypercorn_server` (extra `http2`); `\p{...}` and a variable width behind on the extra `regex`; `RpcGroupWith[M]`, which `research/typing/rpc_group_2_descriptor.py` shows pyright types honestly. A `c open` row states why it was not changed: an Effect bug or a security difference left for a decision, or work not reached. A bare number as a `Duration` is still seconds where Effect's is milliseconds: Python's own APIs take seconds and every timer in the library and its users would change, so it needs a decision.

**M16 decided behaviour (2026-11-06): done.** Where pyfect differed from Effect on purpose, it now does what Effect does, each case run under Node first. `S.Literal(1)` takes `1.0` and `S.Literal(2.0)` takes `2`, bools stay apart, and a decode keeps its input (3,000 imported documents against their generated code: 93,000 checks, none differ; 1,500 against Effect's importer: none differ). `sliding(0)` leaves a parked taker parked. `S.is_int` is `Number.isSafeInteger` and exports Effect's safe bounds (16 JSON Schema outputs identical to Node); `S.Int` stops at `2 ** 53 - 1`. The cluster's and event log's classes have Effect's camelCase keys through `S.field(..., key=)`, replacing `wire_name`; no storage migration, as pyfect is unreleased. Live ndjson: every cluster call both ways with Effect, including `EntityAddress`, `SingletonAddress`, `CronPayload` and `AlreadyProcessingMessage` in an RPC; the event log does not run over ndjson on Effect itself (§4). Construction-time `ValueError` for concurrency, counts and capacities is gone (§5.5), about 75 calls compared with Node. A self-alias reached by a parse dies instead of raising, on an absent key too; the importer reads documents in JavaScript's key order; a class field left at `None` is left off the wire. Older pins changed: `tests/test_queue.py` (`test_offer_to_a_sliding_queue_of_capacity_zero_wakes_a_waiting_taker`, `test_negative_capacity_is_rejected`), `tests/test_schedule.py` (`test_negative_times_is_rejected_at_the_call`, `test_recurs_and_up_to_reject_negative_times`), `tests/test_concurrency.py` (`test_invalid_concurrency_is_rejected_when_building`), `tests/test_for_each_interruption.py` (`test_invalid_concurrency_is_still_rejected_when_building`), `tests/test_tx_coordination.py` and `tests/test_tx_pubsub.py` (`test_a_negative_capacity_is_rejected_*`), `tests/test_schema_out_of_range.py` (a `Millis` duration past the safe integers is now `Expected an integer`, as under Node), `tests/oracle/test_http_api_node_original.py` (no longer strips the safe bounds from Effect's fixture), `tests/oracle/test_mcp_non_object_results.py`, `tests/oracle/test_mcp_protocol_adapters.py`, `tests/test_pool.py` (`test_bad_sizes_are_rejected_when_the_pool_is_described`), `tests/test_schema_concurrency.py` (`test_a_concurrency_below_one_is_rejected_when_parsing`), `tests/test_entity.py` (`test_to_layer_rejects_a_concurrency_that_is_not_positive`), `tests/test_datagram_socket.py` (`test_receive_buffer_capacity_must_be_positive`), `tests/test_schema_checks.py` (the literal table, `S.Int` past `2 ** 53`), `tests/oracle/test_json_schema_import_review.py` (`test_s_literal_keeps_its_kind`), `tests/oracle/test_event_log_message.py` (snake_case paths), and the integer pins of `test_openai_structured_output`, `test_to_json_schema_document`, `test_schema_representation_json_schema`, `test_open_api`, `test_pydantic` and `test_schema_filter_groups`. The review found and fixed, each failing first against Node's values: a `Pool` whose `min` passes its `max` kept `min` items where Effect's `Math.min(Math.max(...))` keeps `max`, and a growing `Pool` with concurrency 0 raised `ZeroDivisionError` where JavaScript divides to Infinity or NaN; a self-alias at an absent tuple position succeeded where Effect dies; `S.Int` rejected `1.0`, the builder's left-over (now `1`, §4); `S.to_strategy` raised on any class with an optional field, since `Undefined` and the event log's bytes had no strategy; two fields could share a key and lose data (now `TypeError` when the class is made); `TokenParsed`, `RateLimitExceeded`, `CircuitOpen`, `ChatNotFoundError`, and GraphQL's `TransportError` and `ResponseError` kept snake_case or `null` where Effect has camelCase keys or none; `metric.summary`, `insert_batch_size`, `prepare_cache_size` and `race_all()` still raised at construction. `tests/test_schema_wire_keys.py` walks every schema class and fails on a key that is not camelCase. Older pins changed by the review: `tests/test_schema_checks.py` (`S.Int` and `1.0`), `tests/test_metric.py` (`test_summary_arguments_are_checked_when_it_is_made`), `tests/test_structural_combinators.py` (`test_race_all_first_needs_an_effect`), `tests/oracle/test_graphql.py` (`retryAfter`). Effect's own classes were fed the JSON pyfect writes for the cluster's, the event log's and these classes under Node: each decodes and re-encodes to the same text, but for the `Transferable.Uint8Array` fields Effect itself cannot read (§4). Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, three runs alternated against the pre-M16 commit on one interpreter, on a machine under load): 2.99–3.04M map steps/s against 2.91–3.03M, 2.12–2.16M generator steps against 2.04–2.14M, 193–200k fork + join/s against 166–192k; no regression. Full check: ruff and pyright clean, pytest 21,189 passed, 8 skipped, 4 xfailed. Runs at load averages above 14 from other work failed timing-bound tests that pass alone (`test_rebalances_on_runner_addition_*`, four `test_usage_ttl_retention_*`, `test_from_json_schema_document_ref_imports_a_deeply_shared_union_graph`, whose 42–48s of a 60s budget is the same before M16); no cause beyond the load was found.

### M15 — Encoding and the M14 deferrals

**Scope.** What M14 deferred: `encoding/SchemaBinary` and what hangs on it (`RpcSerialization.layerSchemaBinary`, the event log's frames, cluster runners on Effect's default wire); `Xml`, `Zip`, `Tar`; `SchemaRepresentation`'s importer and `toCodeDocument`; HTTP/2 and brotli; `RpcGroup.merge` and group-wide `middleware`. **Exit criteria:** every title of `encoding/SchemaBinary.test.ts`, `Xml.test.ts`, `Tar.test.ts`, `Zip.test.ts`, `schema/representation/{fromJsonSchemaDocument,fromJsonSchemaMultiDocument,fromJsonSchemaAnnotations,toJsonSchemaMultiDocument,toCodeDocument,toCodeDocument.annotations}.test.ts`, `schema/jsonSchemaRoundTrip.test.ts`, `http/SchemaRepresentation.test.ts`, `eventlog/EventLogMessage.test.ts`, `rpc/RpcSerialization.test.ts`, `platform/node/test/{RpcServer,NodeHttpCompression,cluster/SocketRunner}.test.ts`, ported or omitted with a reason, and no export missing in `docs/coverage/encoding.md` or `schema.md`.

**`encoding/SchemaBinary` and what it blocked (2026-11-05): done.** `pyfect.encoding.schema_binary` (a package: layouts, fingerprints, encoder, decoder, parser, channels) has `to_codec`, `to_codec_direct`, `encode_unknown_sync`, `encode_many_unknown_sync`, `parser`, `encoder`, `encode`, `decode`, `duplex`, `field_id`, and `wire_name` (a Python addition: a snake_case attribute with the identity of Effect's camelCase field, `field_id` of the name's hash). The wire is pinned: `tests/oracle/schema_binary_wire.ts` under Node wrote the frames in `tests/oracle/_schema_binary_frames.py` (default and fingerprint mode, row runs, unions, native declarations, a dictionary stream, every RPC envelope); pyfect writes those bytes and reads them, and Effect read pyfect's under Node. Every title of `SchemaBinary.test.ts` is in `tests/oracle/test_schema_binary*.py`, and the test file's fingerprints hold. `RpcSerialization.layerSchemaBinary` is `serialization.layer_schema_binary`, over HTTP and sockets. `cluster_socket.layer` and `cluster_http.layer` now default to it as Effect's do (`serialization="ndjson"` for the old wire; pyfect's only differed because this was missing). `envelope.OpaqueHole` and `Reply.Encoded` are a bytes leaf under it, and envelope and reply ids are bigints on the encoded side, so a `Notify` payload has Effect's bytes (pinned in `test_envelope.py`); the JSON form is `envelope.PartialJson`, with strings as before. `eventlog`'s stand-in JSON codec is gone: entries, changes and write frames decode the hex Effect's test pins, and pyfect's encode equals it. The omitted titles and their reasons are in the docstrings: two count hits of a key cache that is a JavaScript optimization, one needs non-enumerable keys. Python differs where its values do: no `Date` schema, symbols or bigint literals; `timedelta` has no infinity and `datetime` covers years 1 to 9999, so reading Effect's values past those fails; a `datetime` is written in whole milliseconds, as Effect's are, and a `timedelta` read from Effect's nanoseconds floors to microseconds; a decoded array is a tuple. This also fixed a parity bug it found: an index signature's key selection ignored `disable_checks` (`schema/ast/_objects.py`). The cluster integration harness now runs on `SchemaBinary` as Effect's does; that showed the runner server ending a stream by reading `_tag` of a reply that is a frame under this format, so it now reads the typed reply, and `reply.OnWire` makes pyright reject reading a serialized reply's fields (storage has its JSON form through `_serialize_json`). Effect's `RunnerServer` reads `reply._tag` on the encoded value the same way, so under binary its queue is not ended by the exit reply. A reviewer's pass compared bytes against Effect with a randomized schema generator and ran live peers both ways (RPC over socket and HTTP, a socket runner with entity calls, encrypted event log sync). It found and fixed: `Redacted` crashed the cycle check; a zoned datetime at offset 0 failed inside a union; a recursive struct in an optional key and a required plus optional field of one schema hashed or numbered differently (Effect memoizes one optional node per schema); integers from 2^53 failed in a union with `Number` and no `BigInt`; `Redacted` of a schema that also appears directly was two nodes (`flip_encoding` now keeps the owner); `field_id` and `wire_name` rejected schema classes; and schemas that Effect peers read used snake_case identities or plain optionals: the event log RPC messages, `EntityAddress`, `SingletonAddress`, `AlreadyProcessingMessage`, `CronPayload`, the workflow parent key and resume payload, and `traceId`, `spanId`, `sampled` of a request envelope, which are `Schema.optional` (a union with undefined) in Effect. The frames are pinned in `tests/oracle/test_schema_binary_interop.py`. The e2e RPC suites and the request defect isolation cases also run on SchemaBinary. Those schemas' JSON forms keep snake_case keys.

**`encoding/Xml`, `Zip` and `Tar` (2026-11-06): done.** `pyfect.encoding.xml`, `pyfect.encoding.zip` and `pyfect.encoding.tar` (packages, with `encoding/_archive.py` for the queue of input chunks and the decoder channel the two archive formats share) are ports of Effect's streaming codecs, not wrappers of `xml.etree`, `zipfile` or `tarfile`: input is decoded as it arrives, bodies stream both ways, and a body is read while the entry stream runs (`"BodyConsumed"` afterwards). Every title of `Xml.test.ts`, `Tar.test.ts` and `Zip.test.ts` is in `tests/oracle/test_xml.py`, `test_tar.py` and `test_zip.py`; the fixtures are copied to `tests/oracle/fixtures/{tar,zip}`. `tests/oracle/test_archive_node.py` runs 1,499 cases of `_archive_corpus.py` (the fixtures, including zip64 and sparse GNU and PAX; truncations and corruptions of them; malformed PAX, base-256, DOS times, Unicode paths and data descriptors; traversal entries; encoder inputs; XML documents, split into single bytes as well) and compares entries, body hashes, and error kinds and messages with what `archive_oracle.ts` recorded from Effect under Node; `fixtures/archive_oracle.json` is that record. Archives of stored entries and all tar archives are byte-identical to Effect's. The differences that remain are the two §4 rows below: 3,000 mutated deflate streams, compared once under Node and not committed, gave the same error kind and bytes every time, so the row about `zlib` is about message wording and one kind at a truncated end. As in Effect, `Zip.decode` and `Tar.decode` return paths as written (absolute, `..`, drive letters, backslashes, control characters, duplicates), `Tar.decode` returns symlinks that can redirect later entries, `Zip.encode` allows `..`, `Tar.encode` also allows absolute paths and backslashes, `Zip.decode` does not read the central directory, so other tools can see different entries, and skipping a deflated body inflates it, bounded only by the caller. This port keeps all of that, pins the paths in `test_paths_are_returned_as_written`, and says so in the module docs and in the streams guide, which has a checked example of validating entries. `Xml` does not expand entities.

**`Xml`, `Zip` and `Tar` review (2026-11-06): done.** About 50,000 further programs ran through Effect under Node and this port without being committed: mutated tar and zip archives (headers re-checksummed, split at random), generated XML documents as text (surrogate pairs split) and as bytes, the boundaries of every character class in 15 positions of a document, encoder and `elements` inputs. Nothing differed but the two §4 rows on `zlib` and `datetime`. So did 73 lifecycle programs on both runtimes (bodies read late, twice and concurrently, failing and dying inputs, interrupts, empty inputs, a channel run twice) and how much input a partial read pulls; a zip each side wrote read back on the other. Found and fixed, each with a test that failed first (`tests/oracle/test_archive_lifecycle.py`). (1) Effect's queue hands out views; `zlib` copies its unused input. A whole archive in one chunk therefore cost a copy of the rest of it per entry and per 64 KiB of output: 3.1 s against 0.19 s for 21 MB in 10,000 entries, growing with the square. The inflater now takes at most 64 KiB of input at a time, which also needed `ByteQueue.unread` to rewind inside a chunk. (2) `Zip.encode` died with `OverflowError` on an `mtime` in the last hours `datetime` holds, in a negative offset. (3) `xml.elements("feed")` passed the types and silently matched nothing, one name per letter; it raises `TypeError`. (4) `safe_extract.py` missed drive letters such as `C:/x`; it reads paths with `PureWindowsPath`. (5) `Entry` was invariant, where Effect's is covariant: pyright reads a frozen dataclass's `__replace__` as using its type parameters, so `tar.file(...)` could not sit in a `list[tar.Entry[ValueError]]` at all. It declares covariant `TypeVar`s now, pinned in `tests/type_checks.py`. 47 mutants found 16 survivors: 13 were real test gaps, now pinned with values from Node (the upstream lock, the done value, a body's scope closing before the next entry, output chunk shapes, the repeated `ZipError`, `DEFLATE_HEADROOM`, PAX record lengths at digit boundaries, the star prefix, a ZIP64 field holding only the compressed size, the oversize check at a later chunk, yields while deflating, output left in `zlib`); 3 are equivalent (collecting small deflated bodies only changes how many compressor calls run, `_PENDING_RUN` is a tuning constant, and one mutant repeated what the next pull does). The three decoders' body pulls are cases of `tests/test_decided_results.py`. No runtime core file changed in this work, so `benchmarks/runtime.py` is not affected by it.

**`SchemaRepresentation` importer, `to_code_document` and the M14 deferrals (2026-11-06): done.** `R.from_json_schema_document` and `R.from_json_schema_multi_document` port `fromJsonSchemaDocument` (`schema/representation/_import/`: import nodes, keywords, intersection, translation and the final AST). All 278 titles of `fromJsonSchemaDocument.test.ts`, `fromJsonSchemaMultiDocument.test.ts`, `fromJsonSchemaAnnotations.test.ts` and `jsonSchemaRoundTrip.test.ts` are in `tests/oracle/test_from_json_schema_*.py` and `test_json_schema_round_trip.py`, generated from the TypeScript with the compiler API and checked against Effect's expected strings verbatim: `tests/oracle/_effect_code.py` renders a representation as Effect's generated TypeScript, for comparison only, and `tests/oracle/_json_schema_validator.py` stands in for Ajv. JSON Schema's `pattern` is an ECMAScript regular expression in Unicode mode, so `S.is_ecma_pattern` / `S.compile_ecma` translate it onto `re`; `tests/oracle/test_ecma_pattern.py` holds a table from Node. The five `toJsonSchemaMultiDocument.test.ts` titles waiting for the importer or Ajv are ported, and found a bug: rewriting a reference to its canonical definition reformatted what followed the definition, where Effect keeps its spelling (`#/$defs/Box%25%2Fproperties...` became `#/$defs/Box%25/properties...`); fixed in `schema/_jsonschema/_compile.py`. `R.to_code_document` writes Python: every node, every built-in check and declaration, the `to_code` annotation for others, and `R.to_code_module` lays the result out as one module. `tests/oracle/test_to_code_document.py` runs the generated code for 109 schemas (it accepts and rejects what the source does, and gives the same document back); `tests/oracle/_generated_code_sample.py` is the generator's output for all of them, checked by `uv run pyright` and pinned to the generator by a test; a test fails when a built-in reviver has no case. Effect's 150 `toCodeDocument` titles expect TypeScript strings and were left out at this point; the M15 completeness pass ports them in Python. `RpcGroup.merge` is multiple inheritance (`tests/test_rpc_group_merge.py`); group-wide `RpcGroup.middleware` stays per RPC, tried again in `research/typing/rpc_group_1_merge_middleware.py` (§4). Brotli joined the live platform through a `brotli` extra (27 lines in `http/_brotli.py`), porting the two titles of `NodeHttpCompression.test.ts` it blocked; HTTP/2 stays out (§4). `scripts/api_coverage.py` over all seven maps (collections, config-data, core-a, core-b, encoding, schema, sql-drivers) lists no export missing.

**`SchemaRepresentation` importer review (2026-11-06): done.** About 34,000 generated JSON Schema documents ran through Effect under Node and this port without being committed (with `patterns` in all three modes, `includeAnnotationKey`, `onEnter`, multi documents, pointers and aliases), comparing the error text, the code `tests/oracle/_effect_code.py` renders, accept or reject and each failure's text for 40 values, and the JSON Schema exported back; about 80,000 generated patterns ran through `new RegExp(p, "u")` and `S.compile_ecma`. 23 mutations of the importer and the translator were tried, and tests caught 22; the other removes a clause another clause implies. Beyond the differences recorded in §4, this found the following, each fixed with a test that failed first (`tests/oracle/test_json_schema_import_review.py`, `test_ecma_pattern.py`, `test_to_code_document.py`, `test_node_http_compression.py`). (1) `{"const": 1}` rejected `1.0`, and `{"enum": [1.0]}` rejected `1`, also inside a union or as a struct's discriminator: JSON Schema calls them one number, as JavaScript does, but `S.Literal` keeps `int` and `float` apart. An imported number literal is now a `JsonInt` / `JsonFloat`, which `same_literal` compares by value; `S.Literal` itself is unchanged (§4). Also, `maxItems: true` closed a one-element tuple, as `True == 1` in Python. (2) `S.is_property_names` stopped at the first bad key unless `errors="all"`, where Effect stops only on `errors="first"`, so a failure listed one key of several. (3) `(a)?\1b` did not match `b`: a reference to a group that took no part is empty in JavaScript and fails in `re`; it is now `(?(1)\1)`. (4) Patterns Node rejects were accepted: a quantified lookaround (`(?=a)*`), a range ending at a class escape that expands (`[\0-\s]`), and `\100`, which `re` read as `@`. `(?<$a>x)` is now accepted, as in Node; the back-reference to a group a loop clears is now refused, where it silently differed. (5) Generated code: `None | None` raised where a `TypedDict` evaluates it; `.annotate(self=1)` raised for any annotation named `self` (`annotate`, `annotate_key`, `annotate_encoded` take their receiver positionally now); a reference named `AType` or `Schema0` overwrote another's alias or a root, and a root name in `to_code_module(names=)` could do the same (`ValueError` now); unused typing imports failed pyright's strict mode. 120 modules generated from those documents go through pyright with one error left: an empty property name, which pyright rejects in any `TypedDict`. (6) A compressed stream at brotli's default quality ran on the server's thread; `compression_live` compresses streams with `blocking`, as `node:zlib`'s transforms run off the main thread. Not changed: `S.is_int` exports `{"type": "integer"}` without Effect's safe-integer bounds, as `_jsonschema/_checks.py` says, since a Python `int` has none; a document naming the property `constructor` is read as an own key where Effect's parser sees `Object.prototype.constructor` and rejects `{}`; property order keeps the document's where JavaScript moves integer-like keys first.

**M15 completeness pass (2026-11-06): done; M15 is complete but for the deferrals at the end of this paragraph.** A title-by-title script over the exit-criteria files finds each title ported or omitted with a reason. It found one large gap: the 150 titles of `toCodeDocument.test.ts` and `toCodeDocument.annotations.test.ts` were left out as "TypeScript strings", and `http/SchemaRepresentation.test.ts` was not ported at all. Those expected strings carry over once spelled in Python, so all are now in `tests/oracle/test_to_code_document_titles.py`, `test_to_code_document_annotations.py` and `test_http_schema_representation.py`, omitting only what Python has no schema for (`Undefined`, `Void`, `Symbol`, `ObjectKeyword`, `UniqueSymbol`, `Date`, `File`, `FormData`, `URLSearchParams`, `mutableKey`, bigint literals), each named with its reason in the module docstrings. Porting them found four bugs, each seen failing first except the type change. (1) `to_code_module` raised `NameError` on a non-recursive reference to a recursive one, since it defined all non-recursive references first; definitions now follow what their runtime reads outside a lambda. (2) A declaration's type was `object` where Effect gives the real one: `Option`, `Result`, `Cause`, `Exit`, `Redacted`, `URL`, `Duration`, the date and time zone types and the rest now write a Python type with its import. (3) `~identifier` and `~constructor` annotations were dropped, where Effect's excluded list keeps them. (4) `Headers`, `Cookie`, `Cookies` and `UrlParams` had no `to_code`, so any document holding one raised. Also: `is_greater_than_big_int(int("10"))` is `is_greater_than_big_int(10)`. The walk also ported "provides handler context for generated rpc handlers" (`test_entity_proxy.py`, on the real sharding where Effect stubs it), pinned `SingletonAddress`'s frame from Node, the one address the review left out, and added the §4 rows the reviews only mentioned (`S.is_int`'s bounds, `None` in `SchemaBinary`, snake_case decode paths and JSON forms). The check of the API reference the builders skipped ran with fumadocs-python installed: `tests/test_api_reference.py` passes and the generator runs; the site itself was not built. Coverage: the weighted total is about 94% (9,383 / 100, 93% after M14); the Schema row's 93% and the Platform row's 99% stay judgments, not measurements; `scripts/api_coverage.py --check` exits 0 for all seven maps, and many of Schema's entries are §4 rows rather than ports. Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, six runs alternated against the pre-M15 commit `865711e` on one interpreter, on a machine under load from other work, which spread single runs widely): best of each, map chain 3.10M steps/s against 3.07M, generator steps 2.22M against 2.24M, fork + join 199k/s against 198k/s, in line with M14's 3.06–3.07M / 2.14–2.21M / 191–202k. No regression; M15 changed no runtime core file. Full check: ruff and pyright clean, pytest 20,987 passed, 8 skipped, 4 xfailed (18,166 passed after M14). Deferred, with the reason: an HTTP/2 server (needs a second server adapter, §4); group-wide `RpcGroup.middleware` (no mapped types, §4); `\p{...}` and the other ECMAScript patterns `re` cannot match alike (refused, §4); the camelCase JSON forms of the cluster and event log classes (done in M16). The questions on `S.Literal(1)`, `sliding(0)` and §5.5 were decided in M16. Not committed: the fuzz and differential runs behind the cluster reviews. The title script was committed in M16 (`tests/test_effect_titles.py`).

### M14 — Final parity

**Scope.** No list was written ahead for M14: it finishes what M12 and M13 deferred and what a walk of this file finds. Four clusters: `mcp-streaming` and `mcp-server-rest` (every title of `packages/effect/test/ai/McpServer/` and its `McpConformance/` suites), `openai-generated` (the `OpenAiClientGenerated` block of `packages/ai/openai/test/OpenAiClient.test.ts`), and `loose-ends` (the rest of "Still open" that could be settled, `encoding/Toml.test.ts`).

**M14 completeness pass (2026-11-05): done; M14 is complete but for the deferrals at the end of this paragraph.** A title-by-title script over those files (428 titles, 26 files) finds each in a test docstring but two, both accounted for: `SCHEMA preserves the log level, logger name, and data` is pinned in `test_mcp_schema.py`, and `Workerd.test.ts`'s template title is adapted to an unadjusted test clock (its module docstring says so). Running the same script over all of Effect (13,007 titles in 627 files, tools and browser packages included) and reading what it could not match in the core and RPC files found two groups of omissions (10 titles) whose reason had gone stale, below. Found and fixed, each with a test that failed first. (1) `input_required`: Effect checks client capabilities on a handler's raw requests and decodes afterwards; pyfect decoded first, so a malformed request from a client missing the capability got -32603 where Effect gives 400 and -32021, and a request the check cannot read (unknown method, no `params`, not an object) got `MCP feature handler failed` where Effect's check throws and answers `Internal error`. The ported table ran these only with every capability declared. 48 requests run through both under Node now agree on status, code, message and data (`test_mcp_input_wire.py`); `reverse_policy.required_input_capabilities` reads raw requests as JavaScript would. (2) `RpcServer.layerProtocolStdio` had no port and no entry here, only an omission note: `pyfect.rpc.server.layer_protocol_stdio` / `make_protocol_stdio` (`rpc/server/_stdio.py`, 5 titles in `test_rpc_server_stdio.py`). (3) Porting it found a lost message in `with_run`, which every server transport uses: a message written while the buffered ones were replayed went into a buffer nobody read again, so stdin ending during a request's startup never ended the client. Effect iterates the live buffer; so does pyfect now. (4) The 5 framed HTTP response titles of `RpcServer.test.ts` were omitted for want of a lazily read body, which `router.in_memory_client` now gives (`test_rpc_server_http_buffer.py`); they passed on first run. Unbounded buffering takes 1,000 yields where Effect's test takes 100, as a fiber's turn here is shorter. (5) `PrometheusMetrics.layerHttp` is `pyfect.http.prometheus.layer_http`; the note saying pyfect has no HTTP router predated M10c. Checked with no change: `HEAD` through `router.in_memory_client` keeps its body, which is what Effect's `HttpApiTest` client does (only `toWebHandler` strips it, and `web_handler` does here too). New coverage where the reviews called it thin, all passing on first run: `test_mcp_asyncio.py` runs a session POST parked on its reader, a listener and a client leaving with a full body on the asyncio runtime; `test_ai_openai_client_generated.py` interrupts an SSE operation and sees its body closed. Docs gained `OpenAiClientGenerated`, `disable_preemptive_shutdown`, `layer_http` and the stdio protocol. Coverage: Platform goes from a recorded 96% to 94%, as it had not subtracted the unported `encoding` modules; the weighted total is 93% when computed from the table, against the 90% carried by hand. Export maps: 0 missing in `collections`, `config-data`, `core-a`, `core-b`, `sql-drivers`; 3 of 781 in `schema`; 33 in `encoding`. Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, three runs alternated against the pre-M14 merge `9fdd71f` in a worktree on one interpreter, at a load average of about 3 after the suite ended): map chain 3.06–3.07M steps/s against 3.06–3.08M, generator steps 2.14–2.21M against 2.19–2.23M, fork + join 191–202k/s against 197–198k/s, in line with M13's 2.93–3.07M / 2.12–2.18M / 178–194k. No regression; M14 changed no runtime core file. The full check: 18,166 passed, 8 skipped, 4 xfailed (ruff and pyright clean). An earlier run of this pass failed only on its own new layer reuse case, which compared the metrics text with empty while other tests had filled the process's registry; the case now checks the status. Deferred, with the reason (M15 has since ported the first). `encoding/SchemaBinary` and what hangs on it (`RpcSerialization.layerSchemaBinary`, `EventLog`'s `payloadSchemaBinary`, cluster runners defaulting to ndjson): a 5.3k-line codec that needs its own builder pass. `Xml`, `Zip`, `Tar`: streaming codecs with standard-library blocking equivalents, needing a port or a §4 decision. `SchemaRepresentation.fromJsonSchemaDocument` / `fromJsonSchemaMultiDocument` / `toCodeDocument`: 257 titles and a design question. HTTP/2 and brotli: not in the standard library, and brotli would be a new extra. Group-wide `RpcGroup.middleware` and `merge`: a group is a class whose attributes keep their own types. Needs a human: `sliding(0)` with a parked taker, and §5's remaining questions. Untested and recorded by the reviews: three surviving mutants judged equivalent, `with_redacted_headers` around the generated client's `make`.


**M14 loose-ends (2026-11-05): done.** (1) The real-SIGINT flake had two causes, both CPython's and neither in the runtime, both reproduced with a script under load. A signal handled while the garbage collector runs a `__del__` on the main thread raises `KeyboardInterrupt` inside it, where it is "ignored in deallocator" and gone, so the run ends with nothing raised (the reported `DID NOT RAISE`; 51 of 100 with cyclic garbage). A signal that lands after the last check but before a blocking C call starts does not interrupt the call, and the handler waits for it to end (7 of 20,000 at 32 busy processes). No Python code can close those for a user's own blocking call or `__del__`. The test now blocks on `signal.set_wakeup_fd`'s descriptor, which the C handler writes, with the collector off; 3,000 of 3,000 passed under load. (2) `rpc/client/_core.py` and `sharding/_singletons.py` start a fiber and then register it, in the same order as Effect's `RpcClient` (`entries.set`, `runForkWith`, `addObserver`) and `stopSingletonUnsafe` (`runForkWith`, `set`, `addObserver`), and `runForkWith` evaluates at once in both, so nothing changed. Only the RPC server's handler fiber, fixed in the dogfood follow-ups, registers first in Effect. (3) Each with a failing test first. MySQL: `bind` takes the session's `NO_BACKSLASH_ESCAPES` from the status flag each result reports (asyncmy's `escape` reads the same flag), and `--` before any whitespace or control character is a comment as in MySQL's lexer (`tests/test_mysql_bind.py`, and on a server in `test_mysql_client.py`). `ANSI_QUOTES` is not followed: the protocol reports no flag for it, so in that mode a `"name\"` identifier is still lexed with the backslash as an escape. SQL Server: the number 0 (or NaN) for `connect_timeout`, `cancel_timeout` or `connection_retry_interval` means tedious's default, as Effect's `options.x ? ... : undefined`, while `timedelta(0)` stays no time at all, as `Duration.zero` is truthy there; `make`'s `SELECT 1` check still uses 0 as its timeout, as Effect's `??` does. Interrupting a connection while it opens now returns at once and a connection made afterwards is closed on the worker's thread, which keeps going until the driver gives up: up to `connect_timeout` per attempt, with its transient-login retries (`Worker.call` with `release`). Effect's `conn.close()` stops tedious at once; pytds has no such call before its socket exists. D1: a `batch` entry holding several statements gives a result for each, with no transform past the statements sent, as Effect's `responses.map` (it was a `ValueError` defect from a strict `zip`); `tests/oracle/_d1_fake.py` is the evidence for how D1 answers, not the real API. libSQL: a failed remote `BEGIN` closes its stream (`tests/test_libsql_hrana.py`). (4) The walk: `encoding/Toml` was missing and unlisted, and is now `pyfect.encoding.toml` (a port of Effect's parser, which accepts what `tomllib` refuses; every title of `Toml.test.ts`; messages compared under Node), also used by `Primitive.file_parse`, which used `tomllib`. Notes that no longer held were corrected in place: the M4 `from_pubsub` difference, and the M10d MySQL / MSSQL persistence titles, which ran once M13 gave them a server. Dated status paragraphs above that say "not ported yet" are history; later paragraphs supersede them: the M12 and M14 mcp-streaming MCP lists by M14 mcp-server-rest, the M10c worker protocols by M10d, the M10f and M10g lists by the M10g follow-up. Still true, with the reasons where they are written: group-wide `RpcGroup.middleware` and `merge` (a group is a class whose attributes keep their own types: add middleware per RPC, and merge by inheriting); HTTP/2 and brotli; a schema for `RpcClientError` (socket reasons are plain errors here, and `S.instance_of` keeps such a failure out of `dehydrate`); `to_codec_json` memoized across calls; a model instance through a variant struct. (5) `scripts/api_coverage.py` over every map: 0 missing in `collections`, `config-data`, `core-a`, `core-b`, `sql-drivers`; 3 in `schema` and 33 in the new `encoding` cluster (`Xml` 7, `Tar` 9, `Zip` 7, `SchemaBinary` 10), all under "Still open". The builder's full check passed: 18,110 tests, 8 skipped, 4 xfailed.

**M14 loose-ends review (2026-11-05): done.** Running `toml.parse` against Effect's parser under Node on 120,000 generated documents and 60,000 date-times found what the 8 ported titles did not: `IndexError` for a table under an empty array (`a = []` then `[a.b]`, Effect's `Cannot redefine existing key`), `OverflowError` for an offset date-time whose UTC instant leaves `datetime`'s range (now `None` from `_js_date.parse`, so `astimezone(UTC)` cannot raise for any of its callers, also `Schema`'s date getter), and the column of an invalid escape at the end of the text or before an astral character. The port of "parses Unicode escapes at the scalar value boundaries" held the characters themselves instead of `\uD7FF` / `\uE000` escapes, so it did not test them; it now does. After the fixes the only differences left are documented ones: years outside 1 to 9999 are refused, integers stay exact where JavaScript rounds them to a double, and the message of an invalid escape holds the whole astral character where JavaScript has half of it. `Primitive.file_parse`'s failure said `ValueError:` where Effect's says `SyntaxError:` (TOML, YAML, JSON), and INI's `Cannot create property` is now a `TypeError`, as in JavaScript; both pinned against Node. The MySQL binder skipped placeholders in `/*! ... */` and `/*M! ... */`, which the server runs as statement text. NaN as a SQL Server driver timeout is unset, as Effect's truthiness check has it. `disablePreemptiveShutdown` had no reason to stay deferred: `uvicorn_server`'s `disable_preemptive_shutdown=True` interrupts requests in flight at once. Checked and left as the builder reported, each against Effect's source or under Node: the start-then-register order of `RpcClient` and `stopSingletonUnsafe`, `from_pubsub`'s `[1, 2]`, `sliding(0)`'s parked taker (still a human call), `D1Client.batch`'s `responses.map`, every coverage count. No runtime core file changed.

**M14 mcp-server-rest (2026-11-05): done.** The McpServer features M12 left out, from Effect's `McpServer`, `McpSchema` and `mcpProtocol/v2026_07_28`.
