(1) `subscriptions/listen` and resource subscriptions (`ai/_mcp/subscriptions.py`): a listener subscribes before it acknowledges, holds its own 64-message backlog, and on overflow releases the upstream listener first, so a transport write that stays blocked cannot pin it; HTTP streams get `: keepalive` every 15 seconds and end at overflow, stdio sends a correlated `notifications/cancelled`. `layer_http(allow_subscriptions=False)` turns it off. 2026-07-28 discovery now advertises `listChanged` / `subscribe` as Effect does. (2) List-change coalescing with revisions in `McpServerService.notify`: changes of a kind in one scheduling window go out once, deferred; a listener ignores revisions at or before the one it started at, so startup registrations are not replayed; a broadcast delivers to each peer on its own fiber, so a stalled client holds up nobody. (3) `input_required` round trips: `mcp_schema.InputRequired` (its overloads require `input_requests` or `request_state`), continuation decoded for stateless `tools/call`, `prompts/get`, `resources/read` and placed on `McpRequestContext`, capability checks with -32021 (`reverse_policy.required_input_capabilities`), legacy revisions refuse it. (4) `Mcp-Param-*` mirroring of `x-mcp-header` parameters (`_mcp/param_headers.py`, `_mcp/routing_header.py`). (5) `mcp_server.layer_http_request_scoped`, typed to take only `ProtocolAdapter[Literal["2026-07-28"]]`, with a runtime refusal for untyped callers. It shares `serve` with `layer_http`; the request's scope closing interrupts the work, also while a body sits unread, and the request's services win over the layer's. Streams are bounded (16) and stdio's output is bounded (8), as in Effect; a watcher ends the server if the stdio writer dies so bounded writers cannot hang. Tests: `test_mcp_conformance_subscriptions.py`, `test_mcp_conformance_multi_round_trip.py`, `test_mcp_request_scoped.py`, the additions to `test_mcp_conformance_{tools,prompts,resources}.py`, harness `_mcp_features.py`. Adapted titles carry their reason in the module docstrings: the custom RpcServer transport titles use `allow_subscriptions=False` and two stdio harnesses on a shared server, the workerd timer title uses an unadjusted test clock, "abort" is the requesting fiber's interrupt or the client's scope closing. Effect's Tools, Resources, Prompts and stateless BaseProtocol conformance suites run a second time in request mode (`test_mcp_request_mode_suites.py`). Review pass, against 233 requests sent to the same server under Node: input requests and responses are decoded with 2026-07-28's own schemas (`_mcp/input_wire.py`, `test_mcp_input_wire.py`), so what goes out and what a handler sees are stripped as in Effect, a URL elicitation has no `elicitationId`, and a malformed request is -32603 "MCP feature handler failed"; a malformed `subscriptions/listen` is -32602 even with subscriptions off; stdio `resources/unsubscribe` on 2026-07-28 is method not found; a subscription event carries only `uri` and `_meta`; every POST's stream is bounded at 16, sessions too, with a broadcast offered to each stream on its own fiber. Remaining difference: an integer argument past a double's range in a parameter header is a -32020 mismatch here, -32602 in Effect (JSON turns it into Infinity, which its schema refuses). Docs: Subscriptions and Request-scoped servers on the MCP page, with checked snippets on the live server.

**M14 mcp-streaming (2026-11-05): done.** The MCP HTTP conformance titles M12 deferred for want of a streaming HTTP test harness. Finding: pyfect's in-memory client (the one `HttpApiTest.groups` builds) already streamed response bodies, one chunk per pull; the M12 note that it buffers whole bodies was wrong, only the tests read whole bodies. The client is now public as `router.in_memory_client` / `router.layer_in_memory_client` (`http/_in_memory.py`, shared with `http_api.test`; row in section 4; a Testing guide section with a checked snippet), pinned by `tests/test_http_in_memory_client.py` (a chunk read while the handler still writes, the handler at most one chunk ahead of the reader, a reader that stops closing the request scope, an unread body closing with the client's scope, unhandled errors as defects, route services from the request's context) and `tests/type_checks.py`. The test side ports Effect's `makeHttpHarness`, `initializeHttpSession`, `readMcpHttpResponse`, `makeMcpSseReader` and the `McpConformance` service (`tests/oracle/_mcp_http.py`), and `McpTestPeer` (`tests/oracle/_mcp_peer.py`: a real client of a stdio session, since pyfect's reverse client belongs to the session). Ports, every title of each suite for every revision whose file runs it: `test_mcp_conformance_transports.py` (TransportsTest, 37 titles, 108 tests), `_base_protocol.py` (BaseProtocolTest, 30 titles, 88 tests), `_utilities.py` (UtilitiesTest, the 14 titles `test_mcp_batch_cancellation.py` did not have, 47 tests), `_elicitation.py` (ElicitationTest, 13 titles plus 2 Python-only cases pinning `v2025_06_18`'s `elicit` projection, 30 tests), and three `McpServer.test.ts` titles that read a live stream (disconnect, DELETE during a POST, DELETE during an elicitation) in `test_mcp_server.py`; `test_mcp_batch_cancellation.py` now reads its SSE events before cancelling, as Effect's test does. Fixes, each with a test that failed first: session ids are dashed UUIDv4 strings; a request whose params are not an object gets InvalidParams (a notification with such params is ignored); 2026-07-28 `prompts/list` on a server without prompts is MethodNotFound with HTTP 404 (Effect's `failWithHttpStatus`, `_mcp/http_status.py`); a cancelled reverse request sends `notifications/cancelled` with its id; URL-mode elicitation (`ElicitRequestURLParams`; `ELICIT` and `ReverseClient.elicit` take either mode) with 2025-11-25's per-mode capability gating (an empty capability means form) and 2025-06-18's projection (form only, sent without `mode`); a reader leaving a request's event stream interrupts the request; a POST pending when its session is deleted gets 404. A second pass ported the other titles the M12 notes held back for this harness, and found the real bugs: `test_mcp_adapters_http.py` (ProtocolAdapters.test.ts, 33 titles; Effect's `makeFixture` / `makeLowLevelFixture`, whose own test file was run under vitest from the checkout's root to confirm it passes), `test_mcp_conformance_sampling.py` (SamplingTest, 11 titles, which M12's notes never listed), `test_mcp_logging.py` (the background HTTP titles and `MUST advertise logging`), `test_mcp_server.py` (URL elicitation completion, the legacy HTTP session log level, stateless stdio notifications, batches after a stateful fallback), `test_mcp_http_admission.py` (the 6 `v2026_07_28.test.ts` discovery and envelope titles that do not need `input_required`). Fixes, each with a test that failed first: on HTTP a notification no request owns reached nobody (`broadcast` was a no-op); it now goes on every open stream of the session (Effect's `clientStates.keys()`), which also delivers `notify_elicitation_complete`; a stateless request has no `McpServerClient`, even through a toolkit handler whose layer was built during a session's request (`toolkit.map_handler_contexts`, Effect's line in `registerToolkit`); client capabilities drop `elicitation` before 2025-06-18; malformed `clientInfo` metadata is InvalidParams; audio before 2025-03-26 and resource links before 2025-06-18 are InvalidParams, after the handler ran, for tools and prompts; a result that is not JSON is InternalError, not a crash of the request; the top-level tool title comes from the annotations' when absent, 2025-03-26 keeps it in annotations, annotations list their defaults; legacy `inputSchema` keeps only `type`, `properties`, `required`; nested resource `_meta` is dropped before 2025-06-18; progress `message` is dropped on 2024-11-05; discovery advertises only object-valued extension settings; `create_message` gates tools and context on 2025-11-25 and refuses tool shapes earlier (`_mcp/reverse_policy.py`). Still deferred from M12, outside this cluster: subscriptions (SubscriptionsTest 17, ResourcesTest 3, the subscription titles of McpServer.test.ts and LoggingTest), `input_required` (MultiRoundTripTest 7, 4 of `v2026_07_28.test.ts`, 2 of ProtocolAdapters), `layerHttpRequestScoped` (8), list-change coalescing, and `Mcp-Param-*` mirroring. Five McpServer.test.ts titles drive RpcServer custom transports pyfect does not expose, and one needs weak references to a dict; each is listed with its reason in `test_mcp_server.py`. Review fixes, each failing first and each checked against Effect's harness under vitest: a caller aborting a POST before any event left the request running (it now interrupts, including a tool that has written nothing); a reader leaving a batch's event stream left the batch's requests running; a batch pending when DELETE ends its session got an empty event stream instead of 404. `_answer` and `_batch` now share `_answer_on_stream`, so a single request and a batch cannot drift apart again. `router.in_memory_client` died on a route miss; a failure a server answers with a response (`Respondable`, so 404 for a route not found) is now that response, and any other unhandled error stays a defect. Also ported: `should preserve the June wire transcript when requests use stdio` (the old note called it covered) and `should reject a tools/call request when its required routing name is missing`, which the tools note had lumped in with `Mcp-Param-*`. Mutation pass over 29 lines of this cluster's logic: three survived and now have tests (a finished request's stream left on its session, a request's own progress on 2024-11-05, the hint defaults on 2025-06-18 and later). The full suite, which had not finished when the cluster was first reported, then found three more gaps: `in_memory_client` and `layer_in_memory_client` took a non-layer argument until it was used (they now check it when called), `layer_in_memory_client` had no layer reuse case, and `toolkit.map_handler_contexts` is public so it stays in `__all__`. Two tests outside this cluster gave false failures and were fixed: the real-SIGINT test failed in any run started with `&` by a non-interactive shell, which ignores SIGINT (it now installs Python's default handler itself), and the persistent collections' linearity check compared medians, which load can still skew (it now compares the fastest of three runs). Performance (`benchmarks/runtime.py`, one run under load): map chain 2.88M steps/s, generator steps 2.05M, fork + join 192k/s, near M13's figures; this cluster changed no runtime core file.

**M14 openai-generated (2026-11-05): done.** `pyfect.ai.openai.client_generated` (`OpenAiClientGenerated`, `make`, `layer`, `layer_config`: the service Effect's `OpenAiClientGenerated` module builds) over `pyfect.ai.openai.generated.client`, all 379 operations of OpenAI's API. `scripts/gen_ai_client.py` now reads both Effect generators' output: OpenAI's `Generated.ts` differs from OpenRouter's in that path parameters go through `__makePathRequest` (emitted as `_c.encoded_path(...)`: percent-encoded as `encodeURIComponent`, an empty or dot-segment parameter or one that cannot be encoded fails with an `InvalidUrlError` without sending), a 2xx can be `decodeBinary` (`bytes`), successes can be per exact status (`inviteUser`'s 200 and 201, a union type), some operations take no `options`, interface members are overloaded, and streams run on the transformed client (`executeStreamRequest`, detected in the source and emitted as `transform_streams = True`; OpenRouter's still skip it). The checked-in OpenRouter client is byte-identical. `Target.client_title` names a provider's client, `generate` takes its target explicitly, and `--check` (run by `tests/test_gen_ai_schemas.py` when the reference checkout is present) covers the new `client.py` as every other generated file; a test pins that every target has a client. `_requests.prepare_requests` is the URL, credentials and headers both OpenAI clients share. Python spelling: Effect's `CreateContainer` and `Getinputtokencounts` are `create_container` and `getinputtokencounts`, and its oddest name, `CreateanAPIkeyforaserviceaccount`, comes out as `createan_ap_ikeyforaserviceaccount`, because method names follow mechanically from the operation ids. `tests/oracle/test_ai_openai_client_generated.py` ports the 3 `OpenAiClientGenerated` titles and tests what they do not reach (path encoding and checks, binary bodies, SSE, documented errors, stream transforms, `layer_config`); `tests/type_checks.py` pins the types. Effect's `make` wraps only construction in `withRedactedHeaders`, so at call time the `openai-organization` and `openai-project` headers are not redacted by this client, as in Effect; `client` redacts them per call. Review: all 379 operations were run under Node against Effect's own `Generated.ts` and compared with Python over a mocked client (6822 combinations of status and body, plus SSE bodies and decoded values): same method, URL, query, headers, JSON body and outcome. It found that `instanceOf(Blob)` was typed `bytes`, which `form_data_record` cannot send, so all 20 multipart operations died or failed pyright: a file is now `pyfect.http.body.File`, and an object field of a form is stringified to `[object Object]` as `FormData.append` does, where it raised `TypeError`.

## 4. Pythonic decisions (intentional mappings and skips)

| Effect module / feature | pyfect mapping | Why | Class |
|---|---|---|---|
| In-memory HTTP test client (Effect's tests pair `HttpRouter.toWebHandler` with a `fetch`; `HttpApiTest.groups` builds one for an API) | `router.in_memory_client(routes)` / `router.layer_in_memory_client(routes)`: the client `HttpApiTest.groups` uses (`http_api.test` shares it), for any router; response bodies stream chunk by chunk as the handler writes them | pyfect's HTTP client has no `fetch` to hand a web handler; this is the same streaming in-memory pair inside the fiber runtime, so tests keep the virtual clock | a |
| Provider config options (`strictJsonSchema`, `fileIdPrefixes`, `structuredOutputs`, `disableParallelToolCalls`, `midConversationSystemMessages`) | snake_case keys of the model's config (`strict_json_schema`, ...), removed before the request; API keys keep the provider's own spelling | Python spelling for the library's options; the wire names are the provider's | a |
| Prompt part options and response metadata (`{"anthropic": {"cacheControl": ...}}`, `{"openai-compat": {"reasoningField": ...}}`) | the same camelCase dictionaries | They are Effect's wire format, shared with prompts encoded by Effect | a |
| `answers.department` (a record keyed by decision name, typed by the definition) | `answers[department]` typed by the decision object (as a `Tag` keys a context), or `answers["department"]` typed as any answer | Python cannot type a dictionary's values per key from a dynamic mapping; the decision object carries its label type | b: no per-key dictionary value types |
| Rate levels `[{label, description}]` or `[label]` | `criteria={label: description}` or `[label, ...]` | A mapping keeps labels unique and literal-typed | a |
| One decision object under two names in `Decision.make` | the same: `make` takes it; `answers[decision]`, which Effect has not, is the first name's answer | names are the mapping's keys, as the record's | c done |
| Provider answers as plain objects (a rate answer may carry a `label`) | frozen dataclasses (`ProviderClassifyAnswer`, `ProviderRateAnswer`, ...); a rate answer has no label, it is always the most probable level | Extra keys cannot leak; the label Effect derives when absent is derived always | c open: AI tier, not reached: provider answers are frozen dataclasses where Effect takes plain objects |
| `undefined` optional fields and absent usage | `None` | Python has no `undefined` | a |
| `AnthropicStructuredOutput.toCodecAnthropic` / `OpenAiStructuredOutput.toCodecOpenAI` (`{ codec, jsonSchema }`, throwing an `Error`) | `to_codec_anthropic` / `to_codec_openai` returning `StructuredOutputCodec(codec, json_schema)`, a `NamedTuple` and so a `CodecTransformer`, raising `ValueError`; the providers use them as Effect's do | A typed pair that unpacks; the JSON Schemas differ from Effect's only where pyfect's JSON Schema does: no `pattern` from `is_pattern` (a Python `re` pattern) and no safe-integer bounds on `Int` | c open: AI tier, not reached: raises `ValueError` where Effect throws `Error`, and returns a `NamedTuple` |
| `Chat` (a service object) and `Chat.Persisted` (the chat spread into a new object with `id` and `save`) | `chat.Service`, and `chat.Persisted`, a subclass that saves after each call; `ChatNotFoundError.chat_id` | One class per role; the subclass keeps the chat's methods and their overloads instead of copying them | a |
| `toolCallValidation: "deferred"` with `ExtractToolParametersMode` choosing the call types | `tool_call_validation="deferred"` accepted only with `disable_tool_call_resolution=True` (an overload), where call names are typed `str` | Effect ignores deferred validation while resolution is on; a type error says so instead | a |
| Package name `effect` on npm | distribution `pyfect-dev` (installed from git), import name `pyfect`; extras as `pyfect-dev[extra]`; `typing-extensions` the one dependency | PyPI's `pyfect` is an unrelated project; `tests/test_install.py` installs the base wheel in a new venv and checks every module imports or names its extra | a |
| `xUnsafe` (`pollUnsafe`, `offerUnsafe`, `currentTimeMillisUnsafe`) | `x_unsafe` (`poll_unsafe`, `offer_unsafe`, `now_unsafe`); Effect's own `unsafeX` names keep the prefix (`unsafe_preserve_checks`, `unsafe_secure_json_parse`, `unsafe_ttl_to_expires`) | Earlier milestones wrote `unsafe_x`; `tests/test_public_names.py::test_unsafe_names_use_effects_suffix` keeps one spelling | a |
| `runSync` of an async effect throws `AsyncFiberError` | `run_sync` of an effect that does not finish synchronously gives `Die(AsyncFiberError(fiber))` (the same under `ManagedRuntime.run_sync`, where `from_async` still dies with `AsyncioRequiredError`); `fx.run_blocking` is the Python addition that blocks until it is done | Same as Effect; `run_blocking` has no counterpart because JavaScript cannot block | c done |
| Array, Iterable, NonEmptyIterable, Chunk, Tuple, Record, Struct | `list`, `tuple` (a Chunk is a `tuple`), `dict`, `itertools`, comprehensions, built-ins (`len`, `sorted`, `zip`, `any`, `all`, `sum`, `min`, `max`), dataclasses and `copy.replace`; docs/coverage/collections.md names the idiom for each export | Same semantics; `tuple` is an immutable, structurally hashable Chunk | a |
| Number, BigInt, Boolean, String, RegExp, Bytes | `int`, `float`, `bool`, `str` (case conversion in `pyfect.case`), `re`, `bytes`, with operators, `math` and `str` methods | Native, and Python ints are arbitrary precision; `BigDecimal` is ported (`pyfect.big_decimal`) because `decimal.Decimal` rounds every result to its context's precision and keeps `-0` and exponents apart, where Effect's is exact for `+ - *`, divides to 100 digits and compares `2` equal to `2.00` | a |
| Duration | `Duration` takes seconds as a number, a `timedelta`, a string (`"10 seconds"`, `"Infinity"`; an invalid one raises), a `(seconds, nanos)` pair or a `DurationParts` mapping | Forms as `Duration.Input`, parsed once by `to_seconds`. A bare number is seconds where Effect's is milliseconds: see the note in the M16 entry | c open: a bare number is seconds where Effect's is milliseconds, so the same `fx.sleep(5)` differs by a factor of 1,000; every timer in the library, its tests and its users reads numbers as seconds, and a float gives no way to migrate mechanically. The other forms match Node (`tests/oracle/test_section4_review.py`) |
| DateTime (pure parts) | aware `datetime` + `zoneinfo` | Stdlib already has it; naive datetimes rejected at boundaries | a |
| DateTime `Utc` / `Zoned`, `TimeZone.Named` / `Offset` | an aware `datetime` in `UTC`, a `ZoneInfo` or a `timezone`; a zero offset is `timezone.utc`, so a `+00:00` Offset zone and a Utc DateTime are the same value | Stdlib types; only effectful helpers, calendar arithmetic, disambiguation and RFC 9557 strings are added | a |
| `zoneMakeNamed` canonical names from ICU (`Asia/Kolkata` is `Asia/Calcutta`, `EST` is `America/Panama`, `Etc/UTC` is `UTC`) | `zone_named` canonicalizes the casing to the tz database's name (`europe/london` is `Europe/London`) and keeps links as written | ICU's link choices change with its version and run backwards to old names; the tz database names are what `zoneinfo` knows | c open: needs ICU's canonical zone table copied from Node and its version pinned |
| DateTime millisecond precision; `endOf` ends on `.999` | milliseconds: `now()` floors the clock to a millisecond, `end_of` ends on `.999`, `add` cuts each sum to a whole millisecond toward the epoch; a `datetime` handed in with microseconds keeps them until an operation cuts them | Same as Effect, checked under Node | c done |
| `DateTime.add(dt, { days: 1.5 })`, `weekStartsOn: 0 \| … \| 6` (0 = Sunday) | calendar units are `int`; `week_starts_on: calendar.Day` (default `calendar.SUNDAY`, Effect's default) | Pyright rejects fractional calendar units and an ambiguous weekday number | a |
| `DateTime.mutate`, `setParts`, `removeTime`, `toEpochSeconds`, `distance`, `formatIso*`, Intl `format*` | `pyfect.date_time.intl` (extra `pyfect-dev[intl]`, on babel): `format`, `format_local`, `format_utc` with `locale`, `time_zone`, `date_style`, `time_style` and the field options; 85 of a 90-case matrix over five locales match Node 24. `mutate` and the rest are the standard library's. babel has no CLDR `atTime` pattern, so a full or long date with a time joins with `, ` where `Intl` writes `at`, and `ja-JP`'s short weekday skeleton has no parentheses | Effect's `Intl`-backed formatting on the same CLDR data; the join is what babel's data lacks | c done |
| `makeZoned(input, { adjustForTimeZone: true, disambiguation })` | `make_zoned(naive_wall, zone, disambiguation=...)`: a naive wall-clock time in, `None` only under `"reject"` (an overload types the others as never `None`) | `fold` means the earlier instant in an overlap but the later one in a gap; the names say what is meant | a |
| Equal, Hash | `__eq__` / `__hash__`, frozen dataclasses | Native structural equality; key TypeVars bounded to `Hashable` | a |
| Order, Equivalence, Combiner, Reducer | `key=` functions, `functools.reduce` | One convention, to be written into CLAUDE.md | a |
| HashMap, HashSet, MutableHashMap, MutableHashSet (and Trie) | ported as classes (`pyfect.hash_map.HashMap`, ...) keyed by Effect's `Equal` (`True` is not `1`, `NaN` equals `NaN`, lists and dicts compare by items, so unhashable values are keys), the mutable ones iterating in insertion order; dual functions are methods, `size` is `len()`, `is*` guards are `isinstance`. `HashMap` / `HashSet` are a persistent HAMT (`pyfect._hamt`) iterating in hash order as Effect's, `Trie` a persistent character trie in UTF-16 key order: an update copies only its path, as Effect's do | A `dict` / `frozenset` would collide `1` and `True` and refuse lists; copying a `dict` per update made a map built one `set` at a time quadratic (16,000 entries took 0.5 s) | a |
| MutableList, MutableRef | `collections.deque`, a plain attribute (or `Ref` for effects) | Identical semantics | a |
| UndefinedOr, Ordering | `X \| None` with `is None` checks; orderings are the ints `-1`, `0`, `1` | Python's own optional and comparison results | a |

| `sql` tagged templates (`` sql`SELECT ${id}` ``) | `sql("SELECT {}", id)`: `str.format` placeholders (automatic, numbered or named, no conversions or format specs) whose values are always parameters, and t-strings on 3.14 (`sql(t"... {id}")`); the template is typed `LiteralString`, so pyright rejects a runtime string, and `sql.unsafe(text, params)` is the explicit escape hatch | Python has no tagged templates; `LiteralString` gives the same "SQL text is written in code" guarantee at check time | b: no tagged templates on Python 3.13; t-strings on 3.14 |
| `@effect/sql-sqlite-node` on `node:sqlite` (synchronous) | `pyfect.sql.sqlite` on `sqlite3`, each connection on its own worker thread; prepared and unprepared statements run the same way (`sqlite3` caches by text, `prepare_cache_size`), `prepareCacheTTL` is not offered, `SafeIntegers` is ignored (Python integers are exact) and `execute_stream` dies with "executeStream not implemented", as Effect's does | `sqlite3` blocks; fibers never block | b: `sqlite3` has one statement cache with no per-statement expiry, so `prepareCacheTTL` cannot be honoured |
| `@effect/sql-pg` with its own wire protocol, codecs (`PgTypes`), TLS and SCRAM | `pyfect.sql.pg` (extra `pyfect-dev[pg]`) on psycopg 3's asyncio API, on a loop thread of its own; values are psycopg's adapters (`config.types` takes an `AdaptersMap`), TLS is libpq's parameters, `application_name` defaults to `"pyfect"` | psycopg is Python's maintained libpq binding; reimplementing the protocol would duplicate libpq without its security fixes | a |
| `multiplex` / pipelining, `maxMessageSize`, `stream` factories | not ported: one statement at a time per session, libpq opens its own sockets | psycopg runs one statement at a time per connection; libpq owns the socket | b: libpq owns the socket and runs one statement per connection |
| a stream abandoned before its end | the session is retired from the pool when the statement was still running, after psycopg cancels and drains it | a late cancel could hit the session's next statement | a |
| `@effect/sql-mysql2` on mysql2 (Node) | `pyfect.sql.mysql` (extra `pyfect-dev[mysql]`) on asyncmy, on the SQL loop thread | asyncmy is async, typed and has server-side prepared statements (`prepare()`), as mysql2's `execute`; mysql-connector-python's asyncio module failed to import on this Python (`ssl.PROTOCOL_TLSv1`), aiomysql is untyped and only has the text protocol | a |
| mysql2's own pool (`createPool`, `poolConfig`) | pyfect's `Pool` (`max_connections`, `connection_ttl` as the idle timeout); `connect_options` passes other driver keywords | one pool type for every driver, with interrupt-safe checkout and replacement of broken connections | a |
| `sqlstring`'s naive `?` replacement for unprepared statements | values escaped into `?` outside quotes and comments; a placeholder count that differs from the values fails | a `?` inside a string literal is data | c open: security hardening, needs a decision |
| `ResultSetHeader.affectedRows` / `insertId`, `DECIMAL` as strings | `ResultSetHeader.affected_rows` / `insert_id`, `DECIMAL` as `Decimal`, `JSON` parsed | Python names; asyncmy's conversions keep exact values | a |
| query parameters in the URL (`?ssl=...`) | rejected when the config is made; driver options go in `connect_options` | one place for each option, checked at the boundary | c open: `?ssl=` parameters in a SQL URL still rejected; not reached |
| a dict or list parameter: JSON in `execute`, sqlstring's `a = 1` pairs / `1, 2` lists in `query` | JSON text over both protocols | one meaning per value whichever protocol `disable_prepared_statements` picks; `sql.in_` already expands lists into placeholders | c open: dict and list parameters in `query` not reached |
| an interrupted statement keeps running on the server | the connection is closed and its thread `KILL`ed from a side connection | an abandoned statement would hold its locks | c open: security hardening, needs a decision |
| `@effect/sql-mssql` on tedious (Node) | `pyfect.sql.mssql` (extra `pyfect-dev[mssql]`) on python-tds with pyOpenSSL, each connection's calls on a thread of its own | python-tds speaks TDS in pure Python as tedious does, with typed RPC parameters, output parameters, transaction-manager requests and attention cancels; pymssql and pyodbc need FreeTDS or an ODBC driver installed, and their cancel and transaction control go through those libraries | a |
| tedious's `Request` / `TYPES` / `addParameter` / `addOutputParameter` | `TYPES`, `Parameter[A](name, type, options)` bound by calling it, `procedure.make(name).param(...).output_param(...)`, `Result.output[parameter]` typed by the parameter | a value can only be bound to a parameter of its type, and an output is read at its type, checked by pyright | a |
| `authentication.type` (NTLM, Azure AD), `domain`, `multiSubnetFailover`, tedious's 15 s `requestTimeout` | not offered: SQL logins only, and a request runs until it ends or is interrupted | python-tds has no Azure AD token flow; a timeout is `.timeout(...)` on the effect, which cancels the request on the server | b: python-tds has no Azure AD or NTLM flow |

| `@effect/sql-libsql` on `@libsql/client` (Node, the native `libsql` package locally) | `pyfect.sql.libsql`: local databases on `sqlite3` (each connection on a thread of its own), remote ones over Hrana over HTTP v2 on httpx (`pyfect-dev[httpx]`); the SDK is the async `LibsqlSdk` protocol, awaited on the SQL loop thread | no native dependency; the client needs only execute, transactions and close, which the HTTP pipeline protocol covers | a |
| `ws:` / `wss:` URLs (Hrana over WebSocket) | served over HTTP / HTTPS at the same address | sqld and Turso answer both protocols on one listener; one transport keeps the client small | a |
| `intMode` (`number` / `bigint` / `string`), `concurrency`, `encryptionKey`, `syncUrl` / `syncInterval` (embedded replicas), `fetch` | not offered: integers are `int`; the client runs one statement at a time; encryption and replicas need libSQL's native engine | Python integers are exact; Effect's client serializes everything on one semaphore; `sqlite3` has neither | b: sqlite3 has neither encryption nor embedded replicas |

| `InsertValues`: arrays, JSON objects, `Readable` streams in or out of object mode | a sequence of rows, a mapping (one document), `bytes` / `str` (a body already in `format`), or a `Stream` (of rows for JSON formats, of `bytes` otherwise); rows are JSON-encoded with datetimes as Unix seconds, `date` / `Decimal` / `UUID` as text | Python's values; Unix seconds parse as every date and time type | a |
| `@clickhouse/client` options `compression`, `keep_alive`, `max_open_connections`, `log`, `json`, `tls`, `use_multipart_params`, `http_agent` | not offered: no compression, httpx's pool and keep-alive, parameters always in the URL; `http_client=` replaces the agent | Effect sets none of them; the HTTP client is pyfect's | c open: ClickHouse client options not reached |

The importer enforces a pattern only with `patterns="apply"`, as in Effect (`"error"`, the default, rejects it; `"ignore"` skips it) | `re` has neither; a pattern that silently matches differently would be worse than an error. `tests/oracle/test_ecma_pattern.py` is a table from Node | c open: `re` and `regex` keep a group a JavaScript loop clears, and a reference to a group opened later or still open and a name used twice stay refused; `\\p{...}` and a variable width behind run on the `regex` extra |

They share `http/_asgi_listener.py` | `NodeHttpServer` serves whichever server it is given, `node:http2`'s included (`BunHttpServer` wraps `Bun.serve`, whose options name no HTTP/2 in Effect's source); this is that choice (`tests/test_hypercorn_server.py`) | c done |

## 5. Open design questions (need a human decision)

1. **Option type.** Should pyfect use `X | None` everywhere, or add a nesting-safe Option? **Recommend** a tiny `Some[A]` with `type Option[A] = Some[A] | None`, used only where A can itself be None (`Effect.option`, `Config.option`, `Queue.poll`, Brand `.option`). Use `X | None` elsewhere.
2. **Schema strategy.** Decided (M8): a native AST-based Schema, typed as in `docs/SCHEMA_DESIGN.md`. The README said to build on pydantic. **Recommended** a native AST-based Schema with pydantic, msgspec and Hypothesis as boundary adapters. Pydantic can't express a single bidirectional transformation, effectful validators or the issue tree. This decision gates M8 and every platform library after it.
3. **Deferred API names.** Breaking rename to Effect's `done`, `complete` and `complete_with`? **Recommend** yes, before 1.0. The current name means the opposite of Effect's.
4. **`Fiber.interrupt` return value.** Keep returning the Exit (a superset) or return void like Effect? **Recommend** void, plus `interrupt_exit()` if needed.
5. **Strictness above Effect.** Decided in M16: none. Invalid concurrency runs one at a time, `recurs(n<1)` and `times<1` never recur, a capacity below 1 acts as Effect's does (bounded queues rendezvous, `sliding` holds one, a `Cache` keeps nothing), a `Semaphore` or `Pool` without room waits, `min` past `max` in a `Pool` holds `max` items, `PubSub` and a datagram socket's receive buffer die when they run, a summary metric's bad quantile dies on its first update, an event log `insert_batch_size` below 1 writes a row at a time, SQLite's `prepare_cache_size` below 1 keeps nothing, `race_all()` of nothing never finishes (`tests/oracle/test_invalid_sizes.py`, each from Node). What Effect itself throws for still raises: `CircuitBreaker` sizes, `JsonArray` options, `ExecutionPlan` attempts. What pyfect adds without an Effect counterpart keeps its check: the `capacity` of Postgres and Redis listeners (`_subscriber_queue.py`), the connection counts of the MySQL and MSSQL clients.
6. **`Interrupted` and `KeyboardInterrupt`.** Should `Interrupted` subclass `KeyboardInterrupt` so exit code 130 comes for free? **Recommend** no. Handle 130 explicitly in `run_main` so library code can't swallow interrupts by accident.
7. **Minimum Python version.** **Recommend** staying on 3.13 with fallbacks (uuid7 shim, an `sql.fragment` builder). Revisit 3.14 for t-strings and subinterpreters at M10. At M10 wave 4 the sql templates take `str.format` placeholders on 3.13 and t-strings when running on 3.14, so 3.13 stays the minimum.
8. **`catch_tags` typing.** **Recommend** a multi-class `catch_tag`, plus chained `.pipe(catch_tag(...))`, and no dict-based form (pyright can't remove several types from E). Decided (trawl T4): the classes are `Tags[T]` (`pyfect._tags`), one class or a tuple of any number, as Effect's `catchTag` is variadic; pyright solves `T` as their union. Every `catch_tag` / `tap_error_tag` (Effect, Stream, Channel, Layer, HttpClient) uses it, and `tests/test_tag_tuples.py` fails on a fixed-length tuple of classes in any signature.
9. **`Stream.range` bounds.** Decided in M16: inclusive, as Effect.
10. **Lazy iteration in `for_each` (#22).** Matching Effect means a one-shot generator is empty on a second run. **Recommend** matching Effect and documenting it.
11. **`Schema.ErrorInstance` / `Schema.Defect`.** Decided in M16: as Effect, an error JSON decodes to an exception named by its `name` (the class stays `S.ErrorInstance`), any other JSON to itself.
12. **A bare number as a `Duration`.** Open for the user's decision; not changed in M16. `fx.sleep(5)` is 5 seconds here and 5 milliseconds in Effect, a factor of 1,000 (§4 `Duration` row). Matching Effect changes every timer in the library, its tests and its users, and a float gives no way to migrate mechanically. Every other `Duration` input matches Node.
13. **Security hardening (39 §4 rows, `c open: security hardening, needs a decision`).** Open for the user's decision; not changed in M16. In each row pyfect keeps a protection Effect does not have, so matching Effect removes it: body and header limits, `Origin` and redirect checks, CORS with credentials, log and SSE line injection, cookies, session and queue limits, redacted names, SQL string parameters. Search §4 for the phrase to list them.
14. **Effect bugs (7 §4 rows, `c open: Effect bug, needs a decision`).** Open for the user's decision; not changed in M16. Each row is a behaviour of Effect that looks like a bug, which pyfect does not copy: an interrupted fiber's cause repeating its reason, `MaxOpsBeforeYield` of 2 or less never finishing, a ClickHouse query with an empty response body, ClickHouse migrations recorded with `INSERT ... VALUES`, `WorkflowEngine.layerMemory`'s `onExit`, `Sharding`'s read of a message due later, and `RpcClient.make` over a closed `Protocol`.
15. **Custom `PubSub` strategies (1 §4 row, `c open: needs a decision`).** Open for the user's decision; not changed in M16. Effect's strategy interface hands deferreds to strategies, which conflicts with the rule in `CLAUDE.md` that woken waiters retry and no value is handed to a parked fiber.

## 6. Coverage gaps of this audit
- **Failed areas:** none. All 14 groups reported.
- **No divergence checks possible:** Stream, Schema, STM and collections have no existing code, so only missing APIs were listed. Platform was surveyed, not audited per API; M10a (PlatformError, Path, FileSystem, Stdio, Terminal, ChildProcess, HttpClient, socket, net) M10b (sql, the SQLite and PostgreSQL drivers, Reactivity, cli), M10c (HTTP server, HttpApi, rpc), M10d (persistence, workers), M10e (workflow, ai) and M10f (graphql, eventlog, devtools, SqlModel, the cluster's base and storage) are ported with their Effect tests, the rest is not.
- **Type-level parity:** every M1 API has `assert_type` pins in `tests/type_checks.py`. Combinators older than M1 are only partly pinned.
- **Scheduler parity under asyncio vs threads:** mostly exercised through SyncScheduler. Asyncio-specific interleavings (cancellation races, `from_async` interruption) are thinly covered.
- **Effect.test.ts long tail:** closed in the M11 completeness pass: of its 305 distinct titles, 300 appear by title in pyfect's tests; the other five are two template titles ported under their expanded names and the three `effectify` titles (§4).
- **Not audited:** memory beyond the scope-finalizer leak. Throughput is tracked by `benchmarks/runtime.py` (numbers under M2), with no comparison against Effect itself.
- **Thinly covered modules:** Exit and Result were checked against source only, not run against oracle tests.

**M10f eventlog and devtools (2026-10-30): done.** `pyfect.eventlog` (a submodule, `from pyfect import eventlog`: `import pyfect` does not load it, since its servers load `pyfect.rpc`; Effect's `effect/eventlog`): `event` (`Event[P, A, E]`, `make(tag, primary_key=, payload=, success=, error=)`, `add_error`, `Write(event, payload)`), `event_group` (`EventGroup`, an immutable builder; `empty.add(event)`), `event_journal` (`Entry`, `RemoteEntry`, entry and remote ids as UUID v7 / v4 bytes, `EventJournalService` and the memory journal `make_memory` / `layer_memory` with Effect's conflict, anchor and remote-cursor rules; `EntriesById`), `event_log` (`Handlers.handle(event, fn)`, `group`, `Registry` / `layer_registry`, `CurrentStoreId`, `HandlerTransaction`, `Identity` / `make_identity` / identity strings, `group_compaction`, `group_batch_compaction`, `group_reactivity`, `EventLogService.write(event, payload)` / `write_many([Write, ...])` / `entries` / `destroy`, `layer(schema, handlers)`, `make_client`), `event_row` (`make(tag, key="id" | KeyDeclaration(fields, derive), schema=)`, `EventRow.set` / `patch` / `remove` / `key_of`, `handler(row, set=, patch=, remove=)` resolving a late entry against its newer conflicts, `compaction(group, row)`), `event_log_encryption` (`EventLogEncryption`, AES-GCM batches of at most 100 entries / 256 KiB with the batch's ids as associated data, `make_encryption_subtle(cipher=None)`, `layer_subtle`), `event_log_session_auth` (Ed25519 challenge signing and verification, identity root-secret derivation), `event_log_message` (the five `EventLogRemoteRpcs`, `EventLogProtocolError`, `ChunkedMessage` split / join at 512,000 bytes, `WriteEntries` / `WriteEntriesUnencrypted`, the changes codecs, `EventLogAuthentication`), `event_log_remote` (`make_encrypted` / `make_unencrypted` / `layer_encrypted` / `layer_unencrypted` over `EventLogRemoteClient`: one cached authentication per identity, `Forbidden` retried five times with a fresh challenge, chunked writes four in flight, a changes queue per subscription; fully known encrypted batches are not decrypted), `event_log_server` (`ConnectionWrites`, which acknowledges a connection's own writes and keeps their echoes out of its changes, `layer_rpc_handlers`, the authentication middleware), `event_log_server_encrypted` (`Storage`, `layer`, memory storage) and `event_log_server_unencrypted` (store mapping, authorization, `Storage`, `compact_backlog`, `EventLogServerUnencrypted.make_write`, `layer`, `layer_no_rpc_server`, memory storage), and the SQL modules on SQLite and PostgreSQL: `sql_event_journal` (`make` / `layer`: entry, remote-cursor and remote tables created or imported from the legacy layout under a PostgreSQL advisory lock, local writes serialized across processes by `LOCK TABLE ... IN SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE MODE`, entry ids above the latest stored id, publication after commit), `sql_event_log_server_encrypted` (`make_storage` / `layer_storage` / `layer_storage_subtle`: a table pair per hashed identity and store, new ids decided by `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING`, ids in chunks of 200) and `sql_event_log_server_unencrypted` (`make_storage` / `layer_storage`: per-store sequences behind a locked store row, history lookups as `UNION ALL` index lookups in chunks of 200, `hashtextextended` index on PostgreSQL, storage-owned transactions holding a permit through publication). `pyfect.devtools` (`fx.devtools`, Effect's `effect/devtools`): `schema` (the `Request` / `Response` TypedDicts and their JSON codecs), `client` (`DevToolsClient`, `make`: pings every 3 s, answers `MetricsRequest` with a `MetricsSnapshot`, reconnects every second while the socket fails, drains the queue on close; `make_tracer` / `layer_tracer` wrapping the current tracer so each span is sent when it starts and ends, with its events), `server.run(handle)` over a `SocketServer`, and `layer_socket`, `layer_web_socket(url)`, `layer(url)` (the `websockets` extra, imported when called). Ports, one pytest per Effect title: `tests/oracle/test_event_journal.py` (21), `test_event_log_session_auth.py` (9, with `EventLogIdentityDerivation.test.ts`), `test_event_log_message.py` (5), `test_event_log.py` (22), `test_event_log_server_encrypted.py` (5 titles, the shared encrypted suite on memory, SQLite and PostgreSQL storage), `test_event_log_server_unencrypted.py` (18 titles, the regression suite on memory, SQLite and PostgreSQL storage), `test_event_log_remote.py` (8, with the three server-backed titles over `rpc` `make_no_serialization` connections to one server, plus a pyfect check), `test_sql_event_journal.py` (26, the conflicts suite and the sql-sqlite-node titles), `test_pg_sql_event_journal.py` (10), `test_sql_event_log_server_encrypted.py` (12 sql-sqlite-node titles plus a per-id ack check), `test_sql_event_log_server_unencrypted.py` (the 12-title storage suite on SQLite and PostgreSQL, the 3 sql-sqlite-node titles, plus a commit-order check), `test_pg_sql_event_log_server.py` (the 2 commit-visibility titles and the 3-title encrypted storage suite on PostgreSQL), `test_event_row.py` (10, plus a key-field check) and `test_devtools_client.py` (6); `tests/type_checks.py::eventlog_checks` pins the event, group, write, handler, row, compaction, layer and devtools types. Pythonic decisions (the first until M15, which replaced it with `encoding.schema_binary`): Effect's `SchemaBinary` (MessagePack) is not ported, so payloads, write requests and changes are their schemas' JSON as UTF-8 bytes (`eventlog/_binary.py`); pyfect peers cannot read Effect journals or talk to Effect servers, and base64 makes payloads a third larger (the chunk-pipelining title writes three chunks of payload where Effect writes four, to get the same five parts). Crypto is the optional `eventlog` extra (`cryptography`, imported lazily); `make_encryption_subtle` takes an `AesGcm` cipher where Effect takes a `Crypto`, which is how the tests count encryptions. Remote and storage callables take keyword arguments (`write(identity=, store_id=, entries=)`); a compactor is `effect(entries, write)` with `write(entry, source | None)`. An `EventRow`'s patch fields and key are typed by `make(..., key_type=, patch_fields=)` (pyright cannot compute `Partial<Omit<Row, Key>>`; see the table in section 4), else `Mapping[str, object]` and `object`; a key field missing from the schema is a `ValueError` at `make`. DevTools metrics come from `metric.snapshot_unsafe`. The EventRow convergence property runs 60 seeded random command lists instead of fast-check's 200 runs. The SQL modules support SQLite and PostgreSQL only: pyfect has no MySQL or SQL Server driver, so another dialect is a `ValueError` defect at `make`, and the MySQL / SQL Server branches of the SQL titles are not ported. Not ported: `EventJournalIndexedDb` (browser-only); `EventLogMessage.test.ts` "entry frames" (a MessagePack framing detail). Review pass (2026-10-30): `EventLogRemote` `write` and `authenticate` now wrap every failure, an authentication error included, in their own `EventLogRemoteError` (only `changes` keeps one as it is, as in Effect); `event_log.layer`, the unencrypted server layers, `StorageService.with_transaction` and `ConnectionWrites.track` / `filter` check their effect, layer and stream arguments; `event_log.group` shares one `RR` TypeVar for handler and build requirements; `EventJournalService.with_lock` returns a typed `WithLock` protocol; `import pyfect` no longer loads `eventlog` (it loaded `rpc`).
